# Iron Asylum — Master Handoff (self-contained)

Single source of truth for continuing the app in a new chat. Upload it alongside the current HTML; nothing else required. **This doc was compacted at V88, V96, V100 and V151** — session logs are replaced by one digest line each; architecture, current behavior, tuning values, invariants and standing decisions are retained in the numbered sections. Prior full detail lives in older chats if ever needed.

**Maintenance rule (standing) — ONE doc, one filename.** This file is the only project document for the app. `IRON_ASYLUM_HANDOFF_1_1.md` is a **permanent stable handle: never version-tagged, always overwritten in place.** The version lives in the Current state block below, nowhere in the filename. There is no separate fold-ins or delta doc — that split existed until V151 and it drifted, which is exactly the failure two changelogs always produce. At the end of a session: update Current state, fold durable behavior into the numbered sections, promote standing lessons to §10b and rulings to §11f, and append ONE digest line at the tail. **Never append a session narrative to the top.** A template for the session entry sits at the very end of this file.

## Current state
- **Working file:** `iron_asylum_v4RUNNINGV190.html` — **V190**; `<meta name="ia-version">`=**190**. Built in order after V189. **D39 BUILT (the section owns the round count). Queue: nothing ruled-and-unbuilt. Open: D11 (evidence-gated re-anchor), V178 deferred single-arm press, F8 beginner anchor (watch), long-run time cap on slow chart rows (advisory, §12), Monday-race race week (§12).**
- **Most recent work (V190): D39 — the section owns the round count.** Mario, from a device screenshot of THE HALF MANNY W2 Saturday (Full Body Support): *"how did the workout say 3 rounds but the workouts say 2 sets?"* **The banner was never prescribed.** A superset's round count and its members' set count were always ONE number — `vsets(3)` at the call site means "3 rounds of this pair" — and it was stamped onto each member's detail string because the string was the only carrier a section had. Three readers then tried to recover it with `^(\d+)×`, a regex that does not know the no-load grammar (`N sets — RPE x`) that `bodyweightSweep`/`_bwSetsFromDetail` rewrites 21.9% of items into, and all three fell to a **hardcoded 3**. Measured over 1,260 builds / 85,977 superset sections: **41.2% of banners fabricated, 29.5% contradicting their own members** (17,032 × banner-3-vs-members-2, 7,783 × banner-3-vs-members-4). **The second reader was `sessionTimeEst`, carrying its own copy of the same regex and the same constant — 45.7% of training days shipped a wrong duration, mean 4.3 min, worst 6.4 on a card reading 75.** The third, `recoveryDeload`'s power cut, silently no-ops on the same grammar. **A separate defect fell out of the same sweep:** `preventionDoseSweep` (D2) rewrote member strings per item and reached only the members carrying `@ RPE`, skipping the `_GRAMMAR_BALLISTIC` ones — 262/6,889 prevention supersets desynced against **0/42,423 on every other focus**, so D2 was the sole source, and the old banner then stamped whichever count landed in `items[0]`. **Shipped on D39-i–v (§11f):** `sec.rounds` written at all 23 generator call sites from the same expression that writes the members; one resolver `_secRounds` with **no fallback constant** (authored number → `rounds:null` meaning not-a-round-block → label → legacy min across members parsing BOTH grammars → null); the header shows the section **label** and synthesizes "N Rounds — no rest between exercises" only when there is a real N; member rows lose the count from the **rendered** string only (`_stripLeadingSets`, `i.detail` untouched — `parseRx` has five callers and the add-sheet's day set-count was CORRECT, it was the banner that was wrong); `sessionTimeEst` reads the same oracle; D2 halves `sec.rounds` once and normalises members to it via `_setLeadingSets`. 9 preset sections get `rounds:null` (AMRAP / EMOM / every-N-min / for-time / alternate-every); the both-grammar min recovers the other 25 that the old derivation fabricated. Gates 16/16 across 2,268 builds / 159,957 superset sections against an **independent oracle derived from the authoring contract, never from `_secRounds`**; 8/8 sabotage, 0 survivors, each tripping a **different** named gate (M1→G6, M2→G8, M3→G1, M4→G3, M5→G4, M6→G9, M7→G10, M8→G7); after-picture fabricated / contradicted / desynced / wrong-duration all **0.0%**; blast radius 1,080 hunks over 756 configs, 36 differing and **all `support_prevention`**, 100% classified into three classes with one root (§12). §5/§8/§10b/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V189): the coaching slate from the V187 program review — D36 + D37 + D38, one build, all three concurred.** Mario asked for a coach's read of THE HALF MANNY as generated; the run side was Nike's shape and correct, the lifting calendar was not. **D36 (hinge placement on NRC programs):** every week 1–12 put the posterior-chain main (RDL / trap bar / sumo / deadlift 3×8–13) plus a plyo superset on Friday, the recovery run, the day before the long run; W11 had `Jump squats 3×10` the eve of the 12.5-mile peak. Root cause: `deconflictLegLiftDays` treats every leg-load run alike and its same-day fallback accepts any non-run day, so the Tue→Fri move was lateral. Measured V188 across 324 NRC configs: 90 leg-heavy days on the long-run eve, 9 on the long-run day. Ruled: hard days hard. New `nrcLegLiftPlacement` (NRC only, keyed off week 1's `isNRC` long run): the long-run day and its eve are FORBIDDEN to pull/legs; every ordered (pull, legs) pair is scored on one cost table (`_nrcLegCost`: pull prefers a speed day; legs prefers a clean day or the day after a speed session so the calf/ankle armor does not stack on the run that already loads the calves; a recovery run is the easy day being protected and costs 1; day-before-speed 2; day-after-long 2); min cost, then widest spacing between the two axial days, then earliest legs day; non-leg roles keep template order over what is left; no finite pair → the NSW rule runs as before (2-day Sat/Sun). `deconflictSameRegion` scores leg roles off the SAME table (forbidden = 10), measured necessary: without it the press-cluster swap walked the hinge onto the Tuesday recovery run on `mon/tue/wed/thu/sat` and onto the long-run day on 60 of 729 fuzz layouts. Two coaching holes the measure pass found in the first draft and closed: a recovery-run day scored as "clean", and day-after-long scored like day-after-speed (`mon/wed/fri/sun` had squats the morning after the long run). **D37 (shakeout the day before):** D14a rested the day that held the race, leaving Nike's 2-mile two days out and a bare rest day on the eve. Now the race pin (a) trades the last pre-race run with an earlier recovery run when the layout dealt the race-week speed session last (a Fri race on `tue/thu/sat/sun` had the marathon's 15:00 progression tempo the day before), then (b) moves the last recovery run onto the eve, or two days out, whichever is first a train day with nothing on it; the day it came from rests; a user rest day is respected (Mon–Fri athlete racing Sunday shakes out Friday); a Monday race has no eve in its week and is left alone. **D38 (no lifting from two days out):** overturns D34's "primer stays". `raceEveLiftPass` finds the race OR time trial by subtype over the last two weeks flattened Mon→Sun; race day and the eve carry no sections; two days out carries no lifting (a tier A post-run mobility block survives, it is recovery); a lift-only train day two days out becomes rest; titles `Race Day` / `Shakeout` / `Easy Run` (a stripped speed day keeps its subtype). MANNY on V189: Thu = speed + hinge, Fri = recovery run + light upper, weeks 1–12; race week Mon sharpener + bench primer, Tue front squat primer + ankle/toe pair, Thu 25:00 + trap bar primer (three days out, flagged, accepted), Fri rest, Sat 2-mile shakeout no lift, Sun race no lift. Note copy: "Your hinge day rides a speed session so hard days stay hard. Nothing heavy lands the day before your long run." Gates 12,344/0 (648-config grid + 372 seeded-fuzz configs, calendar-math oracle: no leg role on long/eve/after, hinge never on a recovery run when a clean-enough day existed, race/eve/two-out lift-free, shakeout on the eve when it is a train day, last pre-race run is a recovery run, no run after the race); 155 non-NRC configs byte-identical V188→V189; 800-config fuzz 0 errors; sabotage 15/15 tripped, 0 not-applied, 0 survivors (S5, the region-pass guard, survived the 648 grid and tripped only after the fuzz section was folded into the gate: a coverage hole, not a dead mutation); 8 hunks +130/−4, 100% classified, every removed line named.
- **Prior work (V188): D14a — race alignment. Count BACK from the race, not forward from today.** Before V188 the race date was stored, warned about, and moved nothing: a half built 10 weeks out ran 14 weeks from today with the table's race week four weeks after the real race. Root cause of the Sunday-race quirk found: `trainDays` is built in `ALL_DAYS_ORDER` (Sunday-first) while weeks are Monday-anchored, so "long run = last train day" could never be a Sunday. Shipped, all nine calls concurred: (1) `raceAlignment(goalId, raceIso, eventOn, todayIso?)` — pure, module-scoped next to the tables: start = Monday of race week − (plan−1) weeks, race weekday from the entered date (THE HALF MANNY: Sun Dec 6 → start Mon Aug 31, race day Sunday; Mario's own Dec 5 slip was caught mid-design), opening week, behind weeks, future weeks, the source's floors (5K/10K 4, half 6, marathon 12) as `underFloor`; (2) days between the derived start and `prog.blockOpen` (creation day, written only by alignment) are "behind you" — `dayBeforeStart` extended, same `.pre` cell, same exclusions, not missed; a manual `setProgStart` back-date keeps V174 behaviour; (3) race further out than the plan → future-dated start so it peaks on race week, copy names the gap; (4) under the floor → first-person warning ("I don't recommend under 6 weeks for the half") and a one-tap offer of the shorter plan (`offerShorterPlan`, marathon→half→10K→5K), never an auto-switch; (5) race-week pin — the race session (found BY SUBTYPE, never by position: the spacer and multi-sport both move it) lands on the race weekday even if it is a rest day, the day that held it and every train day after the race rest; computed once in buildProgram as `cfg._racePin`, read by both the cardio engine and the day loop, deleted before the cfg is stored so `refreshProgram` recomputes it; (6) NRC run days lay out Monday→Sunday (`_ISO_ORDER`), NSW and base cardio keep the file order byte-identical; (7) hero/reminder guards ride the extended predicate for free; (8) the race-date wizard card and the name-step start block are rewritten for the four race goals — derived read-only cell ("set by race date"), resolve copy, week pips (behind / this week / race), no legend; NSW copy untouched; (9) a **Race Day** control on the week view replaces the start control on aligned programs (`setProgRace`: re-derives the start, keeps `blockOpen`, `refreshProgram` re-pins untrained weeks). Plus the no-source rule: three user-facing "Nike" strings removed (two were V186's, one V171's). Gates 80/0 with an independent date oracle (the engine matched it on every case where my hand expectations were wrong); 13/13 sabotage, 0 not-applied; fuzz 700 configs with random race dates / 27,443 run sessions / 0 violations (pin-day, rest-after-race, pin-not-stored assertions); V187→V188 differential 180 builds, 100% classified (ISO reorder on Sunday calendars, race-week pin, cutback-copy); 19 hunks, +186/−7.
- **Most recent work (V187): the NRC fidelity batch — F2 + F6 + F7, one build.** Mario: "Can we go ahead and build it all out and do D14a after?" **F2 (ruled "Easy day concur"):** Nike's week is five runs on all four plans and no half/marathon this app ever built had five — `SPORT_CEILINGS` capped at 4 and the `>=5` layouts in `getNRCSessionTypes` had never executed. `sportDayTargets` gains a fourth arg (the resolved lift goal, passed by BOTH callers so the wizard nudge and the engine cannot disagree); NRC run goals get ceiling 5 when the lift goal is `athletic` or `endurance` (the file's existing "performance lift paths" class — support_strength / support_athletic / support_prevention — the same test the governor and taper use for "cardio IS the objective") AND ≥6 training days. **Interpretation note for Mario:** the ruling said "endurance track"; there is no `support_endurance` focus — the class that exists is the three support_* focuses, and that is what shipped. Measured: a 6-day `support_athletic` half is now Nike's printed week (speed / 15:00 / speed / lift / 30:00 / long); race week carries all three Nike recovery runs with the shakeout the day before; the V153 chooser reaches the brute-force spacing minimum on every 5-run layout; `balanced`/`strength`/`hypertrophy`/`fatloss` byte-identical. **F6:** half W10 "13.1K Dress Rehearsal" gets `rehearsal:true`, subtype `Long Run — Dress Rehearsal`, its own note (race start time, race kit, race fueling, pace stays the progression, distance steps back on purpose); cutback flag kept so the dose is still 8 mi; D34's tier A holds. **F7:** Nike's effort scale on the Run-paces chips' unit line (`/mi · 6/10`), NRC programs only — NSW's CHI calls tempo RPE 8–9, so NSW chips stay bare. Gates 337/0 (independent brute-force spacing minimum, race-week order, chip text, V186→V187 differential 100% classified into F2/F6 with every other config identical); 11/11 sabotage, 0 not-applied; fuzz 800 configs / 31,445 run sessions / 1,135 five-run weeks / 0 violations; 9 hunks classified, +29/−9.
- **Most recent work (V186): race → NRC, test → NSW — the 5K and 10K plans that were in the project and never used.** Mario asked for an audit of the four NRC guides against the build. Half and marathon transcription was clean to the line (chart 15×11 exact, 32 weeks verified); the finding that mattered was that `Run a 5K` / `Run a 10K` routed to NSW — 3 runs/week, one quality session alternating 800s and CHI, no hills/fartlek/ladders, **and no race day** (the last week was `2×12 min tempo` + a tapered LSD while the wizard said "peaks right at race day"). Coaching position, argued and concurred: NSW is the PST's program — austere, not hardcore — and it caps a 5K/10K athlete's ceiling; NRC is the race's program. Rule written down: **race → NRC (5K/10K/half/marathon); test → NSW (pace goal, mile time, 1.5-mile, base).** Shipped `NRC_5K_TABLE` / `NRC_10K_TABLE` (8 weeks each, V171 grammar, 21 of 30 speed slots byte-identical to the half/marathon entries, 9 new sessions transcribed), `NRC_GOALS` hoisted to module scope as the single routing set, fixed 8-week length, ceiling 3→4, **no race date = the full 8 weeks ending in a TIME TRIAL** (the evidence D11 wants), the time trial treated as the event by the lifting taper (§4b — found by the gate: without it a Power + Strength block sat on time-trial day), and **an NRC run goal pins the program length** (§3b — found by fuzz: a 5K + 12-week swim base made a 12-week program whose weeks 9–12 clamped to race week). Folded F3 (half W9 hold flag), F4 (race-week recovery indices in calendar order) and F5 (race week keeps Nike's LAST N recovery runs, not the longest — the shakeout was being dropped on every layout). Gates 379/0 with an independent hard-seconds parser over the OCR-derived expectations; 18/18 sabotage, 0 not-applied; NSW goals byte-identical V185→V186 (24 builds); half/marathon differential 2,240 days, 100% classified (10 F3, 24 race-week); fuzz 1,000 configs / 33,560 run sessions / 0 violations; 23 hunks 100% classified, +123/−29. §2/§3/§11f/§12/§10b.
- **Most recent work (V185): D1 + D2 — `_pattern` disagreed with the exercise library, and `_pattern` wins.** Mario, from a device screenshot of the swap sheet: *"you don't have KB single arm press in here because you think it's a chest exercise right? Cause it's more shoulder."* He was right. **`EXLIB.shoulder` has listed `Kettlebell single-arm press` next to Barbell overhead press and Arnold press since the library was written — the classifier disagreed with the pool, and the swap sheet reads the classifier**, so the sheet tagged it HORIZONTAL PRESS and offered the bench family as "same job" while putting Arnold press and DB incline press under "different job". Cause: the bare `/press/` catch-all defaults horizontal, and the only two real exercises that reached it (`Kettlebell single-arm press`, `Log press (Kettlebell clean + press)`) are both overhead — **it was wrong on 100% of what it caught.** **D2 came out of the same sweep and was the bigger find.** `/row|pulldown|straight-arm/` ran before the vpull test, so `lat pull` in the vpull rule was DEAD CODE and every pulldown in the app scored as a row. Not cosmetic: `lowback/protect` drops the `row` pattern while its own pool override deliberately hands that athlete `rowPool=['Straight-arm pulldown']` and a `backCompoundPool` containing `Lat pulldown` — so **`applyInjuryFilter` deleted the exact movements the override had picked for being spine-safe, and the comment there described the opposite of what shipped. Gate G3 proves the drop on V184 and its repair on V185.** D2-b puts `Straight-arm pulldown` on vpull as a SCOPE call (lat-dominant vertical-plane loading), not a technique one; `null` was rejected because it fills a MAIN slot and an invisible main lift is the Ball slams mistake. D2-c adds `vpull` to the `elbow/workaround` cap so pulldowns don't walk off the cap on the way out — which also closes a pre-existing gap where chinups were already uncapped there. Gates: **163 assertions, 8/8 sabotage**, 4 hunks 100% classified, 3 lines removed / 5 non-comment lines added. **The sabotage runner was wrong before it was right and two sabotages were no-ops — see §10b.** **Both rulings change `_pats` counts in `pick()`, so affected programs re-roll on their existing seed; shipped together so it is one re-roll, not two.**
- **Most recent work (V183): D1/D2 — Done stops following the athlete down the page.** Mario, from a device screenshot of Week 2 Thursday: "move the done button to the bottom of the workout page, so you have to scroll all the way down to see it — right now it's just present all throughout the page which is unsightly and almost rushing the user to be done." **The visual complaint was the smaller half of it.** `.detail-foot` was `position:sticky;bottom:0`, so Done was tappable from anywhere on the card — including from above Log This Session — and a day could be marked complete before the session was logged. Inline, the button sits after the log form and the order reads do the work → log it → close it out. Mocked three placements (sticky / inline-with-band / inline-bare) in a scrollable phone frame; Mario ruled **C, inline bare** — the surface fill and top rule existed to mask content sliding UNDER a floating bar, and with nothing sliding under it a divider with nothing on the far side is a drawn-on footer. **No scroll cue** (the `0 / N LOGGED` header chip already reports progress; a cue at the Done button reintroduces the nudge being removed). `.foot-hint` deleted in the same lines — dead CSS, zero usages, its only copy was the Partial-era line V181 retired. **Measured, not assumed:** nothing in JS reads `#detailFoot` (only `#detailStatusRow`), and sticky keeps an element in normal flow, so document height is unchanged and the V152 scroll-restore offset needed no adjustment. Gates: 30 assertions through an independent CSS-rule reader, **14/14 sabotage**, V182's full 2,662-assertion suite and fuzz re-run green, **extracted JS byte-identical to V182 (sha `ed7c9518`) with a control proving the comparison non-vacuous** — so the build is provably CSS-only. 2 hunks, 100% classified, +13/−4.
- **Most recent work (V182): D1–D6 — the log form's time and distance fields become scroll wheels, and a silent chart exclusion stops being reachable.** Mario, from a device screenshot of the generic run form: "can we make these instead of text boxes the rolly wheel options." **Measured before designing:** the two boxes in that screenshot are the *generic* run form, which fires on 1,404 of 7,464 run sessions (18.8%) across 216 builds — and only on NRC speed days (Intervals/Tempo/Fartlek/Hills, 100% of them, zero on 5K/10K/pace-goal/base). The other 81.2% get a dose form with two number boxes and **derive pace automatically** (V148 `_dd.pace`), so a pace was hand-typed on speed days and nowhere else. **The find that justified the build:** `_paceStrToSec` is `^(\d+):(\d{2})` — `9:3`, `930`, `9.5` and `~9:30` all save into `ia_logs_` without complaint and are then silently dropped by `recoveryPaceWeekly`, `_recoveryPaceEntry` and the V156 wizard re-seed. No error, no empty state, no way for the athlete to know. A wheel can only emit `M:SS`, so the failure stops existing rather than getting validated. **Bounds are measured, not chosen:** the engine prescribes 5:00–14:30/mi across goals × mile-best rows, so Mario's 4–17 pace column clears both ends; the only `reps_dist` distances are 400m and 800m, which at those paces span 0:55–8:27, so rep time takes its own 0–9 column rather than inheriting the 4:00 floor. **Shipped:** `_IAW_SPEC` + `iaWheelHTML`/`iaWheelInit`/`_iawCommit` as a single writer; miles = [—,0–99] × [.0–.9], pace = [—,4–17] × [00–59], rep time = [—,0–9] × [00–59]. **No Log button (D3)** — each wheel drives a *hidden* input that keeps the field's original id and dispatches `input` on scroll-settle, so `persistLogFields` and both listener arrays are byte-identical and Done/Skip remains the only commit. Gates: 2,662 assertions, **15/15 sabotage** after S13 initially survived (the index clamp was never exercised until G16 drove over- and under-scroll — a vacuous guard, closed with an assertion rather than by deleting the clamp); fuzz 864 configs engine-identical, 900 form shapes, 20,000 hostile values, 0 violations; 7 hunks 100% classified. **The fuzz's own reference was wrong first** — see §10b.
- **Most recent work (V181): D1–D4 — the day has two states, both toggle, and the banner keeps only the streak.** Mario: "i dont like the skip partial or completed day buttons … i think we need a 'done' or 'skip' only day because users swapping and skipping workouts is still showing up and getting after it." Measure pass agreed before design: `computeStreak` and `completedCount` already treated `partial` as complete, so Partial was a self-grade with no engine consequence — a guilt button. Mocked first (`MOCK_done_skip_footer.html`, five panels; Mario ruled small Skip button under a large Done, no adaptation ledger, no "Adapted" kicker, streak stands alone). Second finding during ruling: V180 had **no unmark at all** (three exhaustive states; re-tap re-saved the log), and an accidental Done on the final scheduled day burned the one-shot season latch. Shipped: `.status-btn` row → `.status-stack` (full-width signal `Done`, small bordered `Skip`); `handleDayStatus` toggles — tapping the active state deletes the `ia_comp_` record, keeps the `ia_logs_` entry, re-renders the footer, and on the final day calls `releaseSeasonShown()`; `statusOf` normalizes legacy `partial`→`complete` so pre-V181 records render as Done everywhere; reminder popup is Done/Skip; workout and streak popups drop the `This Block` stat (season popup keeps Workouts/Skips/Weeks). 13 hunks all classified, 43 assertions, 12/12 sabotage, 4,320-build engine-identity fuzz zero divergence (engine untouched by construction).
- **Most recent work (V180): D8 — race week foot & ankle is dynamic mobility only.** Mario brought a Gemini prompt on taper-phase foot/ankle logic (T-21 full, T-14 halve + no plyos, T-7 mobility only, no deep static stretching) and invited pushback. Audit against the engine: plyos already drop at T-14 (`inTaper`), accessories already go to zero at T-14 (stricter than the 50%), the main-lift primer continues as a neural touch. The one point that landed: V179 shipped the full foot block through race week, and a draw could put single-leg soleus raises on T-5. D8 fixes that with a fixed race-week pair. Pushed back on `days_until_race` (second clock vs the week invariant), on a per-exercise tag schema (the sidecars already are those tags), and on notification copy (no notification layer pre-Capacitor; the swap sheet cannot re-add plyos because no power block exists to swap into). 131 assertions, 7/7 sabotage, fuzz clean, 3 hunks.

- **Most recent work (V178): the Darren fold — unilateral leg work, foot & ankle prehab, three new power tiers, and the core pillar that never rotated.** Mario brought three Meta AI screenshots of Instagram workouts (a full-body athlete session and a 20-movement unilateral leg/hip "lab") and asked how to fold them in. Ruled D1–D6 (§11f). Two-thirds of the list was already in the file or dropped into existing pools; the real gap was foot/ankle — the calves pool loads plantar flexion only, nothing trained the tibialis, dorsiflexion, bent-knee soleus or ankle range. Three things the gate found unasked: `EXLIB.core` is dead (picked into `ex.core`, consumed by nothing — the pillar table is the live source); the hot-tomorrow core branch took `slice(0,2)` so HALF MANNY was getting Side plank + Pallof press for 14 straight weeks (D6 fixed it with the rotation shape `dynamic_bracing` already used); and D6 immediately exposed `Landmine rotations` with no bodyweight sub because it had never been reachable. 118 gate assertions, 17/17 sabotage trips, 19,440-config fuzz clean.

- **Most recent work (V177): the carry that named the wrong thing, and the side that was never prescribed.** Mario, from a device screenshot of `Suitcase carry (KB) — 3×40 yards heavy`: why can't I swap this for a DB farmer carry, and does the implement matter at all? Both answers were no. **Mechanically:** there is no DB carry anywhere in the file — `EXLIB.carry` held three KB-named movements and `_AUX_FAMILY` added `Trap bar farmer carry` (swap-target only, never generated) plus four laterality variants, so the sheet was offering `Suitcase carry (KB) left` as a SAME JOB substitute for `Suitcase carry (KB)`. **Coaching:** the implement is over-specification on a carry — a 70 lb bell and a 70 lb dumbbell in one hand are the same carry and the same 70 — and it was splitting one `ia_exw_` thread in two. Mario's first instinct was to collapse all carries into one name; **pushed back and he reversed** — farmer is two hands (lateral moment cancels), suitcase is one (anti-lateral flexion is the entire point), overhead adds the shoulder, and one logged number would have meant three things. **Shipped on D-b/D-c/D-d/D-e/D-f + Q-A/Q-B (§11f):** the vocabulary is `Farmer carry` / `Suitcase carry` / `Overhead carry`; `_AUX_GEAR` sidecar replaces name-inference in `_auxGearOK` (the rename made the regexes blind and their fallthrough is `return true`, i.e. a loaded carry on a bodyweight athlete); laterality folds into the base movement with the side in the detail; `EX_RENAMED_V113` **flips direction** so every load logged under either spelling resolves forward with nothing migrated; `Single-arm farmer's carry` and `Farmer carry relay` retarget from farmer to **suitcase** (both are one-hand carries that had been folding into the two-hand thread — the exact error the three-way split exists to prevent); trap bar retained as a swap option, name unchanged; the dead bodyweight carry pool deleted. **The real defect was D-d:** both generated finishers wrote a hardcoded `3×40 yards` over a seeded pick from a pool holding two one-hand movements, so a suitcase or overhead carry shipped with **no side instruction** — forty yards, unspecified arm, half the trunk work the movement exists for. `_carryRx(dist,name,qual)` derives it from `_CARRY_PER_SIDE`, keyed off the pool rather than a regex, in the curated library's house grammar (`3×40 yards each, heavy`). The curated sources never had this bug; only generation did. Gates: syntax; **766 assertions** across vocabulary/alias/gear/grammar, the twelve surviving `/carry/` regex consumers, and 108 end-to-end builds (255 carry rows, all three names, zero bodyweight carries, 255/255 side-grammar correct); **15/15 sabotages tripped** after two overlapping-guard holes were reshaped (§10b) and one mutation was caught **not applying** — a no-op sabotage reads exactly like an under-asserted gate, so the runner now asserts its own anchor count; **D-f proven inert** by a V176→V177 bodyweight differential, 432/432 byte-identical; **full-lattice differential 1,728 configs — 1,072 raw diffs, 1,728/1,728 identical after carry normalisation, 0 unclassified, so the blast radius is provably carries only**; fuzz 3,000 builds / 5,770 carry rows / 0 violations; **23 diff hunks, 100% classified**, +62/−29. §5/§8b/§10b/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V176): the ruled close-out slate, built as one version.** D8 (subtype-first interference + `[\s-]*min`), D18/D33/D34 (the run is the day: `d18LongRunDayPass` tiers every NRC long-run day A/≥75 min on feet, B/45–75, C/<45 off the session's own `dose`; tier A = 5-item post-run mobility block and the day retitles `Post-Run Mobility`; tier B = upper/trunk ≤8 sets, no power, no hinge; carries banned on every long-run tier; race day untouched, dress rehearsal forced A on every row, taper takes normal math; NRC-only scope via `isNRC`), D9 (mile-time validation: <3:00 / >25:00 / malformed rejected at `doGenerate` with a wizard return, out-of-chart entries get a named-row advisory via `#mileAdvisory`; the five wizard `beginner:660` sites folded to the engines' 690), D12b+D31 (W5 half tempo = `8:00 Marathon → 8:00 Half → 7:00 10K` with printed paces; on any chart row where half ≥ marathon — today row 15 only, Nike's own 6:00:00 marathon floor — a `detailIfHalfSlow` two-rung fallback `12:00 Marathon → 11:00 10K` ships instead, self-expiring if Nike fixes the row), D10 (pace-goal programs lead the Run-paces chips with a `Goal` chip: target time over distance). Gates: 194/0 with synthetic D18 units; 18/18 sabotages tripped after two vacuous holes were closed with units (the 8-set cap never binds on MANNY; `isNRC` is masked by `dose` on run_base); differential harness `scan_interference_diff_v176.js` compared 2,048 sessions V175→V176 day-aligned — 624 long-class moves (NRC Long Run + NSW LSD), 114 hyphen-minute moves, 0 unexpected; fuzz 144 builds 0 violations; 23 diff hunks 100% classified.
- **Prior work (V175): the trip that rewrote the week, and the superset of one.** Reported by Diaz over iMessage, screenshot forwarded by Mario: a one-day travel overlay put him on chair and table work for the whole week, and his Tuesday card showed two "3 Rounds — no rest between exercises" headers each over a single exercise. Reproduced exactly at his seed (35757, Balanced / Hybrid / commercial / Mon–Sat / 5 mi base → 6 weeks): `applyOverlays` spliced whole weeks (`rebuilt.weeks[w] = variant.weeks[w]`) even though §6b already said dates were the source of truth; and `bodyweightSweep`'s within-day dedupe collapsed `Barbell row` and `KB single-arm row` onto inverted rows, removed the ITEM and left `superset:true` on a one-item section, which the V162 renderer demotion did not catch because it only fires at `items.length>1`. Measured over 14,112 builds before ruling: zero singleton supersets on any healthy loaded tier, ~6 per program on bodyweight, every injury variant on every tier (`Explosive finisher` losing its plyo under a knee plan, `Strength` losing the push under shoulder-protect). **Shipped on D27/D28/D29/D30 (§11f):** overlays splice by DAY (both types) with the day stamped `_ovKey` and the swap universe keyed by that stamp (D27); a final `singletonSupersetSweep` on every tier plus a render guard for pre-V175 snapshots (D28); `to` is inclusive, the sheet says THROUGH, the chip says "Back to normal <to+1>", the sheet preview speaks in dates and day counts (D29); the Maintenance Week pill is earned only when every training day is a hotel day, trip days get a 2px underline in the strip and a Travel tag on the hero and the session header (D30, mocked and concurred). Gates on the final artifact: syntax; **112 engine assertions** (variants built independently and compared by date, never by the splice's own bookkeeping; a V174 control proves the bridge/halfstep spill); **50 UI assertions** driven through the real `renderWeekView`, `openDetail` and `_renderTravelTab` with date fixtures computed from the clock; **12/12 + 15/15 sabotage** after two vacuous guards were found and removed (§10b); **fuzz 2,500 zero-overlay configs** (healthy loaded tiers byte-identical 432/432; V175 == D28-normalised V174 on 2,500/2,500, so D28 is provably the only generation change) and **600 random-overlay trials, 1,149 overlays of four kinds, 0 violations** against a date oracle; blast radius **21 hunks, 20 semantic + 1 blank line, 100% classified**, +162/−41. **Handoff correction:** V174 never appended its digest line (the header was updated, the tail was not) — reconstructed from the header this session, marked as such. §4/§6b/§10b/§11/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V174): the start date, asked for.** Mario's opener: nothing prompts a start date. Finding: the control existed (week-view subrow, `setProgStart`), the DEFAULT did not — `buildProgram` returns `startDate:null` and nothing ever filled it, and a dateless program has no clock: `calcCurrentWeek` pinned to 1, no day dates or today, `restDayEligible` false, `paceShiftMap` null (injury overlays inert), `maybeShowReminder` early-return, and — the one that bites — the `refreshProgram` freeze was behind `if(prog.weeks && prog.startDate)`, so every trained week on a dateless program was rebuilt by the current engine on every boot, the exact §11e failure the freeze exists to prevent. **Shipped on D19/D20/D21/D23/D24/D25/D26 (§11f):** a `Start date` field on the `name` step under Program name plus a summary row (D19); prefilled with today, editable, never null (D20 — chips and a smart default were struck); a one-line resolve statement under the picker naming what the entry resolves to (D21); the freeze runs on `prog.weeks` alone with the touched-frontier carrying a dateless program (D23); **any weekday accepted, no snap** — week 1's days before the start are OUTSIDE the block: `dayBeforeStart(prog,w,d)` on dates, rendered as a dim non-tappable `.wk-day.pre` cell, excluded from `scheduledDays` (so streak and reminder in one line), from the DONE denominator (0/2 not 0/5), from the hero (a pre-start "today" falls to browse), from `openDayKey` and `restDayEligible` (D24); the one snap that survives is D25 — a start whose remaining week holds zero training days moves to the next Monday and the copy says so; Progress accumulators (ladder, drift, mileage dot) withhold a short week 1 exactly as they withhold the D6 cut week, gated on `startDate !== getWeekMonday(startDate)`, means and per-observation modules keep it (D26). `resolveStartDate(dateStr, restDays)` is pure and feeds the wizard line, `_applyWizardStart` (generate path: resolved start on `prog`, field stripped from stored `cfg` so `buildProgram` stays pure — fuzzed byte-identical) and `setProgStart` (week-view control gets D21/D25 for free). **D24 was reversed mid-session:** first ruled as forward-snap-to-Monday on the argument "no flag says week 1 was short"; that was wrong — `startDate !== getWeekMonday(startDate)` is that flag, derivable, no new state — and the survey (TrainerRoad, Runna both start any day; both align race plans to the race date, i.e. D14a) settled it. Gates: syntax; **93 assertions** (resolver against a hand-checked calendar, copy, predicates, generate path, scheduledDays/streak/reminder, D23 freeze on a dateless program with markers, week-view render, wizard render, Progress D26 pure + end-to-end via a `buildDotChart` spy, setProgStart); **24/24 sabotage** after one re-pin (W4 was passing by clock coincidence — the pre-start Wednesday was also in the future; re-pinned to a past week so only the guard can reject it) and one dead branch removed before the sweep (E10's no-clock `_dateCut:0` is unreachable once `_curWk=1` — provably ≤ `_maxTouched+1`); **fuzz 32,000 resolver trials / 0** (garbage, nulls, out-of-range, ISO-with-time; invariants: start is ISO, `[mon, mon+7]`, snapped ⇒ Monday, partial ⇒ ≥1 train day, copy never prints raw ISO) and **2,592-config `prog.weeks` byte-identical V172→V174 with the wizard field present on both**; blast radius 28 hunks, 100% classified. The V171/V172 suites are not in the project and were NOT re-run. **Handoff correction:** §4 said `setProgStart` writes `prog.weeks`; it never did (sets `startDate`, saves, recomputes `currentWeek`, re-renders) — fixed. §4/§8/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V171): the NRC tables were never Nike-verbatim, and the athlete was never told what Mile Pace was.** Reported from Mario's HALF MANNY device screens (W2 Monday `1:00 Mile, 2:00 5K (11:15/mi), 3:00 10K (11:35/mi), 2:00 5K, 1:00 Mile`; W2 Thursday `4x1:30 5K, 1x1:30 Mile … Recovery 45s/1:00`): unreadable as a session, and "Mile" with no number anywhere. **Mile Pace is Nike's first chart column** — the row anchor itself (`chartRow.mile`, 10:30 on Mario's row) — and `buildNRCSession`'s `paces` object simply had no `mile` key, so every "Mile Pace" in both tables printed blank. **The measure pass audited both tables page-by-page against the PDFs and the transcription contract (§2, V135) was not met:** four half speed sessions stored as titles only (W4 The Rundown, W7 The Shifter, W9 Rock n Roller, W12 Bring It Down — unrunnable), Power Pyramid without its recoveries, Nike's 15:00 recovery run dropped from 12 of 14 half weeks (the `recIdx % length` cycle then handed BOTH recovery days the long one — W12 was 60+60 against Nike's 15+60), W13's 5K recovery run stored as `31` minutes, nine marathon distance recovery runs stored as guessed minutes, and the race week's single Nike speed session run TWICE on a two-speed layout. **Two doctrine defects on top:** every long run built to RACE pace (Nike's progression run averages Recovery Pace; race pace appears only on race day) and the Intervals note demanded paces "precisely" (Nike: each pace is the middle of a range). **Shipped on D1/D1b/D2/D4a–e/D5/D6 (§11f):** `{mile}` fills; line-per-segment detail on `\n` with line 1 as the headline the compact renderers read (D2); both tables re-transcribed verbatim with recovery entries as minutes or `{mi,label}` (D4a/b); keep-the-LONGEST when a layout has fewer recovery slots than Nike entries (D4c); marathon range long runs dose the low end (D4d); half W5 is a `hold`, not a cutback (D4e); long runs carry Nike's progression copy and `tgt = recovery` (D6); the generic speed-day log form says what to log (D5, post-V154 ruling stands). **D3 (print the NRC guided-run name) was rejected — the app stands alone.** Gates: syntax; **1,861 assertions** against PDF-keyed references (recovery arrays, low-end long runs, chart rows for 9:30 and 10:30); **15/15 sabotage** after one anchor re-pin; hostile fuzz 112,982 builds / 0 failures once the FUZZ's own references were corrected (§10b); blast radius 21 hunks, 100% classified. **Knock-on measured, not assumed:** `_cardioInterference` moved on 41 of 384 NRC sessions — 34 because V170's distance regex read `0:30 Mile` as *30 miles* and charged the maximum leg bump (a mis-parse now gone), 7 intended (race-week remap, distance recovery runs). §3/§5/§8/§10b/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V170): the power block stops speaking a triple's grammar to a movement that is not a triple.** Reported from Mario's device: `Kettlebell swing 10×3 — crisp and explosive, ~2 min rest` on a week-1 Saturday, read as “heavy enough for 3 reps.” **The V110 `_REP_FLOOR` fix he remembered was real but reaches a different path** — `_repFloor` is called only from `scheme()` and `_swapDetailFor`, never from the power block, so the two systems held contradictory positions on the same movement at the same rep count. **The dose was not the defect.** A power triple is submaximal load at maximal intent, and 10×3×0.5 = 15 efforts against a 22-effort share is *under* budget. The defect is that the block hands the cheapest movement in `_POWER_COST` the recovery profile of the most expensive one: **`~2 min rest` was a hardcoded STRING LITERAL in both `pwRoles` entries**, so a 2.0 depth jump and a 0.5 swing got identical rest. Twenty minutes to accumulate thirty swings; no coach writes that. **Shipped on D1–D8 (§11f):** rest derives from cost (D1); reps are a property of the COST TIER, not the positional role, at 10 for the 0.5 tier (D2); cost wins and role is the tiebreak only within a tier (D3); the `max(3,…)` floor is preserved (D4); `_powerPrescribe` becomes the single writer and retires V144's 'crisp and explosive' along with the triple it described (D5); no load cue is added because density self-regulates (D6); `_repFloor` stays uncoupled (D7). **Ten was measured three ways.** The clamp binds **0/9** across experience × age at ten reps against **9/9 at three** — every athlete came out at exactly 10×3, so `_powerDose` was a constant wearing derivation machinery, the V130 failure returning at a different cost tier. All **43 hand-authored swing prescriptions** in the one-off library sit at 10–20 reps, one of them literally `10×10 — 30s rest between sets`: generation was the file's sole outlier. And ten lands on the low end of `_REP_FLOOR`'s independently written `[10,15]` window — convergence, not coupling. **D8 was rewritten by the fuzz and that is the durable lesson** — see §10b. **Knock-on, measured not assumed:** `_setCount` reads the leading N, so the power block's fatigue cost falls 10→4 units; section composition changes on **0.66%** of day-cards (+594 items, −113), and over-cap days fall **1145→750** with max session units **32→26** because the protected power block stopped over-charging. Gates: syntax; 1,917 assertions against independently transcribed cost/rest/reps/cue/budget references; **13/13 sabotage**; hostile fuzz 1,500 configs / 744,799 assertions / 0 fail; blast radius 2,011 hunks, **100% classified, 0 unclassified**. §5/§8/§10b/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V169): delete becomes reachable, gated behind archive.** Mario's scope: put a trashcan on the Progress restore bar and call it good. That placement resolves the design questions §12 had open — the bar only renders for an ALREADY-ARCHIVED program, so the destructive control can never sit beside a live one, and the accidental-start flow becomes archive-then-delete (two cheap steps, no new "delete if empty" path). All the machinery already existed: `deleteProg` handed off active, purged 14 store prefixes, and nulled `progressViewId`, with `renderProgressScreen`'s line-13649 fallback already re-resolving a dead view id. **The only wrong part was that it re-rendered the Programs list**, which from Progress leaves a stale bar for a program that no longer exists — `deleteProg(id,fromProgress)` now routes, and the one-arg V168 call site is byte-identical. Confirm is INLINE on the bar (V156's tier-2 card renders inside `progCardActions`, a surface Progress does not have), reusing `confirmProgId`/`confirmTier`; `showProgressScreen` disarms on entry so a confirm can never be stranded across a screen change. **DS compliance forced one addition:** the reference is explicit that chrome uses `asyIcon()` and never emoji, and the 27-icon set had no trash — added as a proper Lucide trash-2 entry, so it inherits `currentColor` and the 2px stroke like every other icon. **The V168 D3 interaction is surfaced, not hidden:** purging a program removes the source of every cross-program reuse pill reading it, so the confirm appends "Other programs are using these loads as starting points — those will go blank" when (and only when) the program holds loaded-lift history AND another program exists. Gates: syntax; 68 assertions; V168's full 2,112-assertion suite re-run green against V169 (the pill is untouched); 9/9 sabotage; blast radius 4 hunks, 4 removed lines, 100% classified. **T8 initially SURVIVED** — the suite never asserted WHICH surface re-rendered, because both render functions are DOM no-ops in the harness. Closed with a spy on the sandbox globals (function declarations bind to the VM global and can be reassigned), which is now the pattern for asserting routing rather than values. §8/§10b/§12.
- **Prior work (V168): loaded lifts stop forgetting across a program boundary — the cross-program reuse pill.** Reported from Mario's device: a fresh THE HALF MANNY week-1 Recovery Lift showed an empty Load box for `Dumbbell bench press` despite 8 weeks of logged data in another program. **Not a bug — two stacked design facts.** (1) V131 means the Load box NEVER pre-fills; the number lives only in the `\u21ba` pill. (2) `getExWeights()` is `ia_exw_`+`activeProgId`, so history is per-program, per Mario's own V156 D2/G1 ruling. V156's `*For(pid)` accessors are read by exactly two consumers — the Progress selector and the wizard seed — and **the seed is run-only** (mile-best + baseline distance). No lift number had ever crossed a program boundary. The gap was a lift seed. **Measure pass, 240 builds, file-harvested universes:** across a 14-week endurance block against an athletic block's first 8 weeks, **78.6% of loaded lift slots (median) have an exKey the prior program already holds**; 72.5% by distinct key; control (prior block also endurance) 85.7%, so goal isolates history far less than assumed. Misses are structural, not random — calf raises, good mornings, overhead carries, the `support_prevention` runner-armor accessories with no athletic ancestor. **The number that changed the design:** modelling the timeline, only **11.1% of slots render fresh (\u226421d) and 67.2% render stale**; fresh hits zero at endurance week 4 and never returns, weeks 4\u201314 sitting flat at 72\u201383% stale. The warning was therefore the dominant surface, not the exception, which forced D8. Shipped on D1\u2013D8 (\u00a711f): cross-program fallback fires only when the active store is empty for the key (D4), most-recent-qualifying-by-`ts` across every other program incl. archived (D3), aliases folded on a read-only clone with no write-back (D5), `STALE_LOAD_DAYS` as its own constant (D6), and `recencyLabel(ts,form)` rebuilt on ONE bucket table serving `ago` (pill sub-label, byte-identical to V167) and `age` (the sentence). **D8: the sentence fires once per rendered day card**, on the first stale pill in document order; every stale pill below goes dashed-and-silent. Reset lives in `openDetail`, which is also the re-render path for swap/skip/add, so an in-session edit re-arms it exactly once. Sub-label is age-only per Mario; it regains the source program name only when that pill's source differs from the one the sentence named. **Option 3 (a hoisted day-header notice) was mocked and rejected on its own merits** \u2014 one sentence summarising a set that varies per day (multiple source programs, mixed ages) is a claim that can be wrong, and Option 1 (glyph past a second threshold) was withdrawn after stacking revealed it gave the OLDEST numbers the LEAST challenge. **KB size chips deliberately stay same-program**: they pre-select a bell, which is closer to a prefill than a pill, and V131 forbids it. Gates: syntax; 2,112 assertions against independently transcribed V167 recency, bucket and staleness references; 10/10 sabotage; blast radius 8 hunks, 100% classified. **The sweep's one survivor was a badly built sabotage, not a dead gate** \u2014 see \u00a710b. \u00a75/\u00a78/\u00a710b/\u00a711f/\u00a712.
- **Prior work (V167): the Lift Ledger rebuilt on slot classification, and a false-negative found in the gate harness itself.** Mario's verdict on V165's ledger was that it was awful; diagnosis confirmed it. It led with e1RM — a number `scheme()` refuses to prescribe from by design — and computed it with `max()` across mixed rep ranges, so on a normal wave a 295×1 in W12 scores BELOW a 285×2 in W10: **the ledger reported a PR single as a regression.** Replaced with the set the athlete actually did. Mains take direction A (heaviest real set as headline, working weight across the block). Accessories take the **Range Ladder**, built on V137's own double-progression doctrine — each track is the prescribed rep range, dots are that session's sets, and when every set reaches the top the row lights ADD WEIGHT. That is the only module on the Progress screen that says what to do NEXT session, and it structurally solves Mario's lateral-raise objection: load is never framed as progress, and ego-loading before the dots reach the top just knocks them back to the bottom. **D11 measured, not assumed:** over 1,080 builds, 27 names ever occupy a main slot and **26 of them also appear as accessories** — DB bench press, the most-prescribed main lift in the app, is a main 62% of the time and an accessory 38%, and inside a single program the split is often an exact tie. No name list can ever be right, so classification now reads the SLOT the engine already froze into `ia_hist_` (V162 D0). Mario ruled one tier only, main-if-ever; the main chart then reads only main-slot sessions and discloses how many were withheld. **The find that matters most is in the tooling:** the V167 gate CRASHED on a sabotaged build, the sabotage harness scraped only the `PASS n FAIL n` summary, no summary printed, and it recorded SURVIVED — **a dead gate read as a passing one, for five of eleven mutations.** Harness fixed to treat a missing summary as a crash, gate hardened to report FAIL instead of dying, and the V165 (18) and V166 (11) sweeps re-run under the corrected harness — both still clean, so those results hold. Two further real holes surfaced once the harness was honest: a heaviest-set fixture where max-weight and max-e1RM happened to agree (unfalsifiable), and a Main section with only one item (making the `ii===0` rule untestable). Gates: syntax; 39 + 41 + 87 assertions green against the shipped artifact; 11/11 sabotage; blast radius 3 hunks, 4 removed lines, 100% classified; 450-config `prog.weeks` digest unchanged from V165, confirming render-only.
- **Prior work (V166): the easy/hard module gets rebuilt against the plan, and `_derived` is deleted.** Mario's device verdict on V165's class-scatter was blunt — he could not tell what it was for. Correct: a strip scatter asks a yes/no question in its title and answers with a distribution, and it compared the athlete's easy runs to their own HARD runs, which still made them do the interpreting. Three variants mocked (A verdict-only, B two-bar ranges, C against the prescription), each in a healthy AND a broken state because the broken state is the case the module exists to catch. **Ruled C.** "Actually easy" now means "at or slower than the ceiling the session was written with", read from `dose.tgt`/`dose.cap` already frozen into every dosed run. Verdict headline ("9 of 10 were too fast"), shaded too-fast region above the ceiling, aim line at target, violating dots in red. **Coverage problem the mock hid:** `dose.cap` is only written by the run_base time-dosed branch, so distance-dosed easy runs — i.e. every event program including Mario's own — carry `tgt` alone and would render with no ceiling, the one element the chart exists for. Fixed without inventing a constant: `_steadyCeilingFor()` reads PACE_CHART's `tempo` column and applies the engine's own steady formula `(tempo+recovery)/2` from line 3395 — the number the engine itself would have written. V165's class comparison survives as variant B behind it, so thin dose coverage still gets an answer. **`_derived` deleted** (4 sites): declared, never written, every read fell through — a silent no-op that read like a feature. **Gate caught two real defects:** (1) `_steadyCeilingFor` clamped `tempo` at the table edges while keeping the raw out-of-range `recSec`, breaking the matched pair the engine's formula depends on — a corrupt 1s/mi target produced a 3:03/mi ceiling that would mark every run "too fast"; (2) a source assertion matched the deletion's own tombstone COMMENT rather than code, a vacuous-assertion near-miss caught and rebuilt. Gates: syntax; 41 V166 assertions against an independently re-derived ceiling reference; V165's 87 still green; 11 sabotage builds, 11 tripped; blast radius 7 hunks, 100% classified. **Engine differential, because `_derived` is engine-side and not render-side:** 450 configs (5 goals × 5 focuses × 3 experiences × 3 equipment × 2 seeds), `prog.weeks` byte-identical to V165, 0 errors — and proven non-vacuous, since the first control chosen (`benchRM`) failed to move the digest and investigation showed why: `scheme()` documents that `rm/hasReal/exName` are retained in the signature only so call sites stay untouched. A wave-set control moves the digest; V166 does not.
- **Prior work (V165): the Progress screen stops counting and starts answering.** Design-first session, six rulings before a line of code. Mario's framing killed the flagship mid-design: **tonnage is a coach's metric, not an athlete's** — it moves when an exercise is added, when a deload lands, when a barbell lift swaps to dumbbells, i.e. for reasons that are not progress. Rebuilt around the questions an athlete actually asks, in their order: *will I be ready → am I doing it right → am I getting fitter → where is this heading → did I do the work.* Six modules, all free-tier (no new capture, no new stores): **Long Run Ladder** (longest SINGLE run per week against a `--signal` race horizon from `RACE_DIST_MI[goalId]` — the one live target in a block, and the only signal spend on the screen); **Is Your Easy Actually Easy** (every logged run as a dot, columned by intensity class read from the `ia_hist_` `subtype` string the engine has always written and Progress had never once read — the diagnostic is the GAP, and collapsed bands get an explicit callout); **Same Effort, More Speed** (easy/recovery runs filtered to RPE 3–4 so effort is pinned and pace is the only free variable — the only module that measures fitness rather than activity); **Where This Is Heading** (D8 forward claim: easy pace → `_mileFromRecoverySec` → new `_halfFromMileSec` → half finish, shown as a ±2.5% band with the athlete's own intake mile plotted as a dashed baseline); **Drift From Plan** (per-day PAIRED prescription vs log against a zero line); **Lift Ledger** (replaces Exercise History — e1RM vs e1RM through `_epley()`, mains ranked by delta, accessories collapsed to a drawer). **Two correctness catches during the build, both design-breaking:** (1) `cfg.bench/squat/deadlift` are 1RMs while `ia_exw_` holds working weights — comparing them directly reports a LOSS for every athlete alive, fixed by running both sides through `_epley()`; (2) `DEFAULT_1RM` is an experience-keyed table the engine anchors to, **not something the athlete ever said**, so using it as a ledger baseline invents progress — a beginner whose true bench is 135 would be scored against the 65 default and shown "+70 lbs" they never earned. Baseline is `cfg[lift]` only when actually entered, else the first LOGGED set, and the row names which (`progSelData().estimated` already drew this line). Gates: syntax; 87 assertions against independently re-derived reference interpolators and an independent Epley (a bug in the engine's own table walk cannot make its own test pass); **18 sabotage builds, 18 tripped, 0 holes**; blast radius 4 hunks, 100% classified. **Caught by the blast-radius diff, not by the gate:** the build had silently dropped the V164 Weekly Running Mileage chart — D7 was Mario selecting from a list of NEW modules, never an enumeration of the whole screen, so the removal was unruled overreach. Restored; lesson promoted to §10b.
- **Prior work (V164): supersets learn where the equipment lives — the station-class rule.** Reported from user Diaz via Mario (iMessage): the engine supersetted `Barbell good mornings` with `Cable pull-through` — two fixtures on opposite sides of a commercial gym, so the "superset" is a walk that kills density and camps two stations. Root cause: pairing draws positionally from pools that mix rack, stack, and dedicated-bench movements with zero station awareness. Baseline measured before design: **324 banned pairs across a 360-config lattice (1.8% of 17,529 accessory supersets)**, all at two sites — Leg superset B (`good mornings + Glute-ham raise`, 225) and Biceps (`Cable curl + Preacher curl`, 99). Shipped on rulings D1–D6 + D3b (§11f): `_stationClass` name classifier (RACK / STACK / BENCH / PORT — sidecar regex, EXLIB stays flat, V113's implement-in-the-name renames make it possible), `_ssLegal` (a fixed class pairs only with itself or PORT), `_ssPair` draw-time resolver (commit member 1, walk member-2 candidates in seeded order; on exhaustion ONE member-1 re-walk — D3b; both-pure-and-banned demotes to straight sets, D3). Five sites instrumented (Accessory, Pull A, Biceps, Arms finisher, Leg superset B — every other generated site proven banned-pair-unreachable from its pool composition); conditioning circuits and curated named workouts exempt (D4). D5 closes the side doors: `swapCandidates` and `auxSwapCandidates` filter against the surviving partner via `_ssPartnerOf` (curated already-cross-station pairs deliberately unfiltered — the filter protects legal pairs, it does not retrofit designed ones), **and the hostile fuzz caught the third door: `deconflictAdjacentDupes` renamed a D3b-rescued `Kettlebell swing` back to `Barbell good mornings` post-build (swing sat on the adjacent day) — the dedupe now carries the same partner guard (E10).** Gates: syntax; G2 classifier vs an independent 92-name hand table; G3 legality truth table; G4 resolver behavior incl. the Diaz rescue; G5 360-config lattice, 0 banned (pair-blind sabotage independently reproduces exactly the 324 baseline pairs — non-vacuous); G7 swap filters incl. aux family + curated exemption; G8 hostile sweep (injuries × rest-day extremes × 108 configs) that exists because it caught E10's hole. Sabotage 9/9, each tripping its named gate. Fuzz 1080 hostile builds: 0 errors, 0 banned, 0 dup pairs. Blast radius 9 hunks, 100% classified. Demotion never fired across the entire lattice — D3b rescues every real draw; the demote path is defensive depth, proven live by gate only.
- **Prior work (V163): the add sheet stops inheriting by position — type-aware dose matching.** Reported from Mario's device (W1 Leg Strength + Mobility): an added `Barbell hip thrust` shipped at **3×40 yards heavy** — the day's optional loaded-carry finisher was the LAST section, the V109 `addedDetailFor` walk takes whatever parseable detail sits last, and `parseRx` reads `3×40 yards heavy` as 40 REPS because its unit token matches `yd`, never the spelled-out `yards`. Every downstream system then did its job faithfully with the poisoned input (banner, set math, prescription-owns-the-fixed-dimension log form). Mario's framing overruled the first patch design (skip-optional + unit guard) as keeping a wrong architecture: **the dose should come from what the program prescribes for that KIND of movement this week, not from textual adjacency.** Shipped D1–D4: **(D1)** `addedDetailFor(day,name,week,prog)` resolves by tier — same pattern on today's card (last match wins; accessories sit lowest) → same pattern anywhere this week (calendar order) → same region anywhere this week → the V109 fallback `3×10 — RPE 7`. Main/primer sections never donate (added work is accessory by nature); supersets, core, hip and `_added` sections keep their exclusions; deload-awareness survives by construction since donors are read from the live week. **(D2)** `_addRxKind` type fence: only rep or open-RPE-set doses are inheritable; distance/time shapes are refused by STRING (`yards?|yd|meters?|sec|min|hold|max time`) AND by parse kind — the string half exists precisely because spelled-out units defeat parseRx and come back dressed as reps. **(D3)** banner is dose-less ("Added work matches the closest thing to it in your week."); the exact dose lands in the existing add toast; the MORE tier's "Sets go to N" is now computed per candidate from its resolved dose (the hardcoded +3 died). **(D4)** the carry finisher itself is unchanged — NSW-doctrine accessory, stays optional, loses only its accidental template authority. Device cleanup ruled: remove-and-re-add (removal resnapshots; migration for one un-logged record on one device is negative value). Gates: syntax; 25/25 behavioral vs hand references incl. a V162 control run that REPRODUCES the defect on the same fixture; 4 sabotage builds each trip their named gate (S1 string-fence→C1a, S2 kind-fence→C1c, S3 tier2-degrade→B3, S4 banner→E1); fuzz 441 builds (7 focuses × 3 equipment × 3 experience × 7 cardio shapes, goal ids harvested live), 464,330 resolved add-cells, 0 violations, 23.4% fallback share; 210-config seeded engine differential `prog.weeks` byte-identical; blast radius 6 hunks, 100% classified, rc guard explicit. Flagged, no action (§12): the carry rows' OWN log forms misparse spelled-out `yards` as reps — separate ruling.
- **Prior work (V162): the control matrix — every exercise skippable, every family swappable, and MAIN means one lift.** Ruled from Mario's device reports (a Push press and a KB single-arm press with no minus): the no-skip gate tested the SECTION label, so every companion riding a `Main —` section inherited main protection — 16,422 wrongly-locked slots across the 2,464-config measure sweep, plus 48,805 fully locked cells (null-pattern superset members: holds, carries, core, ballistic, conditioning). Shipped on rulings D0–D6/R1–R4: **(D0)** main protection narrowed to `itemIdx===0` of a `/^main/i` section via new single-source `exControlFlags` (render AND harness read it); Primer lost protection (dated-build spot check: 10/10 primer items skippable). **(R2)** every non-main, non-note item skippable, including superset members — `applyDayEdits` now applies name skips inside live rounds, block token `__ss:` unchanged. **(D1)** a skipped member drops out and the round continues; at ONE survivor the block demotes to straight sets with rounds×(member reps) stated on the card (`labelRounds` beats first-detail N×; `'10 reps'→'4×10'`, `'8 each'→'5×8 each'`, already-N× details untouched). **(D3, bundled)** `_AUX_FAMILY` sidecar (V160 REP_AFFINITY shape — `_pattern` consumers byte-identical, volume accounting deliberately unchanged): 46 exact names across core/carry/power/cond/hold/chest_iso; `auxSwapCandidates` = same family, on-card dedupe, `_auxGearOK` tier truth, the REAL injury filter, and `_powerAllowed` on the power family so age/experience power doctrine can't be bypassed through a swap. Rehab drills, band correctives and stretches intentionally absent **(D6)** — the overlay prescribed them; skip is the only exit. Note-items (icon-prefixed coaching copy) get neither control **(D5)**. **(D2)** in single-implement rounds, same-implement candidates rank first; cross-implement picks stay offered with a flow note. **(D4)** name-based member-skip storage, pinned by a 0-duplicates assert across universe + RAND_POOLS. **Bonus fixes:** `L-sit chinups` misroute (the `/l-sit/` core token ate a vertical pull — V119 one-token-two-meanings class) → explicit `vpull` rule ahead of the exemption; `Cable crossover`/`Pec deck` (chest flies invisible to swap) → `chest_iso` aux family. Gates: syntax + 555-decl lint (0 dupes); 19/19 behavioral vs 6-passed/2-failed V161 control (control fails exactly the two reported defect classes: Burpees-starved mains, refused member skips); six sabotage builds each trip their own gate (S1 aux-strip→B1, S2 store-revert→D1, S3 L-sit→F1, S4 mapping→E1 — E1 executes the SHIPPED expressions extracted from source, not a replica, S5 note-guard→A4, S6 power-clause→C2); C1 starvation differential proves injured-config empties are the injury filter's doing (0 healthy starved of 21,688 aux cells); blast radius 12 hunks, 100% classified; 630-config engine differential: **0 build errors, 0 changed `prog.weeks`** — every edit is render/store/sheet-side and the L-sit reclassification never pivoted a cap trim at these volumes. R1 verified structurally: 0 starved top-lift slots of 10,020 (824 Burpees mains were the V161 violation; the cond family closes them). §11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V161): the box squat joins the paused squat behind the rep-affinity gate.** Live report from Mario's HALF MANNY (seed 76308, Injury Prevention → endurance wave, 14 wk): `Barbell box squat` printed **3×13 @ RPE 6** on week 4. Diagnosis: NOT a V160 regression — two mechanisms stacking. (1) Week 4 is the SEAL-PFG tactical deload (tw≥10, w%4==0; endurance ∈ TACTICAL_DELOAD_GOALS): reps +3 (10→13), p→block opener, sets held — working as designed. (2) `REP_AFFINITY` held only the paused entry by V160's deliberate minimalism, so box squat passed `_repFit` on the endurance wave (base max 12) and rode the deload to 13. **Mario's coaching challenge held (D1-b over Claude's initial D1-a lean):** the dead stop IS the lift — release, re-brace, break the stretch reflex; past ~6 reps either the pause dies (touch-and-go taps = a worse back squat) or the set becomes bracing endurance. The load-scaler already filed it with paused/close-grip at 0.90. One-line change: `'Barbell box squat':{maxReps:6}` — `_repFit`, the swap-universe harvest, and the fail-closed throw all inherit. Box squat is now strength/athletic-only (base maxima 6/6), symmetric with paused. Accepted consequences: knee/ankle workaround pools lose the box entry on high-rep goals (goblet survives, no pool empties); a depth-gauge 'squat to box' for knee work would be a NEW named movement, parked (§12). Gates: syntax; 275-build fuzz (6 goals × 25 seeds × run/no-run, 14,215 wave mains) against the INDEPENDENT hand base-max table — zero box mains on endurance/balanced/hypertrophy/fatloss, 63 legal draws on strength/athletic (no over-exclusion); sabotage (entry stripped) trips the gate; blast-radius diff = exactly 2 hunks (version tag + map line), 100% classified. Mario's-seed verification: wk4 Tue now `Back squat 3×13 @ RPE 6`, box squat 0 appearances in 14 weeks. Frozen trained days untouched per standing freeze doctrine; unbuilt weeks redraw on next open. Noted, no action: `Close-grip bench press` shares the 0.90 load bucket but is range-biased, not dead-stop — high reps are coherent, no cap.
- **Prior work (V160): a positional-strength lift stops riding endurance rep schemes — the rep-affinity gate.** Diagnosed from Mario's live program (HALF MARATHON BOIZ, seed 22300, endurance wave via Injury Prevention focus): `Paused back squat` drawn from the flat `EXLIB.squat` pool onto week-1 3×12 @ RPE 6.5 — a dead-stop variant whose set terminates on bracing, not quads, and which the app's own curated workouts run at 4×4/3×5. Shipped on rulings D2-a/b/c/d + E1/E2: sidecar `REP_AFFINITY` map (`{'Paused back squat':{maxReps:6}}` — EXLIB stays flat strings, every consumer byte-identical), `_repFit` filter at the THREE wave-main draws only (backMain/squatSel/chestMain; `_slot`'s timed-pool callers untouched), eligibility scored against the BASE wave (`waves[goal]`, deload/taper exempt — post-transform scoring would kill the variant on tw≥12 strength/athletic via one 7-rep deload week and make pool membership a function of totalWeeks), fail CLOSED (emptied pool throws; the V127 latch shape explicitly refused). Cap 6 ⇒ pause variant lives on strength+athletic only (base maxima: str 6, ath 6, bal 8, hyp 10, end 12, fat 15 — hand table). Deliberate side effect: `pick()` harvests the swap universe from the FILTERED pool, so the swap sheet cannot re-open the collision — the scheme follows the slot, not the exercise. Wildcard RAND_POOLS keep their curated pause entries (correct schemes already). Pool-membership change: unbuilt future weeks of live excluded-goal programs redraw the squat main (frozen `ia_hist_` days untouched; old `ia_exw_` loads stay chart-visible, new variant starts blank per D3). §5/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V158): the base block gets its measurement instrument — benchmark runs.** run_base Build 2 per the §12 scope, GO’d whole. Weeks 1 + final each carry ONE benchmark: 20 min at even RPE 6, dose `{k:'time',mins:20}` with deliberately **NO tgt and NO cap** — a printed pace on a benchmark anchors the number the session exists to measure (V131 anti-anchor doctrine extended to prescriptions). Placement: first easy day wk1, last easy day final week (displacing that week’s strides — the block closes on the test). **The ruling’s steady-slot arm is deliberately absent as dead code**: measured, allocation caps run_base at ≤3 run days and the layout table only deals chi at 4+ — re-add it in Build 3 where it becomes gateable. Read-back is render-time only (`_benchmarkEntryFor`/`_benchStripHTML` in `buildCardioHTML`): the retest card shows the wk1 baseline, and once the retest is logged, the pace delta with honest three-way wording (faster / even / slower-check-sleep-fuel-heat); missing wk1 log degrades to baseline-setting instructions, no error. Reader follows the `_recoveryPaceEntry` discipline verbatim — snapshot identifies the benchmark, swap excludes, missing snapshot excludes, corrupt stores return null. Benchmarks are excluded from the pace chart and wizard seed by construction (both filter on `/recovery/i`). §5/§8/§12.
- **Prior work (V157): a base block finally means something — time-prescribed easy running.** Ruled from an elite-coach doctrine session (a live user’s 6-week `run_base` block prescribed one pace, 12:53/mi, for all 12 sessions, with a silent unexplained 19% volume drop in the last two weeks). Four shipped changes, all scoped to `run_base`: **(1) `run_base` NEVER tapers** — killed at the array source in `buildRunProgressionForLength`, ending the two-computation disagreement with the volFreeze-aware `taperWeeksFor`; a base block ends on its peak. **(2) Easy runs prescribe TIME** ("30 min easy"), restoring the one-fixed-dimension doctrine (the old form fixed distance AND pace); chart recovery pace survives as an "around" anchor with a hard **pace ceiling at the steady-aerobic pace** `(tempo+recovery)/2` — the app’s own boundary between easy and quality. Dose `{k:'time', mins, tgt:recovery, cap:steady}` — `cap` is additive, `tgt` keeps its meaning. **(3) Strides** (4×20s) ride the last easy run of each week from week 2 (`_lastEasyRunDay`, arguments[17]; the flag is deliberately unscoped — the single guard is the `run_base` render branch, a goal term on the flag was proven vacuous by the sabotage sweep). **(4) Run/walk permission** whenever the anchor pace is slower than 14:00/mi (Galloway doctrine — mechanics degrade, the answer is walk breaks, never a faster easy pace). Fuzz hardened a latent NaN (junk experience string → tier tables → NaN arrays, present on V156 baseline). §5/§11f/§12.
- **Prior work (V156): programs get a lifecycle, and a new block can learn from the last one.** Three shipped features on D1–D8 + G1–G3 rulings. **Archive replaces delete** as the primary action (two-tier confirm; `purgeProgData` fires ONLY from the explicit "Delete Forever" tier — an archived program keeps all 14 store prefixes). **Progress is per-program** via selector chips (`progressViewId`; one program's charts render at a time, archived programs selectable with a badge + restore bar; a new program's charts are blank by construction). **The wizard seeds from prior logs**: ≥3 qualifying recovery runs within 21 days (D5) offers a banner that pre-fills mile-best (inverting the app's own PACE_CHART recovery→mile, interpolated — zero invented constants) and baseline distance (max single logged run in window). Pre-fill only, never locked — D3's anti-anchor doctrine extends across program boundaries. §4/§5/§8/§11f.
- **Prior work (V155): the Recovery Pace trend chart.** Progress now charts weekly-average recovery-run pace against the plan target (`recoveryPaceWeekly` + `buildPaceChart`), session type resolved at READ time from `ia_hist_` per the post-V154 B ruling. Renders only at ≥2 data weeks; recovery subtypes only; swaps and snapshot-less entries excluded. §8.
- **Prior work (V154): Injury Prevention is a real engine branch.** D1–D5, Mario's GO ×5. `preventionSupport` flag: every main holds ~RPE 7 (p-clamp + the three no-load literal writers via `_effEase`), accessory grammars emit RPE 7 and hold 2 sets (`preventionDoseSweep`, mains excluded by the warmup-clause form test), the legs day runs ONE merged circuit (lunge + patellar isometric + single-leg hinge — V123 pairing preserved, hip thrust dropped) plus a calf/achilles guarantee on run goals, and the power section keeps only the swing (clean gone from the long-run day). §5/§11f.
- **Prior work (V153): the NRC calendar stops fighting the athlete's rest days.** Reported from Mario's own HALF MARATHON BOIZ build (seed 22300, rest Sun+Wed): full leg day → fartlek → long run on three consecutive days. Two shipped fixes. **F1:** NRC run-day selection is spacing-aware — days and session types chosen JOINTLY (`_nrcSpacedRunDays`), hard adjacency scored on the real calendar, long run pinned to the last train day, ties prefer speed sessions riding a rest day and a recovery run padding the long. **F2:** any lift day whose next calendar day carries legLoad cardio gets its hinge pattern clamped to ~RPE 7 — mains via the scheme() p-clamp (`_hotNextDay`), accessories/circuits via `hotNextHingeClampSweep` after every detail writer; plus the deconflict note now has three honest tiers (full 48h / same-day-dodged / stuck) instead of the fallback overclaiming success. §5/§6/§11f.
- **Live program (Mario):** THE HALF MANNY — seed 76308, intermediate, 18–35, CrossFit Box, Injury Prevention → endurance, Race/Event path, half marathon Dec 5 2026, 14 wk, train Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri/Sat (rest Sun/Wed), pace row anchored at a 10:30 mile (5K 11:15 / 10K 11:35 / Rec 13:20). Started the week of Aug 29 2026. Trained days are frozen via `ia_hist_`; every untrained day picks up V171's re-transcribed sessions on the next boot (programs rebuild from cfg + seed).
- **Per-version history lives in ONE place — the digest at the end.** Standing lessons are promoted out of the digest into §10b; standing decisions into §11f.

### Load-bearing invariants (do not break)
- **Zoom-locked viewport is permanent** (`width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0, maximum-scale=1.0, user-scalable=no, viewport-fit=cover`) + `touch-action:manipulation` on `html,body` and `.tabbar`/`.tabbar button`. Removing it reintroduces iOS double-tap zoom, input-focus auto-zoom, and a booted-zoomed tab bar. App is **scoped to iPhone/iOS only** (Mario's call). `#rtCard{touch-action:none}` keeps the rest-timer drag working.
- **Bump `ia-version` on every release, tracking the filename** (`V90`→`content="90"`). It's the single source of truth for `iaCheckForUpdate()` (re-fetches index.html `no-store`, shows the signal-orange update banner on mismatch → cache-busted reload). No service worker anywhere; the meta is how an installed iOS PWA notices a deploy.
- **Grep the file, not the label.** Version/filename lag between sessions is chronic (Mario re-syncs/relabels out of band). At session start: `ls *.html` + `grep 'ia-version' *.html` and confirm engine markers before editing.
- **One HTML file in the project at a time** — replace the old on ship so there's never a "which is most complete" question.
- **⚠️ Two sessions must never claim the same version number.** The V112 work was originally built as a V110 and **lost**: a second session that same day started from V109, shipped its own V110, and V111 built on that branch. Both deliverables were gone — not in the file, not in the digest — and nothing flagged it, because the collision was on the NUMBER while the filename rule and the grep rule were both satisfied. **At session start, after grepping `ia-version`, check the digest tail: if the file's version has no digest line, or a digest line names a version the file doesn't carry, a build has been dropped.** The recovery cost is a full re-derivation against a moved codebase.
- **Mario owns versioning** — Claude bumps only on Mario's explicit instruction (this session's 88→90 was such a call).
- **Deploy:** GitHub Pages — https://bigyerr.github.io/TheBig6V2/ . Edits don't auto-deploy: push, allow CDN lag, then bust the iOS PWA cache (open live URL with `?v=` in Safari, then fully close/reopen the home-screen app, or delete + re-add).

**Stack:** single-file vanilla HTML/CSS/JS, no framework. localStorage for all persistence. iOS PWA — **light "The Asylum" design system** (warm bone paper + ink + rationed signal-orange `#CF4E1A`), Helvetica Neue/Geist type, inline-SVG icons (`asyIcon()`). DS spec in `THE_ASYLUM_DS_REFERENCE.md` (in project) — **V102/103 swap components are documented in §8b of this doc, not there.**

---

## 1. What the app is
Iron Asylum is a personalized fitness-program generator. The user answers a short wizard (goal, lifting focus, training days, age, units, sport[s], optional race date / current mile time), and the app generates a full multi-week program combining **lifting** and **cardio** (run / bike / swim), with age-aware scaling, **RPE-based lift coaching** (no computed weights — see §5), and a logging + progress system. Target user has some fitness background (no true off-the-couch beginner tier yet). Mario is sole developer/architect. Evolution: static tables → formula-driven length calculator → multi-sport cardio → dual-engine (NSW + NRC) run architecture → doctrine-aligned hypertrophy.

## 2. Doctrine sources (in the project folder)
- **NSW (Naval Special Warfare) Physical Training Guide — 2020 edition** (`physicaltrainingguide2020.pdf`) — drives **test** goals (pace goal / mile time / 1.5-mile / base; 5K and 10K moved to NRC at V186) and same-region recovery doctrine; plus `navysealfitnessguide.pdf` (SEAL PFG — source of the strength-deload pattern). The 2020 PTG permits same-day lift + swim, so the leg/run deconflicter is already *beyond* what doctrine mandates — know that before tightening it.
- **Nike Run Club (NRC) plans** — drive **race** goals (5K / 10K / half / full marathon since V186): `NikeRunClub{5K,HalfMarathon,Marathon,10K}...pdf`. Table-driven from the actual PDFs (exact week-by-week long-run progressions incl. irregular cutback weeks), chosen over formula approximation. All four plans print the same pace chart.
- **Reference hypertrophy programs:** `8weekmassbuildinghypertrophyworkout.pdf`, `10weekmass.pdf`, `totalpackage.pdf`, `8weekmusclebuildingprogramforadults402b.pdf`, `6daysplit.pdf`.
- **RG Active 32-week Ironman plan** (`32weekadvancedintermediate.pdf`) — source of the V141 harvest: segmented swim-session grammar (WU/drill/main/CD, rotating 4-week blocks), missed-session doctrine ("never squeeze it back in; >1 week missed → progressive re-entry"), taper doctrine, and the reference ramp curve Q7 was validated against. Independently corroborates existing doctrine (RPE philosophy, 3:1 load/deload, strength as low-load endurance support). Full triathlon path evaluated and PARKED (§12).

**Doctrine fidelity is non-negotiable** — engine logic that drifts gets flagged and corrected. (Doctrine note: for goal-time/pace runs the engine does INT + CHI, but INT is **400m-only** rep-count progression, no short+long interval ladder; the long stimulus is the LSD long run. Defensible simplification.)
