- **The doctrine PDFs are not PDFs.** `physicaltrainingguide2020.pdf`, all four `NikeRunClub*.pdf` files, and `32weekadvancedintermediate.pdf` (RG Ironman) are **ZIP archives** of per-page `N.jpeg` + `N.txt` OCR output. `pdftotext` returns empty and `pypdf` throws `invalid pdf header: b'PK\x03\x04'`. Read them with `zipfile`, sorting `*.txt` numerically, or the doctrine looks unavailable when it is sitting right there.
- **What the two doctrines actually prescribe, weekly (verified against the files, V115):** NSW PTG is **4 run sessions** — 2× LSD (one longer, one shorter, *same pace*), 1× Long Interval (1–4 reps of 7–20 min at 90–95% of max sustainable pace, RPE 8–9), 1× Short Interval (400m repeats, **start at 4, build to 8, never exceed 8**, recovery 2–2.5× work time). NRC 5K/10K is **5 sessions** — 2× Speed, 2× Recovery, 1× Long, where "Speed Run" is a **slot whose flavor rotates** (intervals / fartlek / hills / tempo) rather than a fixed workout. **Tempo is absent from the front half of both NRC plans and present in every week of the back half** (5K introduces it at 5-of-8, 10K at 4-of-8). Both docs independently say to space hard efforts and never run speed work on back-to-back days. **Neither doctrine has a 3-run-day template** — that is the app's own compression problem, and §12 records how it is now solved.

## 3. Architecture overview
### Generation pipeline (decoupled A→B→C→D)
`buildProgram(cfg)` is a thin orchestrator over five facades, strictly in order: **A `engineA_timeline`** (structure, weeks, wave/deload/taper resolver) → **B `engineB_cardio`** (multi-sport cardio; hoisted FIRST; publishes the taper window) → **`planCalendar`** (dayRoles + leg/run deconfliction, finalized read-only) → **C `engineC_kinematics`** (pure-RPE lift selection) → **D `engineD_synthesis`** (week-grid assembly + dynamic core). Cardio leads so the leg-load footprint is a read-only input to lifting selection + deconfliction, never a post-hoc mutation. Each engine takes explicit inputs, returns an explicit payload — no shared mutable god-object. Refactor was proven **byte-identical** (18 structured + 600 random configs).

### Engines & routing
**Race → NRC engine (`NRC_GOALS` = run_5k / run_10k / run_half / run_marathon, module-scoped next to the tables); test → NSW engine (run_pace_goal / run_mile_time / run_15_under10 / run_base).** Both share `PACE_CHART`/`paceChartLookup()`. With no race date, half/marathon truncate at peak week; **5K/10K run all 8 weeks and race day becomes a time trial** (`Long Run — TIME TRIAL`, race-day shape for the lifting layer via `taperEventOn(cfg)` and the `_longRunTier` exclusion). An NRC run goal **pins program length** to its table (§3b) — the race table is the calendar, other sports build inside it. Optional current mile-time personalizes paces (intermediate/advanced) — **it is the ROW ANCHOR: Nike's first chart column IS Mile Pace** (`chartRow.mile`, printed via `{mile}` since V171). Run **base** goal = true aerobic base (80/20 easy, no intervals, one gentle steady session).

**NRC table grammar (V171).** `NRC_5K_TABLE` (8, V186) / `NRC_10K_TABLE` (8, V186) / `NRC_HALF_TABLE` (14) / `NRC_MARATHON_TABLE` (18) are Nike-verbatim, transcribed page-by-page. A named Nike run reads byte-identically in every table it appears in (the V186 edit script asserted this before inserting). 5K/10K use the half's 2-speed layout; `buildNRCSession` selects the table by goal and prints the race's own chart column on race day (`_raceKey`). Speed `detail` is line-per-segment on `\n`: line 1 is a headline ("Mixed-pace ladder — 9:00 of work, 5 efforts") because the week view and day summary read `split('\n')[0]`; the day card converts `\n`→`<br>` (V141's swim path already did). Segment lines carry their pace fill (`{mile}/{fiveK}/{tenK}/{tempo}/{half}/{recovery}`) and their recovery as a suffix; hills stay effort-worded with no pace number; lines inside a "N rounds of:" block are indented with `\u00a0\u00a0` (leading spaces collapse in HTML). `recovery:[…]` entries are minutes or `{mi,label}` (Nike's 2 mi / 5K / 1 mi / 4 mi runs) and emit `dose:{k:'dist'}`; when the layout has fewer recovery slots than entries the LONGEST survive by minutes-at-Recovery-Pace, Nike order preserved (D4c). A week with one Nike speed session on a two-speed layout (half W14) converts `nrc_speed2` into that week's extra recovery run and counts the extra slot on every recovery caller (`hasSpeed2`, `_recSlots` written once at type assignment — the layout is program-constant). `hold:true` prints a hold note and no phase tag; `cutback/peak/taper/race` unchanged. Range long runs (marathon W11/12/14/15/16) dose the LOW end, label keeps the range (D4d). Long runs read Nike's progression definition — start slower than Recovery Pace, finish faster, average it — and carry `tgt = recovery`; Progress consumers filter by subtype before reading `tgt`, so long runs stay out of the recovery-pace and drift charts.

### Lifting + cardio layout model (CRITICAL)
**Every training day carries a lifting block; cardio is layered on top of some days.** A day is "lift-only" when its cardio session is `null`. Roles (push/pull/legs/etc.) assigned to all training days via `roleTemplates`/`dayRoles`; `buildCardioProgression()` decides which days also get cardio; the week assembler merges (`weeks[w][d] = {title, cardio, sections}`). Lifting spans the **full program** — `buildSections(role, w)` gets the true program week (the wave tiles across `totalWeeks`; rotation keeps advancing; the deload cadence + "↓ Recovery Week" badge match the rendered load).

### Hypertrophy doctrine
12–15 sets/muscle/week, direct arm/delt/calf isolation. Age-aware = **joint-friendly exercise selection, not reduced volume**. Dense hypertrophy layout triggered by a size goal or when cardio is fully disabled.

### Governors
- **`isBaseCardio`** = no perf lift path AND no perf cardio goal. Base cardio + strength/size lifting → caps cardio to ≤2 spread days (first+last), easy pacing, frozen volume. High-performance tracks exempt.
- **Rest-day floor** with persistent explanation banners.
- **Day allocator:** `sportDayTargets()` → per-sport ceilings; `multiSportTargets()` → recovery-aware budget (leg sports run+bike share one budget; swim sits on top; tactical/endurance uncapped; strength/size reserve 2 lift-only days, balanced/fatloss reserve 1); greedy spreader assigns sports, leaves the rest `null`.

## 4. Data / persistence
Programs + active id in localStorage; active program drives everything. Per-program keys scoped by id: logs (`ia_logs_*`), completed (`ia_comp_*` — `{title, ts, status}`, status ∈ complete|skipped as of V181; `partial` still exists in pre-V181 records and `statusOf` reads it as complete; absent = pending, and V181's unmark DELETES the key rather than writing a fourth state), **day-history snapshots (`ia_hist_*`, V104)**, exercise weights (`ia_exw_*`), wildcard-done (`ia_wild_*`), popup/reminder/season state, start date. `purgeProgData(id)` clears every per-program key on delete — **including the two added in V102**, `ia_swaps_*` (session exercise swaps) and `ia_swapct_*` (the swap-streak counter behind the coach nudge), **and `ia_hist_*` (V104)**. **V156: delete is no longer the primary action — archive is.** `archiveProg(id)` sets `archived:true` + `archivedAt` on the `ia_programs` entry and touches NO stores; the program leaves the home list (`renderProgList` filters), can't go active (`setActive` guard, boot fallback and delete fallback both skip archived), but stays selectable under Progress and stays a wizard-seed candidate. `purgeProgData` now fires from exactly one path: the tier-2 "Delete Forever" confirm (`askArchive` → `askPurge` → `deleteProg`). `_handoffActiveFrom(id,programs)` is the single active-handoff used by both archive and delete — first non-archived program, else clear. **V156 also adds id-parameterized read accessors** — `getLogsFor/getDayHistFor/getExWeightsFor/saveExWeightsFor(pid)` — used ONLY by the Progress selector and the wizard seed; every zero-arg accessor and its call sites are byte-identical, so nothing else in the app can accidentally read across programs. `init()` restores the active program and auto-routes to the week view. **All data is localStorage — every device/browser is fully independent; nothing syncs** except Bugs-tab emails (all funnel to Mario). Lift data lives in `ia_exw_` (per-exercise history) only — the old `ia_1rm_`/`ia_logs_` lift stores are gone (RPE pivot). **Each `ia_exw_` entry is `{weight, setsW[], setsDone[], setsReps, week, ts, unit}`** — `setsDone` holds actual reps/seconds per set (V101); **`setsW` (V105) holds the per-set LOAD ladder in lbs**; `ts` drives the reuse pill's recency. **`weight` is always `max(setsW)` when a ladder exists** and remains the single scalar every consumer reads — the big-3 weekly max, the Exercise History chart and the reuse pill were untouched by V105. `setsW` is purely additive: pre-V105 entries have none, render as uniform, and are **never migrated or rewritten**, so a rollback stays clean. Older entries without `setsDone` still render (last-weight + recency degrade gracefully); bodyweight logs store `weight:0` and are filtered out of the weight charts.

**⚠️ CORRECTION (a prior revision of this doc stated the opposite — it was wrong).** Programs are **not** frozen at generation. `refreshProgram(prog)` re-runs the **current** engine — `buildProgram(prog.cfg)` with the stable `cfg.seed` — on **every app boot and every program open** (`openProg`, `init`, and the post-delete fallback), preserving `id`/`name`/`created`/`startDate`/`seed`. So an engine change reaches existing programs automatically; **no export/import or migration button is needed, and never was.** Two important properties:
- **The refresh is in-memory only** (assigned to `activeProg`, not written back via `savePrograms`), with **one exception**: the V100 seed backfill below persists `cfg.seed` once. **Consequence, and it bit hard in V104: `prog.weeks` in storage is the build from CREATION DAY with no overlays baked in, so it is NOT a record of what was prescribed** — anything that needs the real prescription must read `ia_hist_` (§6), never `prog.weeks`. The only other writer of `prog.weeks` is `applyRestMove`. (A prior revision named `setProgStart` too — it does not touch `weeks`; corrected V174.) **V174: `startDate` is set at creation by `_applyWizardStart` from the wizard's resolved start and is never null on a wizard-built program; pre-V174 programs may still carry null, and D23 makes the freeze safe for them.** **V175: a day spliced in by an overlay carries `_ovKey` (the overlay's type+patch JSON) on the day object. It rides with the content — through rest-day moves and into the `ia_hist_` snapshot — so the record says a hotel session was a hotel session. Readers: `dayOverlayInfo` / `dayIsTravel` / `travelTagHTML`, and `swapUniverseFor(prog,week,day)` via `prog._swapUniverseByKey[key]` (`_swapUniverseWeeks` is gone).**
- **Seed fallback (V100).** Older programs baked `seed` at the program level but not into `cfg`; the old guard skipped them, so they never received engine updates **and their overlays (injury, travel) silently never applied while the UI still reported them** — the detail card masks this because it displays `prog.seed` when `cfg.seed` is missing. `refreshProgram` now resolves `cfg.seed ?? prog.seed`, backfills into cfg, persists once, and proceeds. Same seed → same exercise picks, so the rebuild is identical. Only truly seedless programs (both null) stay baked. Companion fix: `applyOverlays` failures now surface a toast instead of a console-only error — an injury plan silently not applying is a safety failure.

**Because refresh is total, already-trained weeks must be frozen** — see §6. Without that, shipping any engine change silently rewrites weeks the athlete already completed.

**Key scheme (mapped — this is why an in-place rebuild is safe):** nothing is keyed to program *content*.
| Store | Key | Keyed by | Survives a rebuild? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ia_programs` | — | program `id` | object holds `cfg` **and** `seed` → regeneration is deterministic/reproducible |
| `ia_logs_<id>` | `logKey(w,d)` = `w3_mon` | week + day | **Yes, fully.** Day-level `{rpe, run_dist, run_pace, bike_mins, swim_yards, notes, week, ts}` — no per-exercise data |
| `ia_exw_<id>` | `exKey` = `exStoreKey(name)` — alias-resolved, then slugged | exercise **name** | **Yes.** Old-key rows are folded into the canonical key by `foldExAliasKeys()` (V151); changed/removed lifts show `last —` |
| `ia_comp_<id>` | week + day | week + day | **Yes** — completions, and therefore streak, survive |
| `ia_swaps_<id>` (V102) | `w3_thu` | week + day | **Yes** — re-applied at the tail of `refreshProgram`, pruned once the week freezes |
| `ia_swapct_<id>` (V102) | exercise **name** | exercise name | **Yes** — `{to, n, offered}` per swapped-out lift; drives the nudge, not the program |
| `ia_hist_<id>` (V104) | `w3_thu` | week + day | **Yes, and it is the point** — the day object as RENDERED at first touch (overlays, swaps, moves baked in). The freeze restores from this, not `prog.weeks`. First-touch-wins, never overwritten |

**Consequence: an export/import round-trip is NOT required to move an in-progress program onto a newer engine.** `weeks` can be rebuilt in place on the **same program `id`** (same `name`, `startDate`, `created`, `seed`) with all logs, completions, streak and exercise history still attached. See §12 for the parked feature.

## 5. Current engine behavior

**Prehab rail (V178, D3).** Two prehab blocks ride every leg day that carries hip stability: `buildHipSection` (all athletes) and `buildFootAnkleSection` (runners only, `cfg.cardioTypes` includes `run`, steps aside when `cfg.injury.region==='ankle'` because the rehab section owns that joint). Foot & ankle carries `hip:true` deliberately — every cap exemption, injury-sweep protection and render rule keyed on that flag applies unchanged. In the taper (V179 D7) the leg-day builder returns primer-only, then pushes foot & ankle alone — hip stability stays dropped. Race week (V180 D8) the block is a fixed pair under the label `Foot & ankle — mobility only` (Standing ankle CARs + Toe yoga), no draw, both tiers; `buildFootAnkleSection(seed, bodyweight, raceWeek)`. Bodyweight tier draws `foot_ankle_bw` (no band).

**Experience floor (V178, D4).** `EX_MIN_EXP` is a sidecar in the `REP_AFFINITY` shape: a movement declares the least experience tier that may be handed it; `_expOK(name, cfg.experience)` is applied at pool construction in `buildEx` (hinge pool today). Untagged = any. The power pools already do this by cost; this is the same idea for loaded skill lifts.

**Wizard seed (V156, D4b/D5/D6 + G2/G3).** When any program (archived included) has ≥3 recovery runs logged within 21 days, the run-goal step offers a seed banner: apply pre-fills `mileBestMins/Secs` from the average recovery pace inverted through PACE_CHART (recovery→mile, linear interpolation, clamped at table edges — the app's own forward map run backwards, no new constants) and `baselineDist` from the max single logged run in the window. Both seeds or nothing; most recent qualifying program wins; `buildProgram()` itself is untouched — the seed writes wizard fields the user can edit. Inclusion predicate is `_recoveryPaceEntry`, the V155 chart predicate extracted verbatim and shared, so the chart and the seed cannot drift. The window checks both boundaries (`ts<cut||ts>now`) — future timestamps are hostile data, not evidence.
- **Pure-RPE lifting (no computed weights).** Every lift is prescribed by a **climbing RPE target + reps-in-reserve**; progression is the user's own last-logged number (shown in the log row — beat it). `scheme(w)` is a one-arg RPE function returning `S×R — RPE n (leave ~k reps)` via `rpeFromWave(wv)` (wk1≈7 → peak≈9.5; taper 5–6 primer; deload 6). All percentage-of-1RM / derived-1RM / Epley machinery was removed. The **1RM estimator survives display-only** on the Progress screen (weight×reps → est., Deadlift/Squat/Bench selector). `refreshProgram` migrates old seeded programs off baked weight prescriptions on next open.
- **Movement rep floor (V110).** The wave descends reps (4×6 → 4×3) on one premise: fewer reps at higher RPE means more weight on the bar. That premise holds for a barbell comp lift and nothing else. `scheme()` already knew it — the `loadCapped` / `canAddLoad` branches exist precisely because a hotel dumbbell cannot express a triple — but both gate on the **equipment tier**, not the movement, so any lift reaching a main slot by another route landed on the raw wave line. Two routes did: **injury pool overrides** (lowback swaps the deadlift for `Barbell hip thrust` and the squat for `Goblet squat`, on a full gym at full wave intensity) and the **Mini gym tier with no injury at all** (`hasBarbell` false, `noLoad` also false → `4×3 Kettlebell swing`). `_repFloor(name)` sits beside `_pattern` and returns the window in which a movement is still a coherent prescription: `null` = wave runs free (**unlisted names default here**, so the library is unchanged until a name is deliberately listed); `[0,0]` = unloadable, routes to the shipped open-ended `N sets — RPE 8` grammar; `[lo,hi]` = clamp reps up into the window, RPE carried through untouched. **The floor bites only when the wave descends below it** — a hypertrophy `3×12` goblet squat is already inside the window and comes out byte-identical, which is why this only ever bit the tactical waves. Consulted **after** the config-tier branches so travel and bodyweight are untouched. Two rationales share the mechanism: goblet floors at 8 because the **load** runs out (bell at the sternum — grip and t-spine quit first, so RPE 8.5 at three reps is unreachable, not merely hard); barbell hip thrust floors at 6 because the **movement** runs out (lockout-limited, rarely taken near true failure, and a maximal triple fails into lumbar hyperextension — the exact position the lift is usually prescribed to avoid).
- **Injury caps must follow the substitution, not the original (V110).** Lowback capped `hinge`/`squat`/`row` while its own pool override swapped the deadlift to a hip thrust — pattern `hip_ext`, which was on no cap list. The one lift chosen for being spine-neutral was the only lift running at full wave intensity, on a **return week** whose chip promises "one light week." `hip_ext` added to both lowback tiers. **Standing rule: whenever a pool override changes a movement's `_pattern`, check the cap set still covers it — a substitution can walk a lift out from under its own governor.**
- **Synchronized recovery week — intensity AND volume AND cardio (V100).** Cadence unchanged: `totalWeeks>=10 && w%4===0 && w!==totalWeeks`. Three coordinated layers now fire on it:
  1. **Wave transform** (`resolveWaveEntry`/`_blockOpenerP`): tactical intensity-down/reps-up/sets-held; size intensity-down/volume-held. Reads through the recovery week so w+1 resumes the real curve.
  2. **`recoveryDeload(sections)`** (runs before `capRegionalFatigue`): keeps main work + **one** accessory block + hip prehab; drops carries, finishers, conditioning, extra supersets; dynamic core injection is skipped for the week. **Power sections keep the pattern at 60% of sets** (5×3 → 3×3) — CNS touch, not dose. Never empties a day (falls back to input for exotic builders). Ruling: a deload that only drops RPE is a normal week in a lighter shirt.
  3. **Cardio cutback** (`isCardioCutbackWeek`, same clock): bike LSD → 70% of the prior build week (tagged Cutback + coach note); INT reps → ~60% of prior week via the `getINTReps` choke point (run/bike/swim); CHI → one rep off prior week, single-rep sessions cut duration 30% (`getCHI`); run pace engine **holds last week's target pace** (fewer reps, same pace — the pace clock never tightens on a cutback); swim CHI yards → 70%. Run LSD and swim LSD already cut on this cadence in their distance builders. **NRC plans keep Nike's own irregular cutbacks — untouched by design.** `totalWeeks` is goal-derived, so a "6-week" request with a 50-mile bike goal is really 12 weeks and cutbacks there are correct.
- **Honest `sessionTimeEst` (V100):** main sets cost 3.2 min (work + rest), supersets 1.4, straight accessories 2.0, holds/prehab ~1, stretches 0.75, carries 1.2; runs timed off the printed pace when present; +8 warmup, +4 cardio transition. The old flat 1.6 min/set shipped ~65 on days that ran 85+.
- **`run_base` easy running is time-prescribed with a steady-pace ceiling (V157).** In a base block pace is an OUTPUT, not a target — the athlete runs by effort and the pace drifting faster at that effort IS the adaptation. `lsd_easy`/`lsd_long` for `run_base` emit `N min easy` (distance arrays × chart recovery pace, rounded to 5 min, floor 15), subtype `Easy Run` / `Easy Run — Long` (`runSessionCode` → `EASY`), RPE 3-4, "around" pace = chart recovery, ceiling = `(tempo+recovery)/2` — the Steady Aerobic pace, so an easy run can never silently become the quality session. Dose `{k:'time', mins, tgt, cap}`. Strides on the last easy run of the week from wk2. Anchor slower than 14:00/mi adds run/walk permission. **`run_base` never tapers** — taper is zeroed at the array source per-goal, and the block ends on its peak; every other goal keeps the eventTargeted taper. Every other run goal keeps distance doses byte-identical (gated controls: pace_goal, 5K). **Benchmark runs (V158):** wk1 + final week, one each — `Benchmark Run` / `Benchmark Run — Retest` (`runSessionCode` → `TEST`), 20 min even RPE 6, dose carries no pace on purpose; scheduler picks the day (`_benchDayFor`: first easy wk1, last easy final), buildRunSession owns only the text (arguments[18], single-guard doctrine same as [17]). Retest card reads wk1 back at render via `_benchStripHTML` — ia_hist_ identifies, ia_logs_ measures, nothing is written. **Frequency growth (V159, solo run_base under the governor):** `baseCardioPlan.freq` carries `activeByWeek`/`minsByWeek`/`longCap`; dispatch skips inactive (w,day) pairs writing schedule null, passes budgeted minutes as arguments[19] (scheduler owns the split, buildRunSession owns text; `_minsOv>40` ⇒ `— Long`). Ladder 2/3/4 by thirds, budget 50+(w−1)×15 clamped to (n−1)×40+longCap, longCap = tw≤6?40:min(60,40+⌊(tw−6)/2⌋×5), cutbacks −20% minutes frequency intact. Unfrozen run_base = 4 days (easy/easy/chi/long), benchmark takes the chi slot there.
- **Taper on a single source of truth** (`taperWeeksFor` + `programTaperWindow`): `(eventOn && !isBaseCardio) ? max(2, round(tw*0.12)) : 0`. Engine B publishes the window; A reads it for the wave resolver; D consumes it for `taperWeeks[]`. "Just building fitness" = build-to-peak-and-HOLD (no taper); taper appears ONLY with a real race date. Taper cuts BOTH bar + cardio (Mario's call, max race freshness): sets ×tf (floor 2), intensity ×tf (floor 0.50), reps floored at 3; strips to big-3 only; core off; gold 🏁 Race Taper/Race Week pill.
- **Dynamic core** (`injectDynamicCore`/`getDynamicCoreBlock`/`CORE_PILLARS`, 20-move doctrine pool): runs at week-assembly with tomorrow-look-ahead — no high-fatigue trunk before a speed run or heavy dead/squat day. Always-on (`includeCore=true`). Doctrine-compliant (NSW "≤2 of lifting/calisthenics/core per day").
- **Full-body days differentiated** upper (calisthenic push/pull + power) vs lower (single-leg + hinge lead, hip-extension + push, power); barbell-free by design. `push_light ≠ push` (distinct recovery/pump session). CrossFit equipment tier unlocks explosive lead goal-agnostically.
- **Strength Support vs Athletic Support are distinct** (both resolve to `goal='athletic'` internally but gate on `cfg.liftingFocus`): Strength Support = submaximal maintenance (RPE cap ≤7 on working weeks, accessory volume ×0.8, conditioning dropped); Athletic Support = power-lead full-body day (explosive straight sets, ~2min rest). **Hybrid** path (`id='both'`) = full lifting + progressing cardio, no race taper; taper-dependent races filtered from its cardio-goal step.

- **Bike sessions distinguish long from easy (V114).** `buildBikeSession` collapses `lsd_long`/`lsd_easy` to a single `lsd` branch for *labeling*, but duration now forks: the easy day keeps the original linear `20 + pct*40` curve capped by `lsdMaxes`; the long day runs a **geometric** curve, `min(_longStart, cap) * (1+rate)^(week-1)`, capped by the new **`lsdLongMaxes`** table (`bike_century:240, bike_50:150, bike_ftp:90, bike_cals:60, bike_base:75`). Rate and start are experience-scaled (`.08/.10/.12`, `30/45/60 min`) and the rate deliberately matches `calcProgramLength`'s `weeklyPct` — one safe-build-rate constant, expressed twice. Geometric not linear because linear interpolation to a 150-minute ceiling produces a **36% week-one jump**; volume is added in percentages. Cutback weeks step to 70% of the prior **long** week, not the prior easy week. Long-ride subtype is `Long Ride (LSD)` — **the `LSD` token is load-bearing**, `runSessionCode()` matches `/\blsd\b/` and the badge falls through to a generic code without it. `buildCardioProgression` already set `legLoad:true` on `lsd_long` for bike, so the calendar was **already** protecting a long ride that did not exist; this only makes the ride real.

- **The compressed quality slot is periodized (V115).** `getSessionTypes` is a table indexed by day count, transcribed from NSW's **four**-session week: `nDays===4` is a direct transcription, 5/6/7 extend it correctly (extra days add easy volume, never more quality), and `nDays===3` is **the only row with no doctrinal source** — it sits below the template's floor. At 3 days exactly one quality session fits, so that slot now runs **INT through the front of the block and CHI through the back**, crossing at `qualityCrossoverWeek()` = `ceil(totalWeeks * 0.55)`, floor 3. The 0.55 splits NRC's own crossovers (5K at 62%, 10K at 50%). Mechanically: the slot is tagged `_qslot` at assignment time and resolved **per-week** inside the build loop, because `sportSessionTypes` is built once and read many times. Each phase carries its own dose ramp via `_qPhase = {intSpan, chiFrom, chiTo}` — without it a 6-week interval phase would ramp against a 12-week block and top out at 5 repeats instead of 8, and CHI would enter the back half already mid-progression. `getINTReps` takes `rampSpan`; `getCHI` takes `phaseFrom`/`phaseTo`; **both still read the real calendar week for cutback detection**, because deloads are calendar-aligned and must not shift with the phase.
- **The compression predicate is deliberately wider than `isSpeedGoal` (V115).** `isSpeedGoal` omits `run_mile_time` and `run_15_under10`, while `isMilGoal` inside `buildRunSession` includes them alongside `run_pace_goal` — two overlapping predicates disagreeing about the same goals. The consequence was that a **1.5-mile-under-10 goal, the most interval-dependent goal in the app, got CHI for the entire block and zero interval work**. Fixed with a local `_perfRun` superset rather than by widening `isSpeedGoal`, which also drives program length and the pace-chart anchor.
- **`spaceHardCardio` (V115).** Circular-adjacency pass over the slot map, run after every sport has its slots and before any session is built, so it is invisible downstream. It never moves cardio **between** days — sport allocation upstream is authoritative — it swaps a hard slot for an easy slot **within one sport's own day list**, the only move that cannot break the allocation. Non-run sports are attempted first so the long run keeps its late-week placement when a cheaper move fixes the week. Adjacency is **circular** (Sun↔Mon), the same trap that once scored Sun+Sat as maximum separation.
- **The taper reaches the quality sessions (V115).** `isTaperWeek` had existed since the taper landed but **only the `lsd_long` branch ever read it**. INT and CHI ran peak volume straight through race week. Now: INT reps ×0.55 (floor 2), CHI drops a rep and cuts duration to 60% (floor 8 min), **intensity held** — the same volume-down/pace-held rule the cutback branches already follow. Subtypes gain a ` — Taper` suffix. **Bike and swim still have no taper at all** (§12).

- **The taper is computed once, in `buildCardioProgression`, and handed to all three builders (V116).** §5 had claimed since V95 that `taperWeeksFor` was the single source of truth for taper gating. **It was not.** It was computed inside `buildRunSession` and nowhere else, so bike and swim never saw it: a 12-week `bike_50` build rode 96 → 106 → 117 → **128 min in race week**, its largest ride of the block. Now `_taperFor(w)` returns `{step, of}` — 1-based position inside the window, 0 outside — and each builder ramps its own reduction across however many taper weeks the block earned. `buildRunSession` reads the handed-down value (`arguments[15]`) rather than recomputing, which is what finally makes the claim true; the local fallback stays for direct callers. Endurance volume ramps ×0.70 → ×0.50 (long) and ×0.70 → ×0.60 (easy); quality work is ×0.55 (INT) and reps−1 / ×0.6 duration (CHI). **Intensity is held throughout** — the same volume-down/pace-held rule the cutback branches follow. **Taper wins over cutback where the windows overlap**, or the dose would be reduced twice off the same base.
- **`programTaperWindow` no longer gates on `types.includes('run')` (V116).** That gate meant an event-targeted program with no run in it **never tapered anything, cardio or barbell** — a dated 50-mile bike build rode into race week at peak volume with the lifting wave still climbing. Removal measured at **zero** changed lift lines on any program containing a run, and 1.62% on programs without one, all of which were previously getting no taper at all. It also keeps this window consistent with the hoisted cardio taper, which has no such gate — without the removal the UI would report "no taper weeks" on a program whose sessions were visibly tapering.
- **Tempo pace reads `prog.paceProgression`, not `PACE_CHART` (V116, `run_pace_goal` only).** The chart is keyed on **mile best**; absent one it falls back to an experience default with no relationship to the stated goal, so tempo sat **flat at 11:00/mi for an entire block** while goal pace walked 9:30 → 8:05. By week 12 the session read "RPE 8-9, you should not be able to hold a conversation" at 2:55/mi slower than goal race pace and 70 s faster than the athlete's own recovery pace — **the pace and the RPE in one sentence contradicted each other**. Now `weekPace × 1.08`, which is NSW's own worked example (a 6:00/mi 1.5-mile puts Long Intervals at 6:20–6:40, i.e. +5.6% to +11.1%; 1.08 is the midpoint) and is symmetric with the `×0.95` the INT branch already applies. Cutback holds the prior week's target exactly as INT does. **A floor keeps tempo at least 30 s/mi faster than recovery pace**, because recovery still comes off the chart and the two systems can still cross on slow beginner goals — a threshold session prescribed slower than an easy run is never correct whatever the two sources say.

- **Swim sessions are segmented for performance swim goals (V141).** `buildSwimSession` composes `segments` (Warm up / Drill set / Main set / Cool down) for `swim_tri`/`swim_mile`/`swim_100_time`/`swim_500_time` outside recovery mode; `detail` is ALWAYS the mechanical flatten of the segments in the same pass, so the flat string and the render can never disagree. swim_base, recovery mode, and LSD doses <600 yd stay flat NSW-style by doctrine. LSD: total conserved exactly, no drills on long days. INT/CHI: quality work verbatim (pace splits untouched), drill set ≤25% of session, 4×25 under taper/low reps. Drill menu rotates on the 4-week cutback clock; sighting/hypoxic are `swim_tri`-only, back half. Renderer (`buildCardioHTML`) prefers segments with a mono yardage total, flat fallback covers frozen pre-V141 days; hero preview prefers the main-set segment. The `segments` key is dropped from JSON for flat sessions (`undefined`).

- **Engine B emits a `dose` dimension, and it is a contract (V148).** Every cardio session carries `dose` ∈ `time` / `dist` / `reps_dist` / `reps_time`, naming which dimension the PRESCRIPTION fixed. **The prescription owns the fixed dimension; the log captures the free one; the third derives** — never ask for two when one is prescribed. `doseDerived(dose, v)` is pure and shared by the display and the persist path so they cannot disagree, and `doseFromCardio(c)` is a fallback regex parser covering frozen pre-V148 snapshots. Before this, `cardioFieldHTML(sport, e)` keyed on SPORT alone and the structured numbers lived only in detail prose, so a 15-minute continuous CHI asked for distance and pace and the resulting record contradicted the prescription it was logged against. **NRC speed days stay on the generic distance+pace form (post-V154 ruling, reaffirmed V171 D5)**; V171 adds one `f-sub` line under it — whole session off the watch, warm up and recoveries included — keyed on `sub` matching `/speed run/i`, so the athlete does not log work reps only. **The run anchor is stated (V172).** `runAnchorInfo(cfg)` → `{anchorSec,rawSec,clamped,row,kind,prog,n,race,goalId}` or null (no run in `cardioTypes`, or a goal outside `_CHART_RUN_GOALS`); `runAnchorChips` / `runAnchorSentence` / `runAnchorLine` render it on the program card (`progDetailHTML`, group between Starting 1RM and Cardio) and in the copied selections (`progSelLines`, split out of `copyProgSel` so the text is assertable). The anchor rule is the engines' rule verbatim — beginner ignores the entry, falsy entry → experience default {690/570/450} — and the stated number is `row.mile`, i.e. what the chart clamped to, with the sentence naming the clamp. Provenance is `goal.mileBestSrc`: the seed writes `{kind:'seeded',prog,n}`, the wizard inputs write `{kind:'entered'}`, absent means default; pre-V172 programs with an entry and no src read as entered. **Recovery Pace chart (V155).** `recoveryPaceWeekly(logs, hist, totalWeeks)` walks `ia_logs_`, and for every entry with a `run_pace` reads the frozen `ia_hist_` snapshot at the SAME key (`logKey` ≡ `completedKey` by construction) — read-time resolution, no session-type tag written at log time, because the snapshot is already the prescription record and a write-time tag would be a second copy of a fact the freeze owns. Included: snapshot cardio `type==='run'` AND subtype matching `/recovery/i`, not swapped, parseable pace. Excluded is the only failure mode — a missing snapshot, a cardio-less snapshot, or junk pace degrades to a skipped entry, never a throw or a mislabeled dot (Gate S1/S18, fuzzed at 500 hostile trials). Weekly AVERAGE, never best (best-of rewards running recovery too hot); target line is the mean of the recovery doses' `tgt`. `buildPaceChart` is a DEDICATED sibling of `buildDotChart`, deliberately not an option-set on it: pace needs an inverted axis (up = faster, so improvement reads upward like every chart), a non-zero floor and m:ss labels, and threading three options through shared code puts four existing charts in the blast radius for zero visual gain. Renders after Weekly Running Mileage, only when ≥2 weeks have data — one dot is not a trend. **NRC speed sessions carry NO dose — by design, not omission (ruled post-V154).** `buildNRCSession` doses long runs (`dist`/`time`) and recovery runs (`time`+tgt) but leaves `nrc_speed1`/`nrc_speed2` at null, so those days render the generic distance+pace form. The speed slot is Nike-verbatim compound work (ladders, mixed-pace fartleks, the Shifter) with no single fixed dimension, so no dose grammar fits without inventing structure — and `doseFromCardio` correctly declines the clock notation. See §11f.
- **The pace clock can lag the calendar, and every read goes through `_ppIdx` (V142).** V125 unified every run onto one clock; V142 deliberately added a second. After an injury overlay suppresses quality running, `paceProgression` reads shift by the number of quality-suppressed days (`round(days/7)`, floor 1, day-granular), so an athlete returning from three lost weeks is not handed a pace target the calendar says they earned. Taper freezes at the last pace actually reached. **A future consumer reading `pp[wIdx]` raw reintroduces the lie silently — grep `_ppIdx` before adding any `paceProgression` consumer.**
- **Re-entry is a halfstep, not a switch (D1, V142).** "I'm back" from an injury overlay creates a second cardio-only overlay week for run-suppressed regions: quality runs return at FULL pace with reps ×0.55 (floor 2, reusing the taper's own constants) while lifts run normally. Bridge week (light everywhere, RPE 7) → halfstep week → full send. The popup promises only what the engine enforces.
- **The power dose is governed by the budget layer, never the display layer (V143/V144).** `_POWER_COST` weights movements by landing impact and `_powerDose` spends the experience-and-age-scaled budget; `vsets` must stay out of the power path. Loaded weightlifting derivatives cost **1.0** (a 0.5 classification let the budget-filling math inflate set counts to 10×3 thrusters); **the KB swing stays 0.5 by the V129 no-landing ruling**. Age scales the BUDGET (0.92 / 0.82) and separately caps impact COST via `_POWER_AGE_MAX_COST` at `min(experience, age)` — see §11f for why that split is the coaching position and not a compromise.
- **Superset formation is station-aware (V164).** `_ssPair` resolves every 2-item accessory superset at draw time against `_stationClass` (RACK/STACK/BENCH/PORT): a fixed class pairs only with itself or with portable equipment. Member-2 walks first, one member-1 re-walk (D3b) before demoting to straight sets. Conditioning circuits and curated workouts are exempt. **Every post-build pass that renames a superset member must re-satisfy `_ssLegal` against the surviving partner** — `deconflictAdjacentDupes` carries this guard; a new renamer without it silently rebuilds the banned pair (the fuzz-caught V164 hole).

### The in-progress week: accumulators exclude, observations keep (V165, D6)
Mario ruled "exclude" for the current week. It does NOT apply uniformly, and applying it uniformly would be wrong. Exclusion exists because a partial week **understates**, which is only true of accumulating metrics:
- **Excluded** (per-week sum or series): Drift From Plan, Long Run Ladder. A half-finished week drawn as a deficit reads as a failure and pushes toward training on a day the plan does not call for — the same pressure shape the V155 average-not-best ruling exists to defuse.
- **Kept** (per-observation): intensity-class bands (a run logged Tuesday is a whole run with a real pace), easy-effort weekly MEAN (a mean does not understate on a short week), the projection derived from that mean, and the Lift Ledger (all-time best, not a series).
`_cutWeek(prog)` returns the week to withhold or `0`. A program with no `startDate` has no "now" so nothing is withheld, and the FINAL week is never cut — once the block is over there is no in-progress week left to protect. Note the deliberate inconsistency inside the Ladder: the bar is withheld but "furthest so far" reads every logged run including the current week, because a 13-miler put down on Tuesday is real and hiding it would be absurd. Chart and stat are each right for their own job.

### 5r. The round count is a field, not a substring (V190, D39)
A superset section carries `rounds`. It is written at the call site from the SAME expression that writes the members (`vsets(3)`, `hsets`), so the block and its rows can never disagree by construction. `_secRounds(sec)` is the single resolver and has **no fallback constant**: an authored number wins; `rounds: null` is an authored claim that the block has no round count (AMRAP, EMOM, a clock, for-time, a max-effort attempt) and suppresses the banner; then a round count stated in the label; then — for legacy sections restored from `ia_hist_` or the creation-day `prog.weeks`, which predate the field — the **min** across members parsing BOTH grammars (`N×reps` and `N sets — RPE x`); then null. Min, never `items[0]`, because min can only ever under-state and never inflate above a capped member. Measured: 49,281/49,281 legacy generator sections recover their real number, so the null tail is reachable only from the preset library.
The header shows the section **label**; the "N Rounds — no rest between exercises · Ns rest between rounds" sentence is synthesized only when there is a real N. Member rows render reps and effort only — `_stripLeadingSets` acts on the **rendered** string; `item.detail` is left alone because `parseRx` has five callers and one of them (the add sheet's day set-count) reads the true number off the members and was correct all along. `sessionTimeEst` and the renderer read the same resolver. `preventionDoseSweep` halves `sec.rounds` for a superset and normalises every member to it with `_setLeadingSets`; non-superset accessories keep the original per-item rewrite.

**The V190 defect rates, re-measured against the true previous build (post-ship correction).** The
figures quoted in the V190 header paragraph, the digest line and §11f were measured model-vs-model on
an eleven-times-smaller lattice, and three of them do not describe what shipped. Re-run over 9,450
configs per build against **V189** (`tests/measure/v190_rounds.js`; V189 recovered and now kept in
`baselines/`), denominators identical on both builds at 560,437 superset sections / 559,140 training
days — which is itself the finding that D39 changed content and not structure:

| | V189 | V190 |
|---|---|---|
| banner sourced from the constant 3 | 203,582 / 560,437 — 36.3% | 0 |
| FABRICATED (constant landed on a number the athlete could see was wrong) | 141,324 — 25.2% | 0.0% |
| CONTRADICTED (banner disagrees with its own members) | 146,207 — 26.1% | 0.0% |
| DESYNCED (members disagree with each other) | 4,883 — 0.9% | 0.0% |
| WRONG-DURATION, as shipped (`sessionTimeEst` rounds to 5 min) | 152,546 / 559,140 — 27.3%, mean 6.0 min, worst 15 | 0.0% |
| WRONG-DURATION, model-vs-model unrounded | 268,069 — 47.9%, mean 3.4 min, worst 13.2 | — |

**Read both duration rows or neither.** 47.9% is the honest measure of how broken the logic was;
27.3% is the honest measure of what the athlete lived with. The originally recorded “45.7% of days,
mean 4.3 min, worst 6.4” is the unrounded pair and **cannot describe a shipped value** — the estimate
rounds to 5 minutes, so every error an athlete can see is a multiple of 5 and a 4.3-minute mean is
arithmetically impossible against it. The true worst case is also uglier than recorded:
`body_only / support_prevention / beginner / bodyweight` W3 Friday shipped **est=50 against an oracle
of 35**, not 6.4 on a card reading 75. The desync segmentation reproduces exactly in shape —
**4,883/81,507 (6.0%) confined to `support_prevention`, 0/469,830 across the other six focuses** — and
the cause is narrower than §11f states: the sweep’s trigger digit is a hardcoded 3, and only
`intermediate` authors a base dose of `3×`, so every desync in the file lives in the single cell
`support_prevention × intermediate`. Beginner (`2×`) and advanced (`4×`) were silent because the sweep
never fired, not because they were correct.

The V190 code comments at `index.html` ~9891 and ~10357 still carry the old figures. Left alone on
purpose: editing them is an app change and would need an `ia-version` bump for a comment. Fold into
the next build that touches the block (§12).

## 5b. Injury overlays — slot deconfliction & the rehab day (V111)
Pool overrides substitute at the **pool** level, so several slots can end up pointed at the same narrow pool. Three layers now stop that becoming duplicate output:

1. **Hip-extension reservation** (end of the injury block). If a branch *redirected* `squatPool`/`lungePool`/`hingePool`/`backCompoundPool` to hip extension, `hipExtPool` yields — filtered, and if that empties it the **slot is dropped, not filled**. Gated on an actual redirect via a `_preInj` snapshot: every no-load hinge pool naturally contains a glute bridge, so a content test alone made shoulder and elbow plans narrow a leg pool they never touched.
2. **`_slot()` inside `getEx`** — slots draw in order within a shared domain (`lower` = backMain + the legs day, since `legs`/`pull` are the two `LEG_HEAVY_ROLES`; `upper` = chest/delts/rows/vpull). `backMain` registers in **both**. Name dedup is absolute (multi-draw slots shrink rather than repeat); the pattern cap of 2 yields when it cannot be honoured. Pattern bookkeeping counts **index 0 only** — the legs builder reads `ex.lunge[0]`/`ex.hinge[0]` and nothing else, so charging the cap for an element that never reaches a card starves the next slot on volume never prescribed.
3. **`_swapUniverseAdd(raw)` before the filtered draw** — the swap sheet's harvest must never narrow with the guard.

**The guard is gated to `_inj` AND to the domains the plan actually rewrote.** Ungated it changed **77% of healthy configs**; programs rebuild from `cfg`+`seed` on every boot, so that is mid-program churn for a population with no defect. Scoped, healthy output is byte-identical (32,400 configs, 0 changed).

**Protect-tier shoulder / elbow = `Rehab + Trunk`.** The push day used to become a second pull day. The pull day already trains normally under both plans, so that was pure addition onto the injured joint. It is now `buildRehabSection()` (mirrors `buildHipSection`: 3 stability + 2 mobility, bypasses `_gear()` since the cable regex strips every banded movement off non-commercial tiers, own bodyweight list) plus a real trunk block. **No lower-body fill** — a legs-region main on a day labelled `push` is invisible to `deconflictSameRegion`, which is the exact defect class this release closes. Carries are stripped from the anti-rotation draw (adjacent in the pillar, so the rotation could hand out both; also a scapular/grip demand). `injectDynamicCore` skips any day already carrying a `Trunk ` section.

**Bike `lsd_long` collapsed into `lsd_easy` (V114):** the two bike days in a week were **byte-identical** because `lsdMins` was computed from `week` alone — `dayIdx` never entered it — so a 12-week `bike_50` block finished on two 60-minute Zone 2 spins for a 2.5–3.5 hour event. Measured: **12/12 weeks identical** on `bike_50`, and every goal except `bike_century` (which only escaped because a 4-day ceiling forces an `int`/`chi` in). `bike_century` still topped out at **60 minutes** for a six-hour event. The run engine had always forked `lsd_long`; only bike did not. **Standing lesson: when two sibling branches of a router are written as one, check that the thing distinguishing them actually reaches the arithmetic** — the type name was forked, the duration was not. **`Machine chest press` prescribed as the MAIN lift on tiers with no machine (V114):** `lightPressPool` in the `push_light` branch is a **hardcoded literal**, so the V103 `_gear` sweep — which walked `EXLIB` pools — never reached it. Live on `home_full` and `crossfit`. Second half of the same bug: `_gearOK` blocked `machine seated calf raise` **by literal name** (tightened that way in V113 to stop a substring from eating `Dumbbell seated calf raise`), which meant every *other* machine-named movement walked through; now `\bmachine\b`. Measured **172 → 32** illegal prescriptions across 126 non-commercial tier×focus×seed configs; the residual 32 are all `Leg press` from the known `EXLIB.leg_iso` fallback (§12), untouched on purpose. **Standing lesson: the gate belongs on every pool literal, not just the library ones** — V103 fixed a *class* of bug at one call site and the class survived elsewhere.

**Peak quality volume inside the taper (V115, the worst of the three).** A 12-week pace goal finished on its **single largest interval session** two days before the test — `10x400m` in race week — because `isTaperWeek` was computed in `buildRunSession` and then read by exactly one branch. Measured at **288 of 600** performance-run configs before the fix, **0** after. **Standing lesson: a flag that is computed centrally and consumed in one branch is not a feature, it is a coincidence** — grep every branch that should read a gate, not just the one that motivated it. **INT reps exceeded NSW's own ceiling (V115):** the PTG says never exceed 8 short-interval repeats; the ramp went to 10, and the leak came from the `isMilGoal && week > 26` branch bleeding into the general `Math.min(10, ...)`, with the comment "hard cap: 10" hardening it into a stated rule that then reached athlete-facing copy. **The doctrine number and the code number drifted apart and the COMMENT sided with the code.** **All-CHI-no-INT on `run_mile_time`/`run_15_under10` (V115):** the mirror image of the reported bug, found by dumping the quality sequence for every run goal rather than only the reported one — **dump the whole axis, not the reported point**, which is the same lesson as harvesting the exercise universe from the file.

**Bike and swim had no taper at all (V116).** Measured at **72 of 648** configs peaking their endurance volume inside the taper, 0 after. The cause is worth stating plainly: **the taper was documented as a single source of truth and implemented as a local variable.** `isTaperWeek` lived inside `buildRunSession`; the other two builders did not receive `eventTargeted` and never asked. **Standing lesson: "single source of truth" is a claim about call sites, not about function names — verify by grepping every consumer, not by reading the helper.** Companion finding: `programTaperWindow`'s **`types.includes('run')` gate** silently made "event-targeted" mean "event-targeted *running* program", so bike- and swim-only builds tapered nothing. **A flat tempo pace for an entire block (V116):** `PACE_CHART` is a lookup on mile best, so with no mile best supplied it returned the same tempo every week while every other pace in the program progressed — 54 of 54 sampled pace-goal configs were flat, 0 after. **Two harness bugs caught during this session's gates, both in the metric rather than the code:** `(\d+)\s*mi` truncates decimal miles ("3.8 mi" reads as 8) and reported 36 phantom run-taper failures; and partitioning a diff on the OLD build's `taperWeeks` hides changes on programs whose taper window the new build *creates* — use the union of both windows.

## 6. Regional fatigue + recovery system (current tuning)
Two axes, both in Engine D / `planCalendar`:
- **Cross-day spacing.** `deconflictLegLiftDays` (48h buffer, Sat↔Sun wrap) keeps heavy leg lifts (`LEG_HEAVY_ROLES={legs,pull}`) off/adjacent to leg-taxing cardio — moves the lift, never the doctrine-tabled cardio. `deconflictSameRegion` spaces a PRESS cluster (`{push,push_light}` adjacent) and back-to-back `full` days (leaves `pull→legs` alone — that's normal PPL). Runs after the cardio buffer; only swaps if it reduces region collisions AND doesn't worsen the cardio conflict count.
- **Within-day load cap** `capRegionalFatigue(sections, role, cardio, goal)` (pure, deep-copies): trims junk volume on push/pull/legs days via THREE triggers — pattern-saturation ceiling (`REGION_CEILING push18/pull20/legs16`), **working-set cap** (`REGION_SET_CAP push15/pull16/legs14`), and **distinct-movement cap** (`REGION_MOVE_CAP push5/pull5/legs4`). Cardio adds `_cardioInterference(cardio)` (run 1.0 > bike 0.5 > swim 0.15, scaled by intensity/distance, leg-weighted) which also pulls the set cap down. Trim is **rank-first** (finishers → isolation → secondary compounds; never the main/highest-tier compound, never core). `goal==='hypertrophy'` is **fully exempt** (opt-in volume). `full`/`active` roles exempt (mixed by design). Movement classification is name-regex (`_pattern`/`_compoundTier`); unknown → `null` → exempt from REGIONAL caps only. **Core and hip work never count regionally and are never trimmed.**
- **Session-total set budget (V100, 4th guard)** `capSessionBudget(sections, cardio)`: closes the pattern-null blind spot — 55% of a measured leg day (wall sit, hip circuit, carries) was invisible to every regional cap; the engine believed 10 sets while the athlete trained 22. Runs as the **final pass** after hip/core injection and the injury refilter so it sees the whole day. `SESSION_SET_BUDGET=20`, minus `round(interference×2)`, floor 12. Costing: stretches/mobility 0; carries, holds, isometrics, activation, core 0.5×sets; everything else full sets. Protected: main/power/strength/primer sections, hip, core, barbell-tier compounds. Trim order: carries → optional/finisher/conditioning → pump/isolation → remaining accessories, latest first. `__CAP_OFF` bypass shared with the regional cap for audit harnesses. Rarely fires on clean builds — it's the guard against pathological stacking, and the reason the old "carries are a blind spot" note below is closed.
- **Loaded hip work integrated into leg + full-body days** (`hipExtPool`/`hipExtBW` — hip thrust / glute-ham / Nordic / back-ext / pull-through, equipment-aware): replaced the redundant 2nd-lunge slot with a hinge+hip-extension posterior-chain pairing (glute power + eccentric-hamstring injury prevention for runners). `_pattern` tags these `hip_ext`→legs (also fixed back-extension→push and Nordic-curl→pull misfires). Full-body day uses the barbell-free subset.
- **Hip-stability prehab is always-on and unbundled (V101).** `includeHip` was `cardioTypes.includes('run')` → now `true`: **every** leg day carries glute-med/stability work regardless of cardio type (it matters for run AND bike, and for overall strength; was silently absent for bike/swim/pure-strength athletes). The old bundled "Hip Abductor Circuit" box is broken into **individual logged cards** under a purple "Hip stability" heading; the section keeps `hip:true` so `capRegionalFatigue` still shields it from deload trimming (the drills are `_pattern`-null, so they never counted regionally anyway — the bundle's only real job was trim-protection). `buildHipSection` now composes a **guaranteed 2 stability + 1 mobility** from split pools (`EXLIB.hip_stability` loaded/active drills vs `EXLIB.hip_mobility` stretches; bodyweight-tier equivalents inline), so a leg day can never come up all-stretches. 4 new drills (banded monster walks, standing band abduction, fire hydrants, standing bodyweight abduction), all band/bodyweight — no phantom weight field, `_pattern`-null, per-set rep rows. Placement/frequency unchanged (once, on leg day) per Mario.
- **⚠️ Week freeze (`refreshProgram`) — REQUIRED companion to every engine change.** Because refresh re-runs the current engine on every boot (§4), an engine change would otherwise silently rewrite weeks the athlete already trained (log says "suitcase + farmer carry"; card would show one). Stored weeks below a cut are carried over verbatim; only the untrained remainder gets the new engine. **Cut = `max(date-derived cut, furthest week with any completion/log + 1)`.** The date half is *current week, +1 if the current week has been touched*; the second half is the hardening that matters, because `startDate` is user-editable (`setProgStart`) and Suspend will shift the effective clock, so the date-derived week can move BACKWARD and would otherwise rebuild weeks already trained. "Touched" = any entry in `ia_comp_` **or** `ia_logs_` for that week (deliberately conservative: a logged-but-unmarked day still counts as trained). **THE CUT STILL SETS THE WINDOW, BUT SINCE V133 THE UNIT INSIDE IT IS THE DAY, NOT THE WEEK — see §11e.** The original mid-week hard boundary was justified by the engine planning a week as a **unit** (leg/press spacing, cardio placement, core look-ahead), and that reasoning assumed `prog.weeks` was roughly the current plan. It is not: refresh output is never persisted, so `prog.weeks` drifts a version further from the engine with every release, and the week-as-unit rule was handing whole weeks of a stale plan to untrained days. Implementation notes: **cannot call `calcCurrentWeek()`** inside `refreshProgram` — it reads the global `activeProg`, which is still being assigned the result of that very call; week is computed locally from `prog` instead, and completions are read by `prog.id` rather than `activeProgId`. Whole block is `try`-wrapped: on any failure it falls back to the fully-rebuilt weeks rather than breaking boot. Week-key prefix test uses `indexOf('w'+N+'_')===0` so `w11_` never matches `w1_`.
- **⚠️ The freeze restores from `ia_hist_` snapshots, NOT from `prog.weeks` (V104).** This is the correction to the original design, and it is load-bearing. `refreshProgram`'s output is never persisted (§4), so `prog.weeks` is the creation-day build with **no overlays in it** — restoring from it handed back a prescription the athlete was never shown. Now: **the moment a day is touched, `snapshotDay(week,dayKey)` writes the day object as rendered** — overlays, swaps and rest-day moves already applied — into `ia_hist_<progId>` under the same `w<N>_<day>` key. Five hook points, because any of them can be the first touch: `handleDayStatus`, `persistLogFields` (**a log alone marks the day touched, so it needs its own hook**), `toggleComplete`, `markDayComplete`, `resolveReminder`. **First touch wins, never overwritten** — the prescription on screen when the athlete started working is the record; re-tapping a status later must not rewrite it. The freeze layers snapshots over the stored week (`_histWeek(w)`) and only falls back to `prog.weeks[w][d]` where no snapshot exists.
- **⚠️ Injury pierces the freeze at DAY level (V100) — GENERALIZED IN V133; only the legacy carve-out survives (see §11e).** The freeze protects training *history* — what was prescribed on a day actually trained. A day with **no completion and no log has no history to protect**, and freezing it caused the one failure mode the guard must never cause: an athlete logs Monday, tweaks their back, reports it, and Thursday still prescribes 5×400m all-out. In `refreshProgram`, weeks below the cut that fall inside a **live injury overlay range** are merged rather than carried verbatim — trained days stay frozen, untrained days take the rebuilt (injury-applied) version from `rebuilt.weeks[w][d]`. **Scoped to `type==='injury'` only** (travel is convenience, injury is safety). Architecturally safe because injury application does **not re-plan the week**: `injurySweepCardio` substitutes in the same slot and `applyInjuryFilter` is a per-day post-pass, so no spacing recalc or core look-ahead reshuffle — the week-as-a-unit reasoning that justifies the freeze for general engine changes does not apply. Verified: trained day byte-identical, untrained days regressed, weeks outside the overlay range untouched, healthy programs freeze exactly as before. **V133 made this the rule for every week, so `_injCovers` no longer gates the pierce.** What it still gates is the one case where the two rules genuinely disagree: a day **touched with NO snapshot** (trained before `ia_hist_` shipped). The general V133 rule keeps such a day frozen to `prog.weeks`; the injury rule takes the live, injury-applied version. That carve-out is kept deliberately — on a protected week, showing all-out intervals is the worse of the two wrong answers — and it is now two lines rather than a second code path.
- **⚠️ The V100 pierce was only HALF the fix — completed in V104.** It handled untrained days correctly and left the real hole open: the *trained* branch (`if(_touched[...]) return;`) restored `prog.weeks`, which has no overlays in it. So marking a day during an injury reverted it to the pre-injury prescription on the next boot. **Observed in production on a live injury:** protected easy 35min run + 4 exercises at 18:10, athlete tapped Partial, and the 21:15 reboot showed 5×400m all-out intervals + 8 exercises. Snapshots (above) are the fix. **Backfill rule for days touched before V104 shipped** (no snapshot exists, what was on screen is unrecoverable): inside an injury range, take the **injury** version — showing all-out intervals in a protected week is the worse of the two wrong answers, and it is the same call the pierce already makes for untrained days. Outside an injury range, behaviour is unchanged.
- **Loaded carry de-bloated + cardio-gated** (legs day AND full-body day): **one** carry, not two — suitcase + farmer back-to-back was the same stimulus twice (6 sets) tacked onto a day that often already carries a run. Pick still rotates per block so both variants surface. New gate `_cardioInterference(opts.cardio) >= 1.0` **drops the carry entirely** on hard/long cardio days (measured: dry 0 → kept · easy 3.1mi Z2 0.56 → kept · long 12mi 1.35 → dropped · INT 8×800m 1.4 → dropped · long bike 70min 0.68 → kept · easy swim 0.08 → kept). Required threading `cardio` into `buildSections` via opts (it previously reached only `capRegionalFatigue`). `_cardioInterference` is a top-level declaration in the single script block, so it hoists and is callable from `buildSections` despite being defined later.
- **Blind spot (mostly closed by V100's session budget):** carries/holds/iso `_pattern`-nulls no longer escape total-volume accounting — the session budget counts them at 0.5. The narrower residual: `_pattern` still can't classify some iso movements (cable fly, pec deck) for REGIONAL saturation purposes; the budget is the backstop. Widen `_pattern` when loaded-hip strength enters the pools.
- **`_cardioInterference` is subtype-first (V176/D8).** The subtype names the session and classifies alone; the combined subtype+detail string decides only when the subtype says nothing (a Benchmark Run's "steady" lives in its detail). Fixes NRC long runs (details say "average Recovery Pace") and NSW LSD misfiling as 0.55 recoveries. Minutes regex is `[\s-]*min` — reads `60-minute`.
- **`d18LongRunDayPass` (V176/D18) runs immediately before `singletonSupersetSweep`** — its tier-B leg filter can orphan a superset member, and the sweep demotes it. Tier from `_longRunTier(cardio)`: `isNRC` + `^Long Run` subtype + `dose` (`mins`, or `mi × tgt / 60`); race day null, `/rehearsal/` forced 'A'; ≥75 'A', ≥45 'B', else 'C'. A: sections replaced by `_d18MobilityBlock()` (5 items, all matching the stretch-cost vocabulary — `T-spine mobility`, not "opener", so `_isStretch` regexes see it) + any `[Taper]` note; title → `Post-Run Mobility`. B: power sections dropped, `_D18_LEG_RX` filters items by name (items carry no `_pattern`), ≤8 non-stretch sets kept in document order. Carries stripped on every tier.

## 6b. Temporary overlays (travel) — the subsystem
`prog.overlays[]` is a **sibling to `cfg`**: cfg is the recipe, overlays are modifications applied to the built result. A temporary change must never rewrite cfg (the athlete would have to remember to undo it; they won't). Shape `{id,type:'substitute',from,to|null,patch:{...cfg},created}`. **Dates are the source of truth, weeks derived** (Suspend will shift weeks, so week-anchoring would be circular). `to:null` = open-ended.

**Mechanism = DAY splice (V175, D27; was a week splice through V174).** Build a variant program from the patched cfg; the overlay's week range is only the search window — inside it, a day takes the variant only if its own calendar date falls in `[from, to]`, both inclusive (D29). A Tuesday-only trip changes Tuesday. Rest days stay the base's. A full-week overlay resolves every day and is byte-identical to the old whole-week splice. Works because the engine keys off the TRUE program week, so week 5 from a travel build is still week 5 (same wave position, deload cadence, taper math), and because the split is cfg-driven — equipment only changes the picks — so the variant's same-day is a structural drop-in (measured: 0 mismatches on cardio / core presence / role / tags over 54,432 pairs). Cross-seam adjacent-day repeats run at 4.4%, BELOW the base engine's own 6.9% (bodyweight builds run 19.7%), so the merged week is not re-deconflicted. Each spliced day is stamped `_ovKey` (§4). One `buildProgram` per distinct patch. Overlap: later-created wins, per day. **Both overlay types splice by day.** Bridge and halfstep are built date-exact (`from..from+6`, `from+7..from+13`), so they now cover exactly the seven days the popup promises; under the week splice a Wednesday "I'm back" overlaid THREE calendar weeks (bridge spilled into the halfstep's week, halfstep into a third). **Order in `refreshProgram`: build → overlays → freeze** (freeze last, so a temporary change can never rewrite a trained week).

**`to` is inclusive — the last day the overlay applies (V175, D29).** The engine always was (`applyOverlayDraft` accepts `to === from`; `paceShiftMap` counts `to` as a hurt day; the bridge math depends on it); only the label and the chip copy read as first-day-back. The sheet field is **THROUGH**; equal dates are a one-day trip; the error is "Through can't be before From". Every surface that names a span goes through one grammar: `overlaySpanLabel` (whole weeks keep `Week 2` / `Weeks 2–3`; a day is `Tue Sep 1`; a span is `Sep 1–4` / `Sep 29–Oct 2`; open-ended is `From Sep 1`), `overlayBackCopy` (`<to+1> picks your program back up.` or `That runs to the end of your program.`), `_dayCount` / `_dayCountWord` (One day … Fourteen days, digits past that). The chip sub reads "Room only. Back to normal Wed Sep 2." — never "reverts on its own", which was the `to`-exclusive reading.

**What the week screen says (V175, D30).** Read off the DAYS, never the overlay range: `Maintenance Week` pill only when every scheduled training day in view is a hotel day (`_tds.every(dayIsTravel)`); otherwise the phase pill stands. Trip days carry a 2px underline in the strip (`.wk-day.away`, cream on today's signal cell, ink elsewhere — spans a trip like a bracket, same vocabulary as the injury rail one level down) and a hollow `Travel` tag (backpack glyph) on the hero and in the session-sheet tag row (`travelTagHTML(day)`, derived at render, never pushed into `day.tags`). A commercial patch (pre-V96 overlays) marks nothing.

`applyOverlays` stamps **`_travel:true`** on variant builds (set there, not in the patch, so it applies to overlays saved before it existed).

**Three travel tiers** (`_EQUIP_OPTS`), and the load model they drive:
| Tier | equipment | main lift | log field |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Room only** | `bodyweight` | `3 sets — RPE 8 (stop 2 reps short of failure)` | reps |
| **Mini gym** | `home_basic` **+ `_travel`** | `3 sets of 8 to 12 — RPE 8 (heaviest pair you can find)` | weight |
| **Full gym** | `commercial` | **creates no overlay** (see below) | weight |

- `noLoad` = `bodyweight`‖`minimal` (nothing to add → reps are the variable). `loadCapped` = `_travel` **and** `home_basic` (hotel racks top out ~40 lb; a 155 lb squatter needs ~130 lb for a triple, so the low-rep end cannot be expressed). **Keyed off `_travel`, not equipment** — `home_basic` from the WIZARD is a real home gym with adjustable dumbbells and must keep the normal wave. Verified both ways.
- **Full gym is an ANSWER, not an overlay.** An overlay is only useful when the athlete has LESS than usual, and a real gym is never less. Creating one would splice in weeks identical to what they already had, then announce the non-change with a chip, week markers and a Maintenance pill; worse, for a dumbbell-based athlete it would "upgrade" them onto barbell lifts mid-block, changing the stimulus AND orphaning their logged history (`ia_exw_` keys on exercise NAME). So picking it hides the date fields, shows "You're set. Train your program as written.", and the button becomes **Got it** → `closeOverlaySheet()`. Nothing is saved. The option stays visible so nobody has to guess whether their situation is covered. `applyOverlayDraft` also guards the tier, and the Maintenance-pill suppression for `commercial` is retained to cover overlays saved before V96.
- **Travel = maintain, not progress.** Cardio is untouched and keeps building (verified: 18/18 sessions byte-identical across an equipment swap — running only needs shoes). `Maintenance Week` pill on travel weeks, suppressed for Full gym.
- **Bodyweight tier is travel-only, not in the wizard.** Pools authored EASY→HARD; `_bwRung` starts the athlete on the rung `cfg.experience` earns; `_bwFlat` opts out pools where difficulty is not the axis (conditioning/carries/plyos — narrowing them starved `pick(pool,4,…)` and produced **undefined exercise names**). `bodyweightSweep` is a final catch-all after core/hip injection (gear also arrives from the core engine, hip injector and arm accessories; explicit map then keyword fallback so new gear degrades safely). It also converts fixed-rep accessories to sets-by-RPE and **de-duplicates within a day** (no-load pools are narrow enough that independent picks collide). `_BW_KEEP_FIXED` matches on NAME and exempts holds, plyos/conditioning (**near-failure jumps are how people get hurt**), mobility, carries and core.
- No bands or bag assumed (shoes and clothes only). **Consequence:** bodyweight pulling is inverted rows plus prehab, so pull volume is genuinely lower in a Room-only block. Acceptable for a 2-week maintenance trip.

## 7. Design system & platform
- **"The Asylum" light DS** — warm bone paper + ink + rationed signal-orange (assigned ONLY to live affordances: today marker, Start CTA, Log button, running rest timer, popup accents — "if everything is orange, nothing is live"). Helvetica Neue → Geist; rectilinear radii (4px std, 2px tags, 6px hero, 50% circles); muted earthy sport colors; warm shadows. Full spec in `THE_ASYLUM_DS_REFERENCE.md`.
- **Icon system `asyIcon(nameOrEmoji, size)`** — inline SVG (24×24, 2px, currentColor); ~61 paths (Lucide-family + hand-drawn fitness marks + Design-tool additions). Emoji translate via `ASY_EMOJI` (strips `️` first); unmapped glyphs fall through unchanged. **Rule:** emoji only convert when routed through `asyIcon()` — hardcoded literals in template/`textContent` strings render raw; use `innerHTML` on the host node. **Still literal by choice:** trash-talk/celebration copy-pool emoji, the 🏆 confetti particle, and monochrome text glyphs (✓ ✕ ← → ↓ ↺).
- **This Week screen** — editorial `WEEK n` hero + phase pill (in `.wk-subrow`, RIGHT), Mon→Sun enriched day strip (cardio modality code above / lift-or-run focus code below / date), hero card (today / REST + rotating NSW tip / browsed-week preview), stat tiles (Done/Miles/Streak), tap-to-expand. Bottom 4-tab bar (Programs / This Week / Progress / Bugs), max-width 430px centered.
- **Session sheet (`openDetail`)** — slide-up overlay, pinned editorial header + `X/Y LOGGED` counter, exercise cards with inline logging + binary green logged-state, count-up rest stopwatch (hundredths, timestamp-based, auto-stops on close, pauses on background), pinned tri-state footer. **Header stack (V118):** `.dh-toprow` (Back / counter / RPE) → `.dh-over` (WEEK n · DAY) → `.dh-title` → `.dh-tagrow`, which is a flex row holding `#detailTags` (`flex:1;min-width:0`) with the `+ EXERCISE` button pinned right. **The button must live in `.dh-tagrow`, never inside `#detailTags`** — `openDetail` does `tg.innerHTML = modTagsHTML(day.tags)` on every open, so anything parked in that div is wiped on first render. Chips wrap inside their own box and the button never moves. The row never renders empty in practice: `openDayKey` routes rest days to `openRestSheet`, so a detail header always carries at least the LIFT chip.
- **Reinforcement popups** — `fireCompletionPopup` (precedence season > milestone > workout), streak counter (complete extends — legacy partial reads as complete — skipped breaks, pending transparent), day-after reminder (two actions since V181: ✓ Done / ✕ Skip), milestone tiers (3/7/14/30 then every 30), season = final scheduled day (once ever — but V181 `releaseSeasonShown()` re-arms it when the final day is unmarked). **Workout and streak popups carry ONE stat, the streak (V181 D4)**; the season popup keeps Workouts / Skips / Weeks as the block-end accounting. Confetti = Knicks colors. Context-aware copy pools (`{t,when}` weekend/morning).
- **Expandable Program cards** — tap to recall full onboarding (`progSelData` mirrors the wizard review) + incognito copy-for-troubleshooting (`copyProgSel` dumps exact `cfg` incl. seed). Lifts shown are experience-derived (`DEFAULT_1RM`) tagged `(est.)` when not manually entered.
- **Bugs tab** → Web3Forms POST (`BUG_FORM_KEY` embedded, domain-registered `bigyerr.github.io` so it only succeeds from the live app) with auto-appended context (UA, viewport, PWA mode, active program). Reports land at mario.aeo@gmail.com.
- **Responsive:** `clamp()` on 6 hero display sizes, ceiling-locked to current px so ≥430px renders byte-identical; only sub-430 phones scale down. `viewport-fit=cover` is ON (every header already had the `,44px` fallback; enabling it makes safe-area insets real — fixed the bone-strip-below-tabbar bug).

## 8. Logging, completion & progress

**Progress is per-program (V156, G1 ruling — Mario rejected cross-program chart merging).** `progressViewId` (null = active) picks which program's charts render; selector chips at the top of Progress list active first, then live, then archived (🗄 badge), hidden entirely with <2 programs. One program's chart set renders at a time — lazy, because charts are the heaviest DOM in the app. Every store read inside `renderProgressScreen` goes through the `*For(_vid)` accessors, including the fold+dedupe cleanup (which writes back to the VIEWED program's store) — a viewed program can never leak the active one's data (Gate T7 asserts the leak directly). `showProgressScreen` resets the view to active on every open. Viewing an archived program shows a restore bar (`unarchiveProg`). Old program week 3 ≠ new program week 3 in time — that x-axis mismatch is WHY per-program was ruled over merging, and any future cross-program feature must bucket by `ts`, not week index.
**Wheels, not text boxes, for time and distance (V182).** Three fields in the session-log form are scroll wheels: the generic run form's miles and pace, and `reps_dist`'s typical rep time. `_IAW_SPEC` declares the columns; `iaWheelHTML(kind, hiddenId, val)` emits the markup *plus a hidden `<input>` carrying the field's original id*, and `iaWheelInit()` seeds and wires it. **Nothing downstream knows a wheel exists** — `_iawCommit` writes the hidden node and dispatches `input`, so `persistLogFields` and the two `addEventListener('input')` arrays are unchanged, and Done/Skip is still the only commit (there is deliberately no Log button; a second commit surface would create an unsaved-value trap the text boxes never had). Dose forms keep their number boxes and keep deriving pace. Two properties are load-bearing: the clock form always zero-pads seconds, so the wheel **cannot** emit the `9:3` shape that `_paceStrToSec` silently rejects; and a stored value that is out of range or malformed **clamps for display only** — `iaWheelInit` never commits, so reopening a day can never rewrite what was logged.

**Two distinct logging surfaces (don't conflate):**
1. **Session-log form** (`buildLogHTML(dayKey, dayCardio)`, one per day, bottom of detail): RPE slider → day-appropriate cardio field(s) → Notes → **the status stack (V181): a full-width signal `Done` and a small bordered `Skip` beneath it.** `persistLogFields(dayKey)` writes to `ia_logs_`; called by `handleDayStatus`. **One tap = save + mark. Both states TOGGLE: tapping the active state unmarks the day back to pending** (record deleted, log entry kept, footer rebuilt by `renderStatusFoot`, season latch released if it was the final day). The primary button always shows the CURRENT state and undoes it; the small button is always the other state. No hint copy, no Partial — the athlete who swapped or dropped something still showed up, and the app already has the swap/skip flags if anyone ever wants the accounting.
2. **Inline per-exercise log — per-set (V101)** (`buildExItem`, in the detail sheet AND the Random overlay). The prescription string is parsed by **`parseRx(detail)`** into a per-set spec — `reps` / range→**midpoint** / `sec` (steps 5) / `yd` / open-ended `N sets — max` — and each prescribed set renders as its **own stepper row** (−/＋ around a value prefilled to target; untouched logs as prescribed; a signed delta appears when you go off target; open-ended rows start blank with a `max` placeholder). Loaded lifts (`isKBExercise ‖ isTrackableWeight`) add a **single weight field** plus a **"reuse" pill** — `↺ 135 lb / 8 days ago` — showing the last weight in human time (`recencyLabel`, never the old program-week `Wx`) that taps to refill the box. `saveExWeight(exKey,name,detail)` reads every row + the weight and stores `{weight, setsDone[], setsReps, week, ts, unit}` via `logExerciseWeight`, which **no longer rejects weightless logs**, so bodyweight work now records its actual reps. Prose-only movements (no `parseRx` match, not loaded) render no log block. **`exLoggable(name,detail)` is the shared predicate** for "does this row log"; `sessionLogProgress` uses it so bodyweight rows count toward the day's X/Y. Random Workout renders this path but `completeWildcard` ignores the inputs (marks done + title only).

   **Detection: the name is not the source of truth (V107).** `isTrackableWeight` vetoes on `isRepExercise`, which substring-matches `split squat`, `dip`, `hip thrust`. **The engine draws the SAME string into a loaded pool and a bodyweight pool** — `EXLIB.lunge` holds `Bulgarian split squat` beside `Reverse lunge (KB)`, while `_bw()` arg1 holds `Bulgarian split squat (foot on chair)` — so the veto stripped the weight field from the loaded variant. Library-wide audit (1082 names through the real extracted detectors, classified by **pool membership**, not by eye) found **3** genuine cases: `Bulgarian split squat` (EXLIB.lunge), `Single-leg hip thrust` (`_gear` hip-ext pool + the knee/ankle `protect` fallback `lungePool`), `Bench dips` (EXLIB.triceps, every other member loaded). The other 17 flagged names are correctly bodyweight. Fix is `REP_BUT_LOADED` + `BW_QUALIFIER` in `isLoadedRepMovement`, checked **above** the rep veto: **the library's own convention is that the bodyweight variant is explicitly qualified** (`(chair)`, `(foot on chair)`, `(shoulders on bed)`, `Assisted `, `Bodyweight `, `Banded `) and the loaded one is bare, so the qualifier always beats the allowlist. Diffed before/after across all 1082 names: **exactly 3 rows changed, zero collateral.** Pinned by the detector harness in the gate. **Adding a name to `REP_BUT_LOADED` is a coaching call, not a parser tweak** — keep the list short; the real fix is §12.

   **The store key has ONE writer (V113).** `ia_exw_` keys on `name.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]/g,'_')`, and that slug was inlined at **three** call sites (card render, `logExerciseWeight`, and the swap path). All three now call **`exStoreKey(name)`**, which resolves through `EX_KEY_ALIAS` — built at load from the `EX_RENAMED_V113` old→new map — before slugging. **Nothing in localStorage is migrated**, deliberately: `ia_hist_` day snapshots still hold the OLD strings (a frozen week is a record of what was prescribed, so it is *correct* that it keeps them), both spellings stay live forever, and a rollback to V112 finds its data untouched. The same layer collapses the duplicate-spelling splits (`Barbell bench` vs `Barbell bench press`, `Kettlebell swings` vs `Kettlebell swing`, `Romanian deadlift (DB)` vs `Dumbbell Romanian deadlift`) without rewriting a stored record. **Any future rename adds a line to the map, never a rename in isolation.**
