   **Detection lives behind one map (V101).** Whether an exercise shows a weight field is decided by `isKBExercise` / `isDBExercise` and the load-token override in `isTrackableWeight`, all reading a single **`EQUIP_TOKENS`** table via `hasEquip`/`anyEquip`. Each family lists every surface form INCLUDING the parenthetical suffix (`(kb`, `(db`, `(bb`, `(cable`…), so a new equipment abbreviation is a one-line add and every detector inherits it. This closed 30 loadable movements that previously showed **no log field at all** — see §11.

   **Collapsed set bar + per-set load (V105).** Set rows now fold behind a one-line summary (`Sets  4 × 8  · as prescribed  ⌄`, caret `asyIcon('chevronDown',20)`). **The collapsed state is a WORKING state, not a preview** — it keeps the Load field and the reuse pill, so a lift performed as prescribed at one weight logs with zero extra taps; expanding exists only for divergence. Expanded, each set row gains its own load cell (`exsetw_<key>_<n>`): muted text = inheriting the collapsed Load via `cascadeSetWeights`, solid = individually overridden. **Cascade never clobbers a typed cell.** **Top-set tag (corrected V106):** the orange outline + TOP badge appears **only when the ladder is varied**, and then on **every** row at max. V105 shipped it always-on with ties resolving to the first occurrence, which meant a normal flat 4×8 permanently badged **set 1** — user-reported as confusing on first contact, and correctly so: on a flat ladder every set IS the top set, so tagging one asserts a distinction that does not exist. **The rule to hold: tag visibility is identical to the Varied chip's condition.** They state the same fact and must appear and disappear together — V105's defect was two indicators of one state with different triggers. Tagging only the first of two equal top sets is the same confusion in a smaller costume, hence all-rows-at-max. Footer label flips **Top set** / **Every set** to match. Collapsing after divergence shows the ladder (`185 / 275 / 225`) and a **Varied** chip, and the Load label flips to **Top set** — hiding divergence behind the chevron is the one thing this feature must not do. Reopening a logged exercise **restores** its `setsW`/`setsDone` rather than resetting to the prescription. Unloaded movements render the row with no load cell rather than an empty box. Set index is a bare numeral (was `Set 3`) and steppers narrowed 46→38px to buy the width the load cell needs — **both changes are load-bearing for fitting the row on a 375px phone.**

   **SET DRAFTS — an edit is not a log (V131).** Before V131 the set rows lived ONLY in the DOM until `Log` was tapped: stepping 8→10, leaving the day and returning redrew the card from `ia_exw_`, found nothing, and reprinted the prescription over the edit. **User-reported, and the obvious fix was the wrong one** — routing `stepSet` straight into `saveExWeight` would have stamped `.logged` and the ✓ on one tap of set 1 of 4, moved `X / Y LOGGED`, and (the real problem) written a **weight the athlete never confirmed**, because `saveExWeight` falls back to the field's placeholder and the placeholder was last session's load. A silent false point on the progress chart.

   So the fix splits two facts `ia_exw_` had conflated: *numbers are on screen* vs *the athlete says this is done*. `readSetDraft` / `writeSetDraft` / `clearSetDraft` store the former **inside the existing `ia_logs_` day entry as `sets:{exKey:{r:[],w:[],L:''}}`** — deliberately not a new store. `ia_logs_` is already keyed week+day, already inside the freeze's "touched" test, and already bounded by program length; **a new `ia_draft_` would have been a second re-apply store with no prune function and `ia_moves_` is already that one.** **A draft never reaches `ia_exw_`**, so the tick, the counter and every chart are structurally incapable of reading it.

   **Values are the RAW STRINGS the athlete typed, `''` for untouched — never placeholder-derived.** `rawSetVals` exists precisely because `setRepVals`/`setWeightVals` resolve an empty cell to its placeholder, which is the one inference a draft must not make; restoring would otherwise stamp zeros into load cells nobody filled. On restore, `buildExItem` keeps **two shapes on purpose**: the ROWS take nulls so an untouched rung falls back to its prescribed target, while the collapsed HEADLINE takes `_headR`/`_headW` with those rungs resolved — feeding the headline nulls reads `+null` as 0 and reports a flat ladder as **Varied**.

   **Two writers into one day entry, and both must read-modify-write.** `persistLogFields` rebuilds `logs[key]` from scratch, so it now carries `sets` forward explicitly — without that, moving the RPE slider destroyed every draft on the day. The reverse holds too: `writeSetDraft` merges rather than replaces. **`writeSetDraft` also calls `snapshotDay`**, same reasoning as `persistLogFields`: any `ia_logs_` entry makes the week *touched* for the freeze, so the day has to be captured in `ia_hist_` or the week freezes with no record of what it froze on.

   **LIVE AFTER LOG (Mario's call).** Draft is the state BEFORE `Log`. After it, the weight is confirmed, nothing is left to guess, and edits commit straight through via `commitSetsLive` → `saveExWeight(..., quiet)` — same writer, no toast, `setsReps` carried from the existing entry so a quiet re-save cannot blank the prescription string. **The state this rules out is a ✓ sitting over numbers that are not in the record**, which draft-always would have allowed until a second `Log` tap. `saveExWeight` calls `clearSetDraft` on every write, so a draft and a record for the same exercise/week/day can never coexist.

   **THE LOAD FIELD NO LONGER PRE-FILLS (V131, Mario's call).** `exw_` and every `exsetw_` cell used to carry last session's load as a placeholder. **This is not a redundancy argument, it is a doctrine conflict:** the app prescribes RPE — "stop 2 reps short of failure" is an instruction to read today's body — and a number already sitting in the box is an instruction to match last Tuesday. The number wins, because it is the one already there. Both placeholders are now empty, `cascadeSetWeights` stamps `''` rather than `'0'`, and the ↺ reuse pill (unchanged, Mario's call) remains the one-tap route to that number. **Consequences, all accepted:** logging a loaded lift without entering a weight now stores `weight: 0` instead of last session's — the per-exercise chart already filters `+e.weight > 0` at two levels so it plots nothing, but **the reuse pill had to move to the last entry with a weight** (`lastWEntry`), because keyed on the last entry outright a single weightless log blanked the pill. **And the common path costs one more tap:** a lift done exactly as last week was open→Log, and is now open→↺→Log. That tap is the feature — the zero-tap version was only free because it was guessing.

   **Selector footgun (V105):** `saveExWeight` collects reps via `input[id^="exset_<key>_"]`. The new load cells are `exsetw_` and the delta readouts are `<span id="..._d">` — the first is excluded by the prefix diverging at char 6, the second **only by the `input` tag filter**. Do not relax that selector to `[id^=...]`.

**`parseRx` "N sets" grammar (V120).** Two holes, both pre-existing and both found while scoping B2. (1) The open-set branch also demanded the words `max|amrap|failure`, which only the **RPE 8** copy carries — the RPE 6 and RPE 7 copy says "leave 3 or more in reserve" and "leave plenty in the tank", so `3 sets — RPE 7 (...)` returned **null**, `exLoggable` had nothing left to fall back on for a bodyweight movement, and **756 shipped prescriptions on the bodyweight tier rendered with NO log field and sat outside the `X/Y LOGGED` denominator** (worst: `Pike pushups` 194, `Pike pushups (feet elevated)` 190, `Pushups (slow 3s eccentric)` 122). **The grammar itself is the signal** — a leading `N sets` with no rep target after it IS an open prescription, whatever note follows. (2) The travel/`loadCapped` branch emits `3 sets of 8 to 12 — RPE 8 (heaviest pair you can find)`, a real rep range written in words, which nothing read: **88 unloggable travel prescriptions**. Now parsed to its midpoint. **The `(?!\s*of\s*\d)` guard and the ordering are load-bearing** — a naive `^N sets` loosening reads `3 sets of 8 to 12` as open and throws the rep range away. Harvested, not guessed: the file contains exactly **6 distinct `N sets` literals**, all 6 are asserted in the gate, and **0 already-parsing strings changed** (generation hash byte-identical). `parseRx` has **four** runtime consumers and **none** of them are in generation: `exLoggable` (the fix), `buildExItem` (renders the log block), `addedDetailFor` (inherits the last parseable accessory dose — now inherits real doses it used to skip), and the add sheet's `N SETS` chip (counts non-open rx only, so the 756 stay excluded and the 88 travel rows now count correctly).

   **`parseRx` loose grammar (V108).** The strict `N×M` / `N sets — max` grammar rejected the bare shapes wildcard sessions actually use, so **58 prescriptions across 26 movements rendered with no log surface at all** and 130 more had a load field but no set rows. `_parseRxLoose(d,name)` runs **only when the strict grammar returns null**, so no existing parse can change (verified: 0 of 414 already-parsing strings moved). Shapes handled, all taken from real library strings: `N × max`; interval rounds (`20s on / 10s off × 8` → N open sets); timed max (`1 min max reps`); single set with a REQUIRED unit token (`45 sec`, `12 reps`, `10 each`, `30 yards`); `aim N`; bare `max reps` / `Max time`; **per-set ladders** (`10/8/6/4/2`, `10/10/10/AMRAP`, `1,2,3,4,5,4,3,2,1`) which return a new **`targets[]`** where a null rung is open; and the count living in the NAME (`40 pushups` / "break as needed"). **The unit token on the single-set rule is load-bearing** — without it `3 sets — RPE 8, 2 min rest` reads as one set of 3, which is exactly why the strict grammar could not simply be loosened to accept a leading integer. `\S` after the digits in the name rule keeps `45° back extension` from reading as a 45-rep set. Remaining unparsed: 2 pairs (`Sprint effort`, a cardio instruction covered by the session-log form, and one prose chinup ladder).

   **Divergence display covers BOTH dimensions (V108).** V105 keyed the collapsed ladder and the Varied chip on `setsW` only, so an unloaded movement — 2×45 sec logged as 45 and 75 — stored both values in `setsDone`, restored them on reopen, and still reported "2 × 45 sec, as prescribed". `setsSummary(rx,wkW,wkR)` now falls through weights → reps → prescription. **Weights win when both vary** (weight divergence is the progression-relevant fact) but **only weight divergence renames the collapsed field to "Top set"**. A ladder PRESCRIPTION states itself as a ladder rather than as `5 × 10`. `setDelta` refreshes the summary on every branch — for unloaded work that is the only code path that can report divergence.

   **The collapsed bar has exactly TWO writers, and they must converge (V112).** `buildExItem` writes it at render; `refreshSetSummary` rewrites it live. Nothing else may touch `exsum_`/`exsumsub_` — the hand-written `'logged'` stamp that used to sit in `saveExWeight`'s tail is gone, and `refreshSetSummary` derives that half from the card's own `.logged` class instead (`loggedThisWk` is now `!!thisWkEntry` so the class and the sub-label are provably the same fact, not two predicates over it). **The latch:** `refreshSetSummary` wrote the headline only inside its `anyVary` branch and the `else` did nothing but hide the Varied chip, so stepping into a ladder and back out left the headline frozen on the last varied string it ever saw — 7/7/7 on screen reading `10 / 10 / 6`. Storage was never affected (`saveExWeight` reads live inputs). **`flatDiverged(rx,arr)` is the shared predicate:** `setsVaried` asks "did the ladder tilt", this asks "did the FLAT ladder land somewhere else", and both the headline and the sub-label read it so they cannot contradict — `3 × 10 · as prescribed` sitting over a logged 7/7/7 was the same bug wearing the other half of the line. **Open prescriptions count (Mario's call):** `3 sets to max` logged flat at 12 reads `3 × 12`, because the prescription is what you were told to do and the bar is what you did. The `v>0` guard inside `flatDiverged` is load-bearing, not defensive — an untouched open row's placeholder is `max`, which parses to 0, so every unlogged open card arrives as `[0,0,0]` and without it would report `3 × 0`. **Per-set delta badges render in `buildExItem` at build time**, not only via `setDelta` on input, or a reopened card shows badges on the rows touched since the last render and blanks on identical untouched ones.

   **Add / skip exercises (V109).** `+ Exercise` sits on the day-header overline (`.dh-over` is now a flex row); per-row minus on accessory/hip/core; **block-level minus on the superset header only**, because the round protocol makes its items interdependent — pull one movement and the work:rest ratio, which IS the prescription, is no longer what was programmed. **A superset header is now ALWAYS drawn** (previously suppressed when the label already said "Rounds") since it is the only legal home for that control. No minus on the main lift (skipping it is not editing the day, it is not doing the day) and none on added items, which carry **remove** instead.

   **Skips FLAG, never splice.** `it._skipped` / `sec._skipped` leave a struck-through stub that (a) distinguishes "the engine never programmed this" from "I bailed on this" and (b) carries the only undo. `sessionLogProgress` **excludes skipped work from the denominator** — otherwise skipping one thing makes `6 / 6 LOGGED` unreachable and the counter reads as failure for the rest of the session. **Mario's call: a skipped exercise does NOT force the day to Partial** (the whole Partial/Complete model is the next project).

   **The picker does NOT filter to the day's region.** "I want hip thrusts twice a week" is a real request and a push day must still offer them. Off-region candidates appear under **NOT WHAT TODAY IS FOR** with the collision stated on the row. Blocking the athlete is paternalistic; offering it silently is negligent. `addCandidates` otherwise reuses swap's machinery verbatim — same `swapUniverseFor` harvest, same `_swapInjuryOK`, same on-card dedupe.

   **Added work inherits the accessory block's current dose** (`addedDetailFor`), never a fixed default: on a deload week the block is already lighter so the addition deloads with it, where a hardcoded `3×10` would prescribe full-volume work mid-taper. Falls back to `3×10 — RPE 7` only when there is no accessory block to read. Pure RPE makes the carry-over honest on any movement, same dividend as swap.

   **`applyDayEdits` must stay IDEMPOTENT and ORDER-INDEPENDENT** — `refreshProgram` runs it on every boot. It runs **after** swaps (a swap rewrites a name, so a skip recorded against the post-swap name would miss if it ran first). **Add and skip are disjoint by construction: `_added` sections are immune to skip flagging.** Fuzz (3000 sequences) caught the violation — a store holding both an add and a skip for the same name flagged nothing on pass one (item did not exist yet) and flagged it on pass two, i.e. a different day on every launch. `resnapshotDayEdit` folds an edit into an existing `ia_hist_` entry; without it, editing a day already logged means the freeze restores a day missing the edit — the V104 bug's exact shape.

   **Nothing here touches `cfg`, goal or periodization.** Programs stay deterministic from `cfg`+`seed`; edits apply on top of the rebuild like moves and swaps. **Consequence, deliberate: adding a lift on Tuesday does nothing for next Tuesday.** The standing-preference path (mirroring `bumpSwapCount`'s 3-in-a-row nudge into `cfg`) is scoped but NOT built — it is dead code until adds have three weeks of repeat data.

   **Dark-header contrast (V109).** `.detail-header` overrides `--text`/`--muted`/`--accent` to on-ink but **not** `--surface2`/`--border`. Anything placed in that header which reads a surface or border var gets a light chip with near-white text. `.swap-tag` did exactly that: **2.29:1, reported unreadable from device**. Fixed by stating the chip's colors explicitly rather than overriding `--surface2` on the header, which would silently repaint anything added there later. Same sweep found the `MOD_PAL` fallback at **1.01:1** (near-black on translucent black) — any tag with no palette entry. Now 7.74:1 and 3.55:1. **The rest of MOD_PAL sits at 2.7–4.0 depending on which background it renders against; that is a palette-tuning question for Mario, deliberately left alone.**

   **The Load box restores today's number (V149).** After logging, re-rendering the day read the prescription rather than the day record and the load box came back empty. It now restores today's logged weight from the day record on render. Same family as V131's draft split: a value on screen and a value in the store disagreeing about which is authoritative.

   **The collapsed SETS line speaks reps only (V150).** The summary line used to carry a weight ladder, duplicating what the VARIED badge and the Top Set relabel already say. Ruling: the SETS line states reps (flat / off-plan / varied); the badge and the label carry the weight story. Same principle as V106's top-set tag — **two indicators of one state must not have different triggers, and one indicator must not state two facts.**

   **One movement, one history thread (V151).** `ia_exw_` keys on the slugged name and **the stored row's LABEL is first-writer-wins**, so Progress showed whichever spelling logged first. V113's alias layer resolves new writes only, so old-key rows persist in localStorage forever as a SECOND Progress row for one movement. Three parts now: retired spellings are gone from the pools; `EX_RENAMED_V113` carries the aliases (including defensive future-drift entries); and **`foldExAliasKeys(store)`** folds old-key rows into the canonical key, concatenates and re-sorts entries by (week, ts), unconditionally stamps the canonical name, and returns whether anything changed. It is idempotent and runs at `init()` and at `renderProgressScreen` — **ordered BEFORE `dedupeExDayEntries`, because a fold can create a same-day pair.** The fold is store hygiene through the existing dedupe chokepoint, not a migration layer: read-time resolution still stands, `ia_hist_` snapshots keep their original strings by design, and a rollback to V150 finds every entry (merely re-split). **Any future rename adds a line to the map, never a rename in isolation — and note `ia_swaps_`/`ia_swapct_` key on NAME and are NOT covered by `EX_KEY_ALIAS`.**

   **Name-vs-key drift is a recurring bug family, and it fails silently (V151).** Two features read the exercise store by RAW NAME against a slug-keyed store: the e1RM test-set prefill (`getExWeights()[mainName]`) and the V139 range-top double-progression nudge (`store[it.name]`). Both always resolved undefined, both sat inside `try/catch`, and **the nudge had never fired in production.** Both now key through `exStoreKey()` and read `.entries`. **Any new reader of `ia_exw_` goes through `exStoreKey`; indexing by name is the bug.**

**Progress screen** — dotted-line charts (run/bike/swim volume, RPE trend, big-3 top weights sourced from `ia_exw_`), per-exercise history, collapsible per-week Session Journal, always-visible 1RM estimator. (All section titles/headers on this screen render their icon via `asyIcon()` as of V90.)

**Folded-in current behavior:** one weight entry per exercise per week (re-log overwrites, latest wins; legacy dupes self-clean on Progress open); the Exercise History chart filters to positive-weight entries so bodyweight logs (`weight:0`) never flatten a plot; cardio substitution logging (swap the prescribed sport, credits the sport actually done); single app-wide RPE ⓘ modal (inline RPE keys removed); collapsible journal; no "optional tail" divider.

   **The detail sheet’s scroll contract — `openDetail` is also the re-render (V152).** `openDetail(dayKey,day)` is not only an open. It is the refresh path for **every** in-session mutation: `skipExercise` / `unskipExercise` / `skipSuperset` / `unskipSuperset` (all four via `_afterEdit`), `applySwapChoice`, `undoSwap`, `applyAddChoice`, `selectKBSize` and `acceptSwapNudge`. Its tail therefore runs on eight refresh paths, and the unconditional `scrollTop=0` it used to carry sent the athlete to the top of the card on every tap. Now: `_dFresh = !(sheet already .open && currentDayKey===dayKey)` — captured at the TOP of the function, **before `currentDayKey=dayKey` commits**, or the day test is tautological — and the tail writes `scrollTop = _dFresh ? 0 : _dKeep`. **Both terms are load-bearing.** The sheet-open term is not redundant with the day test: `closeDetail` drops `.open` but leaves `scrollTop` **and** `currentDayKey` untouched, so a day-equality test alone would restore a stale offset when the athlete reopens the same day after backing out. **The decision is deliberately not a parameter** — a flag would put the burden on every future call site and the bug returns the first time one is added without it. **Any new re-render path that routes through `openDetail` inherits the correct behaviour for free; adding a caller requires nothing.** The other five sheets (rest, swap, add, goal, change) keep their unconditional `o.scrollTop=0` correctly — none of them re-render on close, so none of them can move the reader.

## 8b. Exercise swap (V102)
**Prescription carries verbatim, with ONE exception (V119).** Pure RPE makes verbatim honest between loaded movements — `4×5 @ RPE 8` means something on any bar, so there is no re-prescription pass and no stale percentage can follow a swap. It does not survive the jump to bodyweight: `3×10–12 at RPE 7` on a pushup is a guess at the athlete's capacity, and the sheet's own promise ("whatever you pick keeps 3×10–12 at RPE 7") is then false for anyone who can do thirty of them. `_swapDetailFor(name,detail)` converts those and only those, and is called from `applySwapPrefs` (the rebuild + permanent-prefs path), `applySwapChoice` (the live path, which mutates by index and so needs its own call) and `applyAddChoice` (an added movement inherits the day's accessory dose, same defect).
**It reuses three things that already existed rather than growing a fourth dialect:** `_repFloor` decides unloadable, `_BW_KEEP_FIXED` protects the ones that legitimately carry a number anyway (timed holds, plyos, carries, core bracing), `_bwSetsFromDetail` writes the copy the bodyweight tier has shipped since V94. It is the same conversion `bodyweightSweep` runs, reached from the sheet instead of from the config.
**Both loadability systems have to agree.** `_repFloor`'s unloadable clause is a broad regex (`/\bdips\b|pushup/`) and also catches five movements the LOG side hands a weight field to — `Bench dips`, `Weighted dips`, `Glute-ham raise`, `Pushup variation (weighted)`, `Single-leg glute bridge (weighted)`. If the app will show somewhere to write a number, the rep target is a real instruction and stays. Five of 217 harvested names disagree; the list was harvested from the file, not hand-written.
**The toast changes with it.** "Same job, same numbers" is a promise, so when the reps come off it reads "No load to add here, so take the sets to the same effort" instead.

Any lift in the day view carries a **swap control** on its name row (`asyIcon('swap')`, custom path). Deliberately its own button rather than tapping the name: the card already owns per-set stepper rows and a load input, so a name-spanning tap target would fight the log controls. Offered only where `_pattern(name)` is non-null, so holds, carries and core rows have no swap affordance.

**Scope is the session, and only the session.** The program is untouched; next week says what it always said. The scope toggle that an earlier design carried ("just today" / "rest of program") was cut — the permanent path is the nudge below, which is strictly better because it is *earned* rather than a checkbox to mash on day one.

**Candidate legality is harvested, never re-derived.** Every pool in the engine reaches the athlete through `pick()`, so `pick()` registers its input array into `_SWAP_UNIVERSE` (reset per `buildProgram`, emitted as `prog._swapUniverse`). The harvested set is therefore equipment-legal, age-legal and injury-legal **by construction** — it cannot disagree with what the engine would have picked, because it *is* what the engine picked from. Re-deriving legality at swap time with a name regex would have been a second source of truth that drifts the moment a pool moves. `applyOverlays` splices `variant._swapUniverse` into `prog._swapUniverseWeeks[w]` alongside the variant's weeks, so a **travel week offers hotel-legal candidates only** rather than a barbell hinge to someone in a room with two dumbbells.

**Two tiers, and the sort is the guard.**
1. **SAME JOB** — identical `_pattern`. Week keeps its shape.
2. **DIFFERENT JOB, SAME DAY** — same `_PATTERN_REGION`, different pattern, with the trade stated ("Still legs work. You give up the hinge for today.").
3. **Nothing else is offered.** A curl does not replace a main deadlift; that is abandoning the day, not swapping it. (Explicitly cut with Mario after being mocked.)

`capRegionalFatigue` runs at generation, so a post-generation swap bypasses the per-pattern fatigue escalation and the distinct-movement cap. **The tiering is the mitigation** — anything already on today's card is withheld entirely, so the movement that would blow the cap is never offered. That is why this is tiered rather than a flat list plus a warning toast.

**Prescription carries verbatim. There is no re-prescription pass, by design** — because the system is pure RPE, `4×5 @ RPE 8` stays honest on an RDL, a hip thrust or a leg extension; RPE self-corrects for the movement. A percentage-based system would have needed a whole recalculation step here.

**Persistence.** `ia_swaps_<progId>`, keyed `w<N>_<day>` (same shape as `ia_logs_`/`ia_comp_`), re-applied by `applySessionSwaps` at the **tail of `refreshProgram` alongside the rest-day moves** — both are the athlete's own edit and nothing the engine does should undo them. Idempotent by name match: a frozen week already carrying the swap has no name left to match. `pruneSwaps(pid, cut)` drops records below the freeze cut, since the swap is baked into the stored week at that point. **This store exists for one narrow window** — `refreshProgram` rebuilds on every boot and the freeze only protects *touched* weeks, so swapping a lift, walking to the rack and having iOS evict the PWA would otherwise show the old lift while you stand over the new one.

**Safety: the injury plan governs the sheet AND the store.** `_swapInjuryOK` runs each candidate through the real `applyInjuryFilter` (wrapped as a throwaway section, so no second copy of the drop rules), and `applySessionSwaps`/`applySwapPrefs` re-run the filter on any day they touch. The failure case this closes: swap a row for a deadlift, flag a low back two days later, and the swap would otherwise walk a hinge back into a program that just stripped hinges. Covered by a harness check.

**Coach nudge.** `bumpSwapCount` counts *consecutive* swaps of the same lift to the same replacement; any different choice resets the streak. At `SWAP_NUDGE_AT = 3` a popup offers to make it permanent, and is offered **once** — declining sets `offered:true` so the athlete is never asked again. Accepting writes `cfg.exSwapPrefs[from] = to` and rebuilds. **The preference lives in cfg precisely so it needs no re-application pass** — `refreshProgram` rebuilds from cfg on every boot, so it simply regenerates forever, the same way an injury or an equipment tier does. Applied in the `engineD_synthesis` week loop after `capSessionBudget`, with `applyInjuryFilter` re-run on anything it touched.

**Layout bug found on device (V103).** The candidate row originally carried the inherited prescription on the right with `white-space:nowrap` + `flex-shrink:0`. **That was mocked against `4 × 5 @ RPE 8` and the real strings run to 59 characters** (`3 sets — RPE 8 (stop 2 reps short of failure), 2–3 min rest`), so the row forced itself wider than the phone and set the entire sheet scrolling sideways — names clipped off the left edge, tags cut off. **Fixed by removing the prescription from the rows entirely**: it carries over verbatim, so it was identical on all 8 rows and repeating a 59-character string was noise as well as broken. Now stated **once** in the header via `_rxShort()` (`Whatever you pick keeps 4×7 at RPE 6.`), which compacts any real detail string to **15 characters or fewer** (verified against all 37 distinct strings the engine emits). Row is name + the one differing note. Defensive guards added: `overflow-x:hidden` on the overlay/header/body, `box-sizing:border-box` + `max-width:100%` on the card, `overflow-wrap:anywhere` on name/note/title. **Standing lesson: mock with the longest real string the engine can emit, not a representative-looking short one.**

**Design system (folded in — no separate DS-doc update needed).** New `swap` icon in `ASY_ICON_PATHS` (two opposed horizontal arrows): `<line x1="3" y1="8.5" x2="17" y2="8.5"/><polyline points="14 5.5 17 8.5 14 11.5"/><line x1="21" y1="15.5" x2="7" y2="15.5"/><polyline points="10 12.5 7 15.5 10 18.5"/>` — rendered at 16px, inherits `--muted`. The sheet **reuses the existing full-screen `.overlay` slide-up chrome and `.detail-header`/`.dh-back`/`.dh-over`/`.dh-title`; no new sheet pattern was introduced.** New classes: `.ex-swapbtn` (card control, `--surface2` chip, `:active` scale .94), `.ex-swapchip`/`.ex-swapchip-lbl`/`.ex-swapundo` (the SWAPPED IN readout), `.swap-tags`/`.swap-tag` (radius 2, the tightest rung), `.swap-sec-lbl` + `.caution` modifier, `.swap-sec-sub`, `.swap-cand`/`.caution`/`-top`/`-name`/`-rx`/`-note`, `.swap-empty`, `.swap-foot`, and `.swap-nudge-card`/`-hd`/`-body`/`-yes`/`-no` (radius 6, one filled primary). **Signal-orange is drawn three new times** — the SWAPPED IN label, the tier-2 header, and the load-drop note — each marking a live deviation from the prescribed plan. Tier-2 borders are `rgba(207,78,26,0.35)`, never solid signal: choosing a lighter alternative is not an error state.

**Reasoning to preserve:** trading a lift off once is a bad night's sleep; three sessions running is a preference. Session-only means you'd re-swap every week for the rest of the block, which is friction you'd feel by week four — the honest risk being that you stop opening the day at all. The nudge buys the permanent change *without* shipping a persistent-scope control, and defers all rest-of-program overlay plumbing indefinitely.

---
- **Swap candidates for superset members are station-filtered (V164 D5).** `swapCandidates` and `auxSwapCandidates` drop any candidate that is `_ssLegal`-illegal against the surviving partner (`_ssPartnerOf`). Non-superset items are unfiltered; curated pairs that are already cross-station are unfiltered by construction.

## 9. Workflow & versioning
1. Upload current HTML + this handoff → 2. discuss design/doctrine **before** code → 3. implement → 4. `node --check` every `<script>` block → 5. `node` logic-replication to verify numbers against expected output (extract the real functions from the file, don't reimplement) → 6. deliver the verified file. Filenames are **dotless** (`V162`=V16.2). Lineage is linear. Confirm the canonical filename every session (`ls *.html`) — out-of-band relabels/re-syncs by Mario are routine.

- **Verification gate (run all five, in order — `_gate.sh`):** extract inline JS → `node --check` → **window-global lint (which must also flag DUPLICATE top-level declarations — that check is what found the two `computeStreak` bodies in V119, and `node --check` passes them silently because declarations hoist and the last one wins)** → save-path harness → render harness. **The lint is not optional.** **The gate script runs `set -eo pipefail`, not bare `set -e`** — `set -e` does not catch a non-zero exit inside a pipeline, so `node harness.js | tail -2` will happily print ALL GATES PASS over failing harnesses (found V108). Assert the gate actually fails before trusting that it passed. `node --check` passes anything valid as a *Node* script, and Node has no `window`; a top-level `function top(){}` or `function name(){}` collides with a non-configurable window property, throws a SyntaxError at load, and **kills the entire script** — every function in the file, not just the offender. This is a single-file app with ~410 top-level declarations in one global scope, so the exposure is real. Symptom to recognise: one `Identifier 'x' has already been declared` followed by a cascade of unrelated `y is not defined`.
- **Render harness (layout changes).** A real headless Chromium ships in the container at `/home/claude/.npm-global/lib/node_modules/playwright` — `chromium.launch({args:['--no-sandbox']})`. For a header/overlay change, don't boot the app: pull the file's `<style>` block and the target overlay element out verbatim, render at 393×852, drive the same innerHTML writes the real code does, and assert on `getBoundingClientRect`. **Trap: neutralise transitions first** (`*{transition:none!important}`). `.overlay.open` slides in, so a measurement taken mid-transition reports the animation, not the layout — the first V117 baseline run put the button 180px below where it actually sits and produced three phantom failures. Same class as the V115/V116 harness bugs: the metric was wrong, not the code.

## 10. Token efficiency & safe-edit protocol
The HTML is ~470KB (~120–140K tokens for a full read) — **re-reading it wholesale is the biggest token drain.** The safe-edit loop: (1) **`grep -n` the symbol + its consumers first** — maps the full blast radius in a few hundred tokens, more completely than scrolling (grep can't skip a call site). (2) **Read wide at load-bearing seams** (wave resolver, deload/taper, regional caps), **narrow when localized.** (3) Targeted `str_replace`. (4) **Verification scripts are the backstop** (`node --check` + logic-replication + headless-Chromium DOM smoke tests), not the full read. Keep this handoff lean; don't re-read the raw doctrine PDFs mid-session (reference this doc). Fresh chat per development batch.

## 10b. Standing lessons (promoted out of the session digest)
- **A number stamped into a display string is not a field, and every reader will re-parse it differently (V190).** The round count lived only inside `detail`, so three separate consumers each wrote their own regex to get it back and all three defaulted to the same invented constant when a later pass changed the grammar. Nobody owned it, so everybody guessed. Rule: when a value is authored once and consumed by more than one reader, give it a field on the object it describes; a regex over prose is a fork in the source of truth, and the fork is silent because the fallback always returns something.
- **Delete the fallback constant instead of improving it (V190).** The instinct on `: 3` was to replace it with a better default. The correct move was to remove the case that needed a default: when nothing can state the round count, print no round count — the section label is already there and is always true. A default is a claim the code cannot support, and it is indistinguishable from a real answer at the call site.
- **A per-item rewrite over a set whose members carry different grammars will desync the set (V190).** `preventionDoseSweep` matched two effort grammars and skipped the ballistic exemption, halving some members of a superset and not others. The give-away in the measurement was the shape, not the size: 3.80% on the one focus that runs the pass and **exactly 0.00% on the other six**. When a rate is non-zero on one config class and hard zero everywhere else, the pass is the sole source and no further attribution work is needed.
- **The blast-radius diff finds the coaching consequence the gates never thought to ask about (V190).** Four section-count and one item-count hunk traced to `capSessionBudget` totalling `_setCount(it.detail)`: the budget was charged 3 sets for a member the dose had refused to prescribe above 2, and paid for that phantom set by **evicting terminal knee extension on a prevention build**. No gate asked whether the budget agreed with the dose. Classify every hunk, then ask what each class means for the athlete — the invariant it violates becomes the gate (G12: no member is charged above its section's rounds).
- **A fixed-length table cannot join a max-across-sports length without pinning (V186).** `calcProgramLength` is a max; the NSW run engine scaled to whatever won, so nobody noticed. An 8-row NRC table under a 12-week swim base clamps `wIdx` and repeats race week four times. Rule: when a table-driven sport enters, its length is the program's length; the formula sports build inside it. Half/marathon only survived because 14 and 18 always won the max — that was luck, not design.
- **"The time trial is the race" has to be true for every reader, not just the copy (V186).** The session said TIME TRIAL; the taper read `eventTargeted` and put a Power + Strength block on the day. When a ruling says X *is* Y, grep every consumer that tests for Y and make each one agree — the gate assertion is "the sections on the X day equal the sections on the Y day," byte for byte.
- **Keep-longest and keep-last are the same rule with different answers on different weeks (V186/F5).** A selection rule that is right for the volume weeks (drop the 15:00 jog) is wrong for race week (the shortest run is the point). Key the branch off the flag the table already carries; do not invent a second one.
- **Measure the engine's calendar convention before asserting it in an oracle (V186).** `ALL_DAYS_ORDER` is Sunday-first; the long run lives on the last *train* day; the hard-day spacer can move it. A fuzz oracle that assumed "race = last run day in Sun→Sat order" produced 24 false violations and one real discovery (the pre-existing Sunday-race quirk, now a watch item). Write the oracle from the engine's stated convention, then classify every violation before calling any of them a defect.
- **A pool that names a movement is an unenforced claim about that movement's pattern (V185).** `lowback/protect` set `rowPool=['Straight-arm pulldown']` and a `backCompoundPool` with `Lat pulldown` *because they are spine-safe*, then `applyInjuryFilter` dropped both for scoring as `row`. The override and the post-filter were reasoning about the same movement through two different lenses and never compared answers. **Same shape as §11d's hardcoded `3×10 each`:** when one part of the build asserts something about a movement, grep for the other part that has to agree. A comment that describes the opposite of what ships is the tell.
- **Every sabotage tripping every gate is as suspicious as a survivor (V185).** The first sabotage run scored 8/8 with all three gates firing on mutations that touched only one of them. Cause: the runner passed **relative** module paths into `require`, so Node threw MODULE_NOT_FOUND and the non-zero exit read as a trip. `os.path.abspath` on every injected path. A gate that can only ever fail is worth exactly as much as one that can only ever pass.
- **A sabotage that changes no behaviour scores as a survivor and is not a gate defect (V185).** Two of eight mutations were no-ops: reverting `straight-arm` to the row rule left it matching `/pulldown/` first, and moving the D1 rule below the bench rule changed nothing because no D1 name carries a bench-family token. **Run the differential sweep on the survivor before touching the gate** — both showed `0 names changed`. The finding was real but it was about the mutation: the only ordering constraint D1 actually has is *above the bare `/press/` catch-all*, which is what S8 now sabotages.
- **When a classifier and a pool both name movements, X-ray them against each other (V185).** Scoring every `EXLIB` pool against `_pattern` and listing the disagreements found the KB press in three seconds and the pulldowns in the same pass. Deliberate disagreements (lateral raise in the `shoulder` pool is `delt_iso`; incline press in `chest_compound` is `vpress` by V119) are noise you triage, not failures — but the sweep is cheap and it is how both of this session's bugs surfaced.
- **A stub that returns without invoking silently deletes a code path from the gate (V184).** The harness defined `requestAnimationFrame(f){return 0}` — it never called `f`. `iaWheelInit` lands its scroll seeding inside a rAF, so **the entire seeding path had never been exercised by any gate, V182 included**, and sabotage U11 ("seed ignores the middle copy") survived cleanly. Any stub that accepts a callback must call it, or the gate is quietly measuring nothing. Audit the stub list whenever a sabotage survives for no visible reason.
- **After a ruling changes a data shape, every reference must be re-derived — and derived from the spec, not restated (V184).** F3 hard-coded `^(\d+)\.(\d)$` for distance and reported 4,121 violations the moment miles gained a hundredths column. It was the reference that was stale, not the build. Rewritten to read the column count off `_IAW_SPEC`, so the next column move cannot rot it. The tell that it was a reference problem and not a real one: every violation was the same shape (`7.00`, `99.00`), which no genuine defect produces.
- **A gate that indexes the build's own structures directly will crash instead of report (V184).** Sabotage U1 removed a wheel column and the suite died on `_iawRows(undefined)` before printing a summary — scored as dead, indistinguishable from a vacuous gate. Iterate the REFERENCE's shape and null-check the build's, so a missing element is a named failure. The sabotage runner now treats "no summary printed" as not-tripped for exactly this reason.
- **Strip comments before any "is this token gone" scan (V183).** The `.foot-hint` deletion left a tombstone comment naming the class it removed, and the usage assertion matched that comment — reporting the class as still present on a file where it was correctly gone. Same shape as the V166 near-miss (an assertion matching a deletion's own comment rather than code), now in the opposite direction. The gate reader parses a comment-stripped copy for usage questions and the raw source only for structure.
- **An identity fuzz must first prove its own baseline is reproducible (V182).** F1 compared V181 and V182 `buildProgram` output and reported **864 of 864 configs differing** — on a build that touched no engine code. The cause was the reference, not the artifact: `buildProgram` stamps `prog_'+Date.now()` and seeds off `Date.now()%100000` when `cfg.seed` is absent (line 6110), so V181 was not even self-stable across two consecutive calls. **Pin the seed and normalise clock-derived fields, then assert the baseline equals ITSELF before comparing it to anything.** The suite now counts `unstable refs` as violations, so a non-reproducible baseline can never masquerade as a clean identity claim — which is the same failure shape as a vacuous gate, one level up.
- **A guard that the gate never reaches will survive its own sabotage (V182).** S13 stripped the index clamp from `_iawSel` and the suite stayed green, because no assertion had ever driven a wheel past its own row range. The fix is an assertion that reaches the guard (G16 over- and under-scroll), never the removal of the guard. Momentum scrolling and rubber-band on iOS make out-of-range `scrollTop` the normal case, not the edge case.
- **A heredoc breaks an `&&` chain; the line after it runs unconditionally against whatever artifact is on disk (V175).** The UI edit script aborted on a bad anchor, the heredoc that followed ended the `&&` chain, and `node --check` on the next line graded the STALE engine-only extraction and printed PASS. Gate runs are `bash -c 'set -eo pipefail; …'` with `;` separators, and the artifact is deleted before regeneration so a stale one cannot be graded. (`set -o pipefail` is not available in `sh`; the invocation must be `bash -c`.)
- **Two guards that overlap make one of them untrippable; reshape until each guard has a sabotage that trips it (V175).** `dayIsTravel` was `type==='substitute' && patch.equipment && patch.equipment!=='commercial'`: removing the type check tripped nothing because injury patches never carry `equipment`. Reshaped to `type==='substitute' && patch.equipment!=='commercial'` — now each clause is load-bearing and each has a sabotage. Same session, same class: the sweep's clone-on-write was untrippable because section objects are per-build (V137 found only ITEMS shared); removed under the V174 E10 rule rather than shipped with a comment claiming a sharing that does not exist.
- **When the unit of an operation shrinks, every surface that named the old unit is an overclaim — grep the copy for the old unit's noun (V175).** The splice went from week to day; "Takes effect Week 1", "WEEK 1" on the chip and "Maintenance Week" on the pill were all suddenly lies over a one-day trip. Found by reading, not by a gate: the engine assertions would have passed with every one of them still wrong.
- **Prove the bug at the reporter's seed before touching anything (V175, re-affirmed from V104).** Diaz's onboarding dump plus one inferred input (a 5 mi baseline to land on his 6 weeks) reproduced both screenshots byte-for-byte — the exact two orphan rows, the chair Bulgarians on Wednesday. The fix's assertion (A2/B3) is the reproduction inverted.
- **A contract that is asserted is not a contract that is met — verify the transcription, not just the value (V171).** §2 said the NRC tables were Nike-verbatim; V135 taught "check the PDF before calling a Nike value a defect." The converse was never run: nobody checked the PDF before calling the TABLE verbatim. Four title-only speed rows and twelve dropped recovery runs shipped for ~40 versions under a comment that said they could not. When a data table is the doctrine, the audit is a page-by-page diff against the source, and it is a gate, not a one-time chore.
- **Token scanners over prose are latent mis-parses; when copy grammar changes, differential the scanners that read it (V171).** `_cardioInterference` regexed `([\d.]+)\s*(?:mi\b|mile)` and read `0:30 Mile` as thirty miles, so every session written that way carried the maximum leg bump into the hinge clamp. Nobody noticed because the number is invisible. The V171 copy change fixed it by accident; the blast-radius pass caught it only because the interference magnitude was diffed session-by-session across versions. Any function that parses `detail` is in the blast radius of any change to `detail`.
- **A fuzz failure indicts the REFERENCE before the candidate when the failures cluster at extreme inputs (V171).** 131 failures on the first fuzz pass: every one was the fuzz's own invariant — a fixed 13:20 conversion for distance runs (the engine ranks by the athlete's row, and at a 5:00 mile two miles is 14 minutes), value-equality on weeks where Nike legitimately repeats an entry, and a stricter anchor rule than the engine's pre-existing truthiness rule. Same doctrine as the sabotage lesson: fix the instrument, then rerun, and say in the digest that the engine did not move.
- **A gate can pass by clock coincidence (V174).** W4 asserted a pre-start rest day was ineligible and passed on V172-equivalent code because the day was ALSO in the future, and `restDayEligible` already rejects the future. The sabotage sweep caught it (guard off → still green). Re-pinned to a past week. Corollary: the sandbox clock was Sep 1 UTC while the stated date was Aug 31 — three fixtures hardcoded the week and failed as instruments before any code was wrong. Date fixtures are computed relative to `new Date()` in the harness, never typed.
- **A branch the gate cannot trip may be dead by construction, not under-tested (V174).** E10's no-clock `_dateCut:0` looked like belt-and-braces; it is provably ≤ `_maxTouched+1` once `_curWk=1`, so no assertion can observe it. Removed before the sweep rather than shipped as a branch that reads like a feature (§12's `_derived` lesson, applied earlier).
- **A section-level claim about a mutable item set is a LATCH, and no better predicate fixes it (V170).** D8 first made the `Power — …, full recovery` heading CONDITIONAL, computed at push time from `pwRoles`. `applyInjuryFilter` drops items *after* the label is written, so a block built as [Box jumps, Kettlebell swing] kept a claim only the box jump earned. The conditional cut the lies from V169's 3,178 to 122 and could not reach zero — **no push-time computation can**. The fix was deleting the claim, not repairing it: rest is a property of the MOVEMENT (D1) and lives in each item's own prescription string, where a later filter cannot falsify it. Generalises: when a summary and its subject can drift, move the statement onto the subject.
- **Positional diffing is the wrong instrument whenever a change can move section membership (V170).** The first blast-radius pass reported 43.5% identical with ‘selection changed’ hunks whose before/after strings were *byte-identical*. Two stacked faults: an unpinned `Date.now()` seed (`cfg.seed` must be set or every build differs), and then real cap-driven composition shifts once seeded. Key the diff on (week, day, label, movement) and normalise any known constant rename — V170's D8 rename otherwise masqueraded as 4,428 drops plus 4,428 adds and hid every prescription diff underneath.
- **The hostile fuzz found what a 756-config lattice could not, again (V170).** The lattice never generated an injured athlete, so it never built a power block whose composition changes after the label is written. The fuzz did on its 81st config. It also separated a false alarm correctly: a `halfstep` injury cap appends `— hold RPE 7, two in the tank` to any detail lacking an RPE, at **576 occurrences in BOTH V169 and V170** — pre-existing behaviour and an over-strict assertion, not a regression. Run the differential against baseline before calling a fuzz hit a bug.
- **A no-op in the harness is an unasserted behaviour, not an absent one.** V169's routing sabotage survived because `renderProgList` and `renderProgressScreen` are both DOM stubs, so swapping one for the other changed nothing the gate could see. Function declarations bind to the VM global, so they can be reassigned as spies — assert WHICH function ran when the value-level result is identical.
- **Shell double-quotes do not expand `\n`, so multi-line sabotage anchors silently arrive as literal backslash-n.** Three V169 sabotages failed to anchor for this reason and looked like source drift. Keep sabotage anchors to a single line, or pass them through a file.
- **A surviving sabotage indicts the MUTATION before it indicts the gate.** V168's D5 no-write-back sabotage survived and looked like a dead gate. It was not: the mutation called `saveExWeightsFor` BEFORE `foldExAliasKeys` ran, so it wrote back bytes identical to what it had just read, and the byte-comparison gate correctly saw no change. Re-ordering the write after the fold tripped it immediately. Run the differential before concluding a guard is missing — the standing rule now has a named instance.
- **`getExWeightsFor(pid)` already returns a fresh `JSON.parse`, so mutating its result cannot corrupt a store.** The explicit clone in `crossProgLastEntry` is belt-and-braces. The REAL D5 guarantee is the absence of any save call on that path, which a byte-comparison gate cannot see when the fold happens to be a no-op — so it is asserted structurally (G13 greps the function body for writers). Value-level gates cannot protect an absence; assert absences structurally.
- **A gate that crashes reports nothing, and "nothing" is not "no failures".** The V167 sabotage harness classified a build as SURVIVED whenever the `PASS n FAIL n` line was absent — which is exactly what happens when the gate throws. Five of eleven mutations were scored as uncaught guards when in fact the gate had died on each. **Any harness that judges a run by scraping its output must treat missing output as a failure of the run, never as a clean result**, and gates must be written so a defect surfaces as a FAIL line rather than an uncaught exception. This is the vacuous-assertion lesson one level up: the thing that validates the gates was itself unvalidated. When a sweep is fixed, re-run every prior sweep that used the same harness before trusting its historical result.
- **A sabotage that survives is not always a missing assertion — sometimes it is a fixture that cannot tell the difference.** Two V167 holes were fixture defects, not coverage gaps: the heaviest-set test used entries where max-weight and max-e1RM happened to pick the same row, and the slot test used a Main section with a single item so the `ii===0` rule could not be falsified. Before adding an assertion, check whether the existing one is simply blind to the mutation.
- **A ruling that SELECTS is not a ruling that DELETES** (see V165 mileage-chart regression below).
- **Removing a shipped feature needs its own ruling; a ruling that SELECTS is not a ruling that DELETES.** V165's D7 was Mario picking favourites from a list of proposed NEW modules. The build read that list as the complete screen and silently dropped the V164 Weekly Running Mileage chart — a working feature, gone, with 87 gate assertions green because no assertion knew it should exist. The gate cannot catch a deletion it was never told about; only the **blast-radius diff** can, and only if every removed line is classified rather than skimmed. When a diff shows a removal, the question is not "did I mean to write this?" but "was this removal ruled?"
- **A number the engine anchors to is not a number the athlete gave you.** `DEFAULT_1RM` is an experience-keyed estimate that exists so the wave has something to compute percentages from. It is emphatically NOT athlete input, and using it as a *comparison baseline* fabricates achievement: a strong beginner scored against the 65 lb default is shown progress they never made. `progSelData().estimated` already encodes this distinction — any new feature that benchmarks the athlete against "their starting number" must honour it, and must say in the UI which kind of baseline it used. Generalises beyond lifts: before comparing against any stored value, ask whether a human typed it or the engine defaulted it.
- **Two stores that sound comparable often are not.** `cfg.bench` is a 1RM; `ia_exw_` holds working weights. Subtracting one from the other is dimensionally wrong and reports a loss for every athlete alive. Normalise through a shared function (`_epley()`) before any cross-store arithmetic, and name the unit in the variable if there is any doubt.
- **A reader that crosses program boundaries inherits a hostile-input contract the engine's own writers never test.** The engine only ever writes string `type` fields, so `(c.type||'').toLowerCase()` was safe for four versions — until V156's seed read OTHER programs' stores and the fuzz put a number there. `String()`-coerce at every cross-store seam; `_paceStrToSec` already did, the predicate didn't.

- **A cutback that the ramp resumes FROM is a decay, not a cutback.** V136's swim fix generalises the latch lesson to progressions: any stepped ramp with recovery weeks must resume from the running peak, not from the recovery value, or every cycle tops out lower than the last. The bike engine had it right; swim silently didn't, and the sawtooth *looked* like periodization on any single 4-week window. When auditing a progression, assert on successive BUILD-week values with cutbacks excluded — a fixed-window "cycle max" check can launder a lone trailing cutback week into a false decay signal (this exact false positive fired in the V136 fuzz gate before the invariant was corrected).

- **NRC tables are Nike verbatim — verify against the source PDFs before calling any NRC value a defect.** The V134 audit flagged an 80-minute recovery run inside marathon taper week 2 as a "taper inversion." The source page ("03 WEEKS TO GO") prescribes exactly that run — Nike's marathon taper only bites in the final two weeks, and their long-run share of weekly volume is likewise their design. Same class as the V115 lesson (NRC speed sessions legitimately exceed 8 reps). **No harness may assert taper monotonicity, volume ratios, or rep ceilings on NRC-transcribed sessions; the transcription is the contract.**

- **A flat string with silent consumers gets generated FROM the new structure, never maintained beside it (V141).** When a richer shape (the swim `segments` array) is added next to a flat field other systems parse (`detail`), the flat field must be the mechanical flatten of the structure, produced in the same pass. Two independently-written representations of one prescription WILL drift; a derived one cannot. The gate invariant is `flatten(segments) === detail` on every config.

- **A break-the-build that fails to fail proves nothing until the sabotage provably reaches the assertion (V141).** Removing `!recoveryMode` from the V141 segmentation gate produced ZERO gate failures — not because the gate was blind but because recovery mode forces the flat branch upstream, absorbing the sabotage in a second defense. A zero-fail break must be explained and REPLACED with one that demonstrably reaches the target assertion (leaking `swim_base` into the goal list lit A2 with 780 fails). Defense in depth is good; mistaking it for gate coverage is the dishonest-green class.

- **Verify a harness's call convention against real call sites before judging the engine (V141).** Two Q7 "defects" (bike never tapers; `taperWeeksFor` returns 0) were the harness passing a goalId where the signature takes an event flag, and omitting the caller-injected taper argument. The Q7 verdict flipped from two findings to zero once the harness matched the file's own call sites. Same family as "trust the file" — the harness must trust it too.

- **A stub's simplifying assumption can be the exact thing that hides the defect (V146/V147).** The week-strip suite passed 10/10 on animation code that was visibly broken on device, for two reasons: a JS stub cannot observe CSS timing at all (the real defect was a `transition:all 0.15s` outrunning the highlight), and the stub used uniform 62px chips where the real chips vary — which made a desynced counter look correct. **Model the measured widths, and when a gate cannot see a whole dimension of the change, say so in the gate rather than reporting a clean number.**

- **A gate that reports a suspiciously clean number must be proven to have run (V147).** The blast-radius check FALSE-PASSED because it diffed against a file Mario had already replaced in the project: `diff` exited 2, stdout was empty, and empty read as "0 hunks, clean". `set -eo pipefail` did not help because the failure was inside a Python `subprocess.run` whose returncode was never checked. **Fail closed on rc==2 (error), on rc==0 (byte-identical candidate — nothing was built), and on changes-reported-but-no-hunks.** Verify both files exist before diffing.

- **A vacuous assertion passes forever and proves nothing (V147).** A test measured the frame where the animation clock initialises, where displacement is always exactly zero regardless of the easing curve — so it had been passing for every curve including the one it existed to reject. **Sabotage is what finds these:** if a break that should light an assertion doesn't, the assertion is the suspect, not the sabotage.

- **A dose assertion must recompute from an INDEPENDENT reference implementation, never the candidate's own function (V144).** A broken `_powerDose` validating itself passes every time. Where post-passes scale doses without metadata (deload weeks), the contract is: never above reference, and max-across-program equals reference exactly. The same rule produced V151's alias gate, which checks the candidate's `exStoreKey` against a separately written slug.

- **`set -eo pipefail` is a hard invariant for every gate run (V145, restating V108).** A pipe swallowed node's exit code and the run falsely reported 0. Note that `pipefail` protects the shell, not a subprocess: a Python `subprocess.run` whose returncode is never checked is outside its reach (V147).

- **Harvest the universe from the file; never hand-list it (V151, restating V103).** The V151 scrub ran the real `buildProgram` across 4,536 configs and pulled names from the engine grids, the swap universe, `RAND_POOLS` and `EXLIB` — which is how it found that two spellings of one press lived in the SAME pool. A hand-written list of "the exercises we have" would have described the library someone remembered, not the one that runs. The `pick()` harvest doubles as an X-ray on pool legality.

- **A filter that names some of its siblings has to name all of them (V151).** `push_light`'s press pool excluded `chestMain` and `chestAcc[0]` but not `chestAcc[1]`, the day's own Pump item — and its hard-coded fallback walked straight back into the item the filter had just removed. **A fallback must obey the same guard as the pool it backs up, and the chain must end somewhere that cannot be empty.**

- **A re-render must not move the reader — and an open function that doubles as a refresh function will carry first-open behaviour onto every refresh (V152).** `openDetail`’s `scrollTop=0` was correct for the one caller it was written for and wrong for the eight that were added later. **The tell is a function whose name is a verb of arrival (`open`, `show`, `start`) being called from paths that are not arrivals.** When that happens, the fix belongs **inside** the function, derived from state the function can observe for itself — not in a parameter, because a parameter is a rule every future call site has to remember and the defect returns the first time one forgets. Sister case: the V148 `dose` field, where the form keys on data rather than the caller getting it right.

- **When a guard has two terms, sabotage each one separately or you have not tested the guard (V152).** The obvious version of the V152 fix — compare `currentDayKey` to `dayKey` — passes the reported repro and silently breaks reopening a day after Back, because `closeDetail` leaves both `scrollTop` and `currentDayKey` alive. Deleting the sheet-open term (S2) and moving the capture below the assignment (S3) trip **different** asserts, which is the proof that neither term is decoration. A single-sabotage gate on a compound predicate reports PASS on half a fix.

Generic engineering doctrine earned on specific builds. These outlive the versions that produced them, which is why they live here and not in the digest.

1. When a report names one broken item, audit the whole set through the real code before fixing — the answer to "what else" is the deliverable, and 'sounds bodyweight' is not a classification, pool membership is.
2. When a control destroys more than the thing it is attached to, it is in the wrong place.
3. A green light that lies is worse than a red one — test the gate itself, not just the code.
4. An indicator whose default state is "on" for the common case is not an indicator, it is decoration.
5. A naming convention that only the author knows is not a convention — the athlete reads the name and picks up an implement.
6. When a display bug and a data-loss bug have the same symptom, the missing dimension is upstream of both — and check for a third writer that reads on the same key, because a fix that only touches the reader and the writer will be undone by it.
7. A gate that selects one case will break when the rule gains an exception; enumerate the axis instead.
8. When a measured candidate count and an actual diff disagree, the diff is describing scope you did not mean to have — reconcile the number before shipping, never after.
9. When a guard is gated on a condition another pass actively works to prevent, the guard is dead. Assert the positive branch fires, not just that the negative one does.
10. When a fix is "make the legacy thing behave like the modern thing", check whether the legacy thing should exist at all.
11. When a movement resists classification, check how many jobs it is doing. A name that cannot be classified is usually being asked to be more than one thing, and the fix is upstream of the classifier.
12. When a derived value is clamped, assert that the derivation still discriminates. A clamp that binds in every case turns a formula into a constant, and every other assertion will still pass.

**A guard's justification can expire without the guard changing (V133).** The week freeze's mid-week hard boundary was correct when written: the engine plans a week as a unit, so don't splice. What made it wrong was not the reasoning but a fact underneath it — `prog.weeks` is never persisted, so the "other plan" it was protecting the week's coherence against stopped being a sibling of the current plan and became an artifact eighteen versions stale. **When a guard cites a tradeoff, re-check that both sides of the tradeoff still exist.**

**When two days look identical, ask what the content is keyed on (V133).** Exercise selection keys on `seed + week + role`. Weekday is not an input. So "today is a copy of yesterday" is never a duplication bug — it is two sources disagreeing about which role sits on which day. The axis that DOES vary by weekday (cardio) is the diagnostic.
- **A sabotage that does not apply is indistinguishable from a gate that does not fire (V177).** Three mutations in the first sweep reported SURVIVED; one of them had simply failed to match its anchor and never modified the build. The runner now asserts `count(anchor)==1` before mutating and reports NOT-APPLIED as its own outcome, distinct from SURVIVED — the same class as §10b's "zero failures is not the same as gate passed."
- **Rename passes are order-dependent, and an alias table is the thing they eat (V177).** The edit that flipped `EX_RENAMED_V113` to point old spellings AT the new names ran first; the blanket literal rename ran second and rewrote those same old spellings on the LEFT of the map, silently producing `'Suitcase carry':'Suitcase carry'` self-maps and orphaning every historical load. Caught by a gate that resolved twenty legacy spellings through `exStoreKey` rather than by reading the table. **Rule: when a rename touches a map whose KEYS are the old names, rewrite that map LAST, or exclude it from the sweep.** Corollary assertions worth keeping: no alias row may be a self-map, and no alias row's value may itself be an alias key (a chain resolves once, so a two-hop row silently loses).
- **The source can be wrong in its own voice — work around it with its own numbers (V176/D31).** Nike's chart prints a half pace slower than marathon on its slowest row (their 6:00:00 marathon floor). Transcription doctrine survives contact: don't edit the chart, don't fabricate a repaired middle number wearing the source's label — restructure the prescription (drop the rung) so every number printed is still the source's. Design the workaround to self-expire when the source is fixed (`detailIfHalfSlow` fires only where `half >= marathon`).
- **A differential harness finds the classes the ruling never named (V176/D8).** The ruling argued from NRC long runs; the day-aligned V175→V176 interference diff surfaced 624 moves including NSW `Long Slow Distance (LSD)` sessions nobody had mentioned — same misfiling, different vocabulary, correctly fixed by the same mechanism. Enumerate every diff, classify each into a ruled class, fail on unclassified: spot checks would have shipped this blind, and a surprise class that was WRONG would have shipped too.
- **Rotating a pool exposes every member that was never reachable, and each one is an untested path (V178).** The hot-tomorrow core branch took `slice(0,2)` for years; positions 3+ of two pillars had never shipped. Switching to rotation put `Landmine rotations` in front of a bodyweight athlete within one fuzz run, because a name that never renders never needed a `_BW_SUBS` entry. When a selection rule widens, fuzz the newly reachable members on every equipment tier before calling the change small. Corollary from the same build: a pool that is picked but never consumed (`EXLIB.core`) is where additions go to disappear — the gate's reachability check ("the new name appears in at least one of N seeded programs") is what caught it.
- **Every post-build renamer is a swap surface.** Any pass that rewrites an item name after formation (dedupe sweeps, overlays, substitutions) must satisfy every pairing invariant the draw-time filter enforces, or it silently reconstructs the exact defect the filter removed. The V164 adjacency dedupe rebuilt a banned cross-station pair from a legal one; the gate lattice missed it because its rest-day dimension was too narrow — hostile fuzz dimensions (rest patterns, injuries) exist to find the paths the lattice doesn't walk.

## 11. Resolved bugs — don't reintroduce (curated)
**Superset of one (V175):** any pass that removes a member of a `superset:true` section — `bodyweightSweep`'s within-day dedupe, `applyInjuryFilter` drops, `capSessionBudget` — must leave the FLAG to `singletonSupersetSweep`, which runs LAST in `buildProgram` on every tier and demotes a one-item superset to straight sets (flag and `type` cleared, ` superset A/B` scrubbed from the label). The renderer additionally refuses to draw a rounds header over one row (`(sec.items||[]).length>1` on the superset branch) because `ia_hist_` snapshots frozen before V175 still carry the flag. Do not "fix" by refilling (the pool collapsing is why it is a singleton) or by pairing orphans with each other (a pairing the engine never chose). **Whole-week overlay splice (V175):** `applyOverlays` must never assign `rebuilt.weeks[w] = variant.weeks[w]`; the unit is the day, judged on `_progDayDate` against `[from, to]` inclusive.
Log-input wipe on save (inline log now updates only its own row in place); **`Incline pushups (hands on bed)` classified as `vpress` (V119):** "incline" means opposite things on a bench and on the floor — an incline BENCH press angles the torso back and loads the shoulder, an incline PUSHUP raises the HANDS and is the easiest horizontal press there is, the regression you give someone who cannot yet press their own bodyweight off the floor. One regex served both. Consequence was not volume bookkeeping: `elbow/workaround` caps `hpress, tri_iso, bi_iso, row` and **not** `vpress`, so the movement walked straight through the cap written for it and was prescribed at **RPE 8, "stop 2 reps short of failure"** to an athlete training around an elbow. Measured over 1,890 configs: **84 lines / 21 configs, 0 healthy configs, all 21 on the bodyweight tier** — 17 `elbow/workaround`, 4 `shoulder/workaround`. Fixed with an explicit rule ahead of the generic one, same shape as the `handstand|wall walk` rule above it. **Standing lesson: when one regex serves two movement families, check that the shared token means the same thing in both** — the V111 `_pattern` misroutes were the same class, and so was `/lateral/`. tab-bar init-timing null (`DOMContentLoaded` defer); iOS sub-16px input auto-zoom (all inputs ≥16px); leg-recovery same-day blind spot (now 48h + deadlift covered, not just squat); the V17.1 lift-week-index desync (weeks 7–12 replayed 1–6 + deload/badge mismatch — `buildSections` gets the TRUE week); the 5K-no-race phantom taper (taper is single-source now); `push_light`==`push` clone; the two Full-Body days rendering identical; regional-cap tagging misfires (back-extension→push, Nordic→pull); `_cardioInterference` "tempo" negation (match `tempo pace`, not bare `tempo`); **greedy `/lateral/` in `_pattern`** (matched "Lateral bounds" AND "un**lateral**" → counted plyos and unilateral work as shoulder volume; match `lateral raise`); handstand/wall-walk reading as horizontal press (explicit `vpress` rule before the generic pushup rule); harness seed must be pinned when diffing cap-on/off (else different programs); **base-cardio "max gap" day placement treated the week as a LIST, not a CYCLE** (`chosen=[all[0],all[all.length-1]]` scored Sun+Sat as maximum separation when they are ADJACENT — a Sun/Tue/Thu/Fri/Sat athlete got back-to-back weekend runs; now scores every pair by circular distance, tie-broken on raw distance so Mon-Fri still resolves to Mon+Fri; verified across all 119 possible splits); **dense-hypertrophy secondary compound bypassed the equipment gate** (hardcoded barbell list with no `_bw()` wrapper handed a bodyweight athlete "Close-grip bench press" — invisible to every gear regex because the name never says "barbell"; pool now gated like its neighbours, `_BW_GEAR` widened to catch movement-named lifts, and `bodyweightSweep` now rewrites **section labels** too since headings embed the exercise name). **Collapsed set-bar latch (V112):** `refreshSetSummary` wrote the headline only in its varied branch — a conditional write with no else. Reported from device as "why is the numbering different than the reps I inputted". Same session closed the sub-label contradicting the headline it sits under, and delta badges rendering only on rows touched since the last render. **Standing lesson: a conditional write with no else branch is a latch — the boring branch is what releases it.** RPE key rendering has been reworked twice — now a **single app-wide modal** (V88); do not re-add inline per-day keys. **Icon rendering (V90):** emoji only convert when passed through `asyIcon()` — hardcoded literals in template/`textContent` strings do NOT; if a new emoji appears raw, route it through `asyIcon()` (and use `innerHTML`, not `textContent`, for the host node). **Inline-log detection (V101):** loaded movements that also read as bodyweight (barbell hip thrust, DB Bulgarian split squat, weighted dips/chins) got **no log field** — `isTrackableWeight` unconditionally vetoed anything `isRepExercise` matched, and the rep path only fired on open-ended prescriptions, so a normal `4×7` fell through to nothing; fixed with an explicit load-token override that wins when the name carries equipment. `isKBExercise` also couldn't see the `(KB)` parenthetical (only `isDBExercise` had `(db`); both now read the shared `EQUIP_TOKENS` map so every abbreviation form is covered once. `thruster` and a few movement keywords were simply absent from the keyword lists. 30 movements were audited clean afterward via a harness over the real extracted detectors. **Week freeze restored a prescription that was never shown (V104):** the freeze's "trained → history stands" branch read `prog.weeks`, which is the creation-day build — `refreshProgram`'s output is never persisted — so marking a day during an injury or travel overlay reverted it to the pre-overlay version on next boot. **The standing lesson: `prog.weeks` is a BUILD, not a RECORD.** Any future feature that needs to know what was actually prescribed on a given day reads `ia_hist_`. Found from user-reported screenshots six hours apart; reproduced with a control harness against the unpatched build before the fix was written, which is the pattern worth repeating — prove the bug first, then prove the fix. **Wave reps applied to non-barbell mains (V110):** `4×3` goblet squats and `4×3` kettlebell swings — 903 sub-5-rep prescriptions across the non-barbell main-slot universe; the wave prescribed the **slot** while the pool filled it, and the two never spoke. Fixed at the movement, not at the injury table, because the same seam also fired on Mini gym with no injury present. **Injury pool overrides collapsed onto themselves (V111):** every one of the 12 branches. `knee`/`ankle` protect pointed the squat slot, the lunge slot AND the hip-extension slot at hip extension — a legs day could print `Barbell hip thrust` twice under its own name; `lowback` handed the *pull day's main* to a hip thrust, so a deadlift removed for **axial loading** took the week's pulling with it (the pull main is now a hanging vertical pull, which is spine-decompressive, except on no-bar tiers where rows are dropped and hip extension remains the only honest option); `hip`/protect's lunge slot was two quad isometrics that both also live in `EXLIB.knee_stability`, printing `Wall sit` twice. **`_pattern` misroutes found by the same harness:** `Leg press` matched the bare `/press/` catch-all and scored as **hpress** — a leg day whose MAIN lift counted against `REGION_CEILING.push` and was invisible to `REGION_SET_CAP.legs`, reachable via `squat_joint` (older hypertrophy) with no injury at all; `Lying`/`Seated leg curl` matched `/curl/` and scored as **biceps**; `McGill curl-up` likewise; `Eccentric wrist extension` read as triceps and `Scapular pushup` as a press, so the elbow and shoulder plans **dropped the rehab drills built for them**. Lower-body machines and wrist/scapular rehab now resolve before the generic catch-alls. **Shoulder tiers were inverted:** `protect` dropped the whole `delt_iso` pattern, taking face pulls and rear delt work — the scapular-retraction drills the *more* injured athlete needs most — while `workaround` kept them; and `workaround` dropped front raises while keeping **lateral raises**, which is the impingement arc. `protect` now drops the provocative members by name (plus `ab wheel`/`hanging`, which `P.drop` can never reach because `_pattern` returns `null` for core) and keeps the retraction work.

## 11b. COPY RULE (standing, Mario)
**No mid-sentence hyphens or em-dashes in anything the user reads** — menus, prompts, buttons, sheet copy, pills, toasts. They read as machine-written and break the voice. Use short declarative sentences instead: "Lifting holds steady while you're away. Your running keeps building. That only needs shoes." Spec separators inside a prescription line (`4×5 — RPE 8`) and numeric ranges (`Weeks 5–6`) are structural, not prose, and are exempt. **The app should sound like a coach at every touchpoint.** A sweep of pre-existing user-facing sentences is still outstanding.

## 11c. Triage — first moves when Mario reports something looks wrong

Written so a fresh session can isolate a report without re-deriving the last three builds. **Always confirm the running version first** (`grep ia-version`, and have Mario check the in-app banner) — a stale cached `index.html` on GitHub Pages presents as "the fix didn't work".

**Pinned baselines. These are the numbers a regression is measured against.** All from the 378-config healthy sweep: 6 equipment × 7 focus × 3 experience × 3 rest patterns, seed `i*7919`. `gate121.js` prints all three hashes on every run.
- **V125 (current) and V124 share every lifting hash — STRUCTURE `0d1566bf78737bfe`, BODYWEIGHT `956a1ae43bbb7079`, FULL `073acb47a8fc1b35`, 68,420 items.** V125 is cardio-only; the lifting dumps are byte-identical. When a change is supposed to be confined to one engine, assert the OTHER engine's dump is unchanged — that is the cheapest proof of confinement there is.
- **V124: STRUCTURE `0d1566bf78737bfe`, BODYWEIGHT `956a1ae43bbb7079`, FULL `073acb47a8fc1b35`, 68,420 items.**
- **V123: STRUCTURE `5ea424010756c08a`, BODYWEIGHT `956a1ae43bbb7079`, FULL `e89d3eb16e7770e0`, 68,420 items.** Bodyweight hash unchanged V123→V124, as expected — the trim only reached bundled Main items on geared tiers.
