- **V122: STRUCTURE `637426f2f0080710`, BODYWEIGHT `4df0627b0d3ba7c3`, FULL `7f1ed6a0d09b89a6`, 68,420 items.** V120/V121: STRUCTURE `cc7257e0374c1bab`, 67,790 items.
- **V122 → V123 moved the structure hash but NOT the item count** (68,420 both sides). The entire delta is **2,268 items relabelled from `Leg superset A` to a new `Knee stability` section** — the non-runner split, and nothing else. A structure-hash move with a flat item count and a single label pair changing is the signature of a pure re-sectioning.
- **V121 → V122 moved it with +630 items** — pool-membership change, seed reshuffle. See that digest entry.
- **Per-pass bypass measurements (V123, commercial/strength/no-cardio, 12 weeks):** base **224**, `__REGIONAL_OFF` **228**, `__BUDGET_OFF` **229**, `__DELOAD_OFF` **224**, `__CAP_OFF` (all three) **264**. The regional cap trims **4 items** here — the footprint is narrow, as §12 always said.
- **Gate scores, each run against the PREVIOUS version as control:** V123 **11/11** (V122: 2/11) · V122 **17/17** (V121: 8/17) · V121 **18/18** (V120: 13/18) · V120 **24/24** (V119: 13/24) · V119 **35/35** (V118: 9/13) · V118 render **45/45** (V117: 15/23). **V123 also passes the V121 gate 18/18 and the V122 gate 17/17.**
- **0 unloggable open-ended prescriptions** across all six tiers plus travel.

**Symptom → first place to look.**
- *"This exercise has no place to log it / it isn't counting toward X/Y LOGGED."* → `parseRx(detail,name)` returned null and `exLoggable` fell through. Get the exact detail string off the card, run it through `parseRx`. If it starts `N sets` the V120 rules should catch it; anything else is a new grammar shape and belongs with the V108 loose-grammar family (§8).
- *"The reps disappeared off a movement."* → `_swapDetailFor` (V119, §8b). It fires on swap, add, and rebuild. Check the three inputs in order: `_repFloor(name)` must be `[0,0]`, `_BW_KEEP_FIXED.test(name)` must be false, `isTrackableWeight(name,detail)` must be false. If the movement has a stack or a belt, the likely culprit is `isTrackableWeight` returning false — that is the known **B2b** gap (§12) and `Face pull`, `Rope tricep extension`, `Leg extension` are the known members.
- *"The reps did NOT disappear and should have."* → same three inputs. `_repFloor` returning `null` or a `[6,10]`-style window instead of `[0,0]` is the usual cause; that is exactly why **B2c's predicate is `isTrackableWeight`, not `_repFloor`** (§12).
- *"An injured-athlete day is prescribed too hard / too easy."* → check `_pattern(name)` first. The injury cap sets are pattern-keyed, so one misroute silently exempts a movement from the cap written for it. `elbow/workaround` caps `hpress, tri_iso, bi_iso, row` and NOT `vpress` — that asymmetry is what the V119 incline bug rode through. **When one regex serves two movement families, check the shared token means the same thing in both.**
- *"A bodyweight movement is telling me to go to failure when it shouldn't"* / *"activation work has an RPE on it."* → `unloadableRxSweep` reached a block it should not. It skips `sec.core||sec.hip` and the bodyweight tier; anything else with a rep target and no weight field gets converted. If a NEW block type needs protecting, add it to that guard, not to `_BW_KEEP_FIXED` (which is name-keyed and shared with `bodyweightSweep`).
- *"A cable or machine movement has no weight field."* → `_STACK_LOADED` (§12). Add the name there, not a broad token — the four-name list is deliberate. Then re-run `gate121.js` and confirm the 259-name classification diff shows only your addition.
- *"A prescription changed on a program I'm already running."* → expected, not a bug. `refreshProgram` rebuilds from `cfg`+`seed` on every boot; **since V133 only days with a real record (snapshot / completion / log) are protected** — see §11e. Engine pinning is still deferred (§12).
- *"Today looks exactly like yesterday."* → **two builds disagreeing about roles while agreeing about content.** A day's exercises are keyed on `seed + week + role`, not on weekday, so the same role landing twice serves the same session twice. Check whether one of the two days is coming from `prog.weeks` rather than the live build. **The tell is the cardio** — cardio IS day-indexed, so it will differ while the lifting is identical.
- *"The header/day-card layout is off."* → render harness (§9), and neutralise transitions before measuring or you measure the animation.

**Reproducing any of it.** Extract with `re.search(r'<script>(.*)</script>', h, re.S)` → `/tmp/_ia.js`, `node --check`, then `vm.runInThisContext` with the DOM stubs (§9/§10). Harnesses live alongside the build: `gate119.js`, `gate120.js`, `sweep_incline_ab.js`. **Run any gate against the PREVIOUS version first** — a gate that passes on both versions is not testing what it claims.

## 11d. Two slots, one pool — the row partition (V132)

`getEx` drew **both** pull-day row slots with a single `_slot(rowPool,2,…)`, and `buildSections` hardcoded their prescriptions: superset A at `3×8`, superset B at `3×10 **each**`. Those two details describe two different jobs — a heavy bilateral row and a unilateral volume row — and **nothing ever held the pool to either one.** `pick()` guarantees the two NAMES differ. It has no idea two of the four entries are the same lift.

Two defects fell out of that, both measured on V131 across 3,780 configs:
- **Both slots on the same movement, 42.2% of pull days with a slot-B row.** `Dumbbell row` + `Kettlebell single-arm row` is one lift with two handles; so is `Barbell row` + `Pendlay row`. Reported from device by Mario off a week-6 screenshot.
- **`3×10 each` printed on a bilateral row, 23.0%.** `parseRx` reads `each` as `perSide` and `rxSuffix` renders it, so the card said **"Pendlay row — 3 × 10 /side"**. A Pendlay row has no side.

**Fix is the partition, not a dedupe.** `_isUnilateralRow` (next to `_pattern`) splits `rowPool`; slot A draws the bilateral half at `bs+3`, slot B the unilateral half at `bs+16`. Fallback ordering is the load-bearing part and is ordered so no tier gets **worse**: an empty half falls back to the whole pool, slot B always excludes slot A's name, and if nothing legal remains slot B drops its row rather than repeat slot A. The swap universe is unaffected — the halves union back to `rowPool`, and any fallback registers the whole pool anyway (asserted: 0 narrowed, 0 widened across 270 configs).

**`Dumbbell row` and `Kettlebell row` are unilateral by the library's own convention (V113): the bare name is the standard variant, and the standard variant of both is the bench-supported single-arm row.** `_REP_FLOOR` already read them that way — its `/\brow\b/` entry is commented "DB / KB single-arm row — position-limited". V132 writes that call down once instead of leaving it implied in a comment.

**The residual was one injury branch, and it got closed at the cause.** After the partition, every remaining same-movement pairing traced to **`lowback/workaround`**, whose override sets `rowPool = ['Dumbbell row','Kettlebell single-arm row']` — both unilateral, so slot A fell back and the day ran one lift twice on an athlete training around a low back. Its own comment says what it meant: *"rows go single arm and supported."* One row. So when an injury plan rewrote the pool and left nothing bilateral, slot B yields. **Scoped to a pool the plan actually rewrote (V111's lesson), which is why a healthy bodyweight tier is untouched** — its two inverted-row entries are difficulty RUNGS of one movement, and a ladder is a legitimate second slot on the thinnest pulling tier in the app.

**STANDING LESSON: when a slot hardcodes a detail, that detail is an unenforced contract with the pool.** `3×10 each` was a promise the pool was never asked to keep, and it had been shipping wrong on a quarter of pull days. Grep the build for other hardcoded details that assert something about the movement — `each`, `/side`, `each arm`, `each side` — and check the pool behind each one.

## 11e. The freeze is per-day, not per-week (V133)

**Reported from device, six hours apart, on Mario's own week 6.** Tuesday read `Push` + `Long Ride (LSD) 72 min` at 20:03. He logged one exercise inside week 6. On the next boot the same day read `Pull` + `Long Slow Distance (LSD) 38 min`. He attributed it to the V132 push; V132 was innocent.

**Cause.** `_cut = max(_dateCut, _maxTouched+1)`, and `_dateCut = _curWk + (_curTouched ? 1 : 0)`. Week 6 untouched → cut 6 → week 6 rebuilt live. One log → cut 7 → week 6 falls below the cut → the freeze restored `prog.weeks[6]` **whole**. And `prog.weeks` is the creation-day build (§4). His program predated **V114**, the release where the bike stopped collapsing `lsd_long` into `lsd_easy`; a plain 38-minute LSD with the generic Zone 2 note on the day that should own the long ride is that bug's signature. **One log key rolled an untrained day back eighteen versions.**

**The consequence was not cosmetic.** A pull day's exercises are keyed on `seed + week + role` — **the weekday is not an input**. So a stale build landing `pull` on Tuesday while the live build lands `pull` on Monday serves the *identical session twice*: he got `Sumo deadlift 4×3 @ RPE 9` on back-to-back days, the exact collision `deconflictSameRegion` exists to prevent, produced by bypassing it. The cardio was the only thing that differed, because cardio **is** day-indexed — that mismatch is the fingerprint of two builds disagreeing about roles while agreeing about content.

**The rule now.** Inside the freeze window, each day resolves in three steps:
1. `ia_hist_` snapshot exists → return it. Always wins, every week, byte-identical.
2. Touched (`ia_comp_`/`ia_logs_`) with no snapshot → stored day, **except** on an injury-covered week, where it takes the live injury-applied day (the V100 carve-out, §6).
3. No record of any kind → **live rebuild**, provided the week is at or after the current week.

**Two boundaries, both deliberate.** (a) Only weeks `>= _curWk` pierce; a fully past week is a dead record and rewriting its skipped days buys nothing, so past weeks also keep the stored key set. (b) **DAY-KEY UNION on pierced weeks only.** The merged week used to be built from `Object.keys(prog.weeks[w])` alone, so a day present in the rebuild but absent from the creation-day store — which is exactly what a train-day change produces — was **dropped out of the frozen week entirely.** Union fixes it forward without letting a schedule change retroactively add days to a dead week.

**⚠️ THE HYBRID RISK IS REAL, MEASURED, AND NOT REPAIRED (Mario's call: measure, don't patch).** A frozen trained day from an old plan sitting beside live-rebuilt days can produce same-region adjacency. Measured over **630 configs / 17,460 simulated hybrid weeks**, counting adjacent training-day pairs both in `{push,push_light}` or both in `{legs,pull}` (circular, Sat↔Sun):

| drift model | live build | full freeze (V132) | hybrid (V133) | worse than live |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| single adjacent role swap (what Mario's program had) | 0.352/wk | 0.333/wk | **0.852/wk** | 43.0% |
| every role rotated (worst case) | 0.352/wk | 0.490/wk | **1.022/wk** | 52.8% |

**So piercing roughly 2.5× the adjacency rate versus full freeze. That is the honest cost of the fix and it is accepted, for two reasons:** the frozen half of the collision is a day already trained (immutable under either behaviour, so the only live decision is today's), and the V132 behaviour produced a *guaranteed* duplicate session, which is strictly worse than an occasional region adjacency. **If the rate ever needs repairing, it is its own build — do not smuggle a spacing pass into a freeze fix.**

**Blast radius.** 150 drifted + 150 no-drift programs, per-day md5 over every day of every week:
- **No-drift programs (created on the current engine): 0 / 14,336 days changed. Byte-identical.** This is the load-bearing safety claim — V133 is a provable no-op unless `prog.weeks` has actually drifted.
- **Drifted programs: 439 / 14,336 days changed (3.06%), 133 / 150 programs affected — and 100% of the changed days are untouched days in weeks at or after the current week.** Zero changes outside that partition.

**Gate: 8/8 on V133, 5/8 on V132**, the control failing on the reported bug itself (A1), the duplicate-session symptom (A8 — `mon=Sumo deadlift / tue=Sumo deadlift`, reproducing the screenshots), and the day-key union (A7). **Gate integrity proven by breaking the build two ways in separate scopes:** reverting the pierce alone relit A1 and A8 only; reverting the union alone relit A7 only — so the two fixes are independent and each assertion has teeth. Plus **150 fuzzed touched/untouched combinations across random cfgs, 0 build errors, 0 invariant violations** (snapshots byte-identical, no null day objects, every training day present in every week).

## 11f. Standing decisions — do not re-derive
- **(V190 D39-i, concur) THE HEADER SHOWS THE SECTION LABEL; THE ROUND SENTENCE IS SYNTHESIZED ONLY WHEN THERE IS A REAL N.** From `sec.rounds`, or for legacy sections from the min across members parsing both grammars. **No fallback constant anywhere in this path.** The banner is an OVERRIDE of the label — which is why "no number" is not a blank header, it is the label, exactly as already happens when the label carries its own round count. One branch serves new sections, legacy sections and presets alike. Min and not `items[0]` because min can only under-state; inflating above a capped member is the dangerous direction.
- **(V190 D39-ii, concur) `rounds: null` MEANS "NOT A ROUND BLOCK"; OMITTED MEANS "LEGACY".** The two must stay distinguishable or the resolver cannot tell an authored claim from a pre-V190 snapshot. Applied to the 9 preset blocks that have no round count by construction (AMRAP, EMOM, every-N-min, for-time, alternate-every). Not applied where the label states a count — `'8 rounds each: 20s work / 10s rest'` was caught by the not-a-round-block pattern and reverted: **the label wins over a heuristic.**
- **(V190 D39-iii, concur) `sessionTimeEst` READS THE SAME RESOLVER AS THE BANNER.** It carried its own copy of the broken regex and the same hardcoded 3. Fixing the header alone would have left the clock lying on 45.7% of days. Item's own count first (it holds the true per-item number in both grammars), then the section, then — for a detail with no parseable count at all — 3.
- **(V190 D39-iv, concur, amended in build) D2 OWNS THE SECTION, AND KEEPS THE MEMBERS IN SYNC WITH IT.** The ruling as issued said "leave member strings alone"; that is not possible under D39-v, because `parseRx` still reads `item.detail` and a section at 2 rounds with members reading 3 would put a 3-row logger under a 2-round banner — a new contradiction for an old one. So: halve `sec.rounds`, then normalise every member to it via `_setLeadingSets`. **The section is the writer; the members are an enforced copy** — which is what they always were, just never enforced. Scope unchanged: 3→2 only, supersets only; non-superset accessories keep the per-item rewrite.
- **(V190 D39-v, concur) THE MEMBER STRIP IS DISPLAY-ONLY; `item.detail` IS UNTOUCHED.** `parseRx` has five callers and one of them — the add sheet's day set-count — sums member details and was **correct** before this build. Pulling the count out of the data would have broken a working reader to fix a broken one. The rendered string loses the leading count; the data keeps it.
- **(V188, Mario) THE SOURCE IS NEVER NAMED IN THE APP.** No "Nike", no "NRC", no "NSW" in user-facing copy. Doctrine attribution lives in the handoff and code comments only. App copy speaks in the coach's first person — "I don't recommend under 6 weeks", never "Nike recommends". Gate G7 greps every non-comment line and every built session for the word.
- **(V189 D36, concur) NRC RACE PROGRAMS: THE LONG RUN AND ITS EVE ARE OFF LIMITS TO THE HINGE AND THE SQUAT. THE HINGE RIDES A SPEED DAY.** Hard days hard, easy days easy. `nrcLegLiftPlacement` + `_nrcLegCost` own the leg-role days on any program whose week-1 long run `isNRC`; `deconflictSameRegion` reads the same table and may never raise it. The squat/armor day prefers its own day or the day after a speed session, not the speed day itself (calf work does not stack on the run that loads the calves). A recovery run is never "clean". The NSW 48h rule is untouched off NRC and is the fallback when no finite pair exists.
- **(V189 D37, concur) THE SHAKEOUT RUNS THE DAY BEFORE THE RACE.** Speed never sits last before the race when a recovery run exists in race week. The eve, then two days out, takes the last recovery run if it is an open train day; the day it came from rests; user rest days are respected. Overrides D14a §5's "the day that held the race rests": it rests only if it does not become the shakeout.
- **(V189 D38, concur) NO LIFTING FROM TWO DAYS OUT THROUGH THE RACE.** Overturns D34's "the primer stays" on race day. Race day and the eve: no sections. Two days out: no lifting, tier A mobility survives, a lift-only train day becomes rest. The 5K/10K TIME TRIAL is the race (V186 §4) and takes the pass. Three days out keeps its primer (MANNY's trap bar, accepted).
- **(V188 D14a §1) Race goals derive their start from the race date; the field is read-only.** Monday of race week − (plan−1) weeks. The entered date's weekday always wins — "half = Sunday" is a default, never an assumption (THE HALF MANNY was briefly entered as Sat Dec 5; the real date is Sun Dec 6, same start either way).
- **(V188 D14a §2) Behind-you weeks are outside the block.** `prog.blockOpen` = creation day, written ONLY by alignment; `dayBeforeStart` treats days before it like pre-start days. D23's read ("already past days are outside the block") is now code.
- **(V188 D14a §3) Race beyond the plan → future-dated start.** Peaking early is the one thing a race plan must not do. The gap is named in copy ("run easy three times a week until then"); a base block for the gap is a two-program feature, not built.
- **(V188 D14a §4) Floors are the source's own: 5K/10K 4, half 6, marathon 12.** Under the floor: warn (signal colour), offer the shorter plan as a tap, still build. (V186 §3 recorded "4" — that was the 5K/10K number only; superseded here.)
- **(V188 D14a §5) Race-week pin.** Race weekday trains even if it is a rest day; the day that held the race session and every train day after the race rest. The race session is located by subtype (`RACE DAY|TIME TRIAL`), never by position — position fails under the V153 spacer and in multi-sport (fuzz found both).
- **(V188 D14a §6) NRC run days lay out Monday→Sunday.** `cardioTrainDays = _ISO_ORDER.filter(...)` for race goals only. This is the fix for the V186 watch item 1 (race on Sunday at the START of race week). Untrained weeks on Sunday-including calendars re-lay; trained weeks are frozen.
- **(V188 D14a §9) The race date is editable on the program; the start date is not, when aligned.** `setProgRace` replaces `setProgStart` in the week-view subrow on aligned programs.
- **(V186 §2, Mario: "i concur with all GO") RACE → NRC, TEST → NSW.** `Run a 5K` / `Run a 10K` are race goals and ride Nike's tables; `run_pace_goal` / `run_mile_time` / `run_15_under10` / `run_base` are PST-shaped and stay on NSW untouched (proven byte-identical V185→V186). Argued on coaching grounds: NSW-as-built for 5K/10K was one quality session alternating 800s/CHI, nothing above 5K pace, no hills, no sharpener, no race day — a fitness program, not a race program; it is "more austere, not more demanding." NRC is consumer-grade (light VO2max dose, 6/10 tempo) but has the shape a race needs and the median user will execute. The alternative — bolting a second quality day, hills and a race day onto NSW — is a third doctrine and the project rule forbids it.
- **(V186 §1) D14b dissolved into D14a.** With 5K/10K on fixed 8-week tables, race alignment is one mechanism for all four race goals. V187 = D14a alone.
- **(V186 §3) 5K/10K are 8 weeks, fixed.** Nike's floor is 4 ("plan on at least 4 weeks"); under 8 weeks out D14a back-dates the start, under 4 the wizard warns and still builds. The climb/age/ceiling math is computed and discarded for these two goals. A 10K beginner under 2 mi baseline gets the 5K plan offered — that offer is D14a's feasibility check, not built here.
- **(V186 §3b, consequence found by fuzz) An NRC run goal pins the program length.** `_nrcPin` in `calcProgramLength`: after the max, if an NRC goal is present, `maxWeeks` = its table length and the other sports' length warnings are replaced by one line ("Other sports fit inside the 8-week 5K plan"). Half/marathon were never bitten only because 14/18 always won.
- **(V186 §4) No race date on 5K/10K = full 8 weeks, race day is a TIME TRIAL.** Opposite of the half/marathon truncate-at-peak rule, on purpose: 3.1 and 6.2 are trainable distances, a race-less athlete should finish with a number, and it is the evidence D11 wants. `Long Run — TIME TRIAL`, `dose.tgt` = the race's chart column, `_longRunTier` returns null on it (D34 shape).
- **(post-V186) §3b and §4b CONCURRED by Mario ("I concur with all"). Mario then re-sequenced: V187 = F2 + F6 + F7 (built), V188 = D14a.**
- **(V187 F2 — built) The five-run gate is the "performance lift paths" class, not a literal 'endurance' string.** `LIFTING_FOCUS_TO_GOAL` has no `support_endurance`; support_strength/support_athletic → `athletic`, support_prevention → `endurance`, and `cfg.goal==='athletic'||cfg.goal==='endurance'` is already the file's definition of "cardio is the objective" at three sites. F2 uses the same test. If Mario wants the fifth run narrower (support_athletic only), it is one predicate.
- **(V187 F6 — built) The dress rehearsal note wins over the cutback note; the cutback flag stays for the dose.** `rehearsal:true` is a note/subtype flag only. Pace is the progression (Nike lists it as a long run); the note rehearses the routine.
- **(V187 F7 — built) Effort numbers print on NRC programs only.** Same word "tempo," two doctrines, two numbers — the chips do not print both. The unit line is the carrier (`/mi · 6/10`) so `runAnchorLine` (label + value) is unaffected.
- **(V186 §4b, consequence found by the gate) The time trial is the event for the taper.** `taperEventOn(cfg)` = `eventTargeted !== false` OR an NRC 5K/10K run goal; both taper sites (`programTaperWindow`, the hoisted cardio taper) read it. V116's "taper only for a real event" stands — the table's race week is the event.
- **(V186 §5) 5K/10K ceiling 3→4**, half's compression (2 speed + 1 recovery + long at 4; speed/recovery/long at 3). `GOAL_RECOMMENDED_DAYS.run_5k` 3→4.
- **(post-V186, F2 — RULED, Mario: "Easy day concur") Ceiling 5 for the four NRC goals when `cfg.goal==='endurance'` (support_athletic / support_endurance) AND ≥6 training days; 4 otherwise.** Every half/marathon program ever built has been 4 runs — Nike's week is 5 ("Each week you will have 5 runs") and the `nRunDays >= 5` layouts in `getNRCSessionTypes` have never executed. The 4-cap is the right hybrid compression and stays for lifting-priority focuses; on the endurance track the file's own governor comment says cardio is the objective, so the cap contradicted it. Build (V188): the one number in `SPORT_CEILINGS` gated by focus + day count; gate must prove the 5-run type lists through the V153 spacing chooser on real calendars (they have never been through it); D18 already tiers the extra day. Not built yet.
- **(V186 §6/F3) Half W9 "Another Ten Mile Run" is `hold:true`.** Nike's second deliberate hold; W5 had the flag since D4e, W9 never did.
- **(V186 §6/F4) On a single-speed week, recovery indices are CALENDAR order.** `recIdxAll` = ordinal among recovery + speed2 days, written at type assignment, read only when `_single`. The old `_recIdx = _baseSlots` put Nike's last entry on the speed2 day wherever it sat (6-day half: 2-mile Wednesday, 25:00 Friday).
- **(V186 §6/F5) Race week keeps Nike's LAST N recovery entries; every other week keeps the longest N.** Keyed off `wk.long.race`. The shakeout (1 mi / 1K / 2 mi) is the last entry on every plan and keep-longest dropped it on every layout — a 25:00 (or a 5K) was the day-before run.
- **(V186 §7a) 5K/10K W1 "First Speed Run" carries `5:00 warm up`.** Nike's 5K and 10K pages omit it; the half and marathon pages print it on the identically named run. D31 doctrine: the source's own numbers.
- **(V186 §7b) 10K W6 long run = 7 mi.** Nike prints "Seven Mile Run — 11.25K/3.1 Mile Run"; label and kilometres agree on 7.
- **(V186 §8) "Out Strong Back Fast" on 5K/10K is Nike's own 12:00 out / 11:00 back, verbatim** (the marathon entry). D12b's three-rung progression stays half-only.
- **(V185 D1) The bare `/press/` catch-all stays HORIZONTAL — name the vertical presses instead.** `/\bsingle-?arm press\b|^kettlebell press$|\blog press\b/ → vpress`. Flipping the default was considered and rejected: a future `Floor press` reaches the catch-all and IS horizontal. **Its only ordering constraint is that it sit above the catch-all** — the bench rule is irrelevant to it, measured, not assumed. Any new press whose name does not say "overhead" must be added here by name.
- **(V185 D2-a) Pulldowns are vertical pulls and the pulldown test must PRECEDE the row test.** `lat pull` in the vpull rule was dead code for the life of the app because `/row|pulldown|straight-arm/` matched first.
- **(V185 D2-b) `Straight-arm pulldown` is `vpull`, and that is a scope call, not a technique one.** It is a straight-arm pullover with no elbow flexion, so `row` is definitionally wrong (a row is horizontal and elbow-flexed). What this axis measures is tissue saturation, and it loads lat-dominant shoulder extension in the vertical plane like the lat pulldown beside it in the pool. **`null` is not available to it:** it fills the row slot's MAIN on `lowback/protect`, and an invisible main lift is the Ball slams mistake.
- **(V185 D2-c) `elbow/workaround` caps `vpull`.** Added so D2 could not hand a cranky elbow an uncapped lat pulldown. Chinups were already uncapped there, so this closes a pre-existing gap rather than paying for the reclassification: loaded elbow flexion is loaded elbow flexion whichever direction you pull.
- **(V184 D1) Miles carries three columns — whole × tenths × hundredths.** `run_dist` now stores two decimals (`2.35`), finer than the `step="0.1"` the field once declared. Nothing downstream cares (`parseFloat`), and it closes the V182 rounding watch item. Emission is always two decimals, so `6` becomes `6.00`.
- **(V184 D2) Wheel cells are flexed by column count, never 50/50.** `.iaw-f3`/`.iaw-f2` keep every column the same width across wheels with different column counts. Re-measure before adding a fourth column anywhere: 5 columns is 59pt each at 375pt, and 6 would fall under the 44pt target.
- **(V184 D3) The roller reads as a roller because of the SIZE RAMP, not the shading.** `_iawShape` scales and fades rows by distance from centre. It is windowed to ±4 rows on purpose — a wrapped seconds column is 300 nodes and shaping all of them per frame janks on device. Never make it global.
- **(V184 D4) Depth is warm-ink paper depth, never glass.** Inset lip plus a light centre band, DS tint 40,34,22, existing shadow strengths. Frosted glass was mocked and rejected: the DS names blurred glows as out of bounds.
- **(V184 D5) A column wraps exactly when it has no dash row — derived, never listed.** `_iawWraps(c){return !c.nil}`. The left column of each pair carries the `—` and must never wrap, or "didn't log it" becomes reachable by accident on a flick. No carry between columns, ever: a spin on seconds must not rewrite minutes.
- **(V183 D1) The Done/Skip pair is the end of the session, not a fixture attached to it.** `.detail-foot` is inline at the natural page bottom and never sticky. This is an ORDER guarantee, not a cosmetic one: sticky let a day be marked complete from above the log form. Anything later that wants a persistent completion affordance has to answer that first.
- **(V183 D2) No band behind the inline footer, and no scroll cue pointing at it.** Surface fill and top rule exist to mask content sliding under a floating bar; inline there is nothing to mask. A "scroll down to finish" hint reintroduces the exact pressure the move removes — the `0 / N LOGGED` header chip is the progress affordance.
- **(V182 D1) The wheel is for bounded time and distance entry, not for every number.** Miles and pace on the generic run form and `reps_dist` rep time. Reps keep the `exset-ctl` stepper, minutes/yards/weights keep their number boxes. The test is whether the field has natural bounds and a separator the keyboard fights — not whether it holds a number.
- **(V182 D2) Miles split whole × tenths; pace splits minutes × seconds.** Mario overruled a single 1,000-row miles column on ergonomics: "break up the miles into one column miles then the other the .1234." 100 rows then 10 rows is one flick each, and it reads the way the number is spoken. Tenths, not hundredths — the field already declared `step="0.1"`, so nothing is lost against the text box it replaced.
- **(V182 D3) There is no Log button, and there must not be one.** The card's fields autosave on `input` and `handleDayStatus` owns committing — saving and marking the day are a single tap. A wheel-local commit button would give the card two commit surfaces and a state where the wheel reads 9:30 while nothing is stored. The wheel writes on scroll-settle through a hidden input instead.
- **(V182 D4) The dash sits on the LEFT column of each pair only.** A wheel always has a value, so without a `—` row there is no way to express "didn't log it" — and blank means *as planned*. The right column has no dash and defaults (`.0`, `:00`), which makes a half-set entry like `— . 4` unreachable.
- **(V182 D5) Wheel bounds are measured from engine output, never chosen.** Pace 4–17 because the engine prescribes 5:00–14:30/mi; rep time 0–9 because the only rep distances are 400m and 800m. When a new session type widens either envelope, re-measure and re-rule — do not widen the column on intuition.
- **(V182 D6) The wheel is flat and rectilinear — no rotateX, no fade mask.** A true iOS wheel is the only curved, shaded, dimensional object on a print-styled screen. Scroll-snap column, hard selection band, mono numerals (the DS allows mono for technical readouts). Mocked curved as well so the rejection was deliberate.
- **(V181 D1) A day is Done or Skipped. There is no Partial.** Mario: "users swapping and skipping workouts is still showing up and getting after it." Measured: the engine already agreed (streak and count treated partial as complete). Legacy `partial` records are never rewritten; `statusOf` normalizes on read. The swap-sheet "nothing else does this job" copy now points at the minus, not at Partial.
- **(V181 D2) Done and Skip both toggle; unmark has no consequence beyond the record.** Mario: "if someone clicks done on accident they can unclick it to no consequence right?" — they could not (V180 had no unmark). Now: tap active → pending, log kept, streak recomputes, final-day season latch released. The confetti that already fired stays fired (harmless); the snapshot stays (first-touch-wins, and any log entry takes it anyway).
- **(V181 D3) The day-after reminder offers the same two states, same weighting** — Done is the go, Skip is the ghost.
- **(V181 D4) The streak stands alone on the workout and streak banners.** `This Block` was `completedCount()` — a raw lifetime tally with no denominator that could never fall, sitting next to a streak that told the same story better. Recommended an adherence fraction (done / scheduled-to-date) as the replacement; Mario chose to drop the stat instead. The season popup's Workouts/Skips/Weeks are untouched — that is the end-of-block accounting, a different job.
- **(V181, rejected) No adaptation ledger, no "Adapted" kicker.** Proposed stamping `{dropped, swapped, logged}` on the record and reading it out on the banner. Mario: "nah no adapted nothing its not really worth much." The flags still exist on the day (`_skipped`, swap records); nothing reads them into the completion record.
- **(V174 D19) The start date lives on the `name` step — field under Program name, `Start date` row in the summary.** Concurred. The only step about the program-as-an-object, and the last question before Generate. An 8th step for one input is a step-count tax.
- **(V174 D20, amended) Prefilled with today, editable, never null.** Mario struck the first form (This week / Next week chips with a Mon–Wed/Thu–Sun default): "nah lets not do that." Amended to a bare date input prefilled with today. `renderWizardStep` repairs an empty field to today; `startWizard` resets it to today.
- **(V174 D21) The resolve line states what the entry resolves to.** Concurred. Same doctrine as the V172 clamp statement: the engine is going to round through `getWeekMonday()` regardless, so say so. One sentence, one node, updated on `change` (not `input` — a full re-render tears the native picker down on iOS). Copy is one function (`startResolveCopy`) shared with the `setProgStart` toast so the two surfaces cannot disagree.
- **(V174 D23) The freeze does not require a clock.** Mario: "ok". `refreshProgram`'s cut is `max(dateCut, maxTouched+1)`; the second half is precisely the "date can move" hardening and needs no date. Gated behind `prog.startDate` it protected nothing on a dateless program. Now `if(prog.weeks)`; no clock ⇒ `_curWk=1` (every frozen week pierces — nothing is dead past), and the touched frontier carries the freeze. Mario's second D23 answer ("already past days are outside the block") is the D24 rule below, not a freeze rule.
- **(V174 D24, amended — REVERSED from the first ruling) Start any day. No snap. Pre-start days in week 1 are outside the block.** First ruled as forward-snap to the next Monday on the argument that no flag could tell the Progress aggregates week 1 was short. **That was wrong** — `startDate !== getWeekMonday(startDate)` is the flag, free, no state. Coaching: week 1 is the on-ramp, the lightest week in the block; a Friday start on a 5-day layout loses three intro sessions and gains the athlete at the moment they are most likely to begin. Mario, on the survey: "what I want is just ease for the user." Containment is good: the case a short week 1 would actually hurt is NRC race alignment, and D14a back-dates those to a Monday. Outside-the-block means: not missed, not pending, no reminder, no tap, dim cell showing its date, not in the DONE denominator, never the hero. The predicate is on DATES (`dayBeforeStart`) so a back-dated `setProgStart` resolves identically; only week 1 can hold such days by construction.
- **(V174 D25) The zero-training-day guard.** Concurred. A start whose remaining week has no training days (Saturday on a weekend-rest layout) snaps to the next Monday and says so. The one case "start now" means nothing. Applies in the wizard and on the week-view control.
- **(V174 D26) Progress accumulators withhold a short week 1; means and per-observation modules keep it.** Concurred. Ladder (weekly max), drift (weekly sums), the mileage dot (weekly sum) skip week 1 with the D6 grammar (`skip1` beside `cut`, foot copy states it). Recovery-pace and easy-effort weekly MEANS and the class scatter are unaffected — the V167 reasoning on line ~13100 already says a mean does not understate on a partial week the way a sum does.
- **(V175 D27) Overlays splice by DAY, both types.** Concurred. Dates were already the source of truth (§6b); the splice was the one consumer still reading the derived week, and a Tuesday trip rewrote six days. Both types because the mechanism is identical, injury spans are already built date-exact, and the D2b clock already counts days; the observable change on injury is that a mid-week "I'm back" no longer spills the bridge/halfstep into a third calendar week (A8 asserts it, a V174 control proves the spill existed). Seam measured before ruling (structural drop-in, 0 mismatches / 54,432; cross-seam repeats 4.4% vs 6.9% base) so the merged week is deliberately NOT re-deconflicted. The universe moves from `_swapUniverseWeeks[w]` to a key stamped on the spliced day, so it survives a rest-day move and lives in the snapshot.
- **(V175 D28) A superset of one is straight sets.** Concurred. Demote, in one final sweep on every tier; matches the V162 precedent for a skipped partner. Rejected: merging orphans (invents a pairing), refilling (the pool collapsing is the cause). Label scrub is data hygiene only — the card never rendered non-Main labels (§8 `buildSectionsHTML`), so the athlete-visible fix is the header vanishing.
- **(V175 D29) `to` is inclusive: the last day the overlay applies.** Concurred. The engine already was in three places; only the BACK ON label and "reverts on its own" copy disagreed, and the week splice had hidden the contradiction. THROUGH; equal dates = one-day trip; chip "Back to normal <to+1>"; sheet preview in dates and day counts, no week grammar over a partial week.
- **(V175 D30) Partial-trip week: the pill stands, the days are marked.** Concurred on the mock. `Maintenance Week` only when every training day is a hotel day (a five-of-six Mon–Fri trip on a Mon–Sat athlete is NOT a maintenance week); trip days get the strip underline and the Travel tag on hero + session header. Underline kept over dropping it ("the hero tag and chip could carry it") because the strip is where a multi-day trip reads as a span at a glance. Chip title grammar: `TUE SEP 1` for a day, `SEP 1–4` for a span, `WEEK 2` / `WEEKS 2–3` only for whole weeks.
- **(V175, Mario) Version numbers are sequential in build order.** "It should be the next V number; doing this out of order shit will fuck up version control." V174's out-of-order ship is not precedent. The queued slates were renumbered V176 / V177 (header, §12).
- **(V174, on Mario's D22 point) Races are Sunday (half/marathon) and Saturday (5K/10K).** Verified `getWeekMonday` resolves a Sunday BACK six days (not forward), so a Sunday race lands on day 7 of its week — Nike's shape. Two things D14 did not cover and now belong to V175: race day can land on a rest day and must pin to its weekday; 5K/10K are NSW-engine goals with computed length, so "alignment" there is length-lands-the-race, not table back-dating. Hence the D14a/D14b split in §12.
- **(Close-out D18, ruled Aug 31 2026) THE RUN IS THE DAY — tiered by time on feet.** Mario: "after 6 maybe even 8 miles that's the whole workout, just stretch and call it a day"; concurred with the tiers. Measured first: THE HALF MANNY's long-run Saturdays carry `Kettlebell swing 4×10` explosive-first plus two upper sets even on the 12.5-mile peak — a ballistic hinge on hamstrings after two-plus hours of eccentric loading, the textbook hamstring-strain setup, and the exact inverse of the V153 day-before hinge clamp. Rule, keyed off the long-run dose (`mins`, or `mi × chartRow.recovery` — scales with the athlete: 6 mi is 80 min on a 13:20 row and 60 on a 7:30 row): **≥75 min → no lift; a post-run mobility block (~12 min: calves, hip flexors, 90/90, T-spine, foam roll) logged complete/skip.** 75 is the number the long-run note already uses for fuel — one doctrine line. **45–75 min → upper and trunk only, ≤8 sets; no power block, no hinge, no heavy carries on ANY long-run day regardless of length.** **<45 → current interference model** with D8's fix. Race day unchanged; the 8-mile dress rehearsal is tier A. Layout rule at the point the day's lift is built (`legLoad` + `dose` are both on the session); blast radius = long-run days only; own measure pass.
- **(Close-out D8) `_cardioInterference` long-run tier + hyphenated minutes — the two-line fix stays, under D18.** Corrected finding: it is the SAME day's lift (the day-before clamp keys off session type and is fine); the classifier files NRC long runs as easy (0.55) because "Recovery Pace" matches before "long run"; mileage runs are partly rescued by the distance bump, time-based runs are not because `60-minute` has a hyphen the duration regex does not read. Measured on Mario's row: W2 4 mi 0.63→0.93; W7/W11 1.05→1.35; **W12 One Hour Run 0.55→1.00 (heavy carries currently OFFERED after 60 min)**; marathon Two Hour Run 0.55→1.35 (scored as a 15-minute jog). Consumers: session set cap `20−2×i`, leg-set cut `3×i`, carry gate `≥1.0`. Subtype-first, hyphen-tolerant minutes; ships with a scanner differential harness that diffs interference per session across versions on every build.
- **(Close-out D9) Wizard mile-time validation.** Concurred. Reject impossible entries (negative, <3:00, >25:00); for entries outside the chart's 5:00–12:00 say before the build what the clamp will do ("at 13:00 your paces come from the 12:00 row — consider base-building first"). Fold the wizard's beginner `660` into the engines' `690`.
- **(Close-out D10) A `Goal` chip on pace-goal programs.** Concurred, Mario's framing: "so a user has something that measures progress." The chip is the fixed target; the measure of progress is the race-projection card moving toward it — prescription owns the fixed dimension.
- **(Close-out D11) Re-anchor + seed source — logged, OPEN for a design session.** See §12.
- **(Close-out D12a) Week-view headlines that say "Mile Pace" without a number stay as they are.** The number is one tap in; paces in the headline duplicate the segment line.
- **(Close-out D12b) Half W5's progression tempo is SPECIFIED, not described.** Mario: "I don't like users assuming too much." Nike gives only "23:00 Progression Tempo Run"; the app prints `8:00 at Marathon Pace → 8:00 at Half Marathon Pace → 7:00 at 10K Pace`, each with the athlete's number — averages to tempo effort, finishes faster than it starts. The Tempo column is deliberately NOT a rung: Nike's chart has tempo slower than half-marathon pace on the fast rows, so a progression through it would run backwards for a 7:30 athlete; Marathon → Half → 10K is monotonic on every row and the build asserts that across the whole chart. Stays flagged in §12 as the one interpretive line.
- **(Close-out D13) HALF MANNY's provenance reads "entered" — accepted.** The seed cannot be reconstructed honestly after the fact.
- **(Close-out D14) NRC race programs auto-align to the race.** Concurred. Event-targeted half/marathon sets `startDate = raceWeekMonday − (tableLength−1) weeks` at creation, so an athlete 9 weeks out opens in Nike's "9 weeks to go" week with the taper intact; the wizard's "your race simply lands in week N" copy becomes a feasibility check against the baseline (entry-week long run vs `baselineDist`) with the 10K plan as the offered alternative. Pace goals keep their copy. Mechanism already exists (`setProgStart`/`calcCurrentWeek`); weeks before the start render as past and nothing counts them as missed (no adherence denominator exists — checked).
- **(V172 D7a) A "Run paces" group on the program card, in the `lift-chip` grammar, between Starting 1RM and Cardio.** Concurred. The two anchor sets sit together; no new component.
- **(V172 D7b) A provenance sentence under the chips, from a persisted `mileBestSrc`.** Concurred. Four forms; the seeded form names the source program and the number of logged recovery runs, because that is what the number actually is.
- **(V172 D7c) The copied selections carry `Run anchor: … (provenance) → 5k … / recovery …`** after the Cardio line. Concurred.
- **(V172 D7d) The row renders for every run goal that reads the pace chart — half, marathon, 5K, 10K, pace goal and its legacy aliases; never for `run_base`.** Mario: yes for NRC, open on NSW; ruled in on the reasoning that the NSW speed goals print `5K Pace:` and `Tempo:` from the same row and the athlete is equally blind to its origin.
- **(V172 D7e) The anchor is NOT editable mid-program.** Mario asked for the coaching view and concurred with it: a pace row is a prescription, not a fitness readout; Daniels/McMillan/Nike re-anchor on EVIDENCE (a race or time trial), at most every 4–6 weeks, one step at a time. Holding a stable hard-day target is how progress becomes visible (same pace, lower RPE — which the app already logs); the easy days are effort-defined and float on their own (V155 trend, race projection already show it); Nike's tests are built into the plan (8K tempo, dress rehearsal); and a mid-block change would put two plans on either side of the freeze. The provenance line says nothing about changing it. **An evidence-gated re-anchor** (offered once after a logged race or the rehearsal, accept/decline, Progress marks the change) is the future feature — §12.
- **(V172) The stated anchor is the row the engine trains, not the entry.** The chart clamps; printing the raw entry would make the card disagree with every session card. When they differ the sentence says which bound was used.
- **(V171 D1) Mile Pace is printed everywhere Nike says it, from `{mile}` = `chartRow.mile`.** It is the row anchor; the athlete is never asked to remember an onboarding number. Best Pace carries no number — Nike's glossary leaves it to the athlete.
- **(V171 D1b) Mile/Best are defined in the Intervals note, not a separate glossary.** "Mile Pace is what you could race for one mile (9/10 effort)" on every Intervals card; the Best sentence appends only when the detail uses Best. Mario: "intervals note is fine."
- **(V171 D2) Speed sessions are line-per-segment on `\n`, line 1 a headline.** Mario, on the mock: "that is much better." String-level, not the swim `segments` structure — extending that grammar to runs is a structural change with the duration estimator in its blast radius and is parked in §12.
- **(V171 D3, REJECTED) The NRC guided-run name is not printed.** The proposal was line 1 = "NRC Guided Run: Run Strong. Repeat." so an athlete with the Nike app could be audio-coached. Mario: "nah, I want the app to be standalone." Do not re-propose.
- **(V171 D4a) Both tables are Nike-verbatim, and the transcription is re-verified against the PDF as a gate.** Concurred. Includes the race-week fix: a week with one Nike speed session never runs it twice.
- **(V171 D4b) Recovery entries accept distance (`{mi,label}`) and dose as `dist`.** Concurred. The alternative — keep guessing minutes — is what produced `31` for a 5K.
- **(V171 D4c) When a layout has fewer recovery slots than Nike entries, the LONGEST survive.** Concurred. Reasoning: the 15:00 run is Nike's "N weeks to go" intro jog; the long one is the week's aerobic volume, and a 4-day athlete losing it would be the exact inverse of the V153 direction. Mario asked whether onboarding should gate this by limiting rest days: **no** — `CARDIO_FLOORS` already pins half at 4 and marathon at 5 training days, so D4c only ever fires for a 4-day half athlete who still gets both speed sessions, the long run and the long recovery; tightening to 5 pushes hybrid lifters off the endurance path for no coaching gain.
- **(V171 D4d) Marathon range long runs dose the LOW end; the label keeps the range.** Concurred. Coach reasoning: on a lift week the extra miles at the top of 18–22 buy less than they cost; Nike leaves the choice to the athlete, so the app takes the conservative side and prints the range.
- **(V171 D4e) Half W5 is a HOLD, not a cutback.** Concurred. Nike titles it "Another 10K Run" — same distance as W4. The cutback note said the distance "steps back" while the number on the card did not; the hold note says consolidate, do not add.
- **(V171 D5) The post-V154 ruling stands: NRC speed days log on the generic distance+pace form.** Mario: "ok." Readable copy does not create a fixed dimension; the slot still rotates flavors and Progress reads `run_dist` + `rpe`. One copy line added under the form so the athlete logs the whole watch session.
- **(V171 D6) Long runs read Nike's progression-run definition and carry `tgt = recovery`.** Concurred. The old copy built to race pace on every long run — a weekly race rehearsal on a program that lifts the next day, the load that made V153's hinge clamp necessary. Nike: start slower than Recovery Pace, finish faster, AVERAGE it; race pace on race day only. Recovery runs get the one-line progression cue. The dress-rehearsal week is not an exception — Nike gives it no pace instruction.
- **(V170 D1) Rest is a function of `_POWER_COST`, never a literal.** 2.0 → `~3 min rest`; 1.5/1.0 → `~2 min rest`; 0.5 → `60s rest`. Cost already encodes what rest is FOR — landing absorption and CNS drain — so `_POWER_REST` reads it rather than growing a second classification of the same fact.
- **(V170 D2) The 0.5 tier is prescribed for DENSITY, at 10 reps.** Its defining property — no landing to absorb, low CNS — is exactly why clustering it at 3 with full recovery is wrong. A swing's virtue is that it repeats cheaply; a triple with two minutes' rest discards the one thing it is best at. Ten, because the clamp binds 0/9 there and 9/9 at three, because all 43 hand-authored swing prescriptions in the file already sat at 10–20, and because it lands inside `_REP_FLOOR`'s independent `[10,15]` window.
- **(V170 D3) Cost wins; the positional role is the tiebreak only WITHIN a tier.** A 0.5 movement takes density reps from the lead slot or the second one. Load-bearing on the CrossFit pool — the one config where both slots can fill from 0.5 (the swing plus V143's `Wall ball shots`).
- **(V170 D4) The `Math.max(3, …)` set floor is preserved deliberately.** At ten reps the low cases land on raw 3, adjacent but not binding. Written down so nobody later ‘fixes’ a floor that is doing nothing only because the dose is correct.
- **(V170 D5) `_powerPrescribe` is the single writer for the power prescription string.** The literal was duplicated at both call sites and had already drifted once. `'max intent'` stays reserved for loaded triples per V144 Ruling 2; the 0.5 tier's V144 copy `'crisp and explosive'` described a triple and is retired with it, replaced by `'fast and crisp'`.
- **(V170 D6) No load cue is added to the power block.** The original complaint was a LOAD box with no anchor while every other loaded slot carries an RPE. Ten reps at 60s self-regulates — too heavy a bell and the pace fails, which teaches the athlete without being told. A second mechanism for a problem the first one closes is scope, not safety.
- **(V170 D7) `_repFloor` stays OUT of the power path.** It asks whether load can track a descending wave; that is a different question from what a movement needs in order to develop power. Their agreement on 10 is evidence the number is right, not a reason to couple them.
- **(V170 D8-revised) The Power section label makes no rest claim at all.** `Power — explosive first` / `Power — lower explosive`. The conditional version latched (see §10b). Rest is stated per-item under D1, where a later filter cannot falsify it. Claude's call, flagged for override; nothing in the file reads the phrase, and `_labelRegion`, `recoveryDeload` and `capSessionBudget` all key on `/^power\b/`, which is untouched.
- **(V168 D1) A stale load is never suppressed, at any age.** A number the athlete can see and reject beats a blank box, and V131 already makes them tap to take it. There is no upper staleness ceiling.
- **(V168 D2) Staleness is a property of the NUMBER, not of which store it came from.** The 21-day rule applies identically to same-program and cross-program pills; a 7-week-old entry inside the current block is exactly as misleading as one from a prior block.
- **(V168 D3) The cross-program winner is the most recent qualifying entry by `ts`, scanned across every other program including archived ones.** NOT most-recent-program — that is the wizard seed's rule, and it exists there only because the seed averages ≥3 runs.
- **(V168 D4) Same-program always wins, even when older.** The cross-program fallback is consulted only when the active store holds zero `weight>0` entries for the key. Anyone training inside one block sees byte-identical behaviour to V167.
- **(V168 D5) The log screen never writes to a foreign program's store.** Aliases are folded on a clone; `crossProgLastEntry` contains no writer, asserted structurally.
- **(V168 D6) `STALE_LOAD_DAYS` is its own constant.** It shares the value 21 with `SEED_WINDOW_DAYS` today; coupling them would mean retuning the run seed silently retunes lift warnings.
- **(V168 D7, Mario's copy) "That N came from <program>, about X old. Still where you are?"** — same-program stale substitutes "is" for "came from <program>,". The age is stated in the sentence; the pill sub-label carries age alone.
- **(V168 D8) The stale sentence fires once per RENDERED day card, on the first stale pill in document order.** Every stale pill below it is dashed and silent. Scope is `openDetail` (the re-render path), not the app session — a swap re-arms it exactly once, and day two of a week is not silenced by day one. Render-scoped variable, never persisted, so there is no stale-state store to prune.
- **(V168) The KB size chips do not read across a program boundary.** They PRE-SELECT a bell, which is a prefill in substance, and V131 forbids inferring a load the athlete did not ask for. Only the reuse pill crosses.
- **(V167 D11) Main vs accessory is a SLOT property, resolved from the freeze — never a name list.** Measured: 26 of 27 main-slot names also appear as accessories, and within a single program the split is frequently an exact tie, so no name list can be correct. `_slotOfEntry()` reads `ia_hist_['w<N>_<day>'].sections` and applies V162 D0 (item 0 of a `/^main/i` section). Tie-break is **main-if-ever** (Mario: showing a lift twice confuses users), and the main chart reads **only main-slot sessions** — blending the lighter accessory-day sets into the same line makes the progression jump for no reason — with the withheld count disclosed in the footer. Any future feature that needs this distinction must use the slot, not the name.
- **(V167) The ledger states real sets, never estimated maxes.** `scheme()` prescribes purely by RPE and ignores 1RM by design; a ledger headlining e1RM contradicts the engine. Worse, Epley error scales with reps, so `max()` across mixed rep ranges ranks a heavy single below a lighter double and reports a PR as a regression. Headline is the heaviest bar actually lifted, with its reps.
- **(V165 D1/D2/D3) Progress is free-tier, static, rich but not gamified.** No new capture fields, no new stores, no interaction layer (static SVG only). "Rich" means information density, not reward loops. The division of labour that already existed is now explicit doctrine: **the popup layer celebrates, the Progress screen records.** `computeStreak()`, confetti, shake and the kicker copy pools all live in the popup/week-view layer and stay there; Progress carries no badge, no streak, no PR celebration. (This dissolved the original D9 question, which had been asked on the false premise that the app did not already celebrate.)
- **(V165 D8) The app is willing to make a forward claim, bounded.** A projected finish time is permitted because it is a read of the app's own `PACE_CHART` with zero invented constants — `_mileFromRecoverySec` inverted, `_halfFromMileSec` read forward. It ships as a **±2.5% band, never a single time**, because a single number invites racing a Tuesday recovery run to move it, and the copy states it is an estimate from training pace and not a promise. The athlete's own intake mile is plotted alongside so the projection is a comparison they can check rather than a claim they must accept.
- **(V165 D10-A) Benchmarking is against the athlete's own numbers, never strangers'.** Population percentiles were considered and parked. Every credible strength norm is a bodyweight RATIO and the app collects neither bodyweight nor sex, so a percentile would be unsupported rather than merely imprecise. Two further reasons it is a separate decision, not a chart: adding a bodyweight field is a one-way door into weight tracking (the app currently has zero surface area for that, which is arguably a feature), and percentiles would be the first metric in the app that compares the athlete to anyone other than themselves.
- **Rep affinity is a MOVEMENT property, argued as a coach (V161).** The V160/V161 test for a `REP_AFFINITY` cap is not "can the reps be survived" but "what quality does the lift exist to train, and does the rep count still train it." Box squat = dead stop, re-brace, broken stretch reflex — a power/positional stimulus that is incoherent past ~6 reps AT ANY LOAD, so a light recovery week does not excuse it (Mario overruled Claude's D1-a lean on exactly this ground). Close-grip bench shares the 0.90 load-scale bucket but is range-biased, not dead-stop — high reps coherent, deliberately uncapped. A tension-held "squat to a box" used as a knee-friendly depth gauge at 10–12 reps is a DIFFERENT movement; if ever wanted, it enters as a new EXLIB name with its own grammar, never by loosening this cap.

- **(V162 D0, Mario's ruling) MAIN protection is ONE exercise, not a section.** Only item 0 of a `/^main/i` section refuses the minus; companions in Main sections and all Primer items are normal exercises. The top lift's exit is swap, guaranteed: a main slot with zero swap candidates is a build defect (gate B1).
- **(V162 R2/D1) Every non-main, non-note exercise is skippable, including superset members individually.** A skipped member drops out and the round continues; one survivor demotes the block to straight sets with the rounds×reps mapping said on the card. The header minus still takes the whole round.
- **(V162 D3/D6) Null-pattern families swap within `_AUX_FAMILY` (sidecar; volume accounting untouched); rehab, correctives and stretches are skip-only by design** — the injury overlay prescribed them deliberately, and an empty swap sheet on an injured config is the filter working (proven by differential, gate C1b). Power-family swaps answer to `_powerAllowed`.
- **(V162 D2) Single-implement rounds rank same-implement swaps first; cross-implement stays offered with a flow note** — the athlete swapping mid-complex has already decided the flow isn't happening as written.
- **(V162 D5) Note-items (icon-prefixed coaching copy, empty detail) carry no controls.**
- **(V163 D1, Mario's ruling) An added exercise's dose is a TYPE match against the live week, never positional inheritance.** Tier order: same pattern today (last wins) → same pattern this week → same region this week → `3×10 — RPE 7`. Main and primer sections never donate — added work is accessory by nature — and a donated dose is always read from the live week, so deloads donate deload doses without any special casing.
- **(V163 D2) A rep lift may only inherit a rep dose or an open RPE-set dose.** The `_addRxKind` fence refuses distance/time shapes by string AND by parse kind. The string half is load-bearing on its own: spelled-out units (`yards`, `meters`) defeat `parseRx`'s `yd|sec` token and return dressed as reps — exactly the V163 defect vector. Both halves carry sabotage proofs.
- **(V163 D3) The add-sheet banner promises no dose** ("Added work matches the closest thing to it in your week."); the resolved dose is stated per pick (toast) and per candidate (MORE tier set math). A sheet-level dose promise was only true when one dose existed for the whole sheet, which D1 ended.
- **(V163 D4) Optional finisher sections (loaded carries) are doctrine, not dose templates.** The section survives unchanged; only its accidental authority over other movements' prescriptions was removed.
- **(V160 D2-a/b/d) Rep affinity: a movement may declare the max reps a wave-driven prescription can ask of it; `Paused back squat` caps at 6.** The pause is a positional-strength tool (dead stop kills the stretch reflex — the 0.88 swap multiplier already encodes this); at 12 reps the set terminates on bracing, the wrong limiter. The tag is a SIDECAR map keyed on name, not a shape change to EXLIB — chosen so every existing consumer stays byte-identical and a rollback is trivial. Cap 6 is one rep past the curated content's own 4×4/3×5. Measure-pass honesty on record: the tag universe has ONE member today (all other tempo/iso names live in seconds-prescribed pools or bodyweight accessories where reps are correct); D2 was chosen for the declarative guard it puts in front of the NEXT tempo entry.
- **(V160 D2-c) Affinity scores against the BASE wave only; deload and taper are exempt.** Two reasons, both structural: the tactical deload's +3 would kill the variant on tw≥12 strength/athletic via a single 7-rep DELOAD week — which is a stepped-back-intensity technique week, a legitimate home for pause work — and post-transform scoring makes pool membership a function of totalWeeks (a 12-wk and an 8-wk strength block drawing from different pools for no coaching reason). The 55+ transform (+2 at p>.82) provably cannot flip a decision at cap 6: every p>.82 entry on the two eligible goals carries r≤3.
- **(V160 E1) The filter binds at the three wave-main call sites, not inside `_slot`.** `_slot` also serves pools whose call sites prescribe SECONDS (core, knee_stability); a rep cap is meaningless there. The tag means "max reps in a wave-driven prescription," nothing broader.
- **(V160 E2) An emptied pool FAILS CLOSED — throw at build, never fall back to the unfiltered list.** The V127 `f.length?f:pool` latch is the named anti-pattern. Unreachable at cap 6 (squat pool keeps 3 entries), which is exactly when the guard must exist: it fires the day someone tags aggressively.
- **(V160, affirmed in build) The swap universe inherits the filter by construction and this is CORRECT, not a leak.** A manual swap to an ineligible variant would receive the slot's wave scheme — the scheme follows the slot, not the exercise — recreating the collision through the side door. Curated RAND_POOLS keep their pause entries because they carry their own correct schemes.
- **(V156 D1) Archive over delete, two tiers.** History became cross-program valuable (Progress selector, wizard seed), so "delete program" = "destroy the training record" stopped being acceptable. Delete survives only behind an explicit second confirm stating it's irreversible. Note there was NO confirm at all before V156 — the Delete button fired `deleteProg` directly.
- **(V156 D2/G1) Progress is per-program; charts are never merged across programs.** Mario's ruling, replacing Claude's all-time-chart proposal: a new program opens to blank charts, and each block's story is viewed under its own chip. The week-index x-axis makes merging dishonest anyway (old w3 ≠ new w3 in time).
- **(V156 D3, affirmed) The anti-anchor pre-fill doctrine crosses program boundaries.** An old program's logged weight never reaches a new program's input field, reference chip, or draft — history is chart-visible only. Extension of V131.
- **(V156 D4/D6) Cardio seeds by user choice at build time; lifting never seeds.** Banner with explicit "Use this data" — the user knows their block is calibrated off old data. Lifting seed collides with D3 head-on and was ruled out without a design.
- **(V156 D5) Seed staleness window: 21 days, hard, both seeds or nothing (<3 qualifying runs = no banner).** Mario set 3 weeks against the proposed 4.
- **(V156 G2 amendment, flagged during build) `baselineDist` seeds from the MAX SINGLE run in the window, not mean weekly mileage.** The mock said weekly mileage; the wizard field asks single-run capability ("How far can you currently run?") and feeds `baselineMiles`' long-run clamp. Seeding weekly volume into a single-run field is wrong dose math — the field's semantics forced the amendment.
- **(V156 G3) The mile-best seed is an editable estimate, tagged as such, derived by inverting PACE_CHART.** No invented pace-conversion constants — if the forward map (mile→recovery) is doctrine, its inverse is too.
- **(V156 D7) Swap-preference carryover skipped this build** — surfacing old swaps as build-time suggestions is designed (accept/decline, never silent) but unbuilt; whether the preference signal is coaching-legitimate is an open ruling.

Rulings already made, with the reasoning that produced them. Reopening one of these costs a re-derivation against a codebase that has moved; if a change borders on any of them, reason against the entry before proposing it.

- **⚠️ THE 90/110/130 BUDGET DESCRIBES A PROTOCOL WE DELIBERATELY DO NOT RUN (V130). Do not "fix" the gap by inflating sets.** The NSW bands assume the guide's own 10-week table: **4 exercises, 10 reps, 2-3 sets** — that is how you accumulate 80-140 landings. The app's power block is **2 movements at 3 and 5 reps, max intent, full recovery**, which is genuinely higher intensity per rep, and the guide itself says volume should decrease when high intensity exercises are performed. **Sitting at roughly a third to a half of the band is therefore correct, not a shortfall.** Measured after V130: median session 25/37.5/50 against 90/110/130, and **zero sessions over budget in 1,940 samples.** The budget's job is to SCALE the dose and to backstop a future block that gets greedy — not to be reached. Reaching it would require ~22 sets of triples. **Changing the protocol to match the band is a coaching redesign of what a power day IS (option C, declined) and must not be smuggled in as a tuning change.**

- **POWER CLASS — DECIDED AND BUILT (V129). All seven calls are Mario's; do not re-litigate.**
  1. **One class, not two sub-types.** `explosive_efforts`, deliberately not `landings` — the class holds ball slams and wall balls, which have zero foot contacts, so naming the field after the mechanism would have forced a rename the first time upper-body work arrived.
  2. **Costs are weighted, not 1:1.** The NSW 80-140 budgets are about LANDING IMPACT (the guide: vertical jumps more stressful than horizontal, one-leg landings more than two, higher off the ground means a more forceful landing). A ball slam absorbs nothing. Flat counting would let an athlete burn a week's budget on slams and finish undertrained, or get locked out of slams because box jumps spent it. Weighted, a double-leg-jumps-only athlete hits 90/110/130 exactly as prescribed. **2.0** shock/depth · **1.5** unilateral landing · **1.0** double-leg jumps · **0.5** upper-body and med-ball · **0.5** weightlifting derivatives.
  3. **Budget flat across the block** (90/110/130 by experience). The cost weights already produce the guide's volume-DOWN-as-intensity-climbs progression — moving from jump squats at 1.0 to box jumps at 2.0 halves the rep count against the same budget. Stepping the budget too would reduce twice.
  4. **Injury by cost, not by blanket.** Doctrine says no plyometrics with ANY musculoskeletal injury; applied as protect drops all power, workaround keeps only the 0.5 tier — the work with no landing to absorb. Follows the guide's reasoning rather than its sentence: a kettlebell swing does not load a bad ankle.
  5. **Same-day collision prevented, not repaired.** Gated at selection, so `capRegionalFatigue` and the deconflicts never have to unwind a block they already scored. If a week has nowhere legal for lower-body power, it gets none — that is the correct answer, not a failure.
  6. **`experience` gates which cost tiers unlock** (beginner 1.0, intermediate 1.5, advanced 2.0). ACCEPTED RESIDUAL: the guide also bars high-intensity plyometrics above ~198 lbs and the app never asks bodyweight, so **a heavy beginner is the highest-risk athlete in the guide's terms and `experience` will not catch them.**
  7. **2 sessions/week, 48h apart**, matching the guide's stated figure for SEALs.
- **THE POWER AXIS GOVERNS THE POWER ROLE, NOT THE MOVEMENT EVERYWHERE (V129).** Kettlebell swings, ball slams and push press also arrive through the conditioning and hinge pools — a swing in `Leg superset B` is doing a hinge's job, and the existing injury pool overrides already rule on it. Blocks that CLAIM to be power work (`Power`, `Power — …`, `Explosive finisher`) answer to the power gate; the same movement in another role does not. **The gate assertion is scoped to those labels deliberately** — asserting over every appearance would be asserting the wrong contract.

- **⚠️ `__CAP_OFF` GATED THREE PASSES, NOT ONE (found and fixed V123).** `recoveryDeload`, `capSessionBudget` and `capRegionalFatigue` all read the same flag, so **any harness that toggled it to isolate one pass silently measured all three.** It produced a reading of "the regional cap trims 40 items on strength+no-cardio" when the true figure is **4**. Now three flags: `__DELOAD_OFF`, `__BUDGET_OFF`, `__REGIONAL_OFF`, with `__CAP_OFF` retained as the master switch. **Measured with the isolated flag: base 224 items, regional-off 228, budget-off 229, deload-off 224 — so the regional cap's footprint really IS narrow, exactly as §12 always said.** The hypertrophy exemption also verified clean under the isolated flag (identical counts). **STANDING LESSON: a shared test bypass is not an instrument. Check what else reads the flag before trusting a number it produced.**
- **~~#13 extend the regional cap to strength+run~~ — PREMISE WITHDRAWN, back to open.** It was raised as "confirm the footprint shouldn't reach further", which is a question, not a decision. It cannot be answered until someone re-measures with `__REGIONAL_OFF` now that the instrument works. Not built in V123.
- **Cardio doctrine calls answered (V122 session), only the bike one built.** `bike_ftp` at 2 days yields the EASY day to intervals, not the long ride — the long ride is the aerobic anchor and trading it buys quality by spending the base it exists to support. **Volume goals (`bike_50`, `bike_century`) deliberately unchanged.** Still UNBUILT and answered: anchor recovery pace to `prog.paceProgression` instead of `PACE_CHART` (touches every easy and long run, needs its own build), and extend `paceProgression` to `run_mile_time` / `run_15_under10`.
- **Still UNBUILT from the same answered set:** bar cable/machine isolation from a main slot; split the `wall sit` + `walking lunge` pairing for non-run configs; extend the regional-cap footprint to strength+run; reorder the day view so the main lift renders above the run card. All four are decided, none is written.
- **Chinups (V120 session).** `Weighted chinups` already exists as its own library entry and is already `isTrackableWeight`-true; plain `Chinups`, `Neutral-grip chinups` and `L-sit chinups` are already false. **The split the library has is the split Mario wants**, so B2c converts the three unweighted ones to open-ended and `Weighted chinups` keeps its rep target automatically. **L-sit chinups must never become a weighted option** — a vest is the only way to load it and describing that on a card is not worth the complication. No new library entries; nothing to build. 1,438 prescriptions affected by B2c.
- **Triceps residual on `shoulder/workaround` bodyweight beginner (V119). ACCEPTED, closed.** Recommendation given and taken: the entire `EXLIB.triceps` pool is `Bench dips` (`hpress`) plus four cable/dumbbell movements that do not exist on the bodyweight tier, so there is nothing to widen the pool WITH. Capping press volume is the point of a shoulder workaround, the triceps still get worked by the pressing that survives, and it is 4 configs of 1,890 on one tier/experience/injury combination. **Reopen only if bands are ever assumed on the bodyweight tier** — a band pushdown would then be the obvious fill, but that is a doctrine change (Room Only currently assumes no packed bands).
- **B2 ships as three builds, not one (V120 session).** Rationale is attribution, not tidiness: with three changes in one file, a prescription that looks wrong on device cannot be traced to a cause without re-running the whole sweep. B2a also fixes something that was live and broken, and had no reason to wait for the 8,818-line change.

  1. **The predicate is `isTrackableWeight`, NOT `_repFloor`** — measured, not assumed. `_repFloor` answers "what rep window does this movement's LOAD allow", a different question from "can this be loaded at all on THIS tier": it returns `null` for `Walking lunge`/`Reverse lunge` and a `[6,10]` window for `Split squat`/`Step-ups (chair)`/`Single-leg Romanian deadlift (bodyweight)` written for a dumbbell the bodyweight tier does not have. Adopting it would have **regressed 712 bodyweight-tier prescriptions**. `isTrackableWeight` is the same question the LOG side answers to decide whether to draw a weight field, so **the card and the prescription can never disagree: if there is nowhere to write a number, there is no rep target.**
  2. **Bodyweight tier is not swept** (`else` branch off `bodyweightSweep`). That tier has shipped the looser conversion since V94; sweeping it would test `bodyweightSweep`, not this. Makes B2c purely additive and unable to regress the tier with the most history. The guard is `!== 'bodyweight'` rather than a tier allowlist so a NEW tier is swept by default — forgetting to sweep is the worse failure.
  3. **Hip and core blocks are not swept.** `sec.core||sec.hip` return early. "Hip stability" is glute ACTIVATION and a prescribed rep count there is correct — banded side steps and monster walks are prep, not sets taken near failure. Caught only because the diff came back 11,391 against a measured candidate set of 8,818; the gap was 2,807 lines, all of them `Hip stability`, all being told to stop two reps short of failure on a monster walk.

- **Triceps work can vanish on `shoulder/workaround` bodyweight beginner (V119, accepted residual).** Correcting the incline classification made `capRegionalFatigue` count the day's real horizontal-press volume, the shoulder cap then bit, and `Close-grip pushups` dropped out of the `Triceps` block in **4 configs, net −10 prescription lines**. The cap is working correctly on a now-correct count, so this is not a regression — but those days end with no direct triceps work. **Open coaching call: accept, or widen the triceps pool so the slot has a non-`hpress` option to fall back to.** Nothing else lost lines anywhere in the 1,890-config sweep.
- **`computeStreak` is declared TWICE (found V119, not fixed).** Two genuinely different implementations, at ~line 7081 (33 lines, walks the calendar back 120 days off `activeProg.startDate`) and ~line 10380 (11 lines, walks `scheduledDays()` and treats pending as transparent). Function declarations hoist, so **the second silently wins and the first is dead code** — no error, no warning, and `node --check` passes. The later one looks like the intended behaviour but that is a product call, not a mechanical one. **Standing lesson: the §9 window-global lint only looked for collisions with `window` properties; add a duplicate-declaration check to it, which is what surfaced this.**
- **V111 accepted residuals (harness-measured, not bugs to rediscover):** `knee`/`ankle` protect legs day runs `hip_ext` ×3 — that day genuinely has one available pattern once Nordic/GHR are dropped by name, so the question is whether it should be a **shorter** day rather than a more varied one; **coaching call outstanding**. `lowback` on `home_basic`/`minimal`/`bodyweight` still crosses hip extension between the legs and pull days: no bar to hang from and rows dropped at protect, so nothing else is honest there. `shoulder`/`workaround` still allows 3 pressing movements on a push day (RPE is capped, the movement *count* is not) — candidate follow-up: draw one chest accessory from retraction work instead. The ungated slot guard's effect on **healthy** push days (bench + 2 press accessories + a 4th press → 4th becomes a lateral raise) is arguably better programming and is deliberately **not** shipped, because it rewrites live programs.
- **Loaded-ness is re-derived from the exercise NAME, three times over (V101, V103, V107).** Every instance is the same root cause: the pool knows whether a movement is loaded, the name does not, and the name is all that reaches `buildExItem`. `REP_BUT_LOADED` is the third patch on this seam. **V113 shrank it to two entries** by making the library name its implements — `Dumbbell Bulgarian split squat` is caught by `anyEquip` directly, so it no longer needs hand-asserting — and promoted `BW_QUALIFIER` from a one-caller helper into a real veto inside `isTrackableWeight`. Both are the seam getting narrower, not closed. **The real fix is to tag the item at pick time** — carry a `loaded` flag on the item object from the pool that produced it, and have `buildExItem` read the flag instead of re-guessing from the string. Blast radius: `pick()`, the `_bw`/`_gear` wrappers and every hardcoded pool literal would need to emit the flag, and `isTrackableWeight` would keep the string heuristic only as a fallback for swapped-in and legacy names. Worth doing before the exercise library grows again.

- **`_repFloor` is the FOURTH name-derived loaded-ness check (V110).** Deliberate, not lazy: the other three ask "does this take load, for the log surface?"; this asks "can load be added fast enough to track a descending wave?" A banded hip thrust answers **yes** to the first and **no** to this one, so they cannot share an implementation. When the pick-time `loaded` tag lands, `_repFloor` keeps its windows and drops its regexes — the table is the durable part, the string matching is not.
- **Cable/machine isolation on a main slot** is now backstopped to an `8–12` window rather than the barbell wave. It reaches a main slot today by no rule other than nobody having written one; the real fix is still the `_pattern` iso-coverage gap noted below.
- **Regional caps:** confirm the narrow footprint (effectively balanced+run push/leg days) shouldn't reach further (e.g. strength+run); iso-coverage `_pattern` blind spot (cable fly / pec deck / loaded hip thrust read).
- **Constraint editing (parked):** training days, equipment, injury modality swap, adding a race date. **Goal changes stay a new program** — program length is goal-derived, so trained weeks aren't the right base, and mid-block goal-hopping is the most common reason recreational athletes don't progress.
- **Injury UI is its own entry point, not a tab.** "Something changed?" was retired: the action-row button is now **"Traveling?"** (backpack icon, travel-only sheet) and injury lives on a **HURT? button beside WEEK N** (custom `bandage` path in `ASY_ICON_PATHS`). The button doubles as the state readout — muted ink + "HURT?" when healthy, `--red` + region name when live — and reopening it prefills the current region/tier for management. Tab row and the parked "Less time" tab were removed entirely (CSS swept). Injury week wash is scoped to `.is-injury-week` on the week screen: 3px top rail, tinted topbar/subrow, red left border on day cards; the phase pill is **hollow** (outlined `--red`, filled text) precisely because the wash already carries the flag.
- **Remaining verbs (also unbuilt):** **Reduce** (time crunch — should reuse `capRegionalFatigue`'s existing rank order as a more aggressive cap rather than new priority logic) and **Suspend** (pauses the clock; **store a pause offset, never shift `startDate`** — it is user-visible and drives every rendered day date; needs `effectiveWeekMonday(prog)` read by the current-week calc, day dates and the freeze, plus an explicit paused UI state since the app would otherwise point at a week with no "today").
- **JSON export / import (full backup)** — independent value, unrelated to engine upgrades: **deleting the iOS home-screen icon tears down the PWA storage container and wipes every `ia_*` key.** A copy-to-clipboard dump of `ia_programs` + all per-program stores is real insurance; import would also enable device-to-device transfer (nothing syncs — see §4).
