- **Training-day floor is 3, absolute (V98).** `getMinTrainDays()` previously fell back to 2 when no cardio was selected — a gap, not a decision: every cardio goal in the table already floored at 3+, so 2 was reachable only on the lifting-only path. Three is the first number where the engine can hand out distinct day roles; at two you get the same session twice, `capRegionalFatigue` has nowhere to move volume, and there is no spacing decision left. Enforced at the **wizard gate only** — `buildProgram` still builds 2-day configs so any pre-existing saved program keeps loading. Rejecting them in the engine would enforce nothing new (the wizard is the only place programs are created) and would destroy history.
- **Deferred features:** live session player + GPS run tracking (Option B) — **per-set logging is now BUILT (V101, §8);** swipe-to-dismiss gesture; wildcard deck/collection view + contextual surfacing; rower/echo-bike as true cardio modalities; true off-the-couch beginner tier (the placeholder copy on the Beginner card was removed); journal collapse-state persistence; main-first section sort (order is now true program order); **animated SportLoader** run/bike/swim figures (exist in the Design System project — not yet wired into the app).
- **Doctrine/tuning calls for Mario:** deload doctrine (SEAL 7-wk vs every-4th); Strength-Support + zero-cardio `denseHypertrophy` edge; equipment coarseness on the leg-day barbell tier (GHR without a GHD); **run card renders above the main lift** in the day view — if trained in listed order that's a run before a heavy squat, backwards for a strength-priority day (display order vs. prescribed order); **wall sit supersetted with walking lunge** is doubled quad work (defensible as patellar-tendon insurance for a runner, questionable as a pairing).
  **The standing lesson:** in both guards the tier test is only a SECONDARY net — section label already protects main work — so its sole job is "is this a genuinely heavy loaded compound sitting in an accessory section". Since the question is about load, the tier must measure load. **Fixed by promoting the load-aware check into `_compoundTier` itself** (loaded-marker test runs FIRST so `Weighted chinups`/`Trap bar deadlift` correctly stay 3), and deleting the duplicate `_swapTier` that V102 had introduced as a local workaround — one classifier, one truth. **Provably inert: 90 configs x 6 weeks byte-identical output** (`difftier.js` regression proof, baseline derived by stripping the correction lines so the two variants cannot drift into being identical — they did once, and the test silently passed). Fixed while latent because the unbuilt **Reduce / "less time"** verb is specced to reuse this rank order as a *more aggressive* cap, which is the point it would have gone live.
  - **`Overhead press` / `Push press` -> `home_basic` + `minimal`** (neither has a barbell): **384 of 480 sampled programs, ~3,200 occurrences.** Someone in a hotel room with two dumbbells was being prescribed a barbell push press. `home_basic` is a **wizard choice**, so this was never travel-only.
  - **`Cable pushdown` -> `minimal`, `home_basic`, `home_full`**; **`Cable pull-through` -> `home_full`** (has a barbell, no cables).
  - **`Barbell curl` / `Cable curl`** reachable on those tiers, never actually selected in sampling — latent.
  Same class as the no-load leak fixed in V97-98; nothing covered these tiers because none of them is `isBW`. **Found by accident**: the V102 swap sheet harvests its candidates from the engine's own pools, so the travel-week verification surfaced `Overhead press` being *offered* on a mini-gym day — the universe harvest doubles as an X-ray on pool legality, which is worth remembering as a debugging tool.
  **Fix:** one reusable `_gearOK` / `_gear(pool)` gate defined beside `hasBarbell`/`hasCables`, applied to `EXLIB.shoulder`, `.biceps`, `.triceps`, `.shoulder_iso`, `.leg_iso`, `.calves` and the two literal `Cable pull-through` branches in `hipExtPool`/`squatPool`. Includes names that imply an implement **without naming it** — `Rope tricep extension` and `Face pull` are cable movements, `Seated calf raise` is a machine. **`_gear` has a non-empty fallback and that is load-bearing:** `EXLIB.leg_iso` is four machine movements, so filtering it on a cable-less tier would empty the pool and starve a `pick(...,2)`.
  **Behaviour change to know about:** `home_full` output **does change** — it loses `Cable pushdown` and `Cable pull-through`. That is the point (home_full has a barbell, not cables), but it is a real change on a loaded tier, unlike the `_compoundTier` fix. `commercial` is unchanged by construction (`hasBarbell && hasCables` -> the gate is a no-op).
  **KNOWN GAP, deliberately not fixed:** `EXLIB.leg_iso` (`Leg extension`, `Lying leg curl`, `Seated leg curl`, `Leg press`) is **entirely machine work** and reached ungated at its one call site. On a cable-less tier the fallback passes it through untouched. It was never observed prescribed on those tiers in sampling, so it is latent — but the real fix needs free-weight substitutes, and swapping an isolation for a compound changes the character of the day. **That is a coaching call for Mario, not a mechanical one.** *(V114 re-measured it: after the `\bmachine\b` gate, `Leg press` via this fallback is the **only** remaining illegal-gear prescription in the sweep — 32 of 32. It is now the whole residual, not part of it.)* *(V113 closed the sibling case: `EXLIB.calves` was two entries, of which `Seated calf raise` is machine-gated, so five of six tiers ran gastroc-only forever off a `pick(...,1)`. Fixed with `Dumbbell seated calf raise` — bent knee, no machine — and a `_bw()` divert for Room Only. The gate token was tightened `seated calf raise` → `machine seated calf raise` because the loose form ate the new entry by substring.)*
- **`EXLIB.biceps` is one shallower on every non-commercial tier (V113).** `preacher` was added to the `!hasCables` clause because a preacher curl needs a bench only commercial owns. Pool goes 6→4 (crossfit/home_full) and 4→3 (home_basic/minimal/bodyweight) against a `pick(...,2)`; not starved, but note that **`bodyweight` still keeps three dumbbell curls** — the same ungated-DB-name gap that forced the calf pool to divert through `_bw()` rather than be filtered. `_gearOK` blocks by barbell/cable token; **it has never had a dumbbell clause.**
- **Cosmetic, parked (V100):** `run_base` LSD steps back on cutback weeks without a Cutback tag in the copy (the distance builder deloads silently); tag it when touching that builder.
- **POWER + AGE — RULED (V144). Four calls, all Mario's.** **(D4-i)** Age scales the power BUDGET directly (0.92 / 0.82); 18–35 land on V130's measured 6/7/9 and the beginner double-discount is gone. **(D4-ii)** Age caps impact COST via `_POWER_AGE_MAX_COST` at `min(experience, age)`, applied at pool legality and both finisher gates. **The reasoning, which is the durable part: power is the first quality aging steals — rate of force development declines faster than strength — so masters athletes need power work MORE, not less. What age legitimately changes is tissue tolerance for impact: tendons and landings, not set counts of crisp triples with full rest.** Before V144 the engine had zero age gating on impact (a 55-year-old advanced athlete was legal for cost-2.0 depth jumps) while over-guarding volume, which is exactly backwards. **(D4-iii)** The 36–54 accessory 3-set cap does NOT apply to power — stated as a ruling so it is doctrine rather than plumbing. **(Ruling 2)** The 0.5 cost tier gets its own copy line ("crisp and explosive, ~2 min rest"); **"max intent" is reserved for loaded triples** — 30 med-ball throws is sound ballistic volume, but the words and the work disagreed, and coach voice is doctrine.

- **MID-PROGRAM GOAL CHANGE — RULED (D5, V145).** **(D5-i)** Scope is cardio goals ONLY — not equipment, not train days, not experience. **(D5-ii)** Downgrades and laterals get a plain confirm; upgrades are allowed but gated behind a warning with inverted button emphasis — **allowed, never hard-blocked**. **(D5-iii)** An edit pencil per cardio row on the program detail card, reusing the onboarding goal-picker pattern. Two things the measure pass established and a future session should not re-derive: program length is already pinned into `cfg._raceDateCappedWeeks` at wizard creation, so a mid-program switch **can never move `totalWeeks`**; and because the engine arranges lift scheduling at the WEEK level, lift diffs legitimately appear on cardio-stable days inside cardio-changed weeks — **the assertable contract is zero diffs in weeks with no cardio change at all.** Note this rules narrowly: **goal changes otherwise stay a new program** (§12), because program length is goal-derived.

- **EXERCISE-NAME SCRUB — RULED (V151).** MERGED, because they are the same movement with the same implement and were fragmenting one history: flat press / bench press, conventional deadlift / deadlift, close-grip bench / …press, ab wheel / …rollouts, plank / plank hold, hip 90/90 / …stretch, and the circuit-prefixed `RAND_POOLS` names ("Round 1: …", "Min 2,5,8: …", "40 pushups") which now log to their base movement while the CARD keeps its prefix. KEPT SEPARATE, deliberately: **implement splits** (barbell / DB / KB / cable / machine) per V113 doctrine — the whole point of that scrub was that the athlete picks up a different implement; **weighted vs bodyweight** (chinups, dips); travel variants (chair / table / bed); pause-duration and tempo variants; and **either/or circuit items, which must never be aliased because the store cannot know which movement was performed.** Display name and store key are different questions: the card may say "Round 1: Kettlebell swings", the key says kettlebell swing.

- **SCROLL POSITION ON RE-RENDER — RULED (V152, Mario’s report).** The session card holds its scroll position through every in-session edit; only a genuine arrival resets it. **Arrival is defined as: the sheet was not already open, or it is being pointed at a different day.** Skipping, unskipping, swapping, undoing a swap, adding a movement, logging a kettlebell size and accepting the coach nudge are all edits, not arrivals — the athlete is mid-card and the card must stay where they left it. The restored value is the raw offset, not an element anchor: an edit changes the card height by roughly one collapsed row and the browser clamps the write to the new max on its own, so a skip at the very bottom lands at the new bottom, which is the wanted behaviour. **A future “next day” swipe on the open sheet is already covered** by the day-inequality term. Left deliberately untouched: `closeDetail` → `renderWeekView` and the week page’s own window scroll (§12) — not reported, not fixed.

- **Dead code (harmless, sweep later):** `.opt-divider`/`.opt-tail` + `.rpe-key`/`.rpe-legend` CSS, `liftRpeKeyHTML()`, `roundWeight`, residual `benchRM`/`squatRM` plumbing; plus (V101) the old single-input log CSS `.ex-logrow`/`.ex-input`/`.ex-unit-inline`/`.ex-lastinline` and the `.hip-block`/`.hip-header` circuit box (all superseded by the per-set rows and unbundled hip cards), and `buildKBChips`/`selectKBByKey`/`stepExVal` (defined, never wired). Note (V101): `saveExWeight`'s signature dropped its old `isTime`/`isRep` args — anything a future session adds must call the 3-arg form.

---

- **Bike `int`/`chi` are unreachable at 2 bike days (V114, carried).** `getSessionTypes(2)` returns `['lsd_easy','lsd_long']`, so any two-day bike allocation is **all Zone 2, forever** — no threshold or interval work at all. V114 made those two days genuinely different in *duration*; it did not add *intensity*. Defensible for `bike_50`/`bike_century` (event-driven volume goals riding alongside lifting and running), **indefensible for `bike_ftp`**, whose entire premise is threshold work and which can land on 2 days whenever another sport shares the week. Fix is a goal-aware `getSessionTypes` — `isSpeedGoal`-equivalent for bike — mirroring the `protectInt` branch that already exists for run. **Coaching call outstanding on whether the long ride or the easy day yields the slot.**
- **`_gearOK` still has no dumbbell clause (V113, restated V114).** V114 added `\bmachine\b`; the barbell/cable/machine axes are now covered and the **dumbbell axis is not**. `bodyweight` and `minimal` tiers still reach dumbbell-named movements through any pool that does not divert via `_bw()`. Same root as the calf-pool workaround.

- **`spaceHardCardio` cannot always win, by construction (V115).** Residual adjacent hard days remain in **240 of 600** sampled configs, and they are **entirely** the one-rest-day patterns (`['sun']`, `['wed']` → 6 cardio days). With six training days and two sports there is no within-sport swap that separates every hard session; the honest answer is that the week is over-subscribed, not that the pass failed. Total collisions still fell 12,600 → 4,200. **Candidate follow-up: warn at config time when rest days < 2 and both sports carry hard sessions.**
- **NRC speed sessions legitimately exceed 8 reps (V115, not a bug).** `run_half`/`run_marathon` produce `20x0:30` and `10x2:00` from `buildNRCSession`, which is transcribed from Nike's plan and never calls `getINTReps`. NSW's 8-repeat ceiling governs **400m repeats**, not 30-second fartlek reps. Any future harness asserting the cap must exclude the NRC engine or it will report 72 false positives.

- **`_pattern('Ball slams')` returns null (V117).** `isTrackableWeight` says it is loaded, `_pattern` says it is nothing, so `deconflictAdjacentDupes` skips it and 239 of the 283 remaining adjacent-day repeats are ball slams. Same class as the V111 `_pattern` misroutes. Classifying it would also reach `capRegionalFatigue` and the swap sheet, so it is a coaching decision (which pattern does a med-ball slam belong to — `hinge`? a new `power` class?) and is logged rather than implemented.
- **`Slow goblet squat (KB)` sits in an "Explosive finisher" section (V117, observed not fixed).** Surfaced while classifying the 44 pool-exhausted survivors. A slow-tempo squat prescribed inside an explosive finisher block is a pre-existing pool-membership question, unrelated to V117's changes.
- **`deconflictAdjacentDupes` cascades (V117, accepted).** Substituting on day B can create a collision between B and C; the pass walks pairs forward so it heals its own downstream collisions, which is why 19 of the 1,073 renamed lines have no adjacent-day justification when measured against the OLD build. Net effect is monotonic — 0 configs regressed. Measuring cascade against the old build's day membership will always report phantom collateral; measure against the new build.
- **Adjacent-day spacing is scoped to LOADED movements (V117, Mario's call).** 421 unloaded adjacent repeats remain by design — pushup variants and plyos, a monotony complaint rather than a recovery hazard, and forcing swaps there exhausts the small pools on `minimal`/`home_basic`. Revisit if the monotony complaint ever comes from an athlete rather than a sweep.
- **`lsd_easy` is not labelled `— Taper` (V116, cosmetic).** The easy run's distance does dip inside the taper because the underlying array dips, but only `lsd_long` gets the suffix. Bike and swim easy sessions *are* labelled. Purely a copy inconsistency.

- **PER-SPORT EVENT ANCHOR (`cfg.eventSport`) — DESIGNED, NOT BUILT. Decided V116, deferred behind Capacitor.** The app has one `eventTargeted` boolean and one `raceDate` for a program that can carry three sports, so V116's taper applies at full depth to every sport. Logged here so the design is decided rather than re-derived.

  **The framing to reject first.** The obvious ask is "let a support sport keep progressing while the event sport tapers." **That is wrong and should not be built.** Fitness decays over weeks, fatigue over days; in the last 10 days one more long session buys ~zero fitness and costs real freshness, whether or not that sport is the one being tested. Everything tapers. The real variable is **how deep**, and it depends on whether the support sport shares a fatigue channel with the event — a distinction the engine already computes as `legLoad`.

  **Depth matrix (the coaching call, made):**
  | Event | Support | Depth | Rationale |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | run | bike | deep — current ×0.70→×0.50 | same legs, same glycogen; a 2 h ride two days out compromises a run test |
  | run | swim | shallow — ×0.90 flat | upper-body, low impact, near-zero systemic cost at Z2; keeps aerobic touch and routine |
  | bike | run | deep, arguably deeper | running is more muscle-damaging per minute than cycling |
  | swim | run or bike | moderate — ×0.75→×0.60 | legs are not the limiter but systemic fatigue still is |
  | — | same sport as event | full | unchanged |

  **Shape of the build.** ONE new field, `cfg.eventSport` (a single sport id or null) — **not** a per-sport boolean map. It answers "which one is the race"; every other behaviour derives. V116's `_tap` object already carries `{step, of}`, so it grows a `depth`, `_taperFor(w)` becomes `_taperFor(w, sport)`, and the three builders multiply by it. Small diff on top of what shipped.

  **Infer before asking.** Derive `eventSport` from which selected goals are event-shaped; ask only when two or more qualify. Single-sport users never see a new question. Note that the canonical repro config (`run_pace_goal` + `bike_50`) is exactly the ambiguous case — which is the right trigger.

  **Scope fence for v1: do NOT let `eventSport` drive program length.** `calcProgramLength` already takes `Math.max` of each sport's required runway, so **an implicit primary already exists — derived by longest-build, never declared, and nobody knows which sport won.** Making the event sport the length driver instead reshapes the pace progression, the wave, the deload cadence and the reach-dampener, i.e. every in-progress program. Taper depth is contained to two weeks of cardio; length is not. Two separate changes.

  **What a flag cannot fix.** An athlete with event-shaped goals on two sports has **two goals and one timeline**; one will be served worse no matter which is flagged. Correct response is a config-time warning ("your program will peak for the ride; your run target may land short"), not a feature. Cheap, honest, and more useful than silently picking. **Also not built — deferred with the flag at Mario's call so the whole topic returns as one piece.**

  **Priority rationale.** Current behaviour is the *conservative* error: tapering everything fully never produces a dangerous prescription, it costs a support sport roughly 3–4% of block volume. That sits below Capacitor, GPS, native haptics and the `buildExportHTML` stub, which are capability gaps rather than refinements. **Revisit trigger: the first genuinely multi-sport path.** If HYROX ships as its own path, or triathlon-shaped configs show up in real use, the ambiguity becomes constant instead of occasional and this stops being polish.

- **NRC SPEED-DAY LOG FORM STAYS GENERIC — RULED (post-V154, Mario's yes on both).** Reported as a suspected missed build: log form matches every run day except NRC speed days. Measure pass found the V148 boundary working as designed — `cardioFieldHTML`'s own comment names "complex NRC speed work" as a deliberate null-dose path. **The ruling: the generic form is CORRECT for that slot, not a tolerated fallback.** Reasoning: (1) the slot rotates flavors weekly by Nike's design (ladder/fartlek/hills/tempo — §5), so sessions share no longitudinal axis and structured capture produces data nothing can chart; (2) dosing the homogeneous subset (`reps_time` on 8x1:00-style rows) would make weekly mileage LESS consistent, because dosed interval forms store work-rep distance only while the generic form takes total watch distance; (3) Progress consumes exactly `run_dist` and `rpe` from run logs — a form change buys nothing downstream. Option C (inventing a fixed dimension for compound sessions) declined as contradicting both the transcription contract and the dose doctrine. **The real gap identified instead: `run_pace` is captured on every dosed session (derived pace written to the legacy key by `persistLogFields`) and read by NO chart — it dies in the journal.** That is the tracking win, scoped to §12.

- **NRC DAY PLACEMENT — RULED (V153, Mario's GO).** The NRC content tables are Nike verbatim and untouchable; **which weekday carries which slot is ours**, and the chooser optimizes it: minimize hard-run adjacency on the real circular calendar (rest days break pairs, sat→next-sun counts), **the long run is pinned to the last train day** — measured before the pin: a marathon-on-5-days config traded the Saturday long away to put speed after a rest day, the wrong trade, weekend long is convention — then prefer speed sessions riding a rest day, a recovery run padding the long, speed1 before speed2. **Scope is NRC goals with capped day counts only.** Non-NRC paths keep the NSW smart assignment + `spaceHardCardio`; widening the chooser would move day placement on every capped solo-sport config with no measured defect. The chooser subsumes but does not replace `spaceHardCardio` (multi-sport and non-NRC still need it).
- **DAY-BEFORE-HARD-RUN HINGE CLAMP — RULED (V153).** A lift day whose next calendar day carries legLoad cardio holds its **hinge pattern** at ~RPE 7. Hinge only, deliberately: the strain mechanism is velocity × fatigued hamstring — squat volume costs run *quality* but not tissue, and clamping it would gut leg days on every dense calendar. Same p≤0.61 mechanism as the injury cap and strength-support clamp; build-time, so it survives the week freeze. Accessory arm rewrites only the two known detail grammars, exact-form, never a broad regex (V119 lesson).
- **DECONFLICT NOTES HAVE THREE TIERS (V153).** Full-48h success / same-day-dodged-only / stuck. The old fallback branch emitted the success message while delivering only same-day avoidance — the note overclaimed exactly when the athlete most needed the caveat. Worst tier across pull+legs wins. Tier-2 and tier-3 copy states the hinge clamp so the promise matches the enforcement (the V142 rule: the popup promises only what the engine enforces).
- **INJURY PREVENTION FOCUS IS LABEL-ONLY — MEASURED, NOT YET RULED (V153 audit → F4 open).** `support_prevention` maps to internal goal `'endurance'` and has **no dedicated engine branch** — `support_strength` and `support_athletic` both do (measured 43/37 unique day-lines apart on identical config; prevention differs from athletic only by the goal template + labels, 18/20 lines, zero prevention-specific content). The card promises "stability, mobility, posterior chain" and delivers the generic endurance template. F4 design session queued: Mario's rulings needed on accessory set cap, RPE ceiling, circuit count. §12.

- **INJURY PREVENTION ENGINE BRANCH — RULED (V154, D1–D5, Mario's GO ×5).** D1: ~RPE 7 ceiling everywhere — armor is built submaximally; the three no-load main writers compose effort copy as literals and never read `wv.p`, so the clamp rides the beginner-ease seam (`_effEase`), the tested override. D2: accessories 2 sets, mains 3 — enforced by a post-pass keyed on the two accessory grammars, with mains excluded **by form** (the warmup/rest clause identifies the writer; the first gate run demoted bodyweight mains before this guard existed). D3: one legs circuit — merged [lunge, patellar isometric, single-leg hinge]; **the V123 runner pairing survives inside the merged round and is extended to non-runner prevention builds** (prevention is the last focus that should lose tendon insurance); the hip thrust is the drop (glute-max redundancy with the hinge; glute-med lives in the hip section). D4: calf/achilles guarantee on run-goal legs days, eccentric copy, `calf_iso` pattern so ankle plans govern it. D5: power pool filters to the swing (0.5 tier, V129/V143 no-landing doctrine); tiers with no kettlebell drop the section via a length gate at both push sites — max-intent barbell derivatives were landing on the LONG-RUN day of an injury-prevention build.
- **SUPERSET STATION RULE — RULED (V164, D1–D6 + D3b, Mario's concur on each).** D1: classes RACK (bar + plates territory), STACK (cables/pin-loaded), PORT (DB/KB/band/bodyweight); a superset may contain at most ONE fixed class — PORT pairs with anything, same-class fixed pairs are fine (Diaz's own accepted fix, good mornings + barbell hip thrust, is RACK+RACK legal). Reasoning: a superset's stimulus is density; creativity lives in pattern/muscle/rep-scheme, not implement — the filter encodes floor-coach craft ("stay at the rack"), and 5 of 9 pairing sites lose zero pair space. D2: enforce at draw time (V160 REP_AFFINITY precedent), never post-build lint. D3: an unpairable draw demotes to straight sets — never accept a bad pair to keep a header. D3b: one member-1 re-walk before demoting (a draw-order artifact, not a coaching problem, should not cost the athlete the superset; bounded to one level, deterministic array walk, no re-roll). D4: scope is 2-item accessory supersets only — circuits, curated/named workouts, conditioning finishers, and the bodyweight tier are exempt. D5: swap candidates for superset members are station-filtered against the remaining partner; curated already-cross-station pairs stay unfiltered (the filter protects legal pairs, it does not retrofit designed ones). D6: GHR, 45° back extension, and preacher curl form a FIXED dedicated-bench class — single-purpose fixtures ban the same as rack↔stack (this class caught both live defect shapes). Watch item accepted with eyes open: banned pairs always resolve toward portable partners, so superset membership drifts slightly DB/KB over long programs — logged in §12, not pre-engineered.

- **ROW-15 FALLBACK — RULED (V176, D31, Mario's concur after full explain).** Nike's chart, verified at the OCR source: row 15 (12:00 mile) prints half 14:05 SLOWER than marathon 13:45 — their 6:00:00 marathon floor's arithmetic, not a transcription error. V171 doctrine (never edit Nike's chart) holds, so the W5 three-rung progression would run backwards on that row, and row-15 athletes are exactly the clamped ones. Ruling: on any row where `half >= marathon`, drop the middle rung — `12:00 at Marathon Pace → 11:00 at 10K Pace`, 23:00 held, both paces Nike's. The 12/11 split (not 11/12) was chosen on effort-weighted average: both two-rung variants run slightly harder than the three-rung (the vanished rung was the easiest pace on this row), so the gentler split, with more time at the easier pace before the hard close — correct progression-tempo shape. Implemented as `detailIfHalfSlow` on the table entry, picked at build; fires nowhere else, dies on its own if Nike fixes the row. Gate asserts rows 1–14 three-rung monotonic and row 15 half-slow.
- **60-MINUTE RECOVERY BUMP — RULED (V176, D32, concur).** The hyphen fix makes `_cardioInterference` read `60-minute` — the W12 recovery run moves 0.55 → 0.70. Accepted as the fix working: an hour of easy running is real leg fatigue. The differential harness enumerates every move so nothing else rides in unnoticed.
- **D18 SCOPE — RULED (V176, D33, concur).** NRC long runs only, keyed `isNRC` + `Long Run*` subtype + `dose`. run_base's long slot and NSW `lsd_long` carry no dose today and stay under the interference model; extension is a watch item, not silent creep. The scope guard is itself unit-tested (a dosed non-NRC long run must pass through untouched).
- **RACE WEEK UNDER D18 — RULED (V176, D34, concur). SUPERSEDED ON RACE DAY BY V189 D38 (no lifting from two days out).** Race day is excluded from the tier pass on every row — the primer stayed until V189 stripped it. The dress rehearsal is FORCED tier A on every chart row (fast athletes finish it in under 75 minutes; you still don't lift after simulating the race). The taper long run takes normal tier math — no special case.
- **(V177 D-b) Carries are named by the JOB, not the implement.** `Farmer carry` / `Suitcase carry` / `Overhead carry`. A carry has no groove — the implement changes nothing the athlete does — so the modifier was over-specification that named gear they might not own and split one `ia_exw_` thread across two spellings that lift the same number. Contrast a press, where `Kettlebell single-arm press` and `Dumbbell single-arm press` genuinely differ (bell on the forearm, different groove, bottom-up available); that modifier stays. The one real KB/DB difference on a suitcase carry — the bell's handle sits above its centre of mass and wants to rotate, a dumbbell's is its centre of mass and doesn't — is a coaching cue, not a different exercise. **Consequence that must not be lost: `_auxGearOK` inferred gear FROM THE NAME and its fallthrough is `return true`,** so an implement-free name is bodyweight-legal everywhere; the `_AUX_GEAR` sidecar exists solely to answer for the three names that stopped naming their gear. It deliberately does NOT list `Trap bar farmer carry` — that name still declares its implement and the `/trap bar/` regex already answers, and a second guard over the same name would be untrippable (§10b).
- **(V177 D-c) Laterality is a rep scheme, not a movement.** `… left` / `… right` fold into the base name; the side lives in the detail. They were never separate exercises, they logged to their own `ia_exw_` keys, and `auxSwapCandidates` was offering them as SAME JOB substitutes for the movement they are. Two curated complexes held adjacent L/R rows that collapsed into literal duplicates on the rename and were merged into one `20 yards each` row — per-round volume unchanged.
- **(V177, Mario — REVERSED mid-session) Farmer, suitcase and overhead stay THREE movements.** Mario first ruled "just rename them to farmers carry, no suitcase carry, so we only have one thing to log and track," then reversed on the argument: farmer is two hands and the lateral moment cancels; suitcase is one hand and anti-lateral flexion is the entire point; overhead adds shoulder stability at half the load. Collapsing them puts three different demands behind one logged number — worse than three keys, not better. **The principle that separates this from D-b: collapse names when the logged number means the same thing, keep them apart when it doesn't.** Same test, opposite answers, and it is the test to reach for next time.
- **(V177 D-d) The side is derived from the movement, never hardcoded.** Both generated carry finishers wrote a fixed `3×40 yards` over a seeded pick from a pool holding two one-hand carries, so a suitcase or overhead carry arrived with no side instruction. `_carryRx(dist,name,qual)` reads `_CARRY_PER_SIDE` — keyed off the POOL, because the pool is the only thing that knows a carry is one-handed — and emits the curated library's house grammar (`3×40 yards each, heavy`). Farmer must never print `each`. The spelled-out unit stays `yards`: V163's `_addRxKind` fence depends on it defeating `parseRx`'s `yd` token.
- **(V177 D-e) `Trap bar farmer carry` keeps its implement in its name.** The trap bar IS a different carry — neutral handles, load around you rather than beside you, and it goes far heavier — so D-b's argument does not reach it. Stays swap-target-only (never generated), `_stationClass` RACK, so it is filtered out of supersets by `_ssLegal` as before.
- **(V177 D-f) The bodyweight carry pool was dead and is deleted.** `ex.carry` is read at exactly two sites and both exclude the tier (one on `isBW` directly, one through `_carryHardCardio`), so the `_bwFlat` bag-carry list was picked and thrown away on every bodyweight build. Removed under the V174 E10 rule rather than shipped as a branch that reads like a feature. Mario's ruling ("no bodyweight carry workouts, those are not user friendly") was already the shipped behavior — proven by a V176→V177 bodyweight differential, 432/432 byte-identical.
- **(V177 Q-A/Q-B, concurred) `Single-arm farmer's carry` and `Farmer carry relay` are SUITCASE carries.** Both are one-hand movements (`3×50 yards each hand` on the relay) that aliased into the two-hand farmer thread. Retargeted. Found by reading the alias table during the harvest, not by a gate.
- **(V176, Mario, standing process rule) Questions always come with recommendations.** "your questions should always come with your recommendations" — when Claude brings a decision to Mario, it brings a position: the recommendation, the reasoning, and what it trades away. Options without a stake in one of them is unfinished work.

### V178 — the Darren fold (Mario: "i like it lets go")
- **(V178 D1) Source-list movements go into the pool whose grammar already fits; nothing gets a new slot.** Landmine reverse lunge → `lunge` (name says landmine → RACK, barbell-gated by the pool's own `hasBarbell` branch). Dumbbell split-stance deadlift + Zercher single-leg deadlift → `hinge`. Standing cable hamstring curl → `leg_iso`, NOT hinge — `leg_iso` is `_gear`'d so the cable gate applies, hinge is not; `_pattern` gained `hamstring curl` → `leg_iso` because the `/curl/` catch-all would have read it as biceps. Hip airplane (light KB) + Seated banded hip flexion → `hip_stability` with fixed details. Weighted 90/90 hip switch → `hip_mobility`; the source's "with row" flourish was dropped because `row` would have scored it as pull volume. Dumbbell renegade rows → the **anti-rotation core pillar** (not `EXLIB.core`, which is dead). All prehab names are exempt in `_pattern` by an explicit rule, not by falling through. Ruled OUT: glider lateral lunge (sliders are not modeled equipment), the seated-on-floor overhead press (a gimmick, not a stimulus; seated DB press exists), the split-jump + cable anti-rotation press (a cross-station superset on a single rep — V164 doctrine).
- **(V178 D2) New power work is priced by the landing.** `depth fall` joins the 2.0 shock tier; `single-leg landing` is a new 1.5 line (a bilateral takeoff that lands on one leg is unilateral landing work); `pogo` is priced 1.0 — unpriced it would have been the swing latch again (cost 0 reads as "not power work" and walks through every gate). Pool additions: Pogo hops (all tiers), Rotational jump to single-leg landing (barbell + bodyweight tiers), Depth fall to single-leg bounds (barbell tier; advanced AND 18–35 by the existing min(exp, age) cap — no new gate). Ruled OUT: weighted depth fall with rebound — a loaded shock landing is not something a generator hands anyone.
- **(V178 D3) Foot & ankle is its own prehab pool, runner-only, on the hip rail.** Tibialis raise (wall lean) 2×20, Banded dorsiflexion 2×15 each, Single-leg bent-knee soleus raise 2×12 each slow, Standing ankle CARs 2×5 each direction. Two per leg day, seeded off the hip seed so the blocks never share a draw, cap-exempt via `hip:true`. Coaching ground: the calves pool trains plantar flexion under load and nothing else; for a half-marathon athlete the soleus is what fails at mile ten. An ankle-region injury plan owns the joint through the rehab section, so the prehab block steps aside.
- **(V178 D4) Skill lifts carry an experience floor.** `EX_MIN_EXP={'Zercher single-leg deadlift':'advanced'}`. Front-loaded in the elbow crooks on one leg is a positional skill, not a caption to follow. Same sidecar shape as `REP_AFFINITY`; applied where the hinge pool is built so every consumer inherits it.
- **(V178 D5) Verify the source before it enters the universe.** "Russian flys" resolved to nothing — a Meta AI artifact, not a movement; rejected. "Standing clamshell" IS `Standing band hip abduction`; folded as a no-op. Standing rule: a name from a caption is a claim, not a movement, until it resolves to a known drill.
- **(V178 D6, concurred V179) The hot-tomorrow core branch rotates.** Was `P[pillar].items.slice(0,2)` — the first two members, every week, forever. On a half/marathon build every core day is a hot-tomorrow day, so the athlete saw Side plank + Pallof press for 14 weeks and the carries in that pillar were unreachable. Now `[rot(items,R), rot(items,R+1)]` deduped — the shape `dynamic_bracing` already used. PRIORITY guarantee unchanged: every member of both hot-tomorrow pillars is low-fatigue by construction. Reshuffles FUTURE core days on live programs (HALF MANNY included); trained days are frozen. Required a `_BW_SUBS` entry for `Landmine rotations` → `Standing torso rotations (slow)` — deliberately not the Dead bug the other two rotational members sub to, or a rotated pair could collapse to one item under the per-section dedup.

### V179 — the taper pierce (Mario: "Concur! Go!")
- **(V179 D7) Foot & ankle pierces the taper; hip stability does not.** The taper protects the athlete from fatigue and there is none in 2×20 tibialis raises or ankle CARs; what the block buys in weeks 13–14 is tissue readiness in the structures that fail late in a half. Hip stability carries real glute-med fatigue under bands and dropping it there was a deliberate call — untouched. Mechanism: the taper branch's early `return s` is preceded by the same runner/ankle-guarded push the non-taper path uses, restricted to `role==='legs'`. Gate shape: fa on all 14 weeks, hip absent at 13–14, `fa count == hip count + 2`, taper fa only on the Legs day.

### V180 — race-week mobility (Mario: "GO! Concur with new D8")
- **(V180 D8) Race week foot & ankle is dynamic mobility only.** Week 13 (T-14) keeps the full block — halving 2×20 tibialis raises would be theatre, not a taper. Race week the draw is replaced by a fixed pair: Standing ankle CARs 2×5 each direction + Toe yoga (splay + big-toe lifts) 2×10 each foot. Tibialis, dorsiflexion and soleus raises are loaded work on the wrong side of the tendon-stiffness line at T-5. The label carries the reason (`— mobility only`) so the block explains itself on the card; there is no notification layer and no need for one. Standing pushbacks recorded here so they are not re-litigated: (a) the taper is week-resolved by the founding invariant — no `days_until_race` counter; (b) `taper-safe` = `hip:true`, `no-plyometrics` = `_POWER_COST > 0`, intensity modifier = `taperFactor` — the sidecars ARE the tags, EXLIB stays flat; if per-name race-week classification is ever needed it is a `TAPER_CLASS` sidecar in the `REP_AFFINITY` shape; (c) the swap sheet cannot re-add plyos in the taper because no power section exists there to swap into.

## 12. Open / carried forward

- **The V190 gate set exists now, and here is what it does NOT cover.** `tests/gates/g190_rounds.js`
(12 families, 47 assertions), `tests/sabotage/v190.json` (8 mutations) and `tests/measure/v190_rounds.js`
were reconstructed and committed after the fact — the originals were never committed, so between V190
and that session nothing enforced D39. Proven: `PASS 47 FAIL 0` on V190 and an identical `PASS 9 FAIL 38`
on V187, V188 and V189, which is the isolation proof (three builds differing by D14a/D36/D37/D38, one
result). Blast radius V189→V190 is 28 hunks +146/−55, **28/28 classified**, and the handoff's "23
generator call sites" and "9 preset `rounds:null`" both verify exactly. **The holes, all real:**
(1) the **legacy and `rounds:null` tails of `_secRounds` are never exercised** — across 560,437 swept
superset sections, 0 carry `rounds:null`, 0 are legacy, 0 state a count in the label, so §5r's
"49,281/49,281 legacy sections recover their real number" and D39-ii's 9 preset blocks are untested by
any fresh-build sweep; they need sections restored from `ia_hist_` or a creation-day `prog.weeks`
snapshot, plus a sweep of the preset library. (2) The `_live.length===1` demote-to-straight-sets branch
(~`index.html:10450`) needs a synthetic skip to reach. (3) `parseRx`'s five callers, and specifically
the add-sheet day set-count that D39-v was written to protect, are unmeasured — the argument that
`item.detail` is untouched is sound but it is an argument, not a measurement. (4) `G11c` in the gate is
vacuous both ways (no baseline strips, no mutation reaches it) — the weakest row in the file, sharpen
or delete it. (5) The lattice varies `restDays` only; no 3-day or 6-day `days`, no injury or travel
overlay, no age bracket but 18-35, no multi-sport `cardioTypes`.
- **V190 watch items.** (1) **The budget and the dose have to agree, and only one invariant now enforces it.** G12 asserts no member is charged above its section's `rounds`; nothing yet asserts the converse (a member charged BELOW the section, which would under-spend the budget). Not observed — add the assertion if a pass is ever written that lowers a member without lowering the section. (2) `recoveryDeload`'s power cut still uses the bare `^(\d+)×` and silently no-ops on the `N sets` grammar. Currently harmless — `_BW_KEEP_FIXED` exempts jump/bound/pogo so power items never reach the no-load rewrite — but that is a coincidence of two unrelated regexes, not a guarantee. Route it through `_itemSets` next time the power block is touched. (3) The `hsets` sites (Delts, Triceps, Biceps, Leg isolation) are `advanced?4:3` and ignore `volMod` entirely, while every `vsets` site applies experience × age × support modifiers. Both members of each `hsets` section share the expression so nothing desyncs, but a 55+ athlete gets an age-adjusted `vsets` block next to an un-adjusted `hsets` block on the same card. Not ruled. (4) `'Leg circuit — runner armor'` is authored with a literal `'2×10 each'` and now a literal `rounds:2`; it is the one generator site that bypasses `vsets`. Intentional as far as anyone knows, but it will not track a future volume-modifier change.
- **V190 blast-radius classification (retain until the next prevention build).** 1,080 hunks / 756 configs / 36 differing, all `support_prevention`, three classes one root: **1,075** D2 normalisation (`Ball slams 3×15 → 2×15` — the ballistic members the old per-item regexes could not reach); **4** section-count and **1** item-count, both from `capSessionBudget` totalling `_setCount(it.detail)` — V189 charged the budget 3 sets for a member D2 had refused to prescribe above 2, and paid for it by evicting `Terminal knee extension (band)`, leaving `Chest + knee` a singleton and an orphaned empty-label section behind it. V190 keeps the prehab. Coaching-correct in both directions; the invariant is now G12.

- **V189 watch items.** (1) A Monday race clears Nike's whole race week (the pin rests every train day after Monday, and the sharpener / 25:00 / shakeout all fall after it); the taper week's 7-mile lands two days out under the D38 strip. Pre-existing from D14a §5, surfaced by the D37 oracle; ruling needed on whether a Monday race borrows the prior week's Sat/Sun. (2) Race-week type dealing: the V153 chooser can still put the race-week speed session last; D37 trades it back with a recovery run, but if race week holds NO recovery run before the race the tempo stays last, measured 0 such cases in 1,020 configs, watch. (3) `legRecoveryNote` on NRC is now one fixed sentence; the three-tier NSW notes never show on NRC programs. (4) Race-week Thu on MANNY carries a hinge primer three days out (2×12 RPE 5–6) because pull moved to Thu: accepted, not ruled; revisit if it reads wrong on device.

Nike writes distances for the whole chart; at the slow rows time balloons. Candidate rule: cap NRC long runs at 2:30 when the recovery-pace row is ≥13:00. Accepted as-is for MANNY (one confidence run).

- **V187 (was V177/…/V186) = D14a only — D14b dissolved at V186.** *(entry text below predates V186; read "5K/10K" as NRC tables now, and the 10K beginner→5K offer as D14a's feasibility check.)* **D14a + D14b (race alignment, split V174 session).** **D14a (NRC half/marathon):** `startDate = getWeekMonday(raceDate) − (tableLength−1) weeks` at creation; the wizard's name step shows the derived date READ-ONLY with the opening week named (mocked V174 as state C: "Mon Aug 31 — you open in week 5 … weeks 1–4 are behind you and are not counted as missed"); race day pins to its actual weekday and overrides the rest layout. **D14b (NSW 5K/10K):** length is `calcProgramLength`'s, so alignment means the computed length lands the race in the final week — a different mechanism; needs its own ruling on what happens when it can't. Weeks wholly before "today" on a back-dated program render as past and nothing counts them missed (checked V172: no adherence denominator exists). Mario's D23 sentence — "already past days are outside the block" — is the intended read for those weeks too; confirm at V175 design.

But an athlete who picks a FUTURE start (e.g. Friday) can still open week 1 and train Friday's session early; that completion lands on `w1_fri`, so the real Friday shows as done. Named, accepted, not fixed — the alternative is a new day state and a Progress ruling for every consumer.

- **The V156 seed inverts logged RECOVERY paces into a mile anchor — the wrong evidence (V172 finding).** Recovery paces are effort-capped, not fitness readouts: an athlete who runs easy days too hard is seeded a faster row and a harder program, the exact athlete Nike's "argue freely" copy is warning. A logged race, tempo or benchmark result is the right seed; the recovery inversion should be the fallback. Now that the row is visible the athlete can at least sanity-check it.

V172 states the clamp on the card; the wizard still accepts the entry. Wants a wizard-side range check (3:00–20:00) with copy, not a silent clamp.

Numbers in §11f. Superseded above 75 min by D18.

- **Swim `segments` grammar → runs (structural, parked).** Would replace the `\n` strings with `{label,text}` rows and a work-total footer like V141's swim card. Blast radius: the duration estimator, `_cardioInterference` (both parse `detail`), and the compact renderers that read line 1.
- **[CLOSED in V169] Progress-chart / program DELETE is unbuilt (Mario, V168 session).** Users who start a program by accident cannot purge it — the Programs screen offers ARCHIVE only, and V156's "Delete Forever" tier fires `purgeProgData` but is not reachable from the progress surface. **This now interacts with V168 D3:** deleting a program removes the source of every cross-program pill that was reading it, so a purge silently blanks loads in OTHER programs. Needs rulings before build — at minimum: does delete purge `ia_exw_` or preserve lift history detached from the program; is the archived-vs-deleted distinction still the right primary; and what confirmation tier a destructive purge earns. Designed, not scoped.
- **The `halfstep` injury cap appends `— hold RPE 7, two in the tank` to any detail with no RPE in it (V170 measurement).** Power prescriptions carry no RPE by design, so they always receive it, now reading `4×10 — fast and crisp, 60s rest — hold RPE 7, two in the tank`. Pre-existing and identical in V169 (576 occurrences in both), so it is out of scope for the V170 ruling — but the grammar is doubled-up and an RPE instruction on explosive work is arguably a category error of the same family V143 caught with thrusters. Needs its own ruling, not a patch.
- **V168 leaves `weekLabel` same-program only.** A cross-program pill has no `W`-number shown, by construction — a foreign program's week number is meaningless in this block. If a future surface wants provenance beyond the program name, it needs its own ruling.
- **~24% of loaded accessories carry no rep range (found V167, Mario wants to circle back).** Measured over 1,080 builds / 193,470 accessory rows: 49.5% carry a V137 double-progression range, 10.4% are timed or holds (correctly none), 16.3% are the open-set bodyweight form (correctly none per V137 Flag-2), and **23.9% are still plain fixed-rep forms the V137 sweep did not rewrite**. Of double-progression-ELIGIBLE rows, only 67.5% carry a range. V137's stated intent was that every loaded accessory becomes a range prescription, so either the sweep has exclusions not yet identified or it is under-covering. The Range Ladder degrades cleanly to a plain row for these, so it is not a blocker — but the sweep is worth its own measured pass. Start by grouping the 23.9% by grammar shape and checking each against the sweep's guard conditions.
- **V165 ledger internals are now orphaned (V167).** `ledgerRows`, `buildLedgerCard`, `_entryBestE1RM` and `_LEDGER_MAIN` are superseded by `ledgerModel`/`buildLedgerCards`. Note `gate.js` (the V165 suite) still asserts against them, so deleting them requires retiring those assertions in the same build. Same disposition as the other orphans — cleanup pass, not a drive-by.

- **The comp-lift RM chain is dead code (found V166, not removed).** `benchRM`/`squatRM`/`deadRM` → `hasBenchRM`/… → `_benchRM`/… are computed at ~6223–6230 and threaded into `scheme(rm,w,hasReal,exName)` at three call sites (6970/7015/7061), and `scheme()` ignores all three arguments by design — its own comment says they are "retained in the signature so existing call sites are untouched" because the app prescribes purely by RPE. Proven live: `cfg.bench=225` vs `cfg.bench=0` produces a byte-identical `prog.weeks`. Harmless but misleading, and it cost a wasted control in the V166 differential. Removing it touches `expDefaults`, three call sites and the `scheme` signature — its own small build, not a drive-by.
- **`buildClassBandsChart` is orphaned (V166).** One reference: its own definition. Superseded by `buildEasyZoneChart` with `buildEasyBandsFallback` as the fallback. Same disposition as `buildMultiDotChart` — left in place, delete on a cleanup pass.
- **`EASY_ZONE_MIN_RUNS = 4` is an unmeasured threshold (V166),** same class as `EASY_MIN_RUNS`. Judgement, not data.

- **`_derived` is a never-populated stub (found V165, not fixed).** `const _derived={}` is declared at ~line 5770 and read three times as `_derived.bench||cfg.bench` (and squat/dead) at ~6222–6224. Nothing ever writes to it, so the fallback always fires and the engine always anchors to intake/DEFAULT. Either wire it (derive a live 1RM from `ia_exw_` — the Ledger's `_entryBestE1RM` is the obvious donor) or delete it, but the current shape is a silent no-op that reads like a feature. Ruling needed before wiring: a live-derived 1RM feeding the wave changes prescriptions mid-block, which is a coaching decision, not a cleanup.
- **Population percentiles (V165 D10-C, parked).** Prerequisites recorded so they never need re-deriving: (1) a bodyweight field — every credible norm is a BW ratio, so this is not optional to the feature; (2) sex — norm tables are sex-specific and the split is large; (3) a defensible norms table embedded in the file, since the app is offline (the NSW and SEAL guides in the project are military pass/fail standards, NOT civilian percentiles, and will not serve); (4) note that `ageBracket` is three buckets where norm tables are decade-keyed, and that doctrine forbids a default true 1RM so any placement uses an Epley estimate against tables built from tested singles. Mario's framing: this lands with the native build, not before.
- **V165 softened V155 without a ruling — confirm or overturn.** "Same Effort, More Speed" is the recovery-pace chart with effort pinned, so shipping both would be two charts of one number. Implemented as a single slot: Same Effort takes it at `nRuns >= EASY_MIN_RUNS` (6) with `nWeeks >= 2`, otherwise the V155 chart still renders, otherwise an empty state naming what is missing. Nothing that shipped before is lost at any coverage level, but V155 is no longer unconditional and that was a ruled feature. Mario to confirm the supersede-with-fallback shape.
- **`buildMultiDotChart` is now orphaned (V165).** One reference in the file: its own definition. The big-3 top-weight chart it served was superseded by the Lift Ledger. Left in place deliberately — deleting is blast radius for zero user benefit, and a multi-series dot chart is plausibly wanted again. Delete on the next cleanup pass if still unused.
- **`EASY_MIN_RUNS = 6` is an unmeasured threshold (V165).** The coverage gate on Same Effort was set by judgement, not by data — real RPE coverage lives in device localStorage and is not reachable from a build session. If the module reads as perpetually empty on Mario's device, this number is suspect #1 before the module itself.

- **Portable-drift watch (V164).** The station filter resolves banned pairs toward DB/KB partners; over a 14-week program superset membership skews slightly portable. No measured harm; if "supersets feel same-y" ever gets reported, this is suspect #1. Pre-engineering (e.g., same-class-first walk order) is deliberately NOT built.

- **Spelled-out units defeat `parseRx` on the carry rows' OWN log forms (found V163, not fixed).** `3×40 yards heavy` (hybrid finisher, line ~7072) parses as 3 sets × 40 REPS because the unit token matches only `yd|sec|s`; the log form then prescribes 40 as the fixed rep dimension. Cosmetically survivable (the card copy says yards) but it is the same misparse V163 fenced out of dose inheritance. Fix candidates: widen the unit token to `yards?|meters?` (moves log-form shape on every curated `yards` detail — wants its own measured build), or normalize generator output to `yd`. Separate ruling.
- **Mario's device: the V162-era added `Barbell hip thrust` still carries `3×40 yards heavy`.** Never logged, so `ia_hist_` is clean. Action: remove (resnapshot scrubs it), then re-add AFTER V163 installs so the new matcher doses it.

- **REP_AFFINITY is a one-member table by measurement (V160).** Any future tempo/pause/positional entry added to a wave-driven compound pool MUST get a tag on entry — the harvest pattern is in the V160 digest. If a second member ever needs a different axis (e.g. a minimum-reps floor), extend the tag shape, don't fork a second map.
- **Rep-affinity coverage stops at the three wave-main draws (E1, deliberate).** Accessory slots with hardcoded rep text (e.g. the 3×12 light press) are outside the gate; none carries a tagged movement today. If an accessory pool ever gains one, the binding question reopens with the hardcoded scheme as the reference, not the wave.



- **F3 — direction-aware leg-lift buffer.** `_dist` in `deconflictLegLiftDays` is direction-blind: day-BEFORE a hard run harms the run, day-AFTER harms only the lift. A direction-aware score would prefer day-after placements and could relax tier-2 weeks further. Not built; F2's clamp covers the dangerous half.
- **Chooser scope widening (deliberate non-build):** non-NRC capped solo-sport day choice still uses the even-spread formula; no measured defect yet, and the NSW type tables self-space. Revisit only on a device report.

- **Dated bike/swim events get no race-day session (measured V134 audit).** A dated marathon's final week carries a RACE DAY entry; a dated century's carries none — the program tapers into nothing. Folds into the `cfg.eventSport` build below; logged here so the concrete symptom is on record.
- **`Lat pulldown` and `Neutral-grip pulldown` classify as `row`, not `vpull` (found V132, not fixed).** `_pattern` tests `/row|pulldown|straight-arm/` **before** `/chinup|pullup|lat pull/`, so every pulldown matches the row rule first. A lat pulldown is a vertical pull. Consequence is bookkeeping, not prescription: `capRegionalFatigue` and the `_pats` guard count a pulldown against horizontal pulling, which on `back_pull_joint` tiers (older hypertrophy, several injury branches) can let a day run two vertical pulls while believing it ran one of each. **Same class as the `Leg press`→`hpress` and `leg curl`→`bi_iso` misroutes fixed in V111 — a generic catch-all sitting above the specific rule.** Cheap to fix, but it moves `_pats`/cap arithmetic on live programs, so it wants its own measured build.
- **The bodyweight pull day is still three slots deep into one three-item furniture-row family (confirmed V132, pre-existing).** Main, superset A and superset B all resolve to inverted-row variants, `bodyweightSweep`'s exact-name dedupe strips the collisions, and superset B routinely ends up as the conditioning move alone. The file already admits this ("pulling is the known weak point of any equipment-free tier"). V132 deliberately did **not** widen the partition to families here, because the surviving pair are difficulty rungs rather than the same lift twice. **Real fix is a wider bodyweight pull family (a unilateral inverted row, a towel row), which is a library addition and a coaching call.**
- **No bilateral spine-neutral row exists in the library.** It is why `lowback/workaround` has no honest slot-A option and V132 drops the second row there instead. A chest-supported or seated cable row is the movement that branch actually wants. **Adding it pulls in `isTrackableWeight`, `_pattern`, `_gearOK` legality and `_REP_FLOOR`** — a movement addition, not a pool tweak.
- **Hybrid same-region adjacency after the V133 pierce — measured, logged, deliberately unrepaired.** 0.85/wk against 0.33/wk for the old full freeze on the observed drift model (§11e has the full table). Accepted because the frozen half of any collision is a day already trained. **Revisit only as its own build with its own gate; a spacing-repair pass does not belong inside the freeze.**
- **`prog.weeks` is still never persisted, so drift is unbounded and grows with every release.** V133 stops drift from reaching untrained days, but a *touched* day with no `ia_hist_` snapshot (trained pre-V104) still resolves to the creation-day build forever. That population only shrinks. **The real termination is engine pinning (still deferred) or persisting the refreshed weeks — the latter would need its own answer for what happens to the record when the engine changes.**
- **V131 DISPLACED THE PENCILLED-IN QUEUE.** V131 was scoped from a live user report (set edits not persisting), not from the queue. **Still parked, unstarted:** the `recencyLabel` calendar-midnight fix (it divides raw milliseconds, so "yesterday" flips at the wrong hour) and the completion-friction work (sessions finishing but staying marked open). Neither was touched.
- **`_headR`/`_headW` are the third and fourth arrays describing one set ladder** (`wkR`/`wkW` being the first two). They agree by construction today because they are derived in one place, but this is the same shape as the V112 collapsed-bar latch. **If a fifth appears, collapse them into one resolver instead of adding it.**
- **The Load field is empty on a lift with no history at all.** Previously it read a grey `0`, which was also meaningless but at least occupied the box. Watch whether a first-ever loaded session reads as broken rather than blank; if so the fix is a hint in the unit slot, NOT a number in the field.
- **`ia_logs_` day entries now carry per-exercise data.** Anything that iterates `ia_logs_` assuming a flat form-field shape (journal, weekly mileage aggregation, export) should be re-read before its next change — `sets` is additive and no current consumer reads it, but the assumption "this entry is the log form" is no longer true.

- **Full triathlon path + BRICK sessions — PARKED behind the HYROX architecture ruling (V141 session, Mario concurred).** Both ask the identical question at different scales: does the event path learn multi-modality week templates, or does a fourth spine exist? Tri needs a multi-sport weekly scheduler (2 swims + 2 runs + 2 bikes with cross-modality interference), a BRICK session type (bike→run back-to-back, race-pace + nutrition rehearsal), a 32-week phase engine on a race date (per-sport event anchor already deferred behind Capacitor), and it inverts the app's lifting-first identity. Decide ONCE, after Capacitor. BRICK alone could ship earlier as an optional hybrid-path session type if wanted — most distinctive artifact in the RG guide, useful beyond triathletes.
- **Missed-session gap-detection LOGIC deferred (V141 shipped the copy).** The real version — detect a >1-week `ia_hist_` gap and run a progressive re-entry (step the pointer back or reduce volume 1–2 weeks) — touches `refreshProgram` territory and belongs with the program-pinning decision already carried here. The V141 reminder-pop copy (gap≥7 re-entry guidance) is honest without it because it changes no volume.

- **`ia_moves_` still has no prune function (carried).** `ia_swaps_` is pruned once a week freezes; `ia_moves_` is the second re-apply store and has never had the equivalent. **Model the prune on `pruneSwaps` before the rest-day move feature ships** — an unbounded re-apply store replaying against a rebuilt program is the `ia_hist_` bug's shape.
- **`EXLIB.leg_iso` free-weight substitutes — still a coaching call, not a mechanical one (carried from V114).** The pool is four machine movements reached through `_gear`'s non-empty fallback, so on a cable-less tier it passes through ungated. It is the whole illegal-gear residual. Swapping an isolation for a compound changes the character of the day, so this needs Mario's ruling, not a filter.
- **Capacitor migration (native iOS shell) — prerequisite, unstarted.** Gates GPS run recording (`@capgo/background-geolocation` or Transistorsoft + Mapbox GL JS), push notifications, and the per-sport event anchor already deferred behind it. Capacitor Filesystem + Preferences would replace localStorage on native builds — note that the export/import item below is partly insurance against exactly the storage teardown Capacitor would end.
- **HYROX path — parked, and it owns an architecture ruling.** Needs SkiErg/Row decisions and a fourth cardio modality. It asks the same question as the triathlon path below at a smaller scale: does the event path learn multi-modality week templates, or does a fourth spine exist? **Decide once, for both.**
- **Native feel hardening (Option A+B, post-wrap):** haptics via Capacitor, CSS/scroll polish. Explicitly queued behind the wrap, not before it.
- **The week page’s own scroll across `closeDetail` — flagged V152, NOT fixed.** `closeDetail` removes `.open` and calls `renderWeekView()`, which does `daysList.innerHTML=''` and rebuilds the strip, hero and stat tiles with nothing capturing `window.scrollY` across the teardown. V146’s own comment already documents this class on the week strip (“`wscroll.innerHTML=''` collapses the scroll and the browser clamps to 0”). **Mario was offered this alongside the V152 fix and reported only the in-session symptom, so it was left alone rather than fixed speculatively.** If it ever surfaces, the shape is the same ruling as §11f’s: bracket the rebuild with a capture/restore. Note the single-task innerHTML swap may not clamp on every engine — **confirm the symptom on device before building**, because there is no forced layout between the clear and the refill and this may be a non-bug.

- **Swap-preference carryover at build time (V156 D7 — skipped, half-designed).** Repeated swaps in a prior block surfaced as accept/decline suggestions in the wizard. Needs Mario's ruling on coaching legitimacy plus a "repeated" threshold (proposed ≥3 distinct weeks) before it earns a build.
- **`openProg` has no archived guard (V156, deliberate).** No UI path reaches an archived program's Open button (the home list filters them; Progress offers only Restore), so the guard was left unwritten. If a future surface exposes `openProg` by id, add the guard then.
- **The wizard seed reads `run_dist` for baseline without a snapshot check (V156, deliberate).** Any logged run distance in the window is capability evidence, swapped or not — looser than the pace predicate on purpose. If it ever misleads, tighten to prescription-run entries only.
- **The swap record can miss a retired spelling (V151, self-healing).** A recorded swap whose `from` name was retired in the V151 scrub will not re-apply on rebuilt future days, because the name it keys on no longer appears in the pool. Frozen days keep the swap via `ia_hist_`; unfrozen days simply show the engine's pick and the athlete can re-swap. Not worth a migration — but any FUTURE rename must consider `ia_swaps_`/`ia_swapct_`, which key on name and are **not** covered by `EX_KEY_ALIAS`.

## Recent sessions (digest)

- **V136 — the swim "ceiling" that was actually a decaying sawtooth.** The V134 audit logged swim_mile peaking at 932 yd against a 1,650 yd event and framed it as a long-session ceiling in the V114 class. The measure pass found the real mechanism: `lsdYards`' cutback week wrote `cur` from `last`, but the next build week grew from `cur` (the cutback value) and clobbered `last` with it — the pre-cutback peak was permanently lost, every 4-week cycle topped out ~7% lower (V135 mile cycles: **932 → 868 → 810 → 755**), and a 16-week tri advanced athlete's longest swim of the final month (1,078) was shorter than week 3 (1,331). The designed `lsdMax` caps (3,500 mile / 3,000 tri) were always sufficient — unreachable, so never exercised. The bike engine resumes from peak correctly (54 → 38 cutback → 66); swim alone latched. **Fix: one token, `cur*1.10` → `last*1.10`.** The judgment call flagged going in (ramp-rate cap vs full target distance) dissolved: the existing 10% build rate + w%4 cutback clock + designed caps produce doctrine-correct peaks on their own — mile intermediate hits the event distance (in line with the app's own "build to 3,500–4,000" LSD note), mile beginner peaks at 72% of event (ramp-limited, inside the 70–80% band), tri intermediate 2,514 vs a 2,078 max event. Gate **6/6 on V136, 4/6 control on V135** failing exactly A1 (peak 932) and A2 (cycle decay 932→868→810→755); **integrity by re-latching the single token — A1/A2 relight alone.** A6 pins run and bike builds byte-identical via recorded V135 hashes. **Blast radius: 480-config structured diff — 9,472 changed lines, 100% swim LSD lines at week ≥ 5, zero outside** (weeks 1–4 byte-identical by construction; recovery-mode swims frozen at 600 yd; CHI/INT untouched). Fuzz: 300 configs (138 swim), 0 errors, 0 cap breaches, 0 build-week decay — after the fuzz's own decay invariant was corrected: the first version grouped fixed 4-week windows and read a lone trailing cutback week as decay, a dishonest-gate near-miss flagged and rebuilt per standing practice; lesson promoted to §10b. §10b/§12.

- **V135 — the Tier-2 batch, and the defect that turned out to be Nike.** Four of the five queued items shipped; the headline item died in the measure pass: the "taper inversion" (80-minute recovery run in marathon taper week 2) was checked against the source PDF and **Nike prescribes it verbatim** ("03 WEEKS TO GO": 80:00 Recovery Run) — retracted, standing lesson promoted to §10b. The deeper read also reframed the power-ordering item: the non-CrossFit "Power" block was never power training — it is the conditioning pool at conditioning doses (3×15/3×10) after the strength work, and the lower variant already labels the identical construct "Explosive finisher" — so the fix was the honest relabel + optional flag, not a reorder; the true power-first day for body-path strength/hypertrophy stays in Tier 3. Shipped: (D3) pull superset B's conditioning move gated off internal goal strength/hypertrophy — `support_strength` maps to 'athletic' so every support path is untouched; the gated section drops to a plain "Row volume" or is omitted (one item under a superset heading is worse than no heading). (D2) the relabel. (D4) `warmupNote` on the three loaded mains — bodyweight mains excluded, no load to ramp. (D5) rest default line in the app-wide RPE card. **The find nobody queued: the day's item budget was being spent on the junk conditioning.** On gated pull days where the trimmer used to cut the Biceps block to fit jump squats, the freed slot now backfills direct arm work — 26 configs in the sweep gained or upgraded a curl the conditioning move had crowded out. Gate **6/6 on V135, 1/6 control on V134** (A1 87.8% strength conditioning rate, A2 21.1% hypertrophy, A4 90 bare Power labels, A5/A6 copy absent); A3 is the over-gating guard (fatloss 79% / support_athletic 44% keep their conditioning). **Integrity proven by breaking the build two ways in separate scopes** — reverting the superset gate relit A1+A2 only; reverting the relabel relit A4 alone. **Blast radius: 264-config structured diff, 15,240 changed lines, 100% classified into the four expected buckets** (D2 relabel 4,392 · D3 gate 972 · D3b biceps backfill 156 on 26 configs · D4 warmup clause 9,720), zero unclassified. 300-config fuzz: 0 errors, 0 invariant violations, 0 conditioning leaks on gated pull days. §10b/§12.

- **V134 — the governor was deaf to every goal the wizard's own doctrine comment promised.** Found by an elite-coach audit of the full onboarding matrix (VM harness over the real `buildProgram`, 8 personas + 320-config sweep) rather than a device report. Both `_anyPerfCardio` bodies recognised only run speed/race goals and swim TIME goals, so `bike_century`, `bike_50`, `swim_tri`, `swim_mile` fell to base-cardio: **hybrid strength + bike_50 = 28 identical 20-minute recovery spins across 14 weeks**, for a goal `HYBRID_EXCLUDED_GOALS` explicitly keeps available on hybrid as a capability goal. Reachable surface is exactly the hybrid path (body skips the cardio step; event forces support focuses, which pass via `_perfLiftPaths`). Fixed with ONE shared predicate `isPerformanceCardioGoal` consumed by both call sites — deliberately NOT a widening of `isSpeedGoal` (the V115 warning stands: that predicate drives day allocation, interval protection and the pace chart). Taper still fires only on an explicit race date, which is what reconciles the hybrid no-taper doctrine with the fix: no-date capability builds ride the full progression un-tapered; dated builds taper cardio AND drop the bar to primer. `bike_ftp`/`bike_cals` stay governed on purpose (§12 — their fix is a session builder, not a pass through the gate). Gate **5/5 on V134, 3/5 control on V133** failing exactly the two reported behaviors (flat bike_50, missing taper); **integrity proven by reverting the predicate alone — A1/A2 relight, A3–A5 hold.** Window-global lint clean (475 declarations, 0 dupes). **Blast radius: 320-config sweep (path × focus × 10 goal combos × dated/undated × 2 seeds): 96 changed, 224 byte-identical, 0 outside the expected causality** (every changed config carries one of the four ids with a body lifting focus). 300-config fuzz: 0 build errors, 0 invariant violations. Post-fix behavior verified: hybrid bike_50 runs 3 bike days (easy LSD + CHI + long ride 45→150 min, the 70–80%-of-event-duration ceiling), cutbacks on cadence; swim_mile gets INT + CHI + progressing LSD. The audit's remaining findings are queued in §12 (Tier-2 mechanical, Tier-3 coaching-design), with the swim long-session ceiling (932 vs 1,650 yd) and the missing bike/swim race-day sessions logged as their own items. §12.

- **V133 — one log key rolled an untrained day back eighteen versions.** Reported from device six hours apart: week-6 Tuesday read `Push` + 72 min Long Ride at 20:03, a single exercise was logged inside week 6, and the next boot showed `Pull` + 38 min LSD. **Blamed on the V132 push; V132 was innocent** — proved by rebuilding Mario's exact cfg (seed 16718) against V132 and getting `Push` + 72 min, i.e. the current engine still agreed with the "before" screen. The cause was the freeze cut moving past week 6 and the freeze restoring `prog.weeks` **whole** — the creation-day build, from an engine predating V114. **The symptom nobody would have predicted:** because exercise selection is keyed on `seed + week + role` and not on weekday, the stale build's `pull` on Tuesday and the live build's `pull` on Monday served the *identical* session, so he got Sumo deadlift 4×3 @ RPE 9 back-to-back — the collision `deconflictSameRegion` exists to prevent, arriving through the guard that bypasses it. Fixed by making the freeze **per-day**: snapshot wins, touched-without-snapshot falls back to stored (except the injury carve-out), no-record-at-all rebuilds live, and only weeks at or after the current week pierce. Day-key union added so a train-day change stops dropping days out of frozen weeks. Gate **8/8 on V133, 5/8 on V132**, control failing on the reported bug, the duplicate-session symptom and the union. **Gate integrity proven by breaking the build two ways in separate scopes** — reverting the pierce relit A1+A8 only, reverting the union relit A7 only. **Blast radius: 0 / 14,336 days on programs built by the current engine (byte-identical), 439 / 14,336 (3.06%) on drifted programs, 100% of them untouched days at or after the current week.** 150-iteration fuzz, 0 errors, 0 invariant violations. **The hybrid adjacency cost was measured rather than patched (Mario's call): 0.85 collisions/wk against 0.33 for the old full freeze.** §6/§11e/§12.

- **V132 — two slots, one pool, and a prescription that promised a side.** Reported from device off a week-6 screenshot: *"don't KB rows and DB rows the same thing?"* They are — `_pattern` calls both `row`, and a Dumbbell row and a Kettlebell single-arm row are the same bench-supported single-arm pull with a different handle. The cause was that **both** row slots drew from one `_slot(rowPool,2,…)` while `buildSections` hardcoded their two different jobs into the details. **The bigger find was the one nobody reported:** `3×10 each` was hardcoded on slot B, so a `Barbell row` or `Pendlay row` landing there rendered as **"3 × 10 /side"** on **23.0%** of pull days. Fixed with `_isUnilateralRow` + a pool partition, slot B's `each` made conditional, and the `lowback/workaround` residual closed at its own override. Gate **5/5 on V132, 2/5 on V131**, plus a 15-name predicate check over every row name **harvested from the file**, a 270-config swap-universe assertion (0 narrowed), 1,200-config fuzz (0 build errors, 0 invariant violations), and `parseRx` pinned on the new grammar. **Gate integrity proven by breaking the build two ways in separate scopes:** reverting the partition alone relit A1 at 41.9%, which is what shows the assertion has teeth — and the same run showed A3 stays 0 with only the `each` fix in, so the two fixes are independent and A3 is now closed twice over. **Blast radius over 1,800 configs: 31,035 of 588,545 lines (5.27%), and 100% of them in `Pull superset A`, `Pull superset B`, and `Strength`** — the last being the full-body day's `fullPull`, which filters against `backRow` by design and rotates among the same three band movements. **No movement was added to the library and no detector changed.** This rewrites the pull day on every live program; the freeze protects trained weeks. §11d/§12.
- **V131 — an edit that vanished, and the one-line fix that would have corrupted the chart.** Reported plainly: change reps 8→10 with the steppers, leave the day, come back, it is 8 again. The cause was that set rows were pure DOM until `Log`. **Routing `stepSet` into `saveExWeight` would have fixed the symptom and broken three things** — the ✓ and `X / Y LOGGED` would fire on one tap of one set, and `saveExWeight`'s placeholder fallback would have silently written **last session's weight** as today's, into the store the progress chart reads. Split into drafts (`ia_logs_.sets`, raw strings, never `ia_exw_`) with **live-after-log** commits once the weight is confirmed. Mario then called the placeholder itself: an RPE prescription and a pre-filled load are contradictory instructions, and the pre-filled one wins by default — so `exw_`/`exsetw_` placeholders are gone and the ↺ pill is the only route to last week's number, at the cost of one tap on the commonest path. Gate **37/37 on V131, 25/37 on V130**, the control failing on the reported bug itself plus the silent-weight write (`F1`: logging an untouched card stored 185 from last week). Engine hash **byte-identical across 720 configs**; card markup across **470 exercises byte-identical after undoing exactly the onblur handlers and the two placeholders**; **400 fuzzed interaction sequences, 0 invariant violations.** Two writers now share the `ia_logs_` day entry and each carries the other's half — `persistLogFields` preserving `sets` is load-bearing, not defensive: without it the RPE slider wipes the day's drafts. Durable outcomes are folded into the sections above; this is orientation only.
- **V88** logging/display UX: dup-point fix, collapsible journal, cardio substitution, single app-wide RPE modal.
- **V90** icon system: Design-tool import, emoji-map corrections, hardcoded-literal sweep, toast → `innerHTML`.
- **V91** **week freeze** (§6) — the fix that had to land before shipping any engine change, since `refreshProgram` rebuilds every program on every boot. Carry de-bloat + cardio gate. Build version on the bugs screen and in bug reports.
- **V92** bodyweight tier built; two latent `_pattern` bugs fixed (§11).
- **V93** travel reframed as **maintenance, not progression** — the no-load wave was inverted (reps fell as RPE claimed to climb, so blocks got easier). Exotic movements cut. Copy rule established (§11b).
- **V94** no-load work switched to **open-ended sets-by-RPE** — reps belong to the athlete, not to an engine guess at their capacity; progression becomes emergent. `BW_REPS` deleted.
- **V95** three-way travel load model keyed off `_travel` (§6b); stale "and your bag" copy removed.
- **V96** Full gym travel made a no-op answer rather than an overlay (§6b).
- **V97–V98** Injury overlays **built** (engine axis + UI); entry points split into "Traveling?" and a HURT? shortcut; base-cardio circular-spacing bug fixed; **"Minimal / Travel" retired from the wizard in favour of a true "Bodyweight only" tier** (minimal kept in the engine so saved programs still load), which exposed and fixed a barbell leak in the no-load path; rest-day sessions built (move + cardio log); absolute training-day floor raised 2 -> 3.
- **V99–V100** Volume + recovery overhaul: legacy-seed fallback in `refreshProgram` (§4 — closed the trap where overlays silently never applied to pre-seed programs while the injury pill claimed otherwise); loud overlay-apply failure; recovery-week **volume** deload + power-at-60%-sets (§5); session-total set budget as 4th guard (§6 — closed the pattern-null blind spot); honest `sessionTimeEst`; cardio cutback aligned to the
- **V101** Logging overhaul: **per-set rep logging** (`parseRx`-driven stepper rows, one per prescribed set; §8), a single weight field + **reuse pill** replacing `last … Wx` with human recency (§8), inline-log **detection fixes** for 30 loadable movements behind a shared `EQUIP_TOKENS` map that auto-inherits parenthetical suffixes (§11), and a **hip-stability overhaul** (always-on not run-only, bundle broken into individual logged cards, pools split to guarantee 2 stability + 1 mobility per leg day; §6).
- **V102** **Exercise swap built** (§8b): session-scoped two-tier substitution, candidate legality harvested through a one-line hook in `pick()` rather than re-derived, travel-week universe splicing, prescription carried verbatim (pure-RPE dividend), injury filter governing both sheet and store, and a coach nudge at three consecutive swaps that writes `cfg.exSwapPrefs` so the permanent path needs no re-application pass. Tier 3 ("show everything else") cut on Mario's call. Exposed the `_compoundTier` misclassification in §12.
- **V103** `_compoundTier` made load-aware; duplicate `_swapTier` deleted (byte-identical across 90 configs — latent defect closed ahead of the Reduce verb). Then travel-week verification of the swap sheet **surfaced a live barbell leak**: accessory pools gated only on `isBW`, so `home_basic`/`minimal` were prescribing `Overhead press`/`Push press` in 384 of 480 sampled programs. Closed with a reusable `_gear()` gate across six accessory pools. `home_full` loses two cable movements as a result. `EXLIB.leg_iso` remains a documented gap. See §12.
