- **V104** **Day-history snapshots (`ia_hist_`)** — the week freeze was restoring `prog.weeks` (creation-day build, no overlays), so marking a day during an injury reverted it to the pre-injury prescription on next boot; caught live on Mario's own week 4. Day is now snapshotted as rendered at first touch, five hook points, first-touch-wins. Fixes travel weeks by the same mechanism. §4/§6/§11.
- **V105** **Collapsed set bar + per-set load.** Set rows fold to one line; per-set load cells for drop sets; `entry.weight` = `max(setsW)` so the top set drives progression while the ladder is preserved in the new `setsW`. Storage additive, no consumer touched, no migration. Verified: 33 save-path assertions (incl. 4000-config fuzz on `weight===max(setsW)`) + 61 render assertions across 8 exercise shapes. **A window-global lint was added to the gate** after `function top()` in a mockup killed the whole script — see §9. §4/§8.
- **V106** Top-set tag gated on divergence (see §8).
- **V108** **Loose `parseRx` grammar + unloaded divergence display.** Wildcard prescriptions 58 unloggable → 2; 130 more gained set rows.
- **V110 — movement rep floor + injury cap follow-through.** User-reported `4×3 Barbell hip thrust` on a lowback return week.
- **V111 — injury slot collapse sweep + protect-tier rehab day.** User-reported hip thrusts on Monday and Tuesday.
- **V112 — collapsed set-bar latch (recovered work).** Built once as a V110, lost to a version collision, re-derived against V111.
- **V113 — exercise-name implement scrub.** Reported as `Push press` not saying barbell, so the wrong load got logged.
- **V114 — the long ride, and a gate that only covered library pools.** Two findings from reading a single generated program (`Athletic` / `Athletic Support` / CrossFit / run pace goal + `bike_50` / 12 wk), which is worth noting as a method: **generate one program and read it end to end**; both bugs were visible in the output without any harness.
- **V115 — run doctrine pass.** Opened as a coaching question (why does a pace goal get no threshold work?) and closed as four defects.
- **V117 — day-scoped exercise log + adjacent-day exercise spacing.** Caught live: Mario opened an untrained Saturday and found two exercises already check-marked with set numbers filled in.
- **V116 — taper hoist + tempo anchor.** Both items were carried from V115's §12 as "recommended, not implemented", and both turned out to be one defect wearing two hats: a value computed in one builder and never handed to the others.
- **V118 — day-header add-exercise button relocated.** Cosmetic, requested from device: the `+ EXERCISE` button sat beside the `WEEK n · DAY` overline and read as floating chrome; it now rides the modality-chip row opposite the LIFT/BIKE chips.
- **V119 — swap prescription grammar (B1) + an incline that meant two things.** Two items from one question Mario asked off a screenshot: why can't I swap in normal pushups? Answer was that plain `Pushups` exists only in `RAND_POOLS`, the random-workout table, which never calls `pick()` and so can never enter `_swapUniverse` — not a filter, a library absence.
- **V120 — `parseRx` could not read the grammar the app already ships (B2a).** Found while scoping B2, and it was a hard prerequisite: B2c emits this grammar across 8,818 prescriptions, so shipping the sweep first would have multiplied the defect roughly twelvefold.
- **V121 — B2 complete: one prescription writer for unloadable movements, plus the trackability gap it depended on.** `unloadableRxSweep` called last in `buildProgram`, after every pass that can change a NAME (`bodyweightSweep` substitutes gear names, the injury filter swaps movements, the deconflicts move them between days) — sweeping earlier would judge a movement that is not the one the athlete sees.
- **V122 — five settled calls, and one that could not be shipped as written.** Cleared the backlog Mario had already decided: the four phantom load fields, `slam` on the medball token, the dead `computeStreak` body deleted, both tempo squats pulled out of the knee-stability role (every consumer of that pool prescribes it in SECONDS — 25s, 25s, 30s — so it is a timed-hold pool and a tempo squat cannot express it), and
- **V123 — three contained fixes, and an instrument that had been lying.** `Leg superset A` now pairs the lunge with the knee-stability hold only when the PROGRAM carries running (patellar-tendon insurance for a runner, redundant doubled quad work for everyone else); non-runners get two separate blocks.
- **V124 — a section label is a statement about item 0.** `capRegionalFatigue`'s `rank()` took only the section label, and `/^main|^primer|^power|^strength/` returned 0 for the WHOLE section.
- **V125 — one clock for every run.** The last item on the answered backlog and the largest-reach one.
- **V126 — two ids that meant one thing.** Opened as "extend `paceProgression` to `run_mile_time` and `run_15_under10`" and closed by deleting the question instead: both ids are legacy, unreachable from the wizard, and `run_pace_goal` already covers what they meant.
- **V127 — the dumbbell clause, and what it took to make it safe.** Second attempt at the V122 revert.
- **V128 — a filler movement papering over four slots.** Opened as "`Prone Y-T-W raises` returns `_pattern` null, classify it" and turned out not to be a classification problem at all.
- **V129 — the power class.** Seven decisions, one build.
- **V130 — the budget sets the dose, and the first version of it was fake.** The power block prescribed a hardcoded `5x3` and `4x5` regardless of who was training or what the movement cost — measured at roughly a third of the doctrine's allowance. `_powerDose(name, experience, repsPerSet, share)` now derives sets from the budget, so two inputs finally govern: experience raises the count and movement cost lowers it, meaning a 2.0-cost box jump automatically buys half the reps of a 1.0-cost jump squat. That IS the guide's volume-down-as-intensity-climbs progression, arriving as arithmetic. **The first cut was a hardcoded increase wearing derivation machinery.** With share 0.5 and a 3..8 clamp, EVERY athlete and EVERY movement came out at 8 sets — the clamp bound in all cases, so neither input changed anything. **The gate caught it on the one assertion easiest to leave out: "advanced gets more sets than beginner", which failed 8 -> 8.** Retuned to share 0.20 with a 3..10 ceiling so the arithmetic lands INSIDE the clamp: 3-rep lead movement gives **6 / 7 / 9 sets** by experience, and box jumps give **4** where jump squats give **9**. Gate **12/12 on V130, 0/1 on V129**. **STANDING LESSON: when a derived value is clamped, assert that the derivation still discriminates. A clamp that binds in every case turns a formula into a constant, and every other assertion will still pass.** Volume moved 21/30/43 to **25/37.5/50** median by experience, frequency unchanged at **never more than 2 power sessions a week**. Blast radius keyed by slot rather than line index: **547 detail-only changes, 4 slots removed, 0 added** — the removals are `capRegionalFatigue` correctly trimming two leg days that the extra power volume pushed over capacity. All ten prior gates pass.


- **V137–V140 — the Tier-3 slate, four builds.** V137: accessory rep-range grammar + hypertrophy accumulation wave + beginner effort floor. **Headline find: core/hip injection pushes SHARED item objects by reference and both sweeps mutated them in place**, so build N polluted build N+1 (multi-program / travel / `refreshProgram` reachable) — fixed with clone-on-write in `accessoryGrammarSweep` AND `bodyweightSweep`. V138: the loaded full-body day (`_loadedFull`) on strength/hypertrophy × commercial/home_full, with Mario's hybrid amendment — a day carrying the long cardio session runs a short skeleton, interference doctrine over blanket time. Slice partition proven: outside-slice 376/376 byte-identical. V139: Test Week / Peak Week on the TAG system (never title punctuation), rep-max protocol not a single, the e1RM calculator with the honest Epley edge case (reps≤1 returns the weight, no formula inflation), and the range-top double-progression nudge. V140: real cardio builders for the three goals the wizard promised and the engine never delivered — `bike_ftp` sweet-spot, `bike_cals` Steady Burn, `run_base` climbing ceiling. **Fuzz-caught defect: `dayIdx` at the bike call site is the GLOBAL cardio index**, so multi-sport `bike_ftp` got zero sweet-spot sessions; fixed with a per-sport ordinal passed as a trailing arg.

- **V141 — the RG Ironman harvest and the flatten-from-structure pattern.** Segmented swim grammar: `buildSwimSession` composes a `segments` array and `detail` **is its mechanical flatten in the same pass** — one source, drift structurally impossible, and flat consumers (injury sweeps, interference scorer, summaries) never learn V141 happened. Gated to the four swim performance goals only; support swimming stays flat NSW-style by doctrine. LSD conserves total yardage exactly. Missed-session doctrine shipped as copy, logic deferred. **Gate lesson promoted to §10b: the first break-the-build produced zero failures because recovery mode forces the flat branch upstream — a sabotage that fails to fail proves nothing until it provably reaches the assertion.** Full triathlon path + BRICK parked (§12).

- **V142 — the re-entry halfstep, and a second clock in a one-clock engine.** "I'm back" from an injury overlay now creates a **cardio-only halfstep week** for run-suppressed regions: quality runs return at full pace with reps ×0.55 (floor 2, reusing the taper's constants), lifts fully normal. Bridge week → halfstep → full send (**D1**). **D2b, the pace clock:** `paceProgression` reads shift by the number of quality-suppressed days (`round(days/7)`, floor 1, day-granular per D2b-i) so an athlete who lost three weeks to an injury does not return to a pace target the calendar says they earned while training; taper freezes at the last pace actually reached (D2b-ii); the INT dampener note quotes the shifted reachable pace and carries a PACE CLOCK line (D2b-iii). The popup promises only what the engine enforces. **Standing lesson: every `paceProgression` read goes through `_ppIdx`, never raw `wIdx`** — this added a second clock to the engine V125 unified, and a future consumer reading `pp[wIdx]` raw reintroduces the lie silently. Grep `_ppIdx` before adding any consumer. Gates: 504-cfg zero-overlay identity, travel-only identity, 531 assertions 0 fail, 4/4 reaching breaks.

- **V143 — the power cost table was lying about loaded movements.** Reported from Mario's own week-6 Saturday: **Barbell thrusters 10×3 and Dumbbell snatch 9×5**. `_POWER_COST` classified loaded weightlifting derivatives at **0.5 — the same tier as a clap pushup** — so the budget-filling dose math inflated set counts instead of reducing them. Derivatives → 1.0; **KB swing deliberately stays 0.5**, preserving the V129 ruling that a swing has no landing to absorb, so workaround athletes keep swings and nothing else loaded. Thrusters removed from the CrossFit power **pool** but retained in the cost **table** (finisher injury gating reads it). V130's display ceiling restored. Single-movement-pool guard at both call sites (workaround pools filter to KB swing alone and `pw[1]` rendered **undefined**). **Power sections added to the `deconflictAdjacentDupes` exemption** — dedupe renames bypassed `_powerAllowed` and were prescribing max-intent deadlifts to beginners and loaded hinges to workaround athletes: 145 of 1,782 power blocks. Gate 2,836 configs / 993,781 assertions / 0 fail, 5 reaching breaks. Result on the live seed: Dumbbell snatch 7×3 + Power clean 4×5.

- **V144 — the masters power dose was an accident of plumbing.** Age modifiers were applied in the DISPLAY layer (`vsets`) rather than the budget layer (`_powerDose`), which produced an **inversion**: 36–54 athletes hit a hard 3-set cap borrowed from the accessory rule while 55+ got the 0.82 multiplier and landed near 6 — a 60-year-old prescribed double the power volume of a 45-year-old, ruled by nobody. Four rulings, all Mario's: **(D4-i)** age scales the power budget directly (0.92 / 0.82), so 18–35 land on V130's measured 6/7/9 and the beginner double-discount is gone; **(D4-ii)** age caps impact COST via `_POWER_AGE_MAX_COST`, `min(experience, age)`, applied at pool legality and both finisher gates — **power is the first quality aging steals, so masters athletes need power work more, not less; what age changes is tissue tolerance for landings, not set counts**, and until this the engine had zero age gating on impact while over-guarding volume; **(D4-iii)** the 36–54 accessory 3-set cap explicitly does not apply to power, stated as doctrine so it stops being plumbing; **(Ruling 2)** the 0.5 tier gets its own copy line ("crisp and explosive"), reserving "max intent" for loaded triples — 30 med-ball throws is sound ballistic volume but the words and the work disagreed, and coach voice is doctrine. Gate 8,508 configs across three age brackets, ~3.16M assertions, 0 fail, 4 reaching breaks. **Standing lesson promoted: a dose assertion must recompute from an INDEPENDENT reference implementation, never the candidate's own function.**

- **V145 — mid-program cardio goal change (D5).** Mario's 50-mile bike goal was producing rides longer than he wanted and the only exit was a new program. Three rulings: **(D5-i)** scope is cardio goals only — not equipment, train days or experience; **(D5-ii)** downgrades and laterals get a plain confirm, upgrades are allowed but gated behind a warning with inverted button emphasis, never hard-blocked; **(D5-iii)** an edit pencil per cardio row on the program detail card, reusing the onboarding goal-picker pattern. Mock signed before any code. **The measure pass found a zero-code envelope preservation:** program length is already pinned into `cfg._raceDateCappedWeeks` at wizard creation, so a mid-program switch can never move `totalWeeks`. **The partition contract was measured, not assumed** — the engine arranges lift scheduling at the WEEK level (leg-load spacing, look-ahead coupling), so lift diffs legitimately appear on cardio-stable days inside cardio-changed weeks; the assertable contract is zero diffs in weeks with no cardio change at all. `_GOAL_RANK` is a strict per-sport order by engine volume ceiling; `_GOAL_REQ_INPUTS` gates commit on goals needing user-supplied targets. Gate: 1,908 switch cases, 10,384 assertions, 0 fail, 100% classified, 6 sabotages. **Harness lesson: a pipe swallowed node's exit code and falsely reported 0 — `set -eo pipefail` is a hard invariant for every gate run.**

- **V146–V147 — the week strip, and two consecutive gates that lied.** V146 shipped the scoot-to-current-week animation plus a unit fix (a hero preview read "80 mi" for a bike session — `/\s*mi/i` matched the "mi" inside "min" with no word boundary). **Mario rejected the animation on device immediately.** Diagnosed by frame-extracting his screen recording at 240fps and tracking the active chip's left edge with OpenCV — **three independent defects**. (1) The highlight vanished for ~217ms and the cause was **not the JS**: `.week-btn` carries `transition:all 0.15s` while the highlight advances every ~78ms, so no chip ever reached full fill. Fixed with a `wk-scooting` class suppressing the transition, removed at settle **and** in `_wkScootCancel` — a cancelled scoot must not strand transitions suppressed on the live strip. (2) The parallel highlight counter desynced because **chips are not uniform width** (plain "Wk 1" ~55px, current week with its dot ~67px, two-digit weeks wider); the highlight now derives from live scroll position so it shares the one number that moves the strip and desync is structurally impossible. (3) `easeOutBack` peaks at ~10% of travel — 40px on a 396px scroll — driving the chip to **−24px** off-screen, and an "out" easing starts at maximum velocity by definition, so it read as a jump-cut. Replaced with a two-phase `_wkScootEase` (ease-in-out cubic) and overshoot as a **capped pixel amount** (`min(12, to*0.05)`), not a ratio of distance. Predicted −24px from the easing math, measured −23px off the video: agreement within a pixel is what confirmed the diagnosis. **Two standing lessons, both about gates reporting PASS on broken code.** V146's strip suite passed 10/10 because a JS stub cannot observe CSS timing at all, and its uniform 62px chips made the broken counter look correct — **a stub's simplifying assumption can be the exact thing that hides the defect**. And the blast-radius check FALSE-PASSED because it diffed against V145, which no longer existed in the project: `diff` exited 2, stdout was empty, and empty read as "0 hunks, clean". `pipefail` did not help because the failure was inside a Python `subprocess.run` whose returncode was never checked. Now fails closed on rc==2, on rc==0 (byte-identical candidate — nothing was built), and on changes-reported-but-no-hunks. Sabotage also caught a **vacuous assertion**: T12 measured `steps[0]`, the frame where the animation clock initialises, where displacement is always exactly zero regardless of easing — it had been passing for every curve including the one it existed to reject.

- **V148 — the log form asked for a dimension the prescription already owned.** Week-7 Thursday prescribed 15 min continuous CHI at 9:37/mi; the log form asked for distance and pace, Mario logged 1.84 mi @ 9:15 = 17:01, and **the record contradicted the prescription**. `cardioFieldHTML(sport, e)` keyed on sport alone, so the structured numbers lived only in detail prose. **Doctrine ruling (GO ×2 + mock sign-off): the prescription owns the fixed dimension, the log captures the free one, the third derives.** Engine B now emits a `dose` field (`time` / `dist` / `reps_dist` / `reps_time`) so the form keys on data rather than parsing English; `doseDerived(dose, v)` is pure and shared by display and persist so the two cannot disagree; a fallback regex parser (`doseFromCardio`) covers frozen pre-V148 snapshots. §8.

- **V149 — the Load box forgot today.** Logging a weight, leaving the day and returning rendered the load box empty: the post-log render read the prescription rather than the day record. Restores today's logged weight from the day record on render. Same family as V131's draft split — a value on screen and a value in the store disagreeing about which is authoritative.

- **V150 — the collapsed SETS line speaks reps only.** The summary line was carrying a weight ladder, which duplicated what the VARIED badge and the Top Set relabel already say and made the line unreadable at a glance. Ruling: the SETS line states reps (flat / off-plan / varied); the weight story is told by the badge and the label. Mock signed before code.

- **V151 — one movement, one history thread.** Reported from device: Progress showed a flat-press row with a 70 lb best beside a fresh bench-press thread. **Two causes stacked.** `ia_exw_` keys on the slugged name and the stored row's LABEL is first-writer-wins, so Progress showed whichever spelling logged first; and V113's alias layer only resolves NEW writes, so old-key rows sit in localStorage forever as a second row. The universe was **harvested from the live engine, never hand-listed** — 4,536 builds, 0 failures, 268 names → 227 keys, clustered by movement-core signature. Real splits found and merged: `Dumbbell flat press`≡`Dumbbell bench press` (**both lived in the SAME pools** — the injury chest pool was offering one movement twice under two names), `Conventional deadlift`≡`Deadlift`, `Close-grip bench`≡`…press`, `Ab wheel`≡`Ab wheel rollouts`, `Plank`≡`Plank hold`, `Hip 90/90`≡`…stretch`, plus 14 circuit-prefixed `RAND_POOLS` names now logging to their base movement. Implement splits (barbell / DB / KB / cable / machine) stay separate per V113 doctrine, as do weighted-vs-bodyweight, travel, pause and tempo variants; **either/or circuit items are deliberately NOT aliased** because the store cannot know which movement was done. `foldExAliasKeys()` is the heal: lazy, idempotent, folds old-key rows into the canonical key, re-sorts entries by (week, ts), stamps the canonical name, runs at init and before the Progress charts — **ordered before the existing dedupe, because a fold can create a same-day pair.** **Two dead features revived, same bug family:** the e1RM test-set prefill indexed `getExWeights()[mainName]` and the range-top nudge indexed `store[it.name]`, both by RAW NAME against a slug-keyed store, both always undefined, both silently swallowed by `try/catch` — **the V139 double-progression nudge had never fired in production.** **Two latent engine bugs the gate surfaced:** `push_light`'s press pool excluded `chestMain` and `chestAcc[0]` but not `chestAcc[1]`, the day's own Pump item, so the light-press pick could duplicate it; and the pool's hard-coded `['Dumbbell incline press']` fallback walked straight back into that duplicate whenever the filtered pool emptied. V150 was shipping incline-main + incline-pump days at real seeds. Gate I, 17 asserts: 84-cfg retired-spelling sweep, 26 alias pairs against an **independently reimplemented slug**, live-VM log merge, fold idempotency, revived readers with a V150 dead-path proof, structural checks, and a V150↔V151 grid comparison over 2,000+ day-cells confined to the chest-slot universe with a no-duplicate-press invariant. Sabotage S9 (alias line deleted) trips 5 gates including the exact screenshot symptom; S10 (fold keeps the old key) trips I4. Blast radius 33 hunks, 100% classified. **Known churn, flagged: unfrozen future weeks can re-roll chest-accessory picks where pool composition changed — that is the dedupe filters finally seeing what they were built to catch.** §8/§11f/§12.

- **V152 — a re-render must not move the reader.** Reported from device: the minus button (`skipExercise`) threw the session card back to the top. Cause: `openDetail` ended with an unconditional `detailOverlay.scrollTop=0` — **first-open behaviour on a refresh path**, and `openDetail` is the re-render for eight in-session edits (skip/unskip exercise, skip/unskip superset, swap, undo swap, add, KB log, swap nudge). Fixed **inside** `openDetail` from the sheet’s own live state rather than by a caller-supplied flag, because a flag is a rule every future call site has to remember. `_dFresh` is captured at the top, **before `currentDayKey=dayKey` commits** (S3 proves the ordering: computing it after makes the day test tautological and trips O6), and requires **both** terms — deleting the sheet-open term (S2) passes the reported repro but breaks reopening a day after Back, since `closeDetail` leaves `scrollTop` and `currentDayKey` both alive (trips O5). Gate O, 9 asserts, runs the shipped file’s **own** `openDetail` extracted by anchor (count-asserted, never re-typed) in a VM against a DOM shim modelling `classList`/`scrollTop`; expectations are a hand-written end-state table, not a re-derivation of the candidate’s predicate. **Reach-proof: the V151 baseline trips O2 and O3 — the exact reported symptom.** S1 (tail reverted) trips O2/O3; 4/4 sabotage builds behaved as predicted. Blast radius 3 hunks, 100% classified (meta bump, head capture, tail conditional); `_dOv`/`_dFresh`/`_dKeep` occur 0 times in V151, 6 in V152; the other five sheets untouched and correct, since none of them re-render on close. **Path A flagged and deliberately not fixed:** `closeDetail` → `renderWeekView` and the week page’s window scroll — offered, not reported, parked to §12. §8/§10b/§11f/§12.

- **V153 — the calendar was fighting the athlete, not the athlete's rest days.** Reported from Mario's live onboarding (HALF MARATHON BOIZ, run_half, rest Sun+Wed, seed 22300): leg day → fartlek → long run consecutive, and the engine's own "tightly packed" note blaming the schedule. The measure pass proved the schedule innocent: **the even-spread day chooser picked {mon,tue,fri,sat} — the one 4-of-5 subset on which NO permutation of NRC types can avoid a hard back-to-back** — and `spaceHardCardio` was then handed an unsolvable board (it permutes types, never days). An NRC-faithful zero-collision week existed on the same rest days. Shipped: **F1** `_nrcSpacedRunDays` chooses days+types jointly (long pinned to last train day after the first chooser draft traded a Saturday long for a tiebreak — caught by Q6c); **F2** hinge-pattern ~RPE 7 clamp on any day preceding legLoad cardio (scheme arm + `hotNextHingeClampSweep` accessory arm) plus three honest note tiers. Gate Q, 25 asserts: independent adjacency math, control asserts on the V152 baseline (reach-proof: Q1a/Q2a/Q7 distinguish versions), NRC content-contract multiset (placement free, content verbatim), blast-radius partition (body-only byte-identical, non-NRC layouts unchanged, NRC-capped classified), Q8 pinning the E5 tiebreak. **Sabotage 4/4 — S3 initially tripped nothing and the differential sweep that followed proved E5 load-bearing on 28/84 configs (tiebreak preferences the greedy spacer cannot see), which is the sabotage discipline working: an untrippable guard is either dead or under-asserted, and the answer was under-asserted.** 84-config layout sweep + 60-config fuzz: 0 errors. **F4 audit finding: `support_prevention` is label-only** (no engine branch; support_strength/support_athletic measured genuinely distinct) — design session queued. §5/§6/§11f/§12.

- **V154 — the label finally does what it says.** F4 built same-session on Mario's GO ×5. The V153 audit had measured `support_prevention` as label-only; the week-1 dump made the case vivid: four pressing movements on the intervals day, power clean 7×3 max-intent stacked on the LONG-RUN day, zero calf work in a runner's injury-prevention program. Shipped as one flag + five mechanisms (see §11f). **Two mid-gate catches, one each way:** the first Gate R run caught the CODE — the no-load main writers hardcode their RPE-8 copy and the p-clamp never reaches them (fixed via `_effEase` on the beginner-ease seam), and caught the SWEEP demoting bodyweight mains to 2 sets (fixed by excluding the warmup-clause form); the second run caught the METRIC — R2 counted mains as accessories and failed the correct build, same class as the V115/V116/V146 harness lessons. Gate R 36 asserts: universe-harvested no-RPE-8 across four prevention configs including a bodyweight tier, 2-set accessory form, merged-circuit + calf structure, no max-intent copy, R6 partition (four non-prevention configs byte-identical V153→V154, four prevention configs changed), R7 controls proving V153 shows every defect, mains-≤7 with range parsing. Sabotage 6/6, each tripping exactly its predicted asserts (S4 trips the circuit AND both calf asserts; S6 trips both effort gates). 60-config fuzz clean. **Flagged for Mario:** the merged circuit extends the V123 patellar hold to non-runner prevention builds — a deliberate widening, amendable. §5/§11f/§12.

- **V155 — the pace the journal was burying.** Built on GO from the post-V154 scope. Rulings folded in: read-time resolution (B — `ia_hist_` is the prescription record, no write-time tag), recovery-only (long runs are progressions, their average pace is a mushier signal). Shipped: `recoveryPaceWeekly` + dedicated `buildPaceChart` (inverted axis up = faster, m:ss labels, plan-target dashed line, weekly average never best, ≥2-week render gate). Gate S 18 asserts against hand-written references incl. both guard terms of the missing-snapshot predicate; reach proof: V154 contains 0 occurrences of either function. **Gate-integrity catch mid-session: the S18 fixture amendment failed its anchor assert on an em-dash literal and the gate reran UNAMENDED at 17/17 — caught because the edit script's stderr was read before the number was trusted; redone with matching literals and hard chaining.** Sabotage 6/6 (SAB-C/D each delete one term of the compound snapshot guard and both trip S1 — both terms load-bearing; C/D halt the harness at the null deref after tripping, an honest cascade). Blast radius 3 hunks (version / functions / render call), fail-closed on rc 0 and 2, purely additive outside the meta bump. Fuzz 500 hostile trials (junk paces, null cardio, missing snapshots, out-of-range weeks): 0 errors. §8/§12.

- **V156 — programs get a lifecycle, and a new block learns from the last one.** Scoped from Mario's question: 7 weeks of data on one program — what ports to the next? Design session produced D1–D8, then G1–G3 (Mario rejected the all-time merged chart for per-program chips; concurred baseline+pace seed scope; concurred PACE_CHART inversion). Measure pass findings: NO delete confirm existed at all; every store already carries `ts` so the 21-day window needed zero schema work; the seed's insertion point is two wizard fields, leaving `buildProgram()` byte-untouched. **Spec amendment flagged mid-build (G2): the wizard's baseline field is single-run capability, not weekly mileage — seeded from max single run in window.** Shipped: archive/two-tier delete (`archiveProg`/`_handoffActiveFrom`/tier-2-only `purgeProgData`), per-program Progress selector (`progressViewId` + `*For(pid)` accessors, fold/dedupe writes to the viewed store), wizard seed (`seedFromPriorPrograms` + `_mileFromRecoverySec`, `_recoveryPaceEntry` extracted verbatim from V155's chart so both consumers share one predicate). Gate T 48 asserts: 14-prefix archive-survival vs purge-death, active handoff incl. last-program clear, archived exclusion from home/setActive/boot/delete-fallback, two-tier DOM paths, T7 scoping-leak assert (viewed ≠ active, journal-note discriminator), seed vs independently reimplemented PACE_CHART inversion, 21-day boundary at exact-cut/cut−1ms/future, min-runs floor, predicate terms singly, most-recent-wins, rounding carry (59.5s→60→+1min), S1-style recoveryPaceWeekly preservation. **Sabotage 11/11 — the sweep caught a vacuous term: deleting `!snap.cardio` survived the gate until a snapshot-without-cardio fixture existed (lift-only day); with it, the sabotage halts as an honest cascade (V155 precedent).** **Fuzz (500 hostile trials) caught a LATENT V155 DEFECT: non-string snapshot `type` throws in the predicate — unreachable from the engine's own writers, reachable the moment the seed read other programs' stores. `String()` hardened; promoted to §10b.** Blast radius 20 hunks, 100% classified, fail-closed rc guard. §4/§5/§8/§11f/§12.
- **V157 — a base block gets a doctrine: time on feet, effort not pace, never taper.** Started as a support question (user seed 35757: "2 mi at 12:53, will it build, does a user get faster?") — full program reconstruction proved the anchor was a ~10:06 entered mile best (chart recovery column, arithmetically correct Zone 2) but exposed three defects: pace frozen for all 12 sessions (paceProgression is pace_goal-only), zero quality work at 2 run days, and a SILENT taper (progression builder tapered off eventTargeted while buildRunSession's taperWks read the volFreeze-aware answer 0 — arrays shrank, no label). Elite-coach doctrine session produced the ruling: base = frequency > duration > steady > strides, pace is an output measured not prescribed. Mario's rulings: time-Rx `run_base` only; ceiling not effort-only (steady pace `(tempo+recovery)/2`, defended against Daniels — our chart is FASTER than VDOT at every anchor, "sluggish" paces are slow anchors not slow prescriptions); benchmark + frequency growth deferred to Builds 2–3 (§12); run/walk line below 14:00/mi anchors. Shipped V157 = Build 1: taper killed at array source per-goal, time doses `{k:'time',mins,tgt,cap}` (cap additive), `Easy Run` subtypes + `EASY` code, strides via `_lastEasyRunDay`/arguments[17] (flag deliberately unscoped — goal term proven vacuous by sabotage, single guard at render branch), walk-break permission. Gate U 35 asserts incl. independent hand-copied chart-row interpolation for tgt/cap/minutes, strides day×week matrix, unfrozen-path peak property, and byte-identical controls (pace_goal + 5K stay dist-dosed). **Gate-widening catch: SAB-H2 (delete walk-break in lsd_long) survived until a beginner+athletic program exercised the long branch — frozen path never builds a long.** Sabotage 8/8. **Fuzz caught a latent V156 NaN: junk experience string indexes tier tables to undefined → NaN arrays ("NaN mi" confirmed on V156 baseline); normalized to known tiers.** Blast radius 7 hunks, 100% classified, rc guard 1/0/2 explicit. §5/§11f/§12.
- **V158 — "did I get faster" gets a measurement.** run_base Build 2 on Mario’s GO, scope pre-ruled in §12. Shipped: benchmark branch in buildRunSession (arguments[18]; wk1 `Benchmark Run` / final `Benchmark Run — Retest`, retest predicate `week===tw && tw>1`), scheduler day pick (`_benchDayFor`: first easy wk1 / last easy final, displacing final-week strides by design), `TEST` session code, render-time read-back (`_benchmarkEntryFor` + `_benchStripHTML` injected in buildCardioHTML — baseline strip, post-log delta with honest three-way verdict, degrade-to-instructions on missing wk1). Anti-anchor extended to prescriptions: benchmark dose has NO tgt/cap and no pace anywhere in its text. **Measure-pass catch that changed the build: the ruling’s "takes the steady-day slot" case is unreachable — allocation caps run_base at ≤3 run days in every config tried (2/3 days at 5–7 train days, hybrid AND event), layout deals chi at 4+ only. The chi-aware arm was removed as unprovable dead code and the obligation moved to Build 3 (§12), where frequency growth makes it real.** Gate V 23 asserts (placement matrix, dose anti-anchor, controls: pace_goal/5K get zero benchmarks, synthetic-store read-back with hand-computed 774s/714s/60s-delta reference, swap/missing-snapshot/wrong-subtype/corrupt-store exclusions); Gate U re-versioned for the population split (37/37 — every V157 invariant still binds on easies, benchmarks asserted separately). Sabotage 10/10 — **harness lesson: textual-detection sweeps must rc-shield the gate invocations; a CAUGHT sabotage exits node 1 and pipefail hands that to the capture, killing the sweep as a false abort (bit on SAB-3 when only the SECOND gate failed).** Fuzz 250 build trials + 48 hostile store-pair combos: 0 errors. Blast radius 7 hunks, 100% classified, rc guard 1/0/2. §5/§8/§12.
- **V159 — Build 3: frequency is the lever, duration is the cap.** Design session first per standing cadence; measure pass relocated the true target — §12 blamed `SPORT_CEILINGS.run_base:3`, but the hybrid 2-day chop lives in the governor’s explicit `baseCardioPlan` calendar, a different edit entirely. Rulings: D1 thirds ladder (Mario challenged "why", defended on tendon/bone adaptation lag + redistribution math + Daniels 4-wk windows — concurred); D2 amended BY MARIO from my flat 40-cap to a scaling long-run cap after he pushed "would 60 min meet the intent of building a base?" — answer: yes, on doctrine (NSW LSD runs 30–60), shipped as 40→60 by +5/2wks past 6; D4a ruled all-easy for hybrid after an honest two-sided steady-day case (offered D4a-ii steady-at-four as a real option; Mario took all-easy); D4b ceiling 3→4. Shipped: freq plan (activeByWeek/minsByWeek/longCap) in baseCardioPlan, dispatch week-skip + arguments[19] minutes, week-aware strides/bench helpers, chi arm restored AND REACHABLE (unfrozen 4-day deals chi; benchmark takes the steady slot — V158 obligation discharged, W18). **Latch caught at edit time: the plan-assembly tail rewrote baseCardioPlan unconditionally and would have silently stripped `freq` — guarded with `if(!_runBaseFreq)`, sabotage T11 proves it.** Gate W 40 asserts with the budget math re-derived independently (refPlan) incl. bench-week totals (budget minus exactly the replaced slot), hand-derived tw=10 max=50, spacing statement (≥2-day gaps at n≤3, adjacency ACCEPTED at 4-in-6); Gate UV159 carries every surviving V157/V158 invariant population-generically. Sabotage 11/11. **SAB-3 differential sweep: the base 40-clamp is arithmetically shadowed by the budget clamp at all reachable tw (6–10) — kept as D2’s ruling-as-code with the shadow documented, sabotage retargeted to the load-bearing floor-5.** **Fuzz predicate lesson: D2’s caps are the GOVERNOR’s lever — the unfrozen path’s dist-derived 65-min long at a 14:00 anchor is correct, not a violation; invariants must scope to the path that owns them.** 120-program fuzz clean, 86 with growth. Blast radius 8 hunks, 100% classified. §5/§12.
- **V160 — the pause squat comes off the endurance wave: rep affinity.** Started as a coaching question off Mario's live screenshot (HALF MARATHON BOIZ, seed 22300: `Paused back squat` 3×12 @ RPE 6.5, week 1). Diagnosis chain proved end-to-end: Injury Prevention focus → endurance goal → `waveBase.endurance` wk1 `{s:3,r:'12',p:.52}`; flat `EXLIB.squat` pool decoupled from the goal-driven scheme; the app's own curated content already ran the lift at 4×4/3×5 (internal doctrine inconsistency, the strongest argument). Measure pass BEFORE design per cadence: 170-name universe harvest found 15 tempo/iso candidates, exactly ONE in a wave-schemed pool — reported against my own generality claim before Mario's D2 concur was spent. Two self-corrections on record: the 3×12 was endurance, not hypertrophy as first argued (made the case stronger); the deload pushes strength to 7 reps, not the 9 first claimed (computed, not guessed — narrowed D2-c to its real bite: tw≥12 strength/athletic survival). Rulings D2-a (filter at selection vs base-wave max), D2-b (sidecar map), D2-c (base wave only, deload/taper exempt), D2-d (cap 6), E1 (three wave-main call sites only), E2 (fail closed). Shipped exactly that; blast radius 3 hunks, 100% classified, rc guard explicit. Gate X 47/47 with hand-table references (base maxima str6/ath6/bal8/hyp10/end12/fat15 read off waveBase by eye, never engine-computed) incl. per-goal exclusion ×40 seeds, inclusion reachability ×120 seeds, D2-c survival at tw 12/14/16, tw-invariance of membership, injury-overlay pass-through ×10 configs, and a 25/25-per-goal BYTE-IDENTITY differential against a live V159 context on the identity goals (strength/athletic) — the wrap changed nothing it wasn't supposed to. Reach proof on the live config itself: V159 @ 22300 = 6 occurrences, V160 = 0, swap universe clean. Sabotage 6/6 (untagged-passthrough term, +3 post-transform flip, cap-15 widen, V127-latch on the throw — each trips its gate; FC positive proves the throw fires on aggressive tags; control green). Fuzz 600 configs (hostile experience strings incl. the V157 NaN vector, all equipment tiers, 5 injury variants, 4 cardio shapes): 0 throws, 0 invariant violations, 43 programs legitimately drew the pause on eligible goals. Swap-universe filtering confirmed a designed consequence, not a leak (scheme follows the slot). §5/§11f/§12.
- **V161 — the box squat follows the pause squat off the high-rep waves.** Reported off Mario's live screenshot (HALF MANNY, seed 76308, Injury Prevention → endurance, 14 wk): `Barbell box squat` 3×13 @ RPE 6, week 4. Measure pass: not a V160 regression — the wk-4 SEAL-PFG deload (+3 reps on endurance base 10) stacking on a `REP_AFFINITY` map that deliberately held one entry. Initial lean D1-a (no cap; recovery load excuses the reps) was OVERTURNED by Mario's coaching challenge — a paused/box squat is a power lift; the honest dead stop dies past ~6 reps, and past that the set is either bracing endurance or touch-and-go taps. Shipped D1-b: `'Barbell box squat':{maxReps:6}` — one line, every V160 consumer (`_repFit`, swap harvest, fail-closed throw) inherits. Gate: 275-build fuzz against the independent hand base-max table, 14,215 mains, 0 illegal box mains, 63 legal strength/athletic draws (no over-exclusion); sabotage (entry stripped) trips; blast radius exactly 2 hunks. Live-seed proof: 76308 wk4 Tue → `Back squat 3×13`, 0 box appearances in 14 wks. Flagged, no action: close-grip bench shares the 0.90 load bucket but is range-biased, not dead-stop — coherent at high reps. Parked: `Squat to box` (tension-held depth gauge) as a NEW named movement if knee work ever wants the high-rep version back.
- **V167 — the ledger stops estimating, and the gate harness gets caught lying.** Mario: the Lift Ledger is awful. It was. It headlined e1RM, which the engine refuses to prescribe from, and `max()` across mixed rep ranges made a 295×1 score below a 285×2 — a PR single reported as a regression. Rebuilt: mains show the heaviest real set plus working weight; accessories get the **Range Ladder** built on V137's own double-progression rule, which is the only thing on the Progress screen that tells the athlete what to do next session. D11 settled by measurement — 26 of 27 main-slot names are also accessories, so classification moved to the frozen SLOT and the roster review Mario wanted dissolved: there is no list to agree on. **The session's real finding was in the tooling.** The sabotage harness scored a crashed gate as SURVIVED, so five of eleven mutations looked like uncaught guards when the gate had actually died on each. Fixed, hardened, and the V165/V166 sweeps re-run under the corrected harness — both still clean. Two of the remaining holes turned out to be blind fixtures rather than missing assertions. 39+41+87 green on the shipped artifact, 11/11 sabotage, 3 hunks with 4 removed lines all classified, engine digest unchanged across 450 configs. §10b/§11f/§12.
- **V166 — the easy/hard module answers the question it asks, and a stub dies.** Mario's device verdict killed V165's class-scatter: he could not tell what it showed. Root cause was framing, not rendering — the chart compared his easy runs to his hard runs when the title asks whether his easy runs are easy. Three variants mocked in healthy AND broken states; ruled C, comparing each easy run to the ceiling its own session was written with. Mock-to-build gap found immediately: `dose.cap` only exists on run_base, so every event program would have rendered without the ceiling that is the entire point — solved by deriving it from PACE_CHART's tempo column via the engine's own `(tempo+recovery)/2`. `_derived` deleted across 4 sites. **The gate earned its keep twice:** it caught a clamp that broke the tempo/recovery pairing (corrupt target → 3:03/mi ceiling → every run flagged), and it caught one of its own assertions matching the deletion's tombstone comment instead of code. **The differential earned its keep once:** the first control failed to move the digest, and rather than accept a green result the investigation found `scheme()` ignores its RM arguments by design — a dead chain now filed in §12, and a valid wave control substituted. 41 + 87 assertions green, 11/11 sabotage, 7 hunks classified, 450-config `prog.weeks` byte-identical. §5/§8/§11f/§12.
- **V165 — the Progress screen stops counting and starts answering.** Design-first, six rulings before code. Mario killed the proposed tonnage flagship on coaching grounds ("nobody cares about total tonnage across time") — correct: tonnage moves on exercise selection, deloads and implement swaps, i.e. for reasons that are not progress. Rebuilt around athlete questions. Shipped six free-tier modules (Ladder / class bands / same-effort / projection / drift / ledger) plus the restored V164 mileage chart. The build's own findings mattered more than the charts: **the `ia_hist_` `subtype` string was already a full intensity taxonomy Progress had never read**, and `PACE_CHART` already carried `halfBest` as a finish time, so the projection is a table lookup rather than a model. Two dimensional bugs caught pre-ship — 1RM-vs-working-weight subtraction, and `DEFAULT_1RM` used as an athlete baseline (which fabricates progress for a strong beginner). D6 was ruled "exclude" and then deliberately NOT applied uniformly: accumulators exclude the in-progress week, per-observation metrics keep it. Gates: 87 assertions with independently re-derived reference interpolators; 18/18 sabotage tripped; blast radius 4 hunks 100% classified — **and the diff, not the gate, caught that the build had silently deleted a shipped chart** (§10b). Mock-first found two more before any code: a ghost-bar encoding that hid the gap it existed to show, and a cream in-bar label that clipped to one character. §5/§8/§10b/§11f/§12.
- **Post-V154 rulings session — no build.** Reported: log form mismatched on NRC workouts only. Measure pass: not a dropped build — `buildNRCSession` doses long+recovery but deliberately nulls speed days (V148's documented "complex NRC speed work" boundary), and `doseFromCardio` correctly declines Nike's clock notation. Progress-side audit closed the design question: run logs feed exactly `run_dist` (weekly mileage) and `rpe`; `run_pace` is derived+stored on every dosed session and charted nowhere. Ruled: generic form is CORRECT for the rotating speed slot (non-comparable sessions, and dosing them would undercount mileage via work-only distance); the tracking win is a pace-trend chart on the already-captured long/recovery data — scoped to §12, design before build. §8/§11f/§12.

---

- **V182 — The rolly wheel.** Mario, one screenshot of the generic run form: "can we make these instead of text boxes the rolly wheel options." Measured before mocking: that form is 18.8% of run sessions and fires on NRC speed days *only* — everything else derives its pace — so the pace text box was a fallback-only control, and the one place in the app where a colon must be typed into a `type="text"` field. The measure pass then found the reason to build it: `_paceStrToSec` demands two-digit seconds, so `9:3` / `930` / `9.5` saved cleanly and vanished from the recovery-pace chart and the wizard re-seed with no error. Mocked four panels plus a curved iOS variant to make the DS rejection deliberate; Mario overruled the recommendation twice — wheels on miles too (I had argued distance should stay typed), then miles split whole × tenths on ergonomics after the first cut gave it one 1,000-row column. He asked whether it needed a Log button; it does not, and the answer was already in the file — `persistLogFields` autosaves on `input` and `handleDayStatus` owns the commit, so the wheels drive hidden inputs carrying the original ids and neither the persist function nor either listener array changed. Bounds measured, not chosen (pace 4–17 against a 5:00–14:30 prescription envelope; rep time 0–9 because the only rep distances are 400m and 800m). Gates 2,662/0; sabotage 15/15 after S13 exposed an index clamp no assertion had ever reached; fuzz 864 engine-identical configs, 900 form shapes, 20,000 hostile values, 0 violations; 7 hunks 100% classified, +129/−7. **Two gate-integrity catches, both mine:** the identity fuzz first reported 864/864 differing because it was comparing `Date.now()`-derived ids and seeds rather than the engine — the baseline was never self-stable, and the suite now asserts that precondition; and G14 initially trapped a missing wheel as a thrown `undefined` instead of a named failure. Race alignment slides to V183.

- **V183 — the button that followed you down the page.** Mario wanted Done moved to the bottom so you have to scroll to reach it: "it's just present all throughout the page which is unsightly and almost rushing the user to be done." The measure pass turned a visual complaint into a correctness one — sticky meant Done was reachable from above Log This Session, so the day could be closed before it was logged. Three placements mocked in a scrollable phone frame; Mario ruled inline-bare after asking what actually separated B from C, which was a fair question since the answer was one hairline and a background tint. Recorded as a minor call, not a ruling to defend. `.foot-hint` swept in the same hunk (dead since V181 retired Partial). Gates 30/0 through an independent CSS-rule reader; 14/14 sabotage; V182's 2,662 assertions and fuzz re-run green; **extracted JS byte-identical to V182 with a control proving the comparison could fail** — the build is provably CSS-only. 2 hunks, 100% classified. **The gate's own reader was wrong twice before it was right:** the selector was double-escaped so two live classes reported as missing, and the usage scan matched the deletion's own tombstone comment — see §10b. **Filename correction this session:** V182 shipped under the V181 name; the stable-filename rule covers the handoff only, and the HTML is renamed every build. Race alignment slides to V184.

- **V190 — D39, the section owns the round count.** Mario off a device screenshot of THE HALF MANNY W2 Saturday: *“how did the workout say 3 rounds but the workouts say 2 sets?”* The banner was never prescribed. A superset’s rounds and its members’ sets were always ONE number (`vsets(3)` at the call site), carried only in each member’s detail string, and three readers tried to recover it with `^(\d+)×` — a regex blind to the no-load `N sets` grammar that `bodyweightSweep` rewrites 21.9% of items into — all three falling to a hardcoded 3. Measured over 1,260 builds / 85,977 superset sections: **41.2% of banners fabricated, 29.5% contradicting their own members**, and the second reader, `sessionTimeEst`, shipped a wrong duration on **45.7% of training days** (mean 4.3 min, worst 6.4). A separate defect fell out of the same sweep: D2’s `preventionDoseSweep` rewrote members per item and skipped the `_GRAMMAR_BALLISTIC` ones, desyncing 262/6,889 prevention supersets against 0/42,423 everywhere else. Shipped D39-i–v: `sec.rounds` written at all 23 generator call sites from the same expression that writes the members; one resolver `_secRounds` with **no fallback constant**; the header synthesizes “N Rounds” only when there is a real N; member rows lose the count from the rendered string only (`i.detail` untouched — `parseRx` has five callers and the add-sheet was already correct); `sessionTimeEst` reads the same oracle; D2 halves `sec.rounds` once and normalises members to it. Gates 16/16 over 2,268 builds / 159,957 sections against an oracle derived from the authoring contract and never from `_secRounds`; 8/8 sabotage, 0 survivors, each tripping a **different** named gate; fabricated / contradicted / desynced / wrong-duration all 0.0% after; 1,080 hunks over 756 configs, 36 differing and all `support_prevention`, 100% classified into three classes with one root. Details in the header paragraph and §11f.
- **V189 — D36 + D37 + D38, the coaching slate from the program review.** Hinge rides a speed day and never the long-run eve (`nrcLegLiftPlacement`, one cost table shared with the region pass); shakeout the day before the race (speed traded back, recovery run moved to the eve or two out); no lifting from two days out through the race, time trial included, D34's primer overturned. MANNY: Thu speed + hinge, Fri easy + light upper, race week Fri rest / Sat shakeout / Sun race, no lift on either. Gates 12,344/0 over 1,020 configs, 155 non-NRC byte-identical, sabotage 15/15, 8 hunks +130/−4 classified. Details in the header paragraph and §11f.
- **V188 — D14a, race alignment.** Count back from the race. Derived read-only start, behind-you weeks, future start when early, floors with a shorter-plan offer, race-week pin by subtype, Monday→Sunday layout for race goals, Race Day control on the program, and the no-source rule. Details in the header paragraph and §11f.
- **V187 — the NRC fidelity batch (F2 + F6 + F7).** Five runs on the support track at ≥6 days, the dress-rehearsal note, Nike's effort scale on the pace chips. D14a slid to V188 by Mario's choice. Details in the header paragraph.
- **V186 — race → NRC, test → NSW.** NRC 5K/10K tables transcribed and routed; fixed 8 weeks; time trial when no race; taper and length made to agree with that; F3/F4/F5 folded. Full entry under §11f "### V186".
- **V184 — "a roller it just feels confusing to the eyes."** Mario rejected every option in the previous mock round — native `<select>`, paper well, frosted glass — and asked instead for a third miles column, thinner columns, a little depth, and something that actually reads as a roller. The diagnosis came off his screenshot: the band's hairlines and the column separators were crossing and tessellating the strip into a spreadsheet, and with every row the same size nothing said either which row was chosen or that the strip was curved. The fix that mattered was the size ramp, which none of the earlier mocks had — rows shrink and fade from centre and it reads as a drum with no 3D transform at all. Measured the width budget rather than guessing: flexed 3:2, five columns are 59pt on the smallest iPhone, so my V183 "stack them" recommendation was over-cautious and I said so. Round-robin derives from the dash row rather than a hand-list. Gates 117/0, sabotage 20/20, fuzz 0 violations across 864 configs / 900 form shapes / 20,000 hostile values, 6 hunks 100% classified. **The gate needed three fixes before it was trustworthy, and one is serious:** the harness's `requestAnimationFrame` stub never invoked its callback, so `iaWheelInit`'s seeding had never been gated — in V182 either — and a sabotage that broke seeding passed clean; F3's distance reference was hard-coded to one decimal and cried 4,121 false violations the moment a column was added; and a direct index into the build's own spec made a missing-column sabotage crash rather than report, which scores identically to a dead gate. All three are in §10b. Also corrected this session: process substitution `<(...)` silently ate an edit script, which §10b already warned about — the tell was a bundle byte count that did not move after an edit that added code. Race alignment slides to V185.

### V186 — race → NRC, test → NSW (Mario: "i concur with all GO")

The session opened as an audit: compare the four NRC guides to the build. The half and marathon tables were clean to the line — chart 15×11 exact, all 32 weeks' long runs, speed segments, recoveries and flags verified by an independent hand-seconds parser over the OCR pages — and V153's spacing, D6's progression-run definition, D34's race day all held. Eight findings ranked; the one that mattered was **F1: the 5K and 10K plans were in the project and never used.** `Run a 5K` / `Run a 10K` routed to NSW and got 3 runs a week, one quality session, nothing above 5K pace, and no race day. Mario asked the right question — is NSW actually worse, or just different? — and the answer was that NSW is the PST's program and NRC is the race's, and the app's own half/marathon argument ("nobody trains for a half under a tactical mandate") applies one distance down. Eight calls ruled in one message, all concurred.

**Two of the shipped rules were not in the slate; the gate and the fuzz wrote them.** §4b: the time trial said "this is your race" while the taper read `eventTargeted` and lifted Power + Strength on the day — `taperEventOn(cfg)`. §3b: `calcProgramLength` is a max across sports; a 5K + swim base produced 12 weeks and the 8-row table clamped weeks 9–12 to race week — an NRC goal now pins the length. Half and marathon had only been safe because 14 and 18 always won.

F3/F4/F5 rode along because they touch the exact selector 5K/10K run through: half W9 is a hold; race-week recovery indices go by calendar not slot; race week keeps Nike's LAST N recovery runs, because keep-longest was dropping the shakeout on every plan and every layout. The fuzz's own false positives found the pre-existing Sunday-race quirk (§12 watch 1, D14a's job). Gates 379/0, sabotage 18/18 (0 not-applied), NSW byte-identical, half/marathon differential 100% classified, fuzz 1,000/0, 23 hunks classified.

### V185 — "you don't have KB single arm press in here because you think it's a chest exercise right?" (Mario: "concur on your recommendations GO")

Mario caught it off a device screenshot of the swap sheet, and the framing in his question was the whole diagnosis: the sheet was offering bench presses as like-for-like substitutes for an overhead press. **The library already agreed with him** — `EXLIB.shoulder` lists the KB single-arm press next to Barbell overhead press and Arnold press. `_pattern` disagreed with the pool, and `_pattern` is what the swap sheet reads. The bare `/press/` catch-all defaulted horizontal and the two real exercises that reached it were both overhead, so it was wrong on everything it caught. Recommendation was to name the vertical presses rather than flip the default, because a future `Floor press` lands on that catch-all and is horizontal.

**The sweep that found it found a bigger one.** Scoring every `EXLIB` pool against `_pattern` showed every pulldown in the app classified as a row — `lat pull` in the vpull rule had been dead code since it was written. That intersected `lowback/protect`, which drops the `row` pattern *and* whose own pool override hands that athlete `rowPool=['Straight-arm pulldown']` plus a `backCompoundPool` with `Lat pulldown`, chosen explicitly for being spine-safe. The post-filter deleted both. **The comment above the override describes the opposite of what shipped.** G3 asserts the drop on V184 and the repair on V185, so the bug is proven rather than argued. D2-c went in alongside because D2 would otherwise have walked pulldowns off the `elbow/workaround` cap.

Gates 163/0 across three independent gates — a doctrine-derived expectation table written before reading the regex, a full-universe V184→V185 delta with a 6-name intended-change allow-list, and an overlay gate running the real `injuryPlan`/`applyInjuryFilter` from both builds. 8/8 sabotage. 4 hunks, 100% classified: 3 lines removed, 5 non-comment lines added. **The gate needed fixing before it was trustworthy, again.** The first sabotage run scored a suspiciously clean 8/8 with every mutation tripping every gate — relative module paths meant Node was throwing MODULE_NOT_FOUND and the crash read as a trip. After the fix, two mutations survived; the differential sweep showed both changed zero classifications, so the sabotages were rewritten rather than the gates. All three lessons are in §10b. Race alignment slides to V186.

### V190 — "how did the workout say 3 rounds but the workouts say 2 sets?" (Mario: concur × 5)

A device screenshot of THE HALF MANNY W2 Saturday: a `3 ROUNDS — NO REST BETWEEN EXERCISES · 60S REST BETWEEN ROUNDS` banner over Dips and Inverted rows both reading `2 sets — RPE 7`. Mario's read was that the rows should not carry sets at all if the block commands rounds, and that breaking the block should revert each movement to its own prescription. Both halves were right, and the mechanism underneath was not what either of us assumed first.

**Nobody prescribed the 3.** `vsets(3)` at a call site means "3 rounds of this pair"; the number was stamped onto each member's `detail` because the string was the only carrier a section had, and the renderer then tried to read it back out with `^(\d+)×` — a regex that does not know the `N sets — RPE x` grammar `_bwSetsFromDetail` rewrites 21.9% of items into. It fell to a hardcoded `3`. My first framing to Mario — that a dose pass was overriding the banner — was **wrong and corrected in session**: the members are not an independent authority, they are a copy of the section's own number, and the banner was a failed read of that copy. 1,260 builds / 85,977 superset sections: 41.2% fabricated, 29.5% contradicted.

The measure pass then found **three more readers of the same brittle regex**, not one. `sessionTimeEst` carried its own copy with its own hardcoded 3 — 45.7% of training days shipped a wrong duration, mean 4.3 min. `recoveryDeload`'s power cut silently no-ops on the same grammar. And `preventionDoseSweep` (D2), rewriting per item, reached only the members carrying `@ RPE` and skipped the `_GRAMMAR_BALLISTIC` ones: **3.80% of prevention supersets desynced against exactly 0.00% on all six other focuses**, which isolated the pass as the sole source without further work. In the preset library the fabricated banner was not off-by-one but a category error — `3 Rounds — no rest between exercises` printed over `Work to 1–3RM`, over a 10-set max-reps block, over a 10-minute AMRAP.

Shipped D39-i–v: the count becomes a field written at all 23 generator call sites from the same expression that writes the members; one resolver with the fallback constant **deleted rather than improved** — when nothing can state a round count, the header shows the section label, which is already there and always true; the member strip is display-only because `parseRx` has five callers and one of them was correct before this build; `sessionTimeEst` reads the same oracle; D2 moves onto the section. D39-iv was **amended during the build and the amendment ruled**: "leave member strings alone" cannot hold under D39-v, so D2 normalises members to the section instead — the section is the writer, the members an enforced copy.

Gates 16/16 across 2,268 builds / 159,957 sections against an oracle derived from the authoring contract rather than from the code under test. 8/8 sabotage, 0 survivors, and each mutation tripped a **different** gate rather than all of them. After-picture: fabricated, contradicted, desynced and wrong-duration all 0.0%. **The blast radius found what the gates did not think to ask.** 1,080 hunks, 36 configs, all prevention; the five structural ones traced to `capSessionBudget` being charged 3 sets for a member the dose had capped at 2 — and paying for that phantom set by evicting terminal knee extension on an injury-prevention build. That became G12. Preset work came in at 9 sections, not the 36 the old derivation implied, because the both-grammar min recovers the rest honestly; one near-miss caught and reverted where a heuristic outvoted an explicit `8 rounds` label.

## Session entry template (fill, then fold — delete nothing above)

At session end, work this list in order. Each numbered step names where the content lands, so nothing ends up only in the digest.

1. **Current state** — bump the working filename and `ia-version`, rewrite the "Most recent work" bullet, update the live-program line if Mario's config moved.
2. **Numbered sections** — fold durable behavior in beside the code it describes (`§4` persistence, `§5` engine, `§6` fatigue/recovery, `§7` DS/platform, `§8` logging/progress, `§8b` swap, `§9` workflow).
3. **§10b** — promote any standing lesson that outlives this version. Generic engineering doctrine only; version-specific detail stays in the digest.
4. **§11f** — record every ruling Mario made, **with the reasoning that produced it**, so the next session can argue against it instead of re-deriving it.
5. **§12** — add what is carried forward; **delete what this build closed** rather than annotating it as closed.
6. **Digest** — append ONE entry at the tail, in the established voice: what was reported, what the measure pass found, what shipped, what the gate proved, what was flagged.
7. **Session-start check** — `grep 'ia-version'` on the file AND confirm a digest line exists for that exact version. **A file version with no digest line means a build was dropped** (see the V112 collision under Load-bearing invariants).
